### CRITICAL (2)

#### `host-expert-1` — OnInit's object sweep deletes the handover stash before it is read, so the second pass of one-window mode can never find its origin symbol

**`MQL5/Experts/SSReplay/SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1806`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
   SSRPurgeChart(0, SSR_PICK_LINE);
...
   string stashed = ReadStashedOrigin();          // line 1869
...
   if(ObjectFind(0, SSR_PICK_STASH) >= 0)         // line 1884
```

**What goes wrong:** With the shipped defaults (InpOneChart=true), pass 1 stashes the origin in the chart object SSR_ORIGIN_HANDOFF and hands the chart to the replay symbol. Pass 2's OnInit sweeps that object away at line 1806, 63 lines before it reads it at 1869, so stashed=="", origin=="" at line 1903, the on_replay && origin=="" branch fires at 1911 and FailInit() returns INIT_FAILED. Because g_origin is "", FailInit's chart-restore branch (825: `if(g_on_replay_chart && g_origin != "")`) does nothing, so the user is left on a chart showing a custom replay symbol with no expert on it and no automatic way back - the exact outcome the FailInit comment (810-821) exists to prevent. The picked start (SSR_PICK_HANDOFF) and the failed-pass poison ("!"+origin) are destroyed by the same sweep, so neither the recovery path nor the picked window can survive the handover either.

**Why:** SSRPurgeChart (Ui/SSR_Widgets.mqh:676-694) enumerates ObjectsTotal(chart,-1,-1) and deletes every name where StringFind(nm,"SSR")==0 unless nm equals the single `keep` argument. Both handoff objects are named with that prefix: #define SSR_HANDOFF "SSR_ORIGIN_HANDOFF" (line 536) and #define SSR_PICK_STASH "SSR_PICK_HANDOFF" (line 556); keep is SSR_PICK_LINE ("SSR_PICK_LINE", Ui/SSR_Theme.mqh:508). Both are created on chart 0 (ObjectCreate(0, SSR_HANDOFF, ...) line 540; ObjectCreate(0, SSR_PICK_STASH, ...) line 2536) - the same chart the sweep runs on, and the same chart that becomes the replay chart. The sweep is the second statement of OnInit and is unconditional. git log -S confirms the call was moved into OnInit by commit f3f7ddb (v102), after the stash mechanism it breaks (v55): this is a regression, not an original design. The claim needs no assumption about whether objects survive ChartSetSymbolPeriod - the delete and the read are in the same pass of the same function.

**Verifiers:** Traced exactly as described. SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1806 `SSRPurgeChart(0, SSR_PICK_LINE)` is unconditional and is the second statement of OnInit; SSR_Widgets.mqh:676-694 enumerates ObjectsTotal(chart,-1,-1) and deletes every object whose name starts with "SSR" except the single `keep` name (SSR_PICK_LINE, SSR_Theme.mqh:508). Both handoff objects are SSR-prefixed and created on chart 0 (SSR_ORIGIN_HANDOFF, #define at 536, created in StashOrigin; SSR_PICK_HANDOFF created at ~2536). ReadStashedOrigin() runs 63 lines later at 1869 and ObjectFind(0,SSR_PICK_STASH) at 1884 - both after the delete, in the same invocation. So on pass 2 stashed=="" -> origin=="" (1903) -> the on_replay&&origin=="" branch at 1911 -> FailInit(), whose restore branch is gated on g_origin != "" and therefore does nothing. No guard, clamp or earlier check anywhere prevents this; the outcome holds whether or not objects survive ChartSetSymbolPeriod. With the shipped default InpOneChart=true this makes every one-window run fail on the pass that owns the replay chart and strands the user on a custom-symbol chart with no expert / Provable from source with no runtime assumption required. SSRPurgeChart (Ui/SSR_Widgets.mqh:675-693) enumerates ObjectsTotal(chart,-1,-1) and deletes every object whose name satisfies StringFind(nm,"SSR")==0 unless it equals the single `keep` argument, which is SSR_PICK_LINE (Ui/SSR_Theme.mqh:508). Both handover objects match that prefix - SSR_HANDOFF="SSR_ORIGIN_HANDOFF" (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:536) and SSR_PICK_STASH="SSR_PICK_HANDOFF" (556) - and both are created on chart 0 (540, 2536), the same chart the sweep runs on. The sweep is at line 1806, unconditional; ReadStashedOrigin() is at 1869 and ObjectFind(0,SSR_PICK_STASH) at 1884, i.e. 63 and 78 lines later in the same invocation of OnInit. So `stashed` is always "", origin is "" at 1903, the guard at 1911 fires, and FailInit() (824-836) cannot restore the chart because its own guard tests g_origin != "". MQL5 semantics do not rescue this: ObjectGetString on a non-existent object cannot return the text, and whether chart objects survive ChartSetSymbolPeriod is irrelevant because the delete and the read are in one pass of one function. Corollary I verified while judging the rest of the batch: because `stashed` is always empty, the "!"-poison branch at 1894-1900 is also dead, so one_chart_ok can never diverge from InpOneChart anywhere in the program / Verified end to end. SSRPurgeChart (Ui/SSR_Widgets.mqh:676-694) enumerates ObjectsTotal(chart,-1,-1) and ObjectDelete()s every name where StringFind(nm,"SSR")==0 except the single keep argument, which here is SSR_PICK_LINE. SSR_HANDOFF=="SSR_ORIGIN_HANDOFF" (mq5:536) and SSR_PICK_STASH=="SSR_PICK_HANDOFF" (mq5:556) both match that prefix and are both created on chart 0 (540, 2536). The sweep is at 1806, unconditional, and ReadStashedOrigin() is at 1869 in the same function - 63 lines later. So on pass 2 stashed=="", origin=="" at 1903, the guard at 1911 fires and FailInit() returns INIT_FAILED; FailInit's restore branch at 825 requires g_origin!="" and is therefore skipped, leaving the user's chart pointed at a custom replay symbol with no expert and no automatic way back. This is the shipped default (InpOneChart=true, InpPickStart=true), so it is not an edge case - it is every normal run. Impact lens: the product does not function at all in its default configuration and it damages the user's workspace on the way out; the picked start and the '!' poison die in the same sweep, so neither the chosen window nor the two-window recovery path survives either. The claim also does not depend on whether chart objects survive ChartSetSymbolPeriod, because the delete and the read are in the same pass of the same function

#### `trading-exec-1` — Rewind drops positions placed after the cut without reversing their P/L, commission and swap from the balance

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:640`** · category: event-handling

```mql5
long placed = (m_pos[i].request_msc > 0 ? m_pos[i].request_msc
                                        : m_pos[i].open_msc);
if(placed > msc)
   continue;

//--- every exit taken in the deleted future, newest first
UnwindLegsAfter(i, msc);
```

**What goes wrong:** State: a BUY is opened at 10:05 and closed at 10:10 with realised +50 (or by SL with -30), commission 7/lot charged twice. Action: step back to 10:00 (OnRewind(10:00)), or press Reset ('0' key -> GroupPort.Reset -> controller.Reset -> PublishRewind(start_msc)). Outcome: the position vanishes from the log but m_balance keeps the +50 (or -30) and the -14 commission and any accrued swap. After Reset the account shows an empty log with the whole session's realised P/L still in the balance; Equity, stats, prop-evaluation daily-loss/target and the next LotForRisk (sized off Equity()) are all computed on the corrupted figure. A later Save/Restore then prints the 'balance replayed from the trades is X, but the file recorded Y' warning because RestoreFrom recomputes balance from the log.

**Why:** The `continue` at line 643 skips the position entirely, so it is never copied to m_pos[keep] and none of the money it moved is reversed. The money was moved at Open() line 754 (`m_balance -= m_pos[i].commission`), CheckPendings line 356, BookExit line 222 (`m_balance += realised - fee`) and AccrueSwap line 427. The only reversal code, UnwindLegsAfter (263-291) and the un-fill branch (660-690), is placed AFTER the continue and only runs for kept positions. Nothing else recomputes m_balance from the log (grep of m_balance: lines 222, 273, 356, 427, 563, 579, 664, 754, 1167, 1221 only). T9.7 (lines 340-361) only asserts the ticket is gone, with an OPEN trade and commission 0, so the leak is invisible to the test. Fix: before `continue`, run UnwindLegsAfter(i, msc) (all legs are > msc) and then `m_balance += m_pos[i].commission - m_pos[i].swap`.

**Verifiers:** Traced OnRewind (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh 634-702): the `continue` at 643 fires for any position whose request_msc/open_msc is after the cut, BEFORE UnwindLegsAfter(646) and before the un-fill refund branch (660-690). The only money-reversing code in the class is UnwindLegsAfter (273) and that un-fill branch (664), both after the continue, so a position opened AND closed inside the deleted window keeps every balance move it made: Open() 754 (-commission), CheckPendings 356 (-commission), BookExit 222 (+realised-fee) and AccrueSwap 427 (swap). The trailing AccrueSwap() at 701 only restates positions still in m_pos, so a dropped position's swap is also stranded. The Reset path is real: SSR_GroupPort.mqh 425-437 -> CSSRReplayController::Reset (SSR_ReplayController.mqh 1268-1293) calls only PublishRewind(cut) at 1286 and never re-publishes OnSessionStart (the only place m_balance is reset, line 579), so after Reset the log is empty and the whole session's realised P/L, fees and swap remain in m_balance. Everything derived (Equity 971, FloatingPL, LotForRisk via Equity at 767/797, prop evaluation, statistics) reads that figure, and RestoreFrom 1221/1253 recomputes balance from the log so the 'balance replayed from the trades' warning does fire. T9_Trading.mq5 330-363 asserts only ByTicket/OpenCount and never the balance, so the leak is untested / Provable from source and reachable through a first-class button. In OnRewind the 'continue' at 642-643 drops a position placed after the cut before UnwindLegsAfter (646) and before the un-fill refund branch (660-690), so none of the money it moved is reversed: the entry commission (754, or 356 on a pending fill), every leg's realised - fee (222) and any accrued swap (427). The position is not copied to m_pos[keep], so the AccrueSwap restatement at 701 cannot refund its swap either, and no other code recomputes m_balance from the surviving log (only RestoreFrom 1167-1221 does, on file load). The Reset path is confirmed end to end: Panel 2241/2248 -> GroupPort.Reset 425-436 -> CSSRReplayController::Reset, which calls PublishRewind(cut) at 1286 with cut = timeline start and never re-runs OnSessionStart, the only code that restores m_balance = m_balance_initial (579). The host configures the account once, before Load (SSReplayStandalone 1167-1169), so a Reset leaves an empty trade log with the whole session's realised P/L, commissions and swap still in m_balance - and Equity(), LotForRisk sizing off Equity, the prop evaluation and the save-file balance check all read that figure. The test the finder names (T10.9, 'an order placed after the cut simply never existed', SSR_T10_Statistics.mq5 675-689) asserts only Total() == 0 on an open position with commission 0 from e.Init(), so the leak is invisible to the suite / Provable and reachable through the product's headline feature. OnRewind (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:640-643) `continue`s past any position whose placed msc > cut, so it is dropped from m_pos without any of its money being reversed: the entry commission subtracted at line 754 / 356, every leg's realised-fee added at line 222, and the swap moved at line 427 all stay in m_balance. The only reversal code (UnwindLegsAfter 263-291 and the un-fill branch 660-690) sits after the continue. Grep of m_balance writers (222, 273, 356, 427, 563, 579, 664, 754, 1167, 1221) shows nothing recomputes the balance from the log after a rewind, and OnSessionStart - the only place m_balance is reset to m_balance_initial - is published exactly once, from Load (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:837), never from Reset or a step-back. Every backward path lands here: SeekTo/step-back (controller 1240), Reset (controller 1286, which cuts to timeline.start_msc and therefore drops the ENTIRE session's trades while keeping their P/L, commission and swap in the balance), and checkpoint restore (1644). User-observable: an empty or truncated trade log with a balance that does not match it; Equity(), FloatingPL(), MarginLevel(), the prop-evaluation daily-loss/target gates and the next LotForRisk (sized off Equity()) all run on the corrupted figure, and a later Save/Restore surfaces it only as the soft 'balance replayed from the trades is X but the file recorded Y' warning (1253-1257). Silent money-arithmetic corruption in the core account, so CRITICAL stands

### HIGH (14)

#### `core-sync-1` — Group idle gap-skip fires mid-bar at human speeds and bulk-writes the unfinished bar, so observers (trading engine) never receive the rest of that bar's ticks

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_MasterClock.mqh:208`** · category: sl-tp-execution

```mql5
      else if(AnyPlaying() && ++m_idle_pumps >= 25)
        {
         m_idle_pumps = 0;
         long nb = NextBarAcross(m_master.now_msc);
         if(nb != SSR_INVALID_TIME && nb > m_master.now_msc + 2 * SSR_MSC_PER_MIN)
           { ... SeekAllTo(nb - 1); }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate as described, with one clarification: bar B is still drawn correctly on the replay chart (SeedBars writes it whole); what is lost is the observer stream - no OnBarContext and no OnTicks for bar B reach the trading/statistics engines, so a stop or target inside B's range is never evaluated and the engine's next price is bar nb's first tick. Trigger needs >= 2 consecutive missing M1 bars immediately after the current bar (so nb >= B_open+3min), which is the last bar of every index/futures CFD session, every weekend edge, and any 2-minute hole in thin forex.

**Why:** Trigger arithmetic: synthetic stamps are open + i*59999/7 (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:147), so at 1x consecutive ticks are 8571 ms apart and 25 pumps x 40 ms = 1 s of idle is reached inside every minute; the condition 'nb > now + 120000' with now = B_open + ~1s holds whenever the next existing bar is >= B_open + 3 min. Skip path: SeekAllTo (line 296) -> m_member[i].JumpTo(t) -> controller JumpTo (1484) -> JumpForward (1333): lo = m_cursor.emitted_msc + 1; bar_lo = SSRBarOpenMsc(lo) (1363-1364) which is B_open when B is partially emitted; last_bar = SSRBarOpenMsc(target) = nb - 60000 (1365); 'if(last_bar > bar_lo)' (1370) is true because nb - 60000 > B_open; ReadBars(bar_lo, last_bar - 1) returns bar B and m_sink.SeedBars(bulk, n) (1383) writes it; m_cursor.Advance(last_bar - 1, 0, n) (1390) moves the cursor past B. JumpForward never calls PublishBar/PublishTicks/OnClock (1333-1420) — the header comment says so ('publishes none of them', EA 2792-2794). The comment at lines 202-204 assumes 'the pause between two live ticks - never more than a minute of bar time - is left untouched', but the threshold is measured from the mid-bar clock, not from the current bar's end. Mt5 provider NextBarOpen (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:302-321) returns the first open strictly after after_msc via CopyTime, confirming nb = next EXISTING bar. Fix direction (RECOMMENDATION): measure the gap from the end of the current bar (BarOpen(now)+60000) rather than from now, or skip only when cursor.emitted >= BarOpen(now)+59999.

**Verifiers:** Traced end to end and found no guard. Synthesizer stamps are base+i*59999/(n-1) (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:147), so at 8 ticks/bar consecutive ticks are 8571 ms apart; InpPumpMs defaults to 40 (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:78) and g_group.Pump is called every timer tick (2788), so 25 emitted==0 pumps (=1 s) is reached inside almost every minute at human speeds. CSSRReplayGroup::Pump only resets m_idle_pumps when emitted>0 (206-208) and then measures the gap from m_master.now_msc, i.e. from the MID-BAR clock, not from the end of the current bar (212). NextBarAcross -> NextTimelineBarOpen -> CSSRMt5BarProvider::NextBarOpen (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:302-322) filters `open > after_msc`, so it returns the next EXISTING bar strictly after the mid-bar clock; with two or more missing M1 bars after the current bar B, nb >= B_open+180000 > now+120000 and the skip fires. SeekAllTo(nb-1) (296-322) -> controller JumpTo (1484-1519) -> JumpForward (1333): lo = m_cursor.emitted_msc+1 (mid-bar B, because EmitWindow advances consumed_to=hi every pump, SSR_ReplayController.mqh:523-531), bar_lo = B_open, last_bar = nb-60000 > bar_lo, so ReadBars(B_open, last_bar-1) returns bar B and m_sink.SeedBars writes it in bulk (1383) with the cursor advanced past it (1390). JumpForward never calls PublishBar/PublishTicks/OnClock, and CSSRTickObserver has no seek/jump callback at all (SSR_ITickObserver.mqh) - so the trading engine receives no OnBarContext and no ticks for bar B. The 'market closed from <mid-bar time>' print (214-216) is emitted from the same mid-bar instant / Every link is provable from source. Synth stamps are base+i*59999/(n-1) (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:147), so with InpTicksPerBar=8 and InpPumpMs=40 (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:75,78) consecutive ticks are 8571 ms of bar time apart and 25 empty pumps = ~1 s of wall time is reached inside a bar at any speed up to ~8x. NextTimelineBarOpen -> CSSRMt5BarProvider::NextBarOpen uses CopyTime and returns the next EXISTING bar open strictly after after_msc (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:302-323), so with the clock at B_open+dt the test nb > now+120000 is satisfiable exactly when the two M1 bars after B are absent (session close, weekend edge, 2-minute hole) - the threshold is measured from the mid-bar clock, not from B's end, as the comment at SSR_MasterClock.mqh:202-204 assumes. SeekAllTo -> JumpTo -> JumpForward then has lo = emitted+1 (mid-bar B), bar_lo = SSRBarOpenMsc(lo) = B_open, last_bar = nb-60000 > bar_lo, so ReadBars(B_open, last_bar-1) returns bar B and m_sink.SeedBars writes it in bulk (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1360-1390). JumpForward's bulk branch calls neither PublishBar nor PublishTicks (only Publish(), which touches state, and m_sink.OnSeek), and the trailing EmitWindow covers only [last_bar, nb-1], a minute with no bar, so it returns 0. CSSRTradingEngine drives CheckPendings/CheckStops/CheckStopout exclusively from OnTicks (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:600-615), so ticks 1..7 of bar B (its high/low/close path) are never evaluated; at BAR fidelity the bar's single tick at B_open+59999 is lost entirely. Chart correctness is preserved (the bar arrives via SeedBars); the loss is to observers / Every precondition is provable from source and the path is the only one the shipped host uses. The EA always pumps through the group (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1320 g_group.Add(g_ctrl), :2788 g_group.Pump(delta)) at InpPumpMs=40 with InpTicksPerBar=8, so at human speeds consecutive synthetic stamps are 8571 ms apart (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:147) and 25 emit-less pumps (1 s) elapse inside a bar whose path is only partly emitted. SSR_MasterClock.mqh:212 measures the gap from the mid-bar clock, not from the current bar's end, so whenever the next EXISTING bar (CopyTime-based, SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh NextBarOpen) is >= B_open+3min - the last bar before any daily close, any Friday close, any 2-minute hole - the test passes and SeekAllTo(nb-1) runs. JumpForward then bulk-writes bar B: lo=emitted+1 is still inside B so bar_lo=B_open, last_bar=nb-60000 > bar_lo, ReadBars(bar_lo,last_bar-1) includes B, SeedBars writes it and cursor.Advance(last_bar-1) steps past it (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1361-1391). SeedBars is a sink call only - no PublishBar/PublishTicks - and CSSRTradingEngine evaluates CheckPendings/CheckStops/CheckStopout exclusively inside OnTicks (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:600-616; OnClock only moves m_now_msc, :617). So the account never sees B's high/low: a stop or target inside that bar is not filled at its price and is instead evaluated against the next session's first tick, i.e. the gap price. That is corrupted money arithmetic in the virtual-trading guarantee, on the last bar before every closure, so HIGH is right. The chart itself is not blank (the candle arrives via SeedBars), which is why nothing looks wrong

#### `core-engine-4` — FULL_TICK window with zero broker ticks is consumed silently; tick availability is never re-evaluated per window

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_ReplayController.mqh:349`** · category: zero-empty

```mql5
            int n = tp.ReadTicks(m_state.symbol, lo - 1, hi, m_ticks);
            ...
            n = m_guard.FilterTicks(m_ticks, n);
            ...
            emitted = n;
            m_cursor.Advance(hi, emitted, 0);
            return emitted;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate as written, with one qualification: the retry path at SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:425-440 means a broker that answers a tick-less range with -1 rather than 0 would surface SSR_ERR_LOAD_FAILED instead of freezing silently. The silent-freeze outcome requires CopyTicksRange to return 0 for the range, which is the ordinary MT5 answer for held-but-empty tick history but is not verifiable from this repo.

**Why:** Lines 349-369: no branch handles n == 0 while bars exist; the only degradation trigger is `tp == NULL` (line 337-346), and CSSRFidelityPolicy::Decide (SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh line 114-118) consults m_ticks_available which is set only at Load (line 806) and at line 344. CSSRTickProvider::HasTicks(symbol, from, to) exists (SSR_IDataSource.mqh line 179) but is never called by the controller. Whether a given broker's tick depth is shorter than its M1 depth is an inference about deployments (it is the common case for MT5 brokers); the engine behaviour for that case is confirmed from code.

**Verifiers:** Traced the whole FULL_TICK branch and found no guard anywhere on the path. SSR_ReplayController.mqh:334-369: the ONLY degradation trigger is `tp == NULL` (337-346). With a live provider, `n = tp.ReadTicks(...)` is taken at face value: CSSRMt5TickProvider::ReadTicks (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:401-470) returns 0 (not -1) for a range the broker holds no ticks for — `if(got == 0) break;` — so n==0 is not an error, EmitTicks/PublishTicks are skipped by `if(n > 0)`, and line 367 `m_cursor.Advance(hi, 0, 0)` unconditionally consumes the window before `return emitted`. No bar provider is consulted, no PublishBar, no SetTicksAvailable(false), no log line. m_ticks_available is written in exactly two places (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:344 and 806) and CSSRTickProvider::HasTicks has zero callers anywhere in MQL5/ (grep: only its declaration and the Mt5 implementation), so availability is never re-evaluated per window. The upstream chain is as claimed: SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:133-139 sets has_ticks from a CopyTicksRange probe of the last 24h of held data only, and SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1153 requests FULL_TICK whenever range.has_ticks. Progress/Publish still advance (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:172-186), so the clock and the panel keep moving over a market that emits nothing. Only the deployment premise (broker tick depth shorter than M1 depth) is outside the source; the engine behaviour is fully provable / The engine-side code path is exactly as described and I cannot refute it: SSR_ReplayController.mqh:349-368 is the whole FULL_TICK branch — `n = tp.ReadTicks(...)`, `n = m_guard.FilterTicks(...)`, `emitted = n; m_cursor.Advance(hi, emitted, 0); return emitted;`. There is no `n == 0` branch, no bar fallback, no log, and the only degradation trigger is `tp == NULL` (337-346). CSSRMt5TickProvider::ReadTicks (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:410-472) returns 0 and calls Succeed() when CopyTicksRange yields `got == 0` (line 442-443 `if(got == 0) break;`), so a tick-less window is indistinguishable from a quiet one at this layer. Ticks() is offered whenever m_has_ticks (SSR_Mt5DataSource.mqh:93-96), and m_has_ticks comes from Discover's probe of only the last 24 h of held data (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:133-139: `from_msc = to_msc - 24*60*60*1000`), while the host pins FULL_TICK off that same flag (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1153). CSSRTickProvider::HasTicks exists (SSR_IDataSource.mqh:179) and is called nowhere in the controller — grep confirms only tests and the provider itself reference it. Clamped to POTENTIAL_RISK for one reason only: manifesting requires a broker whose stored tick depth is shorter than its M1 depth, and how MT5 answers CopyTicksRange for a range outside the server's tick retention (0 vs a -1/ERR_HISTORY_LOAD that the retry loop at 425-440 would turn into an error) cannot be settled from this source. Both the common case and the error case are consistent with the finding, but neither is provable here / Every link in the chain is in the source I read. SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:133-139 sets range.has_ticks from a CopyTicksRange probe over the LAST 24 h of held data only; SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1153 turns that single boolean into SSR_FIDELITY_FULL_TICK for the whole session; SSR_ReplayController.mqh:806 hands it to the policy, which re-consults nothing per window (SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh:114-118). With tp != NULL (SSR_Mt5DataSource.mqh:93 only NULLs the provider on the same 24 h flag) EmitWindow takes the FULL_TICK branch and, on n == 0, runs EmitTicks nowhere, PublishTicks nowhere, and still executes m_cursor.Advance(hi, 0, 0) and returns 0 (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:347-369). CSSRTickProvider::HasTicks(from,to) exists (SSR_IDataSource.mqh:179) and is called by nobody. ReadTicks returns 0, not -1, for an empty range (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:442-443), so nothing transitions to ERROR. Observable result in normal use — the flagship case of replaying history older than the broker's tick depth: the clock, progress bar and Bars/Ticks counters keep moving, the panel keeps saying FULL TICK, the replay candle never forms, and the trading engine's CheckStops/CheckPendings/CheckStopout never run because they only fire inside OnTicks (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:600-614). A frozen market with a running clock and no diagnostic is a total functional failure of the product; HIGH stands

#### `core-engine-1` — Per-pump budget cap silently DROPS bars instead of deferring them (dead 'stop short' branch)

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_ReplayController.mqh:524`** · category: future-data-leakage

```mql5
      int cap = m_budget.MaxBars(m_synth.TicksForBar(fid));
      if(nb > cap)
        {
         nb = cap;
...
      long consumed_to = hi;
      if(bars_used > 0 && (first + bars_used) < nb)
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The logic error is exactly as described; two details in the write-up are wrong. (1) 'the UI reports the pump as deferred' overstates it: m_pump_deferred reaches only CSSRMetrics (perf.deferred_pumps) and CSSRPumpBudget::Deferrals(), and grep shows no UI consumer — SSRUiState carries no deferral field and BudgetText() is used only by SSR_T7_Performance.mq5:236, so the drop is not merely mislabelled, it is entirely unreported. (2) The PgDn scenario as written needs a calibrated us/tick high enough to clamp MaxTicks to 32 (cap 4 bars); with the uncalibrated fallback cap is 4096/8 = 512 bars, so the reachable triggers are a slow-calibrated machine plus either a >cap-bar step or a large owed window after a stall (at BAR fidelity, cap == MaxTicks, so >32 owed minutes). The determinism point stands: the cut-off is a function of wall-clock measurement, so the emitted stream is machine-dependent.

**Why:** Line 399-404 overwrite `nb = cap`. The loop at line 437 runs `i = first..nb-1` and increments `bars_used` for every iteration (w > 0 is guaranteed because m_ticks was resized to `need` at line 418), so after the loop `first + bars_used == nb` always holds and the condition at line 524 `(first + bars_used) < nb` is false whenever the cap bit. `consumed_to` therefore stays `hi` and line 531 `m_cursor.Advance(consumed_to, ...)` moves the cursor past the unread bars. The comment at lines 519-522 documents the opposite intent ('Anything above it stays owed'). T7.6 cannot catch this: after the capped pump it only checks that TicksEmitted keeps rising, which the following bars satisfy. Because the cap comes from wall-clock measurement (CSSRPumpBudget::MaxTicks via CSSRMetrics::UsPerTick), the emitted stream also becomes machine-dependent, breaking the determinism claim. Trigger frequency on a real terminal depends on the measured cost of CustomTicksAdd (unmeasured in the repo); the logic error itself is unconditional.

**Verifiers:** The 'stop short' branch is provably dead for the case it was written for. SSR_ReplayController.mqh:398-404 OVERWRITES nb with cap, destroying the original bar count, so the test at 524 `(first + bars_used) < nb` compares against the already-truncated nb. The loop at 437 runs i = first..nb-1 and increments bars_used at 472 on every iteration; the only escape is `if(w <= 0) break` at 441, and w cannot be 0 because need = (nb-first)*per_bar was allocated at 406-418 after the cap, so Synthesize's `if(ArraySize(out) < offset + n) return 0` (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:117) can never fire. Hence first + bars_used == nb exactly whenever the cap bit, consumed_to stays hi, and line 531 advances the cursor past bars that were neither written to the sink nor published; the next pump's lo = hi+1 can never reach them. The comment at 519-522 states the opposite intent. m_budget.NoteDeferral()/m_pump_deferred are still set (402-403), so the pump is reported as deferred while the data was discarded. Reachability is runtime-dependent but real: MaxBars = MaxTicks/ticks_per_bar with MaxTicks = 12000us/us_per_tick clamped to [32, 32768] (SSR_PumpBudget.mqh:62-80), so once IsCalibrated() (>=16 samples, SSR_Metrics.mqh:159) yields us_per_tick above ~150, cap(8) drops below 10 and a 10-bar step discards bars; at the 32-tick floor a non-bulk window caps at 4 bars. The cap being wall-clock-derived also makes the emitted stream machine-dependent / The dead branch is provable. After `nb = cap` (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:398-404), the loop at 437 runs i = first..nb-1. The only early exit is `if(w <= 0) break;` (line 442), and w cannot be 0: Synthesize/SynthesizeClose return 0 only when ArraySize(out) < offset + n (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:119-120, 191-192), while `offset` is `written`, which accumulates only the trimmed `keep` counts (line 480) and is therefore always <= i*per_bar — and m_ticks was resized to need = (nb-first)*per_bar at 415-421. So the loop always runs to completion, bars_used == nb - first, and the guard at 524 `(first + bars_used) < nb` is unconditionally false. consumed_to stays hi and line 531 advances the cursor past every bar above the cap; PendingRange (SSR_ReplayCursor.mqh:52-58) then starts the next window at emitted+1, which is after them. Nothing re-reads them, so those minutes reach neither the sink (no EmitTicks) nor the observers (PublishBar/PublishSegment run only over `segs`), i.e. a hole in the custom symbol and no CheckStops evaluation for that span (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:600-614). The comment at 519-522 states the opposite intent. cap can genuinely be small: MaxBars = MaxTicks()/ticks_per_bar with MaxTicks clamped to SSR_PUMP_MIN_TICKS = 32 (SSR_PumpBudget.mqh:61-72), so 32/8 = 4 bars at synthetic fidelity. T7.6 (SSR_T7_Performance.mq5:278-297) only asserts that TicksEmitted keeps rising afterwards, which later bars satisfy, and its 30-minute pump degrades to BAR fidelity (1 tick/bar) against a memory sink, so the cap never even bites there / The dead branch is provable from the source. Line 399-404 overwrite nb with cap; `first` is always 0 because the skip loop at 407-409 compares against the read's own lower bound; the loop at 437 cannot break early since need = (nb-first)*per_bar was ArrayResize'd at 418-423, so Synthesize never returns 0 and bars_used reaches nb-first exactly. The guard at line 524, `bars_used > 0 && (first + bars_used) < nb`, therefore evaluates against the ALREADY-TRUNCATED nb and is false in precisely the case it was written for; consumed_to stays hi and line 531 advances the cursor past bars that were never synthesised, never written to the sink and never published. The comment at 519-522 states the opposite intent, and m_pump_deferred/NoteDeferral report the pump as deferred while the data is gone. Impact when it fires is the worst class in this product: a hole in the replay candles plus minutes in which CheckStops/CheckPendings/CheckStopout never run (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:600-614), so a stop or target inside the dropped bars is silently never evaluated. I keep HIGH even though the trigger is machine-dependent — cap = MaxTicks()/8 with MaxTicks = 12000us/us_per_tick clamped to [32, 32768] (SSR_PumpBudget.mqh:60-84), so the cap only bites once metrics are calibrated and the measured CustomTicksAdd cost is high enough to put cap below the number of M1 bars in one window (a StepBars(10) window, or a post-stall pump at high speed). Nothing in the repo measures that cost, so how often it fires is unknown; that it loses the data when it does is unconditional

#### `core-engine-2` — StepBars(n) from a bar end advances n-1 bars; StepBars(1) is a no-op after the first step

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1169`** · category: boundary

```mql5
      long base = SSRNextBarOpenMsc(m_clock.now_msc, PERIOD_M1);
      long nb   = NextTimelineBarOpen(m_clock.now_msc);
      if(nb != SSR_INVALID_TIME && nb > base)
         base = nb;
      long target = base + (long)(bars - 1) * SSR_MSC_PER_MIN - 1;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct. Precise statement of the invariant: StepBars leaves the clock at (last emitted bar's open + 59999), and from that resting point the formula re-derives the same instant for bars == 1 and lands bars-1 bars ahead for bars > 1. So '->' is a no-op on every press after the first, and PgDn moves 9 bars instead of 10, until something else advances the clock (Play, a jump, or a backward step). The group path reports success (CSSRMasterClock::StepBars returns true) while nothing moves.

**Why:** SSRNextBarOpenMsc(msc) = SSRBarOpenMsc(msc)+60000 (SSR_Time.mqh line 37-43). For now = open+59999, SSRBarOpenMsc floors to `open`, so base = open+60000 and target = open+60000-1 = open+59999 = now. `nb` (first bar strictly after now) equals base in dense data and cannot raise it. Lines 1173-1178 then skip emission because now did not move. T1.7 (SSR_T1_CoreEngine.mq5 line 283-291) calls StepBars(1) ten times but asserts only `after10 > start`, which the first step alone satisfies; no test asserts the position after repeated steps.

**Verifiers:** Arithmetic verified against the helpers. SSRBarOpenMsc floors by PeriodSeconds*1000 and SSRNextBarOpenMsc adds one period (SSR_Time.mqh:27-43). For now = open+59999: base = open+60000; NextTimelineBarOpen returns the first bar strictly after now, which in dense data is open+60000 and so fails `nb > base` (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1170-1172); target = base + (1-1)*60000 - 1 = open+59999 == now. m_clock.SeekTo clamps but does not move (SSR_ReplayClock.mqh:146-153), so `if(m_clock.now_msc > before)` at 1176 is false, no EmitWindow runs and StepBars returns 0 — and since every StepBars lands on target = base + (bars-1)*60000 - 1, i.e. always an xx:59.999 instant, the state is self-perpetuating: step-forward works once per play/pause cycle and PgDn advances 9 bars, not 10. The production path is identical, not merely analogous: CSSRMasterClock::StepBars (SSR_MasterClock.mqh:341-352) uses the same formula on the master, then PumpTo(t) per member, and AdvanceTo returns early on `t <= now_msc` (SSR_ReplayClock.mqh:131) so nothing is emitted — yet StepBars still returns true, so the panel (SSR_Panel.mqh:2293-2294 -> SSR_GroupPort.mqh:439) reports success. The test blind spot is as claimed: SSR_T1_CoreEngine.mq5:283-291 calls StepBars(1) ten times and asserts only `after10 > start`, which the first step alone satisfies; SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:743-752 asserts only that the clock moved at all / Fully provable from the integer arithmetic in source. SSRBarOpenMsc floors by PeriodSeconds*1000 and SSRNextBarOpenMsc adds one period (SSR_Time.mqh:27-43). With now = open+59999: base = open+60000, and target = base + (bars-1)*60000 - 1 (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1169) gives target == now for bars == 1. `nb` cannot raise base: CSSRMt5BarProvider::NextBarOpen (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:302-323) searches from `after_msc/1000 + 1` = open/1000+60 seconds and returns the first open strictly greater than after_msc, which in dense data is exactly open+60000 == base, and the condition at 1167 requires nb > base. m_clock.SeekTo(target) then leaves now_msc unchanged (SSRReplayClock::SeekTo clamps to the same value), so the `if(m_clock.now_msc > before)` at 1173 is false and StepBars returns 0 having emitted nothing. The production path is identical: SSR_Panel.mqh:2293 -> SSR_GroupPort.mqh:439-440 -> CSSRMasterClock::StepBars (SSR_MasterClock.mqh:340-367), which repeats the same base/target formula on the master and then calls PumpTo(t) on each member, where AdvanceTo returns immediately for t <= now (SSR_ReplayClock.mqh:127-129) so before == now and EmitWindow is skipped. T1.7's `after10 > start` assertion is satisfied by the first step alone / I reproduced the arithmetic against the source. SSRNextBarOpenMsc = SSRBarOpenMsc + 60000 (SSR_Time.mqh:36-43). A step always lands on target = base + (bars-1)*60000 - 1, i.e. mm:59.999 (controller line 1169). From there base recomputes to the same next open, and NextTimelineBarOpen cannot raise it: CSSRMt5BarProvider::NextBarOpen returns the first open strictly greater than after_msc (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:288-302), which in dense data equals base, so `nb > base` is false. target == now; SSRReplayClock::SeekTo clamps to the same value (SSR_ReplayClock.mqh:139-147) and the emission block at 1173-1178 is gated on m_clock.now_msc > before, so nothing is read, nothing is published, 0 is returned. The production path is identical: SSR_Panel.mqh:2293-2294 -> SSR_GroupPort.mqh:439 -> CSSRMasterClock::StepBars (SSR_MasterClock.mqh:341-352) uses the same formula, and its PumpTo(t) hits AdvanceTo's `if(t <= now_msc) return now_msc` (SSR_ReplayClock.mqh:127-131) yet StepBars still returns true, so the panel reports success. What the user observes: on a paused replay the step-forward button works once and is then dead until Play moves the clock off the mm:59.999 boundary, and PgDn advances 9 bars, not 10. For a bar-by-bar training tool that is its primary interaction, and T1.7 (SSR_T1_CoreEngine.mq5:283-291) cannot catch it because it only asserts after10 > start. HIGH stands

#### `core-engine-3` — JumpForward feeds bulk bars to the sink but never to observers; StepBackward routes through it, so every step back replays up to 5 minutes blind to the trading engine

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1383`** · category: sl-tp-execution

```mql5
            if(!m_sink.SeedBars(bulk, n))
              {
               SetError(SSR_ERR_SINK_FAILED, "bulk write failed: " + m_sink.LastErrorText());
               return -1;
              }
            m_metrics.RecordSeed(n, (double)(GetMicrosecondCount() - t0) / 1000.0);
            written += n;
            m_cursor.Advance(last_bar - 1, 0, n);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct. One scope note: JumpForward also never calls the observers' OnClock (unlike Pump/PumpTo at 1023-1025 and 1098-1100), so the trading engine's m_now_msc only moves with the target bar's few ticks — swap accrual and time-based logic skip the bulk span as well, not just stop evaluation. The window is bounded by the checkpoint interval (SSR_CHECKPOINT_INTERVAL_MSC = 5 replay minutes, SSR_SnapshotStore.mqh:32) for a step back, but unbounded for a forward J-jump or a session resume through JumpTo.

**Why:** JumpForward lines 1333-1420 contain m_sink.SeedBars and m_cursor.Advance for the bulk part and EmitWindow only for the bar containing the target (line 1397-1398). Observers are reached exclusively via PublishBar/PublishTicks/PublishSegment (lines 191-231), which are called only from EmitWindow (lines 366, 506-515). CSSRTradingEngine::OnTicks (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh line 599-614) is the only place CheckStops/CheckPendings/CheckStopout run; OnBarContext only caches the bar. StepBackward calls JumpForward at line 1458 after RestoreSnapshot. The host comment at SSReplayStandalone.mq5 ~line 2795 ('A JUMP WRITES ITS BARS IN BULK and publishes none of them') acknowledges the behaviour for the strategy view but nothing compensates for the account. The design rationale (avoid tick injection for fast-forward) is sound for the sink; it does not require observers to be skipped.

**Verifiers:** Traced both the publish surface and the consumer. Observers are reached only through PublishBar/PublishTicks/PublishSegment/PublishRewind (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:189-236), and the only callers of the first three are inside EmitWindow (366, 506-515). JumpForward's bulk leg (1370-1392) calls m_sink.SeedBars + m_metrics.RecordSeed + m_cursor.Advance(last_bar-1, 0, n) and nothing else; only the target's own bar goes through EmitWindow (1395-1402), and when the target is a bar open the keep filter leaves exactly the i=0 tick. CSSRTradingEngine evaluates AccrueSwap/CheckPendings/CheckStops/CheckStopout only inside OnTicks (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:600-613); OnBarContext merely caches (593-598) and OnClock only bumps m_now_msc (616-617). StepBackward reaches this via RestoreSnapshot -> PublishRewind (which un-does the stop-out, SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:634+) then JumpForward(target) at 1458, so the bars between checkpoint and target are replayed blind to the account — exactly the described chart/account divergence, for every '<-' press, GotoBookmark -> JumpTo, forward J-jump and RestoreFrom's JumpTo(want_now) at 1826. Nothing compensates: the host's only post-jump repair is PrimeView() for the strategy market view (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2794-2798), which does not touch the trading engine / Provable. JumpForward's bulk branch (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1368-1392) calls only m_sink.SeedBars + m_metrics.RecordSeed + m_cursor.Advance(last_bar - 1, 0, n); there is no PublishBar/PublishTicks/PublishSegment and no PublishRewind on that path, and those three are the only routes to observers (lines 187-231), reached exclusively from EmitWindow (366, 506-515). EmitWindow is then invoked only for the target's own bar (1395-1402), because PendingRange starts at cursor.emitted_msc+1 == last_bar after the bulk Advance. CSSRTradingEngine evaluates stops only in OnTicks (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:600-614: AccrueSwap/CheckPendings/CheckStops/CheckStopout inside the per-tick loop); OnBarContext (593-598) merely caches the bar. StepBackward routes through it: RestoreSnapshot -> PublishRewind (which un-does exits after the cut, SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:634+) -> JumpForward(target) at line 1458. JumpTo forward (1503) and GotoBookmark/ResumePosition/RestoreFrom (1522, 1585, 1826) all funnel into the same JumpForward. The host confirms the gap and compensates only for the strategy market view, not the account (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2793-2799 'A JUMP WRITES ITS BARS IN BULK and publishes none of them' -> PrimeView()) / Confirmed end to end from source. Observers are reached only through PublishBar/PublishTicks/PublishSegment (controller 183-231), called only from EmitWindow (366, 506-515). JumpForward's bulk range goes to m_sink.SeedBars and m_cursor.Advance(last_bar - 1, 0, n) with no publish (1370-1385); only the target's own minute reaches EmitWindow (1388-1398), and when the target is a bar open that window is a single millisecond, so exactly one synthetic tick is published. CSSRTradingEngine evaluates CheckPendings/CheckStops/CheckStopout only inside OnTicks (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:600-614); OnBarContext merely caches (593-598) and OnClock only moves m_now_msc (617-618). StepBackward routes through this: NearestAtOrBefore over a 5-minute checkpoint ring (SSR_SnapshotStore.mqh:32, 119-136), RestoreSnapshot -> PublishRewind, which actively UNWINDS exits taken after the cut (OnRewind, SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:634+), then JumpForward(target) at line 1458. So a stop-out inside the replayed-forward span is undone and never re-triggered, leaving the virtual account holding a position the chart shows as stopped out. Reachable on every back-step, every bookmark return via GotoBookmark -> JumpTo, and every resume via RestoreFrom -> JumpTo. Divergence between the chart and the virtual account is exactly the class my lens says to keep high; nothing compensates

#### `data-1` — has_ticks is probed over the last 24 hours of history but then selects FULL_TICK fidelity for a replay window that may be years earlier — the replay emits nothing, the clock runs to the end, and nothing reports it

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Data/SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:136`** · category: future-data-leakage

```mql5
      long to_msc   = out.last_msc;
      long from_msc = to_msc - 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
      int  got = CopyTicksRange(symbol, probe, COPY_TICKS_INFO,
                                (ulong)MathMax(from_msc, 0), (ulong)to_msc);
      out.has_ticks = (got > 0);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct as a trace; two qualifications. Category is wrong - nothing future-dated leaks; this is a range-blind capability probe producing a silent no-data replay plus a fidelity claim the run cannot honour. And the failure is not total: Decide() falls to SSR_FIDELITY_BAR on any bulk pump (owed_msc >= SSR_BULK_THRESHOLD_MSC, SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh:119-123), so bars DO flow at high speed and after jumps while a 1x pump emits nothing - which makes the symptom intermittent and the misdiagnosis worse rather than better. Severity HIGH rather than CRITICAL: the session visibly fails to play instead of producing wrong results, and no trade or price data is corrupted.

**Why:** The probe range is hardcoded to [last_msc-86400000, last_msc] — the newest day of history — and is the only thing that sets out.has_ticks. CSSRMt5DataSource::Ticks() (SSR_Mt5DataSource.mqh:91-96) returns the provider whenever m_has_ticks is true, so the engine's only fallback (`if(tp == NULL) { ... m_fidelity.SetTicksAvailable(false); }`, SSR_ReplayController.mqh:337-345) never fires. CSSRFidelityPolicy::Decide degrades FULL_TICK only on !m_ticks_available (SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh:114-118), which was set true. The range-aware question already exists — CSSRMt5TickProvider::HasTicks(symbol, from_msc, to_msc) at SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:379 — and a repo-wide grep shows it has no caller. This is exactly the failure the Ticks()-returns-NULL contract was written to prevent: its own comment at SSR_Mt5DataSource.mqh:86-89 says "Returning it unconditionally would let the engine run at FULL_TICK fidelity against an empty tick history and emit nothing - a replay that silently shows a frozen market." The file header itself states "Tick history is OPTIONAL and usually shallow, especially for CFDs" (lines 16-19). If CopyTicksRange instead returns -1 while the terminal tries and fails to fetch old ticks, ReadTicks burns its 15 s retry budget per pump and then returns -1, which at least surfaces an error — so the silent variant is the 0 case.

**Verifiers:** Every link in the chain checks out and I found no guard that breaks it. Discover()'s probe window is hardcoded to the newest 24h (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:135-139) and is the ONLY writer of out.has_ticks (grep confirms: the only other has_ticks assignment in production is the memory source). CSSRMt5DataSource copies it once (SSR_Mt5DataSource.mqh:68) and Ticks() returns the provider whenever it is true (91-96), so the NULL-fallback at SSR_ReplayController.mqh:337-345 never fires. Load() sets SetTicksAvailable(range.has_ticks) (806), and Decide() only degrades FULL_TICK on !m_ticks_available (SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh:113-117). With a non-bulk pump, EmitWindow takes the FULL_TICK branch, ReadTicks returns 0 with Succeed() (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:428-430: got==0 -> break -> return total==0), n>0 gates both EmitTicks and PublishTicks, and the code does `emitted = n; m_cursor.Advance(hi, emitted, 0); return emitted;` (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:365-368) - clock advances, nothing emitted, no warning. I grepped for any stall/zero-emit detector in Core and Data: none exists. CSSRMt5TickProvider::HasTicks(symbol, from, to) (line 379) genuinely has no production caller. The host's own diagnostic asserts the opposite (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2267-2271) / Every link in the chain is in the source I read. Discover() decides a range-wide capability from a fixed probe over the newest 24 hours of history - from_msc = last_msc - 86400000, out.has_ticks = (got > 0) (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:132-138) - and nothing else ever sets it. CSSRMt5DataSource copies it into m_has_ticks (SSR_Mt5DataSource.mqh:68) and Ticks() returns the provider whenever it is true (91-96), so the engine's only degradation hook - `if(tp == NULL)` at SSR_ReplayController.mqh:337-345 - cannot fire; CSSRFidelityPolicy::Decide degrades FULL_TICK solely on !m_ticks_available (SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh:114-118), which Load set from range.has_ticks (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:806). The host requests FULL_TICK unconditionally on has_ticks (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1153). With ReadTicks returning 0 for the older window, the FULL_TICK branch does `emitted = n; m_cursor.Advance(hi, emitted, 0); return emitted;` (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:366-368) and never reaches the bar path below, so no bar is emitted and no error is raised; I searched for a stall/no-emission watchdog and there is none (the only `stalls` counter is in the history-download loop, SSReplayStandalone.mq5:652-691). Old windows are fully supported inputs (InpStart is a free datetime at line 70, plus InpPickStart and random start), the range-aware HasTicks(symbol, from_msc, to_msc) at SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:379 has no caller anywhere, and the file's own header (lines 16-19) and SSR_Mt5DataSource.mqh:84-89 both describe precisely this failure. Severity reduced from CRITICAL to HIGH: the failure additionally requires the broker's tick base to be shallower than its M1 base over the chosen window, which is runtime/broker behaviour, and the outcome is a non-functioning replay rather than data corruption or a real order / Confirmed end to end from source, and reachable on a headline feature. Discover() hardcodes the probe to [last_msc - 86400000, last_msc] (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:135-139) and that single newest-day answer is the only thing that sets out.has_ticks. From there: SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1153 selects FULL_TICK on it; SSR_ReplayController.mqh:806 SetTicksAvailable(range.has_ticks); SSR_Mt5DataSource.mqh:68 m_has_ticks = m_range.has_ticks and line 93 hands the provider out whenever it is true, so the NULL-provider fallback at SSR_ReplayController.mqh:337-345 never fires; SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh:114-118 degrades only on !m_ticks_available. EmitWindow's FULL_TICK branch (337-370) then returns emitted = 0 after m_cursor.Advance(hi, 0, 0) with no warning path. The range-aware HasTicks(symbol, from, to) at line 379 has no caller outside tests - I re-grepped. Reachability is high, not exotic: CSSRRandomPicker::Pick (SSR_RandomPicker.mqh:155-175) draws uniformly between EarliestStart and LatestStart over the WHOLE history, so random-start training on a broker with 30 days of ticks and years of M1 lands outside the probe window with near-certainty, and InpStart / the picker do the same deliberately. The user sees the warmup candles, a clock that walks to COMPLETED, and no candle ever forming - while SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2267 prints the opposite reassurance. I hold it at HIGH rather than CRITICAL: nothing is silently WRONG - no money arithmetic is corrupted, no future bar or tick is exposed, no virtual-trading guarantee is weakened, and there is nothing to trade against because the market never moves. It is a total, visible functional failure of the session with a misleading diagnostic, and the category is wrong

#### `mt5-symbol-1` — Warmup repair after a jump is silently skipped whenever the seed was reused from the cache

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Mt5/SSR_CustomSymbolSink.mqh:265`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
bool is_warmup = (m_warmup_to_msc > 0 &&
                  SSRToMsc(bars[count - 1].time) <= m_warmup_to_msc);

if(m_reused_seed && is_warmup)
  {
   m_seed_bars += count;
   return true;
  }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Not merely "a session where CanReuse returned true": because the handover only fires after pass 1 has seeded the warmup and saved the manifest, m_reused_seed is true on pass 2 of the DEFAULT one-window flow, so the silent repair no-op is on the normal path of every session, not an edge case.

**Why:** m_reused_seed is set once in Prepare (line 162) and never cleared for the life of the session; the early return keys only on it and on the bar range, so it cannot distinguish the initial warmup delivery (which the cache legitimately covers) from a later repair of bars the terminal has since discarded. The controller cannot tell either: SeedBars reports success. The next session self-heals only because CanReuse's SERIES_FIRSTDATE evidence check misses.

**Verifiers:** Full trace confirms the silent no-op, and it is worse than latent. m_warmup_to_msc is set by OnWarmupPlanned to m_timeline.start_msc-1 (Controller:814-816). RepairWarmupIfLost (Controller:934-968) re-reads exactly lo=warmup_first_msc..hi=start_msc-1, so bars[n-1].time <= m_warmup_to_msc and is_warmup is unconditionally true at Sink:262-263; with m_reused_seed still set the method returns true at 265-269 without calling WriteBars, and the controller logs "%d bars written back" at 963-967 regardless. m_reused_seed is assigned only in Prepare (Sink:145, 162, 170) and never cleared for the life of the session - there is no other writer in the file. The state is not exotic: pass 1 writes the manifest (Sink:283-293), so on pass 2 of the one-window handover CanReuse (SSR_SeedCache.mqh:161-196) finds a matching version/origin/range with the bars still in the symbol and returns true, meaning the repair is disabled for the normal second pass of every session. RepairWarmupIfLost is reached only from the jump path (Controller:1409), so every forward jump in such a session silently loses the warmup while reporting success / I tried to refute this on three fronts and failed on all three. (1) Range arithmetic: RepairWarmupIfLost reads lo = warmup_first_msc, hi = start_msc - 1 (Controller:951-952), and m_warmup_to_msc is set to exactly start_msc - 1 by OnWarmupPlanned (Controller:814-816 -> Sink:366-367). MqlRates.time is the bar OPEN, so SSRToMsc(bars[count-1].time) <= start_msc-1 always holds for the repair payload; is_warmup (Sink:262-263) is therefore unavoidably true on the repair call. (2) Flag lifetime: m_reused_seed is written in exactly three places, all inside Prepare - cleared at Sink:145, set from CanReuse at Sink:162, re-cleared only if Adopt fails at Sink:170. Nothing in SeedBars, EmitTicks, TruncateFrom, OnReset or Release clears it, so it stays true for the whole session. (3) Reachability: Prepare sets it true on pass 2 of the one-window handover and on T6 run 2, and RepairWarmupIfLost is reached from the jump/seek path at Controller:1409, right after the bulk write the comment blames for the loss. So SeedBars takes the early return at Sink:265-269, does m_seed_bars += count and returns true with WriteBars never called, and the controller - which only checks the boolean - logs 'the sink dropped the warmup ...; %d bars written back' (Controller:963-967). Silent no-op reported as a repair, repeating on every subsequent jump. The precondition (terminal discarding tick-less warmup bars) is the repair routine's own documented, measured trigger, not an assumption this finding introduces. refuted=true records that I could not break it; classification CONFIRMED / Provable end to end, and far more reachable than the finding suggests. m_warmup_to_msc is set to start_msc-1 (Controller:814-816); RepairWarmupIfLost reads lo=warmup_first_msc, hi=start_msc-1 and calls SeedBars (Controller:952-958), so bars[count-1].time <= m_warmup_to_msc and is_warmup is true; with m_reused_seed still true the early return at Sink:265-269 writes nothing and returns success, and the controller then logs "%d bars written back" (963-967). m_reused_seed is set once at Sink:162 and never cleared - not by TruncateFrom, not by OnReset - so the no-op repeats for the life of the session. Reachability: the seed cache is enabled by default (SeedCache:92, the host never disables it), the one-window handover is the default (InpOneChart=true) and is only performed once the replay symbol demonstrably HAS bars (Standalone:2609-2611), which means pass 1 always wrote the warmup and saved the manifest - so pass 2, i.e. every ordinary session, runs with m_reused_seed=true. Any forward jump (the only caller, Controller:1409, placed there precisely because the bulk write is what costs the warmup) therefore leaves the warmup permanently gone while reporting it restored. Observable: the replay chart loses all pre-session history, every indicator lookback on it is wrong for the rest of the session, and attached strategies trade off those values - and the one log line that would have revealed it says the opposite. HIGH stands

#### `ui-port-session-3` — Restore never checks that the streams reached the saved instant, and cannot rewind to it

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Session/SSR_SessionManager.mqh:350`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
      long now = m_group.At(0).Now();
      m_group.SeekAllTo(now);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate except for the mechanism of the trade damage: the account restored from the 10:00 file is not fed the 10:00-10:30 ticks at all (JumpForward bulk-seeds those bars to the sink only, and the wind-forward is skipped entirely on this path), so stops and targets crossed in that stretch are NOT "evaluated half an hour late" - they are never evaluated. The restored open positions simply resume at 10:30+ prices with an unrecorded gap in MAE/MFE and stops that should already have fired still standing, while the panel and ResumeReport announce 10:30 as the resumed instant with no warning.

**Why:** `master_now` is written at l.176 and read only by Peek (l.224) - Restore never reads it, so it has nothing to compare against. The instant is taken from whatever the streams happen to be at, and the stream restore only moves forward: ReplayController::RestoreFrom does `if(want_now > m_clock.now_msc && !JumpTo(want_now))` (l.1826), even though JumpTo handles backwards jumps through StepBackward (l.1494-1515). SeekAllTo(now) is then a no-op (the master is already there), its result is discarded, and ResumeReport prints `SSRFormatMsc(m_group.Now())` (l.391-393) as the resume point. Scenario: save "A" at 10:00, play on to 10:30, open the Session dialog and press Load on "A" (CSSRGroupPort::LoadSession, l.960-972, has no guard of any kind). Streams stay at 10:30; m_acct.RestoreFrom replaces the account with the 10:00 log; every position the file recorded as open is re-installed and marked to 10:30 prices, so any stop or target crossed between 10:00 and 10:30 is evaluated half an hour late, with an MAE/MFE that never happened. The user is told "resumed 1 stream(s) at 10:30".

**Verifiers:** I could not find a guard anywhere on the path. "master_now" is written at SSR_SessionManager.mqh:176 and read only by Peek (l.224); Restore never reads it. The stream restore moves forward only - ReplayController.mqh:1826 is `if(want_now > m_clock.now_msc && !JumpTo(want_now))`, so a saved instant behind the live clock is silently skipped and no warning is set (the fingerprint check that follows at l.1836 compares the file against the range up to the CURRENT clock, and will typically match, so it says nothing either). The instant is then taken from the streams (l.350) and SeekAllTo(now) is a no-op for stream 0 (SSR_MasterClock.mqh:296-322, master.SeekTo(now) then member.JumpTo(now) with target == now), its result discarded. MaxSkewMsc (SSR_MasterClock.mqh:416-430) compares streams against each other, so with one stream it is 0 and the l.360 warning never fires. The mid-session path is reachable with no guard: panel SSR_CMD_SESSIONS -> g_session_dlg.Open (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3088-3092) -> load button -> CSSRGroupPort::LoadSession -> Restore. ResumeReport (l.388-398) and the dialog message then report the CURRENT instant as the resumed one / Verified: `master_now` is written at l.176 and read only by Peek (l.224) - Restore never reads or compares it. CSSRReplayController::RestoreFrom winds forward only, `if(want_now > m_clock.now_msc && !JumpTo(want_now))` (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1826), even though JumpTo handles the backward case through StepBackward (l.1493-1518). Nothing warns when the stream stands past the file's instant: the out-of-window check (l.1786-1795) only rejects instants outside the timeline, the fingerprint check is taken at the CURRENT clock (FingerprintUpTo(m_clock.now_msc)) so it compares the wrong range, and MaxSkewMsc() is 0 for a single stream. m_acct.RestoreFrom then installs the saved log wholesale (m_count=0, positions re-read, balance replayed), and ResumeReport prints SSRFormatMsc(m_group.Now()) - the later instant - with no BUT. The dialog Load path (GroupPort l.960-972) has no guard / Every link is provable and I could not find a guard. master_now is written (l.176) and read only by Peek (l.224) - Restore never reads it. CSSRReplayController::RestoreFrom winds forward only: `if(want_now > m_clock.now_msc && !JumpTo(want_now))` (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1825-1830), while JumpTo itself handles backwards through StepBackward (l.1495-1517). The window check at l.1787 passes for an earlier instant, so nothing errors. MaxSkewMsc() cannot catch it - both streams stay at the same (wrong) instant, skew 0 - and SeekAllTo(m_group.At(0).Now()) only drags the master to where the streams already are (SSR_MasterClock.mqh:296-323). CSSRGroupPort::LoadSession has no guard (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:960-972), and it is user-reachable: SSR_CMD_SESSIONS opens the dialog (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3089-3092) and the Load button calls straight through (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:273). Upgraded confidence in the money consequence: the restored open positions are checked against the current (later) price on the next tick, and StopFill takes the WORSE of the level and that price (`double fill = m_pos[i].sl; if(px < fill) fill = px;` SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:492-511), so a stop that should have filled at its level mid-gap fills at a price half an hour further on - an arbitrarily larger loss booked into the account. The equity curve is restored to the earlier samples with m_eq_last_msc set from them (SSR_Statistics.mqh:656-658), so the curve gains a hole across the skipped stretch and its drawdown is wrong. This corrupts virtual money arithmetic and is silent, so HIGH stands

#### `spikes-audits-1` — SSR_BarToTicks never emits a tick at the bar's high or low unless (n-1) is divisible by 3

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:579`** · category: sl-tp-execution

```mql5
double u   = (cnt == 1) ? 0.0 : (double)i * 3.0 / (double)(cnt - 1);
int    seg = (int)MathFloor(u);
if(seg > 2) seg = 2;
double f   = u - (double)seg;
double p   = k[seg] + (k[seg + 1] - k[seg]) * f;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Confirmed and broader than the title: the same defect is in the shipping engine (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:134-144) at its default 8 ticks/bar, where the shortfall is ~14.3% of the adjacent leg, and because CheckStops evaluates SL/TP off the tick price alone it silently misses stops placed near a bar's extreme. The spike kit is only where it was first written.

**Why:** u only hits the integer keypoints when (cnt-1) is a multiple of 3. Replaying the formula for cnt in {4,7,10,16} gives an exact high and low; for cnt in {8,20,50} it does not (shortfalls of 1.43, 0.53 and 0.20 on an O=100/H=140/L=90/C=130 bar). cnt = MathMax(n,4) at kit:565, and the final forced write at kit:595-597 fixes only the close, never the extremes. The header at kit:560 calls this 'the same assumption the engine will make, tested here first', and the engine's CSSRTickSynthesizer::Synthesize (Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:134-143) is the identical formula with a default InpTicksPerBar of 8 - the worst case measured - while its own header (line 13) claims 'The OHLC of the resulting bar is exact'.

**Verifiers:** The arithmetic replays exactly and the defect reaches the product. In SSR_BarToTicks (SpikeKit:565-598) u = i*3/(cnt-1), so k[1] and k[2] are sampled only when some i makes u an integer, i.e. when (cnt-1)%3==0; the forced write at 594-597 fixes the close alone. For cnt=20 (B1:23 InpPerBar) the nearest samples to u=1 and u=2 are 0.9474/1.1053 and 1.8947/2.0526, giving the quoted ~5.3% shortfall on each extreme. Crucially CSSRTickSynthesizer::Synthesize (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:134-183) is the identical formula with m_ticks_per_bar defaulting to 8 (line 71) and InpTicksPerBar=8 in the shipping host (Standalone.mq5:75, 1148), where the step is 3/7 and the extremes are under-reached by 14.3% of the adjacent leg - contradicting the file header's "The OHLC of the resulting bar is exact" (line 11). The sl-tp-execution category is right, not a stretch: CSSRTradingEngine::OnTicks feeds m_bid/m_ask per tick and CheckStops (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:513-544) compares only that tick price against sl/tp, so a stop sitting in the outer 14% of the O-to-extreme leg is never triggered even though BarIsAmbiguousFor considers the bar's real range / I re-derived the arithmetic rather than trusting it, and it holds. u = i*3/(cnt-1) reaches the interior keypoints k[1] and k[2] exactly only when some i makes u == 1.0 and u == 2.0, i.e. when (cnt-1) % 3 == 0. cnt = MathMax(n,4) (SpikeKit:565). For B1's InpPerBar=20: cnt-1 = 19, nearest u values to 2.0 are 12*3/19 = 1.8947 (seg 1, f 0.8947) and 13*3/19 = 2.0526 (seg 2, f 0.0526), giving a peak of H - 0.0526*(H-C); nearest to 1.0 are 0.947 and 1.105, giving a trough of L + 0.0526*(O-L). On O=100/H=140/L=90/C=130 that is 139.47 and 90.53 - the 0.53 the finding states. The forced final write (SpikeKit:595-597) fixes only the close, and i = cnt-1 already lands on the close anyway (u=3 -> seg clamped to 2, f=1.0 -> p = k[3]). The load-bearing part is that the product shares the formula verbatim: CSSRTickSynthesizer::Synthesize (Core/SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:130-175) is the same u/seg/f interpolation with m_ticks_per_bar defaulting to 8 and the host input InpTicksPerBar = 8 (Standalone:75, 1148, 497). With n=8, cnt-1 = 7: the low is missed by 0.143*(O-L) and the high by 0.143*(H-C) - a 14.3% shortfall on every synthesised bar - while that file's own header line 12 states 'The OHLC of the resulting bar is exact'. No integer-division or array-bounds misreading is involved; the arithmetic is all double. Only cnt in {4,7,10,13,16,...} is exact. refuted=true records that I could not break it / The arithmetic checks out exactly. u = i*3/(cnt-1) reaches the interior keypoints only when (cnt-1) is a multiple of 3; for cnt=8 the samples straddle 1 and 2 at 0.857/1.286 and 1.714/2.143, so the nearest approach to the extreme keypoint k2 is k2 + (k3-k2)/7 - the replayed high falls short by (H-C)/7 and the low by (O-L)/7 on a bullish bar (1.43 on the finding's 100/140/90/130 bar, matching). The forced write at SpikeKit:595-597 (and Synthesizer:181-185) repairs only the close. What earns HIGH is not the spike file the finding is filed against but the identical formula in the shipped engine (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:134-143) with default InpTicksPerBar=8 (Standalone:75, 1148): live replay at synthetic fidelity puts bars into the symbol ONLY through these ticks - SeedBars/CustomRatesUpdate is used exclusively for warmup, repair and jump bulk (Controller:893, 960, 1383), and nothing ever rewrites a completed bar - so the replayed candle is genuinely narrower than the source bar and the virtual engine's stop/target checks never see the wick tip. On a wick-heavy bar (O=100, L=99, H=140, C=101) the replayed high is 134.4 against a real 140: an SL or TP in the top 13% of that bar's range does not trigger in replay when it would have in the market, and no test asserts otherwise (QA smoke uses SetTicksPerBar(10), one of the values that happens to be exact). This is execution fidelity and therefore money outcomes, and it contradicts the engine header's own claim at Synthesizer:11 that "The OHLC of the resulting bar is exact". Reachable whenever the chosen range has no real tick history (Standalone:1153 selects FULL_TICK only when range.has_ticks)

#### `strategy-integration-report-1` — In FULL_TICK fidelity the market view is never fed bars, so a strategy reads a snapshot frozen at the first pump

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Strategy/SSR_MarketView.mqh:173`** · category: future-data-leakage

```mql5
   virtual void      OnBarContext(const MqlRates &bar, const bool synthetic) override
     {
      m_synthetic = synthetic;
      Grow();
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate except for "no OnBarContext for the whole session": any pump owing >= SSR_BULK_THRESHOLD_MSC (10 minutes, e.g. after a real timer stall) degrades to SSR_FIDELITY_BAR in CSSRFidelityPolicy::Decide and does take the bar-driven path, publishing bars and refreshing the view. In steady FULL_TICK playback, which is the normal case, no bar is ever published and the view stays frozen at the first prime, so the defect stands; it is also gated on InpRefStrategy (default false) plus broker tick history existing for the symbol.

**Why:** CSSRMarketView's only source of bars is OnBarContext (this line) and Prime(). In SSR_ReplayController.mqh the FULL_TICK branch calls PublishTicks(m_ticks, n) at line 366 and returns at line 369 without ever calling PublishBar; grep shows PublishBar has exactly one call site, line 506, inside the bar-driven branch that FULL_TICK has already returned from. So m_count only ever changes through Prime(). SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2797 gates PrimeView on g_view.M1Count()==0, which becomes false after the first successful prime and never returns to 0 unless a rewind trims every bar. The view's m_now_msc, m_bid and m_ask keep advancing through OnTicks/OnClock, so Now()/Bid()/Ask() are live while every bar accessor is stale - the strategy has no way to detect the divergence, because Available() still reports the frozen groups.

**Verifiers:** The starvation path is provable. CSSRMarketView's only bar inputs are OnBarContext and Prime; grep shows OnBarContext has exactly one caller (PublishBar, SSR_ReplayController.mqh:191-196) and PublishBar exactly one call site (506), inside the bar-driven section that the FULL_TICK branch has already returned from at line 369 (it calls PublishTicks at 366 and returns). Prime has exactly one caller, PrimeView (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:403), which OnTimer gates on `g_view.M1Count() == 0` (2797) - false forever after the first successful prime, since nothing resets m_count except OnSessionStart or a rewind that trims everything. Meanwhile OnTicks (191-198) and OnClock (200-201) keep m_bid/m_ask/m_now_msc live, so Now()/Bid()/Ask() advance while every bar accessor is frozen, and Available() keeps reporting the frozen groups, so the strategy cannot detect it. SSR_RefStrategy.mqh:96-128 does exactly what the claim says: Bar(m_tf,1), HighestHigh/LowestLow(m_tf,2,lookback), then compares sl against ctx.market.Bid()/Ask(). FULL_TICK is selected whenever the scanned range has tick history (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1153) and the pump does not hit the bulk threshold / Every link verified. CSSRMarketView's bars enter only via OnBarContext (173) and Prime (389). grep shows PublishBar has exactly one call site, SSR_ReplayController.mqh:506, inside the bar-driven section that the FULL_TICK branch returns from at line 369 after PublishTicks; SeedWarmup pushes only to the sink, not to observers, and Load broadcasts only OnSessionStart (837). CSSRFidelityPolicy::Decide keeps FULL_TICK whenever the requested fidelity is FULL_TICK, ticks are available and owed < SSR_BULK_THRESHOLD_MSC (10 min), which at InpPumpMs=40 holds for every speed on the ladder (top 1000x gives 40 s per beat). SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2797 gates PrimeView on g_view.M1Count()==0, which is false after the first successful prime, and the EA chooses FULL_TICK whenever range.has_ticks (1153). So with InpRefStrategy on a tick-carrying symbol the bar buffer is written once and then frozen while OnTicks/OnClock keep Now()/Bid()/Ask() live, and Available() keeps reporting the frozen groups / Confirmed end to end. CSSRMarketView's only bar inputs are OnBarContext and Prime; grep shows PublishBar has exactly one call site, SSR_ReplayController.mqh:506, inside the bar-driven branch, and the FULL_TICK branch returns at line 369 after PublishTicks without reaching it. SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2797 gates PrimeView on g_view.M1Count() == 0, which is false forever after the first successful prime. So in FULL_TICK the bar buffer is written once and then frozen while m_now_msc/m_bid/m_ask keep advancing through OnTicks/OnClock. Reachability is high, not exotic: line 1153-1154 selects FULL_TICK whenever range.has_ticks, and has_ticks is just CopyTicks returning something (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:139), true for most brokers on recent history - so FULL_TICK is the DEFAULT for the normal case, and the only other condition is turning on the strategy layer. What the user observes is worse than the finding states: because the frozen snapshot never changes, the `last.close > hi` test in CSSRRefBreakout::OnBar evaluates identically on every bar for the rest of the session, so the strategy either never signals again or re-enters the same stale breakout every time MyOpenCount() drops to zero, with a stop anchored to an hour-old range and a risk-derived lot sized off that distance. Available() keeps reporting the frozen group count, so no refusal counter or status line reveals it. HIGH stands: the layer whose stated purpose is that a strategy can only see published reality delivers a stale snapshot in the product's default fidelity, and virtual trades are placed on it

#### `trading-analytics-1` — Resuming a saved session voids a running prop evaluation on startup

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_PropEvaluation.mqh:443`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
virtual void      OnRewind(const long msc) override
     {
      if(!m_rules.enabled || m_state != SSR_PROP_RUNNING)
         return;
      m_state  = SSR_PROP_VOID;
      m_reason = "the clock was moved backwards at " + SSRFormatMsc(msc) +
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate as to mechanism and reachability. Severity is MEDIUM rather than HIGH: the evaluation fails safe and visibly (VOID plus an auto-pause message naming Reset), never producing a false verdict; the defect is that the restore-to-now notification is indistinguishable from a real rewind, so the reason text is also factually wrong.

**Why:** Host SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1197 calls g_prop.Reset() (state RUNNING when enabled) and :1200 Watch(g_prop); g_ctrl.Load (:1252) fires OnSessionStart -> Reset() again (RUNNING); then :1483 g_session_mgr.Restore() -> SSR_SessionManager.mqh:381 m_group.At(i).NotifyRestored() -> SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1669 PublishRewind(m_clock.now_msc) -> every observer's OnRewind including this one, whose only guard is m_state != RUNNING. g_ctrl is in g_group (host :1320). The rewind is to 'now', so no clock actually moved backwards, yet the reason text says it did.

**Verifiers:** Every link in the chain is in the source I read. Host: g_prop.Reset() runs unconditionally after SetRules and Watch(GetPointer(g_prop)) runs when CfgProp() (SSReplayStandalone.mq5, the prop block around l.1184-1203), and Watch() is g_ctrl.AddObserver (l.869-877), so the evaluation is an observer of g_ctrl. g_ctrl is m_group's first stream (l.1320 g_group.Add(GetPointer(g_ctrl))). g_ctrl.Load (l.1252) fans OnSessionStart out to every observer (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:835-837), whose override calls Reset() (l.343-349) leaving m_state = SSR_PROP_RUNNING because enabled. Later, l.1483 g_session_mgr.Restore(CfgSession()) ends with 'At(i).NotifyRestored()' per stream (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:379-381) = PublishRewind(m_clock.now_msc) (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1668-1669) = OnRewind on every observer (l.233-238). OnRewind's only guard is m_state != SSR_PROP_RUNNING (l.445-446), which does not hold, so the run goes VOID with m_pause_pending = true before a single candle plays, and the reason text asserts a backwards clock move that did not happen - the rewind is to 'now'. The pending pause is then consumed by CheckObserverPause at the end of the first Pump. Requires InpProp plus InpResume plus an existing session file, exactly as claimed / The whole chain is in source and unconditional. Host: g_prop.SetRules/Reset() (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1195-1197) leaves m_state = SSR_PROP_RUNNING whenever rules.enabled, Watch(g_prop) adds it to g_ctrl's observer list (Watch at l.869-871 calls g_ctrl.AddObserver), g_ctrl is in g_group (l.1320), and g_ctrl.Load fires OnSessionStart -> Reset() again (RUNNING). Then Restore (l.1481-1486 when resuming) ends with 'for i: m_group.At(i).NotifyRestored()' (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:381-382) -> CSSRReplayController::NotifyRestored = PublishRewind(m_clock.now_msc) (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1668-1669) -> every observer's OnRewind (l.233-237). CSSRPropEvaluation::OnRewind guards only on enabled && state == RUNNING (l.445-446), so it flips to SSR_PROP_VOID and sets m_pause_pending, which CheckObserverPause consumes on the first pump. The reason string claims the clock moved backwards when the 'rewind' is to now_msc itself. No runtime assumption is involved - this is pure call-graph, and it disables the evaluation feature entirely for anyone who uses InpSession + InpResume / The chain is fully verifiable. Host: g_prop.Reset() at :1197 (state -> RUNNING when CfgProp()), Watch(g_prop) at :1200 adds it to g_ctrl's observers (Watch -> g_ctrl.AddObserver, :869-877); g_ctrl.Load at :1252 publishes OnSessionStart to every observer (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:835-837), whose prop override calls Reset() again -> RUNNING; g_ctrl is in g_group (:1320); then :1483 g_session_mgr.Restore -> SSR_SessionManager.mqh:378-381 m_group.At(i).NotifyRestored() -> SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1668-1669 PublishRewind(m_clock.now_msc) -> l.233-238 calls OnRewind on every observer, and CSSRPropEvaluation::OnRewind (l.443-452) guards only on enabled && RUNNING, so it goes VOID with a reason text asserting the clock moved backwards when in fact it was published AT now. Both preconditions are user inputs (InpProp, plus a session name with InpResume defaulting true), so a prop evaluation can never survive a restart. I downgrade from HIGH because the failure is fail-safe and loud rather than silent: it produces VOID, never a false PASS or FAILED, announces itself through PauseRequested ('evaluation VOID - ...') and names the remedy. No money arithmetic is corrupted and no future data leaks; what is lost is the usability of prop-plus-resume, which lands at MEDIUM

#### `trading-exec-2` — SL/TP changes, trailing-stop movement, break-even and MAE/MFE are not versioned, so they survive a rewind

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:263`** · category: event-handling

```mql5
void              UnwindLegsAfter(const int i, const long msc)
  {
   while(m_pos[i].leg_count > 0 &&
         m_pos[i].legs[m_pos[i].leg_count - 1].msc > msc)
     {
      int e = --m_pos[i].leg_count;
```
