**What goes wrong:** State: long at 1.1000 with trail_points=200 (SetTrailing is applied automatically to every new trade when a trail distance is set, GroupPort 1062-1063). Price runs to 1.1100 so ApplyTrailing moves sl to 1.1080 and trail_peak to 1.1100. Action: step back to the minute when price was 1.1020. Outcome: OnRewind keeps the position (placed before the cut) but leaves sl=1.1080 and trail_peak=1.1100. The first re-emitted tick has bid 1.1020 <= sl, so CheckStops closes the trade at StopFill = 1.1020 - slip, reason SL, and AutoPause announces 'stop loss hit' - at a moment when the stop had really been at 1.1000 and the trade was +20 pips. The same applies to a manual Modify() or BreakEven() done in the deleted future: the un-happened stop/target stays. mae/mfe and spread_at_exit likewise keep values from the deleted future for kept positions.

**Why:** SSRTradeLeg (SSR_TradeTypes.mqh 143-169) records only volume/price/msc/realised/fee/closing/prev_swap_locked/prev_swap_from_msc/prev_ambiguous. OnRewind (634-702) touches sl/tp/trail_peak/mae/mfe only in the un-fill branch for pendings (676-679) and never for a kept open position. ApplyTrailing (302-323) is the only writer of trail_peak and it only ever moves the long stop up (`if(want > m_pos[i].sl)`), so a future-trailed stop is never lowered again. CheckStops 526-527 then compares the current bid to that stale sl.

**Verifiers:** Verified from the leg struct and OnRewind. SSRTradeLeg (SSR_TradeTypes.mqh 143-168) stores volume/price/msc/realised/fee/closing/prev_swap_locked/prev_swap_from_msc/prev_ambiguous only - no sl, tp, trail_peak, mae or mfe. OnRewind (634-702) resets trail_peak/mae/mfe/risk_at_entry only inside the pending-un-fill branch (676-679); a position placed before the cut and still open is copied forward untouched (692-694), keeping the sl a future ApplyTrailing wrote. ApplyTrailing (302-323) only ever raises a long's sl (`if(want > m_pos[i].sl)`), so the future stop is never retracted, and on the first re-emitted tick CheckStops 526-527 compares the current bid to it and 539/543 books a SL close at StopFill (492-511) = bid - slip. GroupPort MarketAt/Market apply SetTrailing to every new trade when m_trail_points > 0 (SSR_GroupPort.mqh 1061-1063), so the trigger is one panel setting, not an exotic path. Modify (844-853) and BreakEven (920-927) write sl/tp with no versioning either, so a stop moved in the deleted future also survives / Provable from source. SSRTradeLeg (SSR_TradeTypes.mqh 144-168) records volume/price/msc/realised/fee/closing/prev_swap_locked/prev_swap_from_msc/prev_ambiguous and nothing about sl, tp, trail_peak, mae or mfe, so there is no snapshot for a rewind to restore. OnRewind (634-702) writes sl/tp/trail_peak/mae/mfe only inside the pending-un-fill branch (670-679); a position that was already open at the cut keeps every value the deleted future set. ApplyTrailing (302-323) ratchets one way only ('if(want > m_pos[i].sl)' for a long), so a stop trailed up in the deleted future is never lowered again, and CheckStops 526-527 compares the re-emitted bid against it, closing at StopFill (492-511) with reason SL. GroupPort 1060-1064 does arm trailing automatically on every risk-sized trade when m_trail_points > 0, so the precondition is a user setting, not an exotic path. Severity trimmed from HIGH: the fabricated SL close needs trailing armed or a Modify/BreakEven in the deleted future; what is unconditional for every rewound open position is stale mae/mfe (and spread_at_exit on a re-opened leg), which corrupts reported excursions rather than the balance / Confirmed from source and reachable without any unusual user action. SSRTradeLeg carries no sl/tp/trail_peak/mae/mfe, and OnRewind (634-702) rewrites those fields only inside the pending-un-fill branch (676-679); a position that was already open before the cut keeps whatever sl/tp/trail_peak/mae/mfe the deleted future gave it. ApplyTrailing (302-323) only ever ratchets the long stop up (`if(want > m_pos[i].sl)`) / the short stop down, so a stop trailed in the deleted future can never be walked back, and CheckStops (526-527) compares the re-emitted bid to that stale level. The trailing path is armed automatically, not manually: GroupPort.MarketAt applies m_trail_points to every new trade (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:1062-1063) and GroupPort.SetTrailing back-applies it to everything already open (811-829), so one typed trailing distance in the panel puts every subsequent trade on this path. The same hole covers a manual Modify (844-853) or BreakEven (920-927) performed in the deleted future. What the user observes: they step back to a moment when the trade was in profit, the very next re-emitted tick books it as 'stop loss hit' (auto-pause announces it) at a level the replay has just un-happened, and the loss lands in m_balance. That is future price data leaking across a rewind into a real money outcome, which is exactly the guarantee the file's own REWIND CORRECTNESS header claims, so HIGH is right

#### `ui-panel-3` — HideBody(false) at line 729 undoes HideSheetArea(true) at line 682 inside the same Render(), so compact mode leaves the tab rail and action strip on the candles

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:682`** · category: object-lifecycle

```mql5
      if(m_compact != was_compact)
        {
         HideSheetArea(m_compact);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate, with two refinements. (1) The stranding only happens when the panel has already painted a FULL frame and the chart then shrinks: if the panel starts compact, HideSheetArea and HideBody both act on objects that do not exist yet, so nothing is left behind. (2) HideSheetArea is not a total no-op - its HideSheets() call (:2049) genuinely deletes the sheet contents; it is a no-op only for the eight ids in its own list, all of which HideBody(false) re-shows 47 lines later.

**Why:** HideSheetArea (:2042-2050) hides those ids, but it runs at :682, forty-seven lines BEFORE :729 `HideBody(false);` - and HideBody's id list (:2079-2098) contains 'tabline','lines','sessions','fidelity' and the loop `for(int t = 0; t < SSR_TAB_MAX; t++) m_w.Hide("tab" + IntegerToString(t), hidden);`. With hidden==false, CSSRWidgets::Hide writes OBJPROP_TIMEFRAMES = OBJ_ALL_PERIODS (SSR_Widgets.mqh:620-621), making every one of them visible again in the same call. The `if(!m_compact)` guard at :735 then skips DrawActions/DrawRail/DrawSheet, so nothing removes or repositions them; only the sheet contents are genuinely gone, because HideSheetArea's HideSheets() (:2049) Removes rather than Hides. HideSheetArea is therefore a no-op for everything it names.

**Verifiers:** Traced Render() line by line. HideSheetArea(m_compact) runs at :682 only on the compact/full TRANSITION; HideBody(false) at :729 runs on every non-collapsed, non-closed frame and its id list (:2078-2083) contains "tabline","lines","sessions","fidelity" plus the loop `for(int t=0;t<SSR_TAB_MAX;t++) m_w.Hide("tab"+t, hidden)` (:2088-2091). CSSRWidgets::Hide with hidden==false writes OBJPROP_TIMEFRAMES=OBJ_ALL_PERIODS whenever ObjectFind>=0 (SSR_Widgets.mqh:619-621), so every one of those objects is made visible again 47 lines after being hidden, in the same call. The `if(!m_compact)` guard at :735 then skips DrawActions/DrawRail/DrawSheet, so nothing repositions or removes them. No other call site exists (grep: HideSheetArea only at :682). Geometry checks out: SSR_PANEL_H = 23+32+27+21+21+186+18+8 = 336, so compact fires below 360 px; cy walks y+23 -> +32 -> +27 -> +21 = y+103 for DrawActions (y+103..y+124) and rail cells at y+128/153/178/203/228 at 22 px each, against a compact body of SSR_PANEL_COMPACT_H=128 with the status strip at y+109..y+126. PollClicks (:2476-2486) walks ObjectsTotal(OBJ_BUTTON) by prefix with no visibility filter, so the stranded tab buttons are live. The only precondition the finding already states correctly: the objects must have been created in full layout first (a panel that starts compact never creates them, because Hide() no-ops on a missing object) / Provable from source and arithmetic. SSR_Panel.mqh:682 calls HideSheetArea(m_compact) on the transition; :729 HideBody(false) runs unconditionally in the same Render() and its id list (:2079-2085) contains "tabline","lines","sessions","fidelity" plus the loop `for(int t=0;t<SSR_TAB_MAX;t++) m_w.Hide("tab"+IntegerToString(t), hidden)` (:2091-2092). CSSRWidgets::Hide (SSR_Widgets.mqh:616-625) writes OBJPROP_TIMEFRAMES=OBJ_ALL_PERIODS when hidden==false, so every id HideSheetArea just hid is made visible again 47 lines later, and `if(!m_compact)` at :735 then skips DrawActions/DrawRail/DrawSheet so nothing repositions or removes them. Geometry checks out: SSR_PANEL_H = 23+32+27+21+21+186+18+8 = 336 (SSR_Theme.mqh:499) so compact fires under 360 px; cy runs y+23 -> DrawClock +32 -> DrawTransport +SSR_BTN_H+SSR_GAP(27) = y+82 -> DrawSpeed +SSR_ROW_H+2 = y+103; DrawActions draws at y+103..124 and DrawRail at y+128 with 22+3 cells at y+128/153/178/203/228, all past the 128 px compact body (SSR_PANEL_COMPACT_H, SSR_Theme.mqh:504) whose status strip is y+109..127. Only the sheet contents are genuinely gone, because HideSheetArea's HideSheets() Removes. Note the bug is latent on a chart that is compact from the first frame (the objects were never created, and Hide/Forget on a missing object is a no-op) - it needs the full->compact transition the finding describes / Order is provable: the compact transition calls HideSheetArea(m_compact) at :682, and HideBody(false) at :729 runs unconditionally in the same Render() on the same ids ('tabline','lines','sessions','fidelity' and the tab0..tab4 loop at :2088-2090), writing OBJPROP_TIMEFRAMES=OBJ_ALL_PERIODS (SSR_Widgets.mqh:619-621). With `if(!m_compact)` at :735 skipping DrawActions/DrawRail/DrawSheet, nothing repositions or removes them. Geometry checks out: SSR_PANEL_H=336 (23+32+27+21+21+186+18+8) so compact fires under 360 px; compact body is 128 with the status strip at y+109..y+127, while the stranded action strip sits at y+103..y+124 and the rail cells at y+128/153/178/203/228. Reachable in ordinary use (Ctrl+T opens the Toolbox mid-session). Downgraded from HIGH on impact: the damage is purely visual/layout - stray still-clickable tab buttons over the candles and an action strip overlapping the status strip. No money arithmetic, no future-data leak, no weakening of the virtual-trade guarantee; the operated controls (clock, transport, speed) still work, and a click on a stranded tab only changes m_tab, whose sheet is not drawn

#### `ui-dialogs-1` — ReadAll() wipes session_name and extra_tfs whenever it runs on a step that has no edit boxes

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:300`** · category: input-validation

```mql5

     {

```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Broader than stated: because 'go' only exists on the START step, which never contains the xtf/ses boxes, extra_tfs and session_name are wiped on EVERY start path (wizard and quick), not only after a MODE-step Next - so panel-set extra timeframes never take effect, session saving never happens, and a session name previously stored in setup.ini is overwritten with empty.

**Why:** CSSRWidgets::EditText returns "" both for an empty box and for an ABSENT box (SSR_Widgets.mqh:350-356 - `if(ObjectFind(m_chart,n) < 0) return ""`). Num() defends against this by returning its fallback (SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:285-287), and ReadAll's seed read defends explicitly with `m_w.Exists("eseed")` plus a comment naming this exact hazard (:1180-1189) - but Str() cannot tell the two cases apart and ReadAll calls it unguarded at :1174-1175 (`m_v.extra_tfs = Str("xtf", m_v.extra_tfs); m_v.session_name = Str("ses", m_v.session_name);`). ReadAll runs on every 'next' press regardless of step (:1061-1067) and on 'go' (:1076). Repaint() calls m_w.RemoveAll() (:658) which is ObjectsDeleteAll by prefix (SSR_Widgets.mqh:637-643), so on steps 0/2/3 the ses/xtf objects genuinely do not exist. Host chain: SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2518-2520 Values->Save, :224-241 CfgSession(), :2135-2143 the OnDeinit save gated on CfgSession() != "".

**Verifiers:** Traced end to end and it is worse than described. Str() (:300-307) cannot distinguish an emptied box from an absent one because CSSRWidgets::EditText returns "" for both (SSR_Widgets.mqh:350-356), and ReadAll calls it unguarded at :1174-1175 while the neighbouring seed read explicitly defends with m_w.Exists("eseed") (:1184). Repaint() calls m_w.RemoveAll() (:658 -> ObjectsDeleteAll by prefix, SSR_Widgets.mqh:637-643), and only RenderSettings creates "exf"/"eses" (:786, :804) - RenderQuick, RenderMode and RenderStart create none. Since 'go' exists only on step 3 (:941) and ReadAll runs on every 'go' (:1076) and on every 'next' (:1061-1063), m_v.extra_tfs and m_v.session_name are ALWAYS "" by the time the session is built, on every path including the wizard. Downstream is as claimed: host :2518-2520 copies the wiped values and sets g_setup_ready, CfgSession()/CfgExtraTfs() (:229, :231) then return "", the OnDeinit save is gated on CfgSession() != "" (:2135) and never runs, and CSSRSetupPanel::Save writes session=/extra_tfs= empty back to setup.ini (:1223, :1225), destroying a name restored from a previous run. No clamp, fallback or caller guard prevents it / Provable from the source read. CSSRWidgets::EditText returns "" for an ABSENT object as well as an empty one (SSR_Widgets.mqh: `if(ObjectFind(m_chart,n) < 0) return ""`), and Str() (:300-307) discards its `fallback` parameter entirely in that case - the parameter is dead. ReadAll assigns m_v.extra_tfs = Str("xtf", ...) and m_v.session_name = Str("ses", ...) unguarded at :1174-1175, while the two siblings in the same function defend correctly: Num() returns its fallback on "" (:285-287) and the seed read is wrapped in `m_w.Exists("eseed")` with a comment naming this exact hazard (:1181-1188). ReadAll is called from the unconditional 'next' handler (:1061-1067), reached from step 2 (RenderMode creates a "next" button, :862), and from 'go' (:1076) on step 3 - neither step draws eses/exft, and Repaint()'s m_w.RemoveAll() (:658 -> ObjectsDeleteAll by prefix) guarantees the objects are gone. So both fields are cleared on the normal wizard path and on the quick path. Host consequence verified: g_setup_ready is set at SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2519, CfgSession() then returns the empty g_setup.session_name (:230), and the whole-session save in OnDeinit is gated on `if(CfgSession() != "")` (:2135) - it never runs. CfgExtraTfs() (:228) likewise loses InpExtraTfs, so no extra-timeframe streams are built / Provable and worse than described. Str() (:299-306) returns "" for an absent box, which CSSRWidgets::EditText cannot distinguish from an emptied one (SSR_Widgets.mqh:350-356), and ReadAll calls it unguarded for both xtf and ses (:1174-1175) while Num() and the seed read both defend against exactly this (:285-287, :1180-1189). Repaint()'s RemoveAll is ObjectsDeleteAll by prefix (:658, SSR_Widgets.mqh:637-643), so on steps 0/2/3 SSRS_eses genuinely does not exist. ReadAll runs on every 'next' (:1061) and on 'go' (:1075), so the wipe is not an edge case - it is on every path that can reach START. Impact reachable in normal use: step1->next->step2->next clears a name the user typed one screen earlier, and the quick path (qlast/qcont -> step 3 -> go) clears the name restored from setup.ini. Downstream the loss is real data loss, not cosmetic: g_setup_ready becomes true so CfgSession() returns the emptied value and OVERRIDES InpSession (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:231), the OnDeinit whole-session save is gated on CfgSession() != "" (:2135-2143) so the account, trades and position are never written, and BuildSession's `resuming` test (:1052) is false, so pressing "Continue <name>" does not continue. Note the one mitigation: when a named session file already exists and InpResume is on, will_resume (:2015) skips the picker entirely and the panel never opens - so it is the FIRST save under a name that can never succeed, which is what makes the feature unusable rather than intermittently lossy. HIGH stands

### MEDIUM (58)

I grepped the whole file: `g_group.Play()` at 1712 is the only Play site, so nothing else starts the replay. The correct predicate for "about to hand over" is `one_chart_ok && !g_on_replay_chart`, which the same file writes correctly at 1275 and 1689-1694. Latency noted by the finder is accurate and does not make the guard correct / The condition is provably wrong. one_chart_ok is re-derived from InpOneChart at 1893 on EVERY pass, and the only thing that clears it is the '!' poison, so on pass 2 (g_on_replay_chart true, g_replay_chart==ChartID() at 1275, g_ready true) `!one_chart_ok` is false and Play() is never reached, while the else branch prints 'this pass has no chart to play on for more than a moment' on the pass that owns the chart. The same file expresses the correct predicate twice (1275, 1689-1694): only `g_on_replay_chart || !one_chart_ok` identifies the handover pass. Downgraded from HIGH on the impact lens: the state is unreachable in the shipped build, because pass 2 always dies at host-expert-1 before this line runs, and in two-window mode (one_chart_ok false) auto-play works correctly. It becomes the user-visible 'motionless chart' failure the moment host-expert-1 is fixed, so it must be fixed in the same change - hence MEDIUM rather than LOW

#### `host-expert-6` — Pass 2 restores setup.ini unconditionally, so a run that never opened the setup form inherits an unrelated earlier run's balance, prop rules and session name and they overrule this run's inputs

**`MQL5/Experts/SSReplay/SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1948`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
      g_setup_ready = CSSRSetupPanel::Restore(g_setup);
      if(g_setup_ready)
         Print("[host] carrying the setup across the handover");
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate, but latent behind host-expert-1 (pass 2 currently returns INIT_FAILED at 1911 before reaching line 1948) and it additionally requires a setup.ini left by an earlier run - HIGH overstates present-day impact.

**Why:** CSSRSetupPanel::Restore (Ui/SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:1238-1260) only checks FileIsExist(SSR_SETUP_FILE) and overwrites every field it finds; it carries no run identity, no timestamp and no origin symbol. The only writer is the picker's START button (2520), which runs on a pass-1 path the four skip conditions bypass. The file's own comment at 1926-1931 states the rule it means to follow ('Only on the replay chart. A first pass reading the file would overrule inputs a user had deliberately set for THIS run') - the second pass overruling those same inputs is the same defect in the other direction. Latent behind host-expert-1.

**Verifiers:** Traced: the `if(on_replay)` block at 1935-1949 calls CSSRSetupPanel::Restore(g_setup) with no test of whether THIS run wrote the file, and Restore (SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:1238-1260) checks only FileIsExist and then overwrites every field it finds - it carries no run identity, timestamp or origin symbol. The handover is armed by `one_chart_ok && !g_on_replay_chart` (1786) and is independent of whether the picker ran, while setup.ini's only writer is the picker's START button (~2520), which the four skip conditions at 2020 (InpPickStart false, InpStart>0, CfgRandom(), will_resume) bypass. So on those runs pass 1 builds from inputs and pass 2 switches every Cfg*() - balance, risk, spread, speed, chart tf, blind, session name, prop rules - to a stale file. CfgSession() then drives both the resume decision (1043-1045) and the OnDeinit overwrite (2136-2143) / Verified end to end. CSSRSetupPanel::Restore (Ui/SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:1237-1259) gates on nothing but FileIsExist(SSR_SETUP_FILE) plus a successful Load/Select, carries no run identity, timestamp or origin symbol, and overwrites every one of the fourteen fields it finds. It is called unconditionally inside `if(on_replay)` at 1948 with only the inputs pre-seeded as defaults, so a stale file overrules them all. The four skip conditions at 2025 (`InpPickStart && !on_replay && InpStart == 0 && !CfgRandom() && !will_resume`) do not skip the handover - the handover is armed at the tail of BuildSession (1798-1805) whenever one_chart_ok && !g_on_replay_chart - so runs that never wrote setup.ini still reach pass 2 and inherit someone else's balance, prop rules, blind mode, chart timeframe and session name. The session-name consequence is real in both directions: CfgSession() drives the resume read at 1057-1070/1124 and the Save in OnDeinit at 2131-2141. Latent behind host-expert-1 / Confirmed from source: the pass-2 block seeds g_setup from the inputs and then calls CSSRSetupPanel::Restore(g_setup) unconditionally at 1948; Restore only tests FileIsExist and overwrites every key the file holds (Ui/SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:1238-1260), carrying no run identity, no timestamp and no origin symbol, and Save() always writes all fourteen keys (1211-1234), so every Cfg*() switches to the stale file. The four skip conditions at 2025 do mean pass 1 can build from the inputs while pass 2 builds from a month-old file, and CfgSession() feeds both the resume at 1063-1070/1124 and the OnDeinit overwrite at 2130-2141. The balance and prop-rule override is the money-relevant part: CSSRTradingEngine::SetBalance (Trading/SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:562-563) stores it unclamped and every lot size and percent derives from it, which under my lens would be HIGH if reachable. It is not reachable today - pass 2 always returns FailInit at 1911 before line 1948 - so MEDIUM, but it must be fixed together with host-expert-1 rather than after it

#### `host-expert-8` — Replay and extra-timeframe chart windows are leaked on every re-init that is not a user removal

**`MQL5/Experts/SSReplay/SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2203`** · category: object-lifecycle

```mql5
   bool user_removed = (reason == REASON_REMOVE || reason == REASON_PROGRAM ||
                        reason == REASON_CLOSE);
   if(user_removed)
     {
...
      g_charts.CloseOwned();
```

**What goes wrong:** The header of this file says a timeframe change on the host chart 'rebuilds the session from scratch. The replay chart is unaffected'. It is worse than unaffected: OnDeinit closes no chart unless the user removed the EA, and CSSRChartManager::OpenChart always calls ChartOpen with no reuse, so the rebuild opens a fresh set of windows and orphans the old ones. Two-window mode (InpOneChart=false): each period change on the host chart leaks one replay window plus one window per entry in InpExtraTfs. One-window mode: pass 2 opens the InpExtraTfs layout (1300-1305, g_replay_chart != 0 there), and every later REASON_CHARTCHANGE or REASON_PARAMETERS on that chart opens the layout again. A user who changes the period three times with InpExtraTfs="M15,H1" ends with six stale windows nothing will ever close; once the registry passes SSR_MAX_CHARTS the newest ones also lose the follow policy (Chart/SSR_ChartManager.mqh Sync, m_untracked). The same path leaks a window when BuildSession returns false after opening the replay chart, because INIT_FAILED deinits with a reason that is not in the user_removed set.

**Why:** OnDeinit's only close site is inside `if(user_removed)` (2203, plus 2206 for the extra streams); REASON_CHARTCHANGE, REASON_PARAMETERS, REASON_RECOMPILE and REASON_INITFAILED all fall outside it, as the comment at 2115-2122 intends for the symbol but not for the windows. CSSRChartManager::OpenChart (Chart/SSR_ChartManager.mqh:204-215) is `long id = ChartOpen(m_symbol, tf); ... NoteOwned(id)` with no search for an existing chart on that symbol/period, and OpenLayout (229-242) just calls it per timeframe. The new pass's g_charts is a freshly constructed object, so the previous ids are no longer owned; CSSRLeakGuard only counts charts (Chart/SSR_LeakGuard.mqh:44-63), it never closes one.

**Verifiers:** Confirmed by trace. CSSRChartManager::OpenChart (SSR_ChartManager.mqh:204-215) is an unconditional ChartOpen + NoteOwned with no search for an existing chart on that symbol/period, and OpenLayout (229-242) just loops it. The only close site in the whole Chart tree is CloseOwned (line 292; grep for ChartClose finds no other), and OnDeinit calls it only inside `if(user_removed)` at 2202-2206, so REASON_CHARTCHANGE, REASON_PARAMETERS, REASON_RECOMPILE and REASON_INITFAILED all leave the windows open while the replay symbol is deliberately kept. The next pass's manager is a fresh object, so the old ids are no longer in m_owned and Sync (332-377) only discovers and policies them - it never closes. CSSRLeakGuard::Scan (SSR_LeakGuard.mqh:44-63) only counts. So each non-removal re-init opens a new replay window plus one per InpExtraTfs entry and orphans the previous set / Verified. OnDeinit's only close sites are inside `if(user_removed)` at 2201-2211, and user_removed covers REMOVE/PROGRAM/CLOSE only, so REASON_CHARTCHANGE, REASON_PARAMETERS, REASON_RECOMPILE and REASON_INITFAILED all skip CloseOwned. CSSRChartManager::OpenChart (Chart/SSR_ChartManager.mqh:204-215) is an unconditional ChartOpen + NoteOwned with no search for an existing chart on that symbol/period, and OpenLayout (229-242) loops it. Crucially I confirmed the ownership gap the claim rests on: Sync() registers a rediscovered chart with Add(id) only (332-351), never NoteOwned, and CloseOwned closes m_owned exclusively (278-306) - so windows left by a previous instance are tracked but can never be closed by any later pass. Add() also returns -1 past SSR_MAX_CHARTS (82-83), so surplus windows drop out of the follow policy as claimed. The INIT_FAILED leak is sound under MQL5 semantics: OnDeinit runs with REASON_INITFAILED after OnInit returns a non-zero value, which is outside the user_removed set / Confirmed from source on all three legs: OnDeinit's only close sites are inside `if(user_removed)` (2203/2206) and REASON_CHARTCHANGE, REASON_PARAMETERS, REASON_RECOMPILE and REASON_INITFAILED all fall outside it; CSSRChartManager::OpenChart is an unconditional ChartOpen + NoteOwned with no search for an existing chart on that symbol/period (Chart/SSR_ChartManager.mqh:204-215) and OpenLayout just loops it (229-242); CloseOwned walks m_owned (278-306), which a freshly constructed manager on the next pass no longer holds; and the destructor is empty (139). Reachable without any other fix: in two-window mode (InpOneChart=false, a configuration a user can and does pick) simply clicking OK in the inputs dialog raises REASON_PARAMETERS and orphans the previous replay window, and the same happens on every recompile. Kept at MEDIUM rather than raised: the leak is visible - stale windows on a replay symbol the user can close by hand - and costs no money arithmetic or data integrity; kept at MEDIUM rather than lowered because it accumulates silently, the orphans keep showing a frozen replay that looks like the live session, and past SSR_MAX_CHARTS the newest windows also drop out of the follow policy

#### `host-expert-7` — Keys are not withheld while the Sessions or Jump dialog is open: Space, Tab and the arrows drive the replay behind the dialog and the panel reprints over it

**`MQL5/Experts/SSReplay/SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3167`** · category: event-handling

```mql5
   if(g_session_dlg.IsOpen())
     {
      if(g_session_dlg.OnEvent(id, lparam, dparam, sparam))
        { g_panel.Render(); return; }
     }
...
   if(g_panel.OnEvent(id, lparam, dparam, sparam))   // line 3228
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The z-order half is overstated. Render() does not recreate every panel object: each widget primitive creates only when ObjectFind < 0 (Ui/SSR_Widgets.mqh:203-208, 240-245 and the rest of the family), backed by the 512-slot Same()/Keep() cache, and OBJPROP_ZORDER=100 in Common() governs click priority, not paint order. Objects created before the dialog therefore stay behind it across a Render(). What can land on top is only the subset Render() actively removes and later recreates - the per-position rows and the tab/state-dependent controls at Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:659, 900-906, 932-934, 1168-1243, 1318-1339, 1603-1645, 1721, 1835-1852 - which includes the rows for the very trade Tab just opened. Accurate claim: keys are not withheld while the Sessions or Jump dialog is open, so Space starts the replay and Tab opens a virtual trade behind it, and the repaint that follows can draw newly recreated panel rows over the dialog.

**Why:** Ui/SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:227-228 `if(m_mode == SSR_SD_CLOSED || id != CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_CLICK) return false;` - keydowns are never claimed. The same is true of the range dialog block at 3177. The panel acts on any key it owns: Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:2854-2876 maps the key with SSRKeyToCommand, returns false only for the four commands Owns() excludes (SESSIONS/JUMP/REVIEW/REPLAY_FROM_HERE), and otherwise runs `Execute(c); Render(); return true;`. Space is TOGGLE and Tab is SSR_CMD_OPEN_LINES ('TAKE THE TRADE', Ui/SSR_Keys.mqh:165-167). The timer already computes exactly the right predicate one screen away - line 2888 `bool modal = (g_session_dlg.IsOpen() || g_dialog.IsOpen() || g_reveal.IsUp() || g_review.IsUp());` - and uses it to gate PollClicks and Render, but RouteEvent's keydown guard at 3217-3226 lists only the two cards.

**Verifiers:** Verified end to end. CSSRSessionDialog::OnEvent begins `if(m_mode == SSR_SD_CLOSED || id != CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_CLICK) return false;` (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:227-228), so a keydown is never claimed and RouteEvent's dialog block at 3167 falls through; the range-dialog block behaves the same. The keydown guard at 3217-3226 covers only g_review and g_reveal. Control therefore reaches 3228 g_panel.OnEvent, which for CHARTEVENT_KEYDOWN maps the key with SSRKeyToCommand, returns false only for the four commands Owns() excludes (SSR_Panel.mqh:2165-2177: NONE/SESSIONS/JUMP/REVIEW/REPLAY_FROM_HERE) and otherwise runs Execute(c); Render(); return true (2855-2876). Space is TOGGLE and Tab is SSR_CMD_OPEN_LINES ('TAKE THE TRADE', SSR_Keys.mqh:164-166), both owned. Render() recreates the panel's objects, and creation order is the only z-order, so it reprints over the open dialog. The timer already computes the right predicate at 3052 (`modal`) and uses it for PollClicks and Render, but RouteEvent does not / The key-routing half is provable. CSSRSessionDialog::OnEvent returns false unless id==CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_CLICK (Ui/SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:227-228) and CSSRRangeDialog::OnEvent handles only OBJECT_ENDEDIT/OBJECT_CLICK (Ui/SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:258+), so a CHARTEVENT_KEYDOWN passes both blocks at 3160-3195 unclaimed. The keydown guard at 3217-3226 covers only g_review and g_reveal. g_panel.OnEvent then maps the key and, for anything Owns() does not exclude (Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:2165-2177 excludes only NONE/SESSIONS/JUMP/REVIEW/REPLAY_FROM_HERE), runs Execute(c); Render(); return true (2854-2875). Space is TOGGLE and Tab is SSR_CMD_OPEN_LINES (Ui/SSR_Keys.mqh:164-167), so the replay starts and a virtual trade is opened behind a dialog the user is reading, while OnTimer's own `modal` predicate at 3051-3052 proves the right test already exists one screen away / Provable and, unlike most of this batch, reachable in normal use right now. CSSRSessionDialog::OnEvent returns false for anything that is not CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_CLICK (Ui/SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:227-229) and CSSRRangeDialog::OnEvent only claims ENDEDIT/click traffic, so a keydown falls through both blocks in RouteEvent (3167, 3177). The keydown guard at 3217-3226 covers only g_review and g_reveal. CSSRPanel::OnEvent then maps the key and, for anything Owns() does not exclude (Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:2165-2177 excludes only SESSIONS/JUMP/REVIEW/REPLAY_FROM_HERE), runs Execute(c); Render(); return true (2854-2876). Space is TOGGLE and Tab is SSR_CMD_OPEN_LINES (Ui/SSR_Keys.mqh:163-167). The dialogs and the keyboard are both on g_panel_chart, so 'press S, then press Space' is an ordinary sequence. The Render() then recreates panel objects over the dialog, and creation order is the only z-order, which is the v102 defect returning through the key path - the correct predicate already exists one screen away at 2888. Held at MEDIUM rather than raised: the Tab-opens-a-trade case additionally requires the planning lines to be armed (CSSRGroupPort::OpenFromLines refuses with 'no lines on the chart yet' otherwise, Ui/SSR_GroupPort.mqh:715-719), and everything that does get through is a virtual action the user can undo or see in the journal; nothing escapes to a real order

#### `chart-4` — Blind mode's record of the user's original chart settings does not survive a reinit, and the next Apply saves the blinded state as the original

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Chart/SSR_BlindMode.mqh:152`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
      if(Find(chart_id) < 0)
        {
         if(m_saved >= SSR_BLIND_MAX_CHARTS)
            return false;
         int i = m_saved++;
         m_had_dates[i]  = (bool)ChartGetInteger(chart_id, CHART_SHOW_DATE_SCALE);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate. Worth adding that the poisoned-record path is only one of two: if the rebuilt instance never calls BuildSession, m_policy is never set, RestoreAll() is a no-op over an empty registry, and the chart stays blind with no record at all - so the chart is left blinded across a non-user-removal deinit regardless of whether Apply runs again.

**Why:** The save-once guard is keyed on Find(chart_id) within the live instance only; nothing persists m_had_dates/m_had_ohlc/m_had_price (no session-file field, no terminal global - grep for CHART_SHOW_DATE_SCALE matches only this file). The chart properties themselves are terminal state and outlive the program, so after a reinit the 'before' state is unrecoverable. One-chart mode guarantees at least one reinit per session (the handover), and that first one is harmless only because pass 1 has no chart on the replay symbol yet (Expert:1421-1425 with Count()==0); every later reinit while blind is applied poisons the record.

**Verifiers:** Every link in the chain is present in source. CSSRBlindMode::Apply (SSR_BlindMode.mqh:147-176) records m_had_dates/m_had_ohlc/m_had_price only when Find(chart_id) < 0, and Find scans only the live instance's m_id[0..m_saved). The record has no persistence: a repo-wide grep for CHART_SHOW_DATE_SCALE / CHART_SHOW_OHLC / CHART_SHOW_PRICE_SCALE matches only this file and the QA smoke test - no session-file field, no terminal global. OnDeinit (SSReplayStandalone.mq5) computes `user_removed = (reason == REASON_REMOVE || REASON_PROGRAM || REASON_CLOSE)` and calls g_blind.RestoreAll() only inside that branch (line 2176), so REASON_CHARTCHANGE / REASON_PARAMETERS / REASON_RECOMPILE tear the instance down with the chart still blinded. The rebuild then re-runs BuildSession, which calls SetPolicy (1002) and Apply on every synced chart (1421-1430); with m_saved back at 0 the already-hidden properties are recorded as the originals, and Restore (184-186) later writes those falses back. Chart properties are terminal state and outlive the program, so the true 'before' values are unrecoverable. The one runtime dependency is MQL5 reinitialising program globals across a deinit/init cycle, which is standard terminal behaviour rather than broker-specific; on that basis I cannot refute it / The control flow is provable. OnDeinit computes user_removed = (REASON_REMOVE || REASON_PROGRAM || REASON_CLOSE) (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2152-2153) and calls g_blind.RestoreAll() only inside that branch (2176), so REASON_CHARTCHANGE / REASON_PARAMETERS / REASON_RECOMPILE leave the chart blind. Nothing persists m_had_dates/m_had_ohlc/m_had_price: they are plain member arrays (SSR_BlindMode.mqh:117-119), CollectSettings writes no such field, and a repo-wide grep shows CHART_SHOW_DATE_SCALE / CHART_SHOW_OHLC / CHART_SHOW_PRICE_SCALE are touched only in SSR_BlindMode.mqh and the QA smoke script. The global CSSRBlindMode is reconstructed on reload with m_saved = 0 (SSR_BlindMode.mqh:131), so Find(chart_id) < 0 and Apply() records the already-hidden values as the originals (152-164) - the exact failure the boxed comment at 143-145 warns about, defended against only within one instance's lifetime. I also checked the branch the finding does not mention: if the rebuilt pass never reaches BuildSession, SetPolicy is never called, IsOn() is false and RestoreAll() restores nothing, so the chart stays blind anyway. Both branches end with the user's date scale, OHLC and price scale off. Residual runtime dependence is only that a chart's display properties outlive an EA reload, which is standard MT5 chart-level state and is the same assumption the class itself is built on / Mechanism verified. The save-once record is per-instance only: m_id/m_had_* are private members with no persistence anywhere (grep for CHART_SHOW_DATE_SCALE hits only this file), the guard is Find(chart_id) < 0 against the live registry (line 152), and OnDeinit calls g_blind.RestoreAll() only inside `if(user_removed)` (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2152 defines user_removed as REMOVE/PROGRAM/CLOSE; 2176 restores). REASON_CHARTCHANGE / PARAMETERS / RECOMPILE therefore destroy the record while the chart stays hidden, and the rebuilt instance's Apply (via BuildSession:1421-1430 or the housekeeping loop at 2822-2824) records the already-hidden values as the originals, so the eventual Restore writes false back. Only properties the policy actually hides are poisoned, which matches the claim in FULL. Downgraded from MEDIUM on impact: the consequence is that the user's own chart (one-chart mode; tool-opened charts are closed on removal anyway) comes back with date scale, OHLC and price scale off - three checkboxes in the chart properties dialog, self-evident on sight and trivially reversible. No trade data, journal, money arithmetic or session content is lost, and nothing about virtual-trading safety is weakened

#### `chart-3` — The blind-mode reveal is undone within ~200ms: RestoreAll leaves the policy on and the host re-applies on IsOn()

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Chart/SSR_BlindMode.mqh:203`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
      while(m_saved > 0)
         if(Restore(m_id[0]))
            n++;
         else
            break;
      m_applied = false;
```

**What goes wrong:** State: InpBlind=STANDARD or FULL, session reaches SSR_STATE_COMPLETED, user presses the reveal card. Expert:3021 calls g_blind.RestoreAll(), which puts CHART_SHOW_DATE_SCALE / OHLC / PRICE_SCALE back and clears m_applied - but not m_policy. The housekeeping block runs unconditionally every 5th timer tick (Expert:2807-2808, outside the if(playing) guard) and tests IsOn(), not IsApplied(): 'if(g_blind.IsOn()) for(...) g_blind.Apply(g_charts.IdAt(bi));' (Expert:2822-2824). Apply() re-hides everything on the next pass. Net effect: the date scale (and in FULL the price scale and OHLC) come back for at most one 200ms frame and then disappear again, so the payoff of a blind session - looking at the chart you just traded with its dates restored - never happens. The chart only truly comes back when the EA is removed (Expert:2176).

**Why:** IsOn() returns m_policy.AnyOn() (line 136) and nothing in the class's public surface can clear m_policy - there is no Off()/ClearPolicy(); SetPolicy is called once, in BuildSession (Expert:1002). Apply() is idempotent by design (152-165) and re-saves the freshly restored state as the new 'original', so the record stays correct while the visible state flips back to blind.

**Verifiers:** Traced and confirmed, including the control flow the claim depends on. RestoreAll (SSR_BlindMode.mqh:200-210) clears m_applied but never touches m_policy, and the class exposes no Off()/ClearPolicy() - only SetPolicy, called once in BuildSession (Expert:1002). IsOn() returns m_policy.AnyOn() (line 136). In the pump function I verified there is no top-level function boundary and no bare `return` between line 2715 and line 3070, so the housekeeping block and the reveal block are in the same pass: `g_slow_tick++; if(g_slow_tick % 5 == 0){ ... if(g_blind.IsOn()) for(bi...) g_blind.Apply(g_charts.IdAt(bi)); ... }` sits at 2806-2825, outside the `if(playing)` guard at 2784-2789, and tests IsOn() rather than IsApplied(). Nothing in that block consults g_revealed (grep shows g_revealed only at 278, 2998, 3006, 3020). So the reveal at 3021 restores the scales and the next 5th tick re-hides them; with InpPumpMs defaulting to 40 (line 78) that is ~200 ms. Apply also re-saves the freshly restored values, so the record stays correct and final removal does restore properly - the defect is that the reveal payoff is undone, not that the chart is damaged / I verified both halves of the asymmetry the finding rests on. RestoreAll() clears m_applied but never touches m_policy (SSR_BlindMode.mqh:200-210), and the class exposes no way to clear the policy - SetPolicy is the only mutator (134) and is called once, in BuildSession (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1002). IsOn() returns m_policy.AnyOn() (136), not m_applied. The housekeeping block gated on `g_slow_tick % 5 == 0` re-applies on IsOn(), not IsApplied() (2807-2824), and I checked it is outside the `if(playing)` guard (the guard closes at 2790 after g_group.Pump) and that no earlier `return` in OnTimer fires for a COMPLETED session - the early exits are g_picking, g_switching, the g_switch_to handover and !g_ready only. The reveal at 3021 calls RestoreAll() later in the same OnTimer body than the re-apply at 2824, so the restore survives to the end of that tick and is undone on the next pass with %5==0 - about 200ms at InpPumpMs = 40. g_revealed latches (3020), so the card never returns and the chart stays blind until REASON_REMOVE reaches 2176. Apply() does re-save the freshly restored values (152-164), so the record stays honest while the visible state flips back, exactly as claimed. Only the precise interval is runtime-dependent; the undo itself is not / Every link is provable. RestoreAll (lines 200-210) clears m_applied but not m_policy; IsOn() reads m_policy.AnyOn() (136) and no public method clears the policy - SetPolicy is called only in BuildSession (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1002). The housekeeping block at 2807-2824 sits in OnTimer after the `if(playing)` pump with no dialog guard and no early return for a completed session (the only earlier returns are g_picking and !g_ready), and it tests IsOn(), not IsApplied(), then calls g_blind.Apply on every registry chart. The reveal handler at 3006-3027 runs later in the same OnTimer, so the restored state survives at most until the next slow tick (5 x InpPumpMs, ~200ms at the default) and is then re-hidden. The user presses the reveal card, watches the chart come back for one frame, and it goes blind again for the rest of the session - the whole payoff of a blind session, and the review it opens into, is read against a chart with no dates (and in FULL no price scale or OHLC). Reachable in ordinary use of the mode; kept at MEDIUM because it destroys no data - Apply re-saves the freshly restored state, so final removal does restore correctly

#### `chart-5` — Blind FULL claims the price level is hidden while the entry-line label and the panel's deal buttons print the absolute price

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Chart/SSR_BlindMode.mqh:243`** · category: future-data-leakage

```mql5
      if(m_policy.hide_dates)
         s += "the date under the crosshair and in the Data Window; ";
      if(!m_policy.hide_price_scale)
         s += "the price level, which identifies the period on an "
              "instrument you know well; ";
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Leaks() does not assert that the price is hidden; it omits the price clause in FULL mode, which under the class's stated ethic (list every leak it cannot close) understates what remains visible - the absolute price is still shown on the panel's Buy/Sell buttons at all times and permanently on each position's entry-line description once a trade is taken. Category is better described as incomplete-disclosure / blind-mode leak than future-data-leakage: no future data is exposed, only the price level that identifies the period.

**Why:** Leaks() branches on !m_policy.hide_price_scale, so in FULL the price clause is suppressed; no other string mentions it. CSSRTradeLines has no knowledge of blind mode - it takes prices and formats them with m_digits - and the panel's price formatting is not routed through MaskTime/MaskSymbol (GroupPort:180-184 masks only the clock text and the symbol name).

**Verifiers:** All three code sites verified. Leaks() (SSR_BlindMode.mqh:238-250) gates the price clause on `!m_policy.hide_price_scale`, and Full() sets hide_price_scale = true (line 79), so in SSR_BLIND_FULL the returned string mentions only the crosshair date and Market Watch/Navigator - the price is absent from the disclosure. Meanwhile CSSRTradeLines builds `tip = StringFormat("%s %s at %s", side, DoubleToString(volume,2), DoubleToString(entry, m_digits))` and passes it as both tooltip and label to Level(base+"_E", ...), and Level sets OBJPROP_TEXT to that string, with Attach forcing CHART_SHOW_OBJECT_DESCR true (line 183) precisely so it renders without hovering; SSR_Panel.mqh:1478-1487 formats the buy/sell buttons with Price(m_state.ask) / Price(m_state.bid) with no blind-mode routing. CSSRBlindMode's only masking helpers are MaskTime and MaskSymbol, neither of which touches price formatting. The defect is a real incompleteness in a disclosure string the class header explicitly commits to / Leaks() does branch on !m_policy.hide_price_scale (SSR_BlindMode.mqh:245-247), so under SSR_BLIND_FULL - where Full() sets hide_price_scale = true (79) - the price clause is suppressed and no other string in the class mentions price. Meanwhile the absolute price is printed on two channels I verified directly. CSSRTradeLines builds `tip = StringFormat("%s %s at %s", side, volume, DoubleToString(entry, m_digits))` and puts it on the entry line's description and tooltip (SSR_TradeLines.mqh:413-422), with CHART_SHOW_OBJECT_DESCR forced true in Attach (183); the class holds no reference to blind mode. And the panel formats Price(m_state.ask)/Price(m_state.bid) into the Buy and Sell button captions (SSR_Panel.mqh:1478-1486) through a helper that only picks a digit count (266-271). The port hands those through unmasked - CSSRGroupPort's blind block rewrites only clock_text and symbol (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:180-184) while out.bid/out.ask come straight from the account (263-264). One correction: the deal buttons are drawn inside SheetTrade, so they are visible on the Trade tab rather than 'unconditionally' - which does not rescue the finding, since that is the tab a trader uses. Given the class's stated ethic of naming what it cannot hide (233-237), the omission is a real disclosure defect / Both halves verified. Leaks() (238-250) emits the price-level clause only `if(!m_policy.hide_price_scale)`, so FULL - where Full() sets hide_price_scale = true - silently drops it, i.e. the disclosure implies the price is covered. It is not: SSR_Panel.mqh:1479-1484 formats the Buy/Sell buttons as "Buy %s" / "Sell %s" with Price(m_state.ask)/Price(m_state.bid) at full price_digits, unconditionally and with no blind-mode routing, and SSR_TradeLines.mqh:411-421 writes "BUY 0.20 at 4438.48" as the entry line's description with CHART_SHOW_OBJECT_DESCR forced true by Attach (line 183). So a FULL-mode trader reads an exact price off the panel before taking any trade. Two corrections to the framing, which is why I am not upgrading further: this is a de-anonymisation channel, not future-data leakage - the price shown is the current replay instant, never ahead of it - and it is not "the one channel that is always open", since with the price scale hidden the crosshair and Data Window still expose price and the class already concedes the Data Window and Market Watch. Money arithmetic and the virtual-trading guarantee are untouched

#### `chart-2` — Secondary-symbol charts never get Redraw(): the explicit view-snap and ChartRedraw exist only for the primary stream

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Chart/SSR_ChartManager.mqh:574`** · category: multi-symbol

```mql5
if(advanced && m_charts[i].follow && !m_charts[i].user_detached &&
   ViewOffset(m_charts[i].id) > 0)
  {
   ChartNavigate(m_charts[i].id, CHART_END, 0);
   m_charts[i].last_offset = 0;
   m_snaps++;
  }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Secondary-stream managers are Sync()ed but never Redraw()n, so no explicit ChartNavigate/ChartRedraw reaches those charts and their per-instance bar tracking (m_last_bar_time, m_snaps) is never updated. Beyond the missing repaint, the untouched m_last_bar_time means the offset on those charts is never zeroed, so their own DetectScroll will eventually detach them and turn CHART_AUTOSCROLL off - converting a possible drift into a guaranteed frozen view. The visible symptom without that step depends on runtime repaint behaviour.

**Why:** Redraw() is per-instance state (m_last_redraw_us, m_last_bar_time, m_snaps) and per-instance work; nothing inside CSSRChartManager reaches other instances. The primary manager is the only one the host redraws. Secondary charts do receive one ChartNavigate per play press, because Follow() (484) is reached from g_charts2[i].FollowAll() at Expert:2780, so they snap once on each Play and then drift for the rest of the run.

**Verifiers:** The asymmetry is provable by exhaustive grep of g_charts2 in SSReplayStandalone.mq5: Configure (510), OpenChart (511), OpenLayout (513), Sync (1428), IdAt (1430), CloseOwned (2207), FollowAll (2780), Sync (2830). Redraw() never appears, while the primary manager is redrawn at 2825 inside the same slow-tick block, immediately above the g_charts2 Sync loop - and the host's own comment there states Redraw is 'the only thing that moves the chart at bar fidelity, where no tick arrives to do it'. Redraw's state is per-instance (m_last_redraw_us, m_last_bar_time, m_snaps) and the class reaches no other instance, so nothing substitutes. The single ChartNavigate secondary charts do get comes from FollowAll at 2780, which is gated on the !playing -> playing transition, i.e. once per Play press, exactly as claimed. A second consequence the finding did not draw: because Redraw never runs on these managers, m_last_bar_time stays 0, so votes_needed stays at SSR_SCROLL_DETACH_VOTES and - with no snap to zero the offset - a secondary chart whose autoscroll is not honoured will accumulate drift, latch user_detached and have CHART_AUTOSCROLL switched off at line 463, freezing it permanently with no path back except the next Play press / Verified: grep of g_charts2 in SSReplayStandalone.mq5 shows Configure/OpenChart/OpenLayout (510-513), IdAt (1430), CloseOwned (2207), FollowAll (2780) and Sync (2830) - Redraw() appears only as g_charts.Redraw() at 2828, for the primary manager. Redraw's state is per-instance (m_last_redraw_us, m_last_bar_time, m_snaps) and its loop touches only that instance's m_charts, so nothing reaches the secondary charts. Two consequences are source-provable independent of any runtime question: (1) no ChartNavigate(CHART_END) and no ChartRedraw ever runs on a secondary chart except the single Follow() snap on each play transition (line 484 via FollowAll at 2780); (2) because only Redraw sets m_last_bar_time, the secondary managers keep it at 0 forever, so their Snaps() is permanently 0, LastBarTime() permanently 0, and DetectScroll's votes_needed stays at 2 - and with nothing ever zeroing the offset, any drift accumulates monotonically, so two consecutive Sync passes past 3 bars will detach those charts and explicitly switch CHART_AUTOSCROLL off (463), which is the one path that guarantees a frozen view. Whether the freeze appears without that also depends on native autoscroll/repaint on a custom symbol, which cannot be settled from source / The asymmetry is provable: grep of g_charts2 in SSReplayStandalone.mq5 yields only Configure/OpenChart/OpenLayout/IdAt(+Blind Apply)/FollowAll/CloseOwned/Sync - line 2830 Syncs each secondary manager but Redraw() is called on g_charts alone (2828). Redraw holds the only ChartNavigate(CHART_END) and the only ChartRedraw, and its state (m_last_bar_time, m_snaps) is per instance, so secondary charts get neither, and Snaps()/vitals cannot see the problem. Two corrections keep this from being HIGH: SSR_FIDELITY_BAR does emit one tick at each bar close (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:187-210), so native repaint and autoscroll still have a trigger on secondaries - the guaranteed loss is the explicit fallback, not all motion; and this path requires InpAlsoSymbols, a non-default multi-instrument board. No price, fill or PnL arithmetic is touched and no future data is exposed, so MEDIUM

#### `ui-port-session-2` — Saving truncates the previous good session before writing the new one

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Common/SSR_SessionFile.mqh:138`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
      m_handle = FileOpen(path, FILE_WRITE | FILE_TXT | FILE_ANSI | FILE_SHARE_READ);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Confirmed as an atomicity defect (old file destroyed at Create, no temp-then-replace, no verify). Severity lowered to MEDIUM because actual loss additionally requires an interruption or write failure between Create and Close, which source cannot establish; the guaranteed part is only that the previous good save ceases to exist before the new one is complete.

**Why:** FILE_WRITE without FILE_READ resets the file to zero length on open, and CSSRSessionManager::Save passes the final path straight in: `if(!f.Create(Path(name)))` (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:167). There is no staging path anywhere in the class. This is not a rare path: the host writes CfgSession() on EVERY OnDeinit reason including REASON_CHARTCHANGE (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2130-2142), and the dialog's Replace branch overwrites in place (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:242) after telling the user there is no undo. Scenario: a trader has a 40-trade session in "friday"; they change the chart timeframe, the EA is torn down and re-initialised, the autosave opens friday.ssr (old content gone) and the terminal is closed or the write fails part-way - the whole session is lost, and finding ui-port-session-1 means they were told it was saved.

**Verifiers:** SSR_SessionFile.mqh:138 opens the destination with FILE_WRITE and no FILE_READ, which truncates on open - the project's own convention confirms this reading, since every file it means to preserve is opened FILE_READ|FILE_WRITE (SSR_Log.mqh:44, SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:97). Save passes the final path straight in (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:167) and there is no staging path, no rename and no write verification anywhere in the class. The exposure is on a routine path, not an exotic one: the host saves the whole session on every OnDeinit reason including REASON_CHARTCHANGE (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2133-2142), and the dialog's Replace branch overwrites in place (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:242) right after telling the user there is no undo / MQL5 FileOpen with FILE_WRITE and no FILE_READ resets an existing file to zero length, so Create(Path(name)) (l.138, called with the final path from SSR_SessionManager.mqh:167) destroys the previous save before a single entry is written; there is no staging path or rename anywhere in either class. The path is routine, not exotic: the host saves CfgSession() on every OnDeinit reason (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2134-2141, explicitly including chart change), and the dialog's Replace branch (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:242) overwrites in place after promising no undo / The mechanism is provable: FileOpen(path, FILE_WRITE|FILE_TXT|FILE_ANSI|FILE_SHARE_READ) on the final path (SSR_SessionFile.mqh:138, called with Path(name) at SSR_SessionManager.mqh:167) truncates the destination on open, there is no staging path in either class, and - worse than the finding says - not one FileWriteString return is checked anywhere in Set/Section/Comment, while Save() returns an unconditional `true` at l.203. So a write that fails partway (disk full, quota) destroys the previous save and still reports success. The live path is real: SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2130-2142 saves CfgSession() on EVERY OnDeinit reason including REASON_CHARTCHANGE, so an ordinary timeframe change truncates and rewrites the user's session file. One cited path is dead, though: CSSRSessionDialog::RequestSave has no callers anywhere in the tree, so m_mode can never become SSR_SD_CONFIRM_SAVE and the "Replace" branch at l.242 (and the "no undo" warning) is unreachable. Downgraded from HIGH because the exposure window is one short synchronous write, so total loss needs an actual interruption or write failure rather than following from normal use; the consequence when it happens (the whole session, silently, reported as saved) is what keeps it at MEDIUM rather than lower

#### `core-engine-5` — bars_consumed counts a bar once per pump that touches it, not once; the panel 'Bars' figure and the legacy resume check are wrong by 10-1500x

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_ReplayController.mqh:472`** · category: boundary

```mql5
         bars_used++;
         m_cursor.NoteBar(SSRToMsc(m_bars[i].time));

         if(keep > 0)
           {
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct, with two refinements: (1) the legacy bar-count warning at SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1858-1863 only fires for session files written without a fingerprint — SaveInto always emits one when FingerprintUpTo succeeds — so it is a compatibility path, not the normal resume path; (2) SpreadAverage() is not inflated in the same direction as the counters: m_spread_sum_pts and m_bars_recorded inflate together, so the average becomes a pump-count-weighted mean of each bar's spread rather than a plain mean — wrong, but not scaled. The counters themselves (SpreadBarsRecorded/SpreadBarsFixed) and the panel's Bars line are inflated exactly as claimed.

**Why:** Lines 380-383 read bars [floor(lo), floor(hi)] every pump; a 40 ms pump at 1x has floor(lo) == floor(hi) == the current bar, so nb == 1 and the loop at 437 executes once, incrementing bars_used (line 472) regardless of `keep`. Line 531 `m_cursor.Advance(consumed_to, emitted, bars_used)` adds it to bar_count; Publish() copies bar_count into m_state.bars_consumed (line 185). Synthesize() is called at line 440-442 on every iteration and calls SpreadFor (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh line 123), which increments m_bars_recorded/m_bars_fixed.

**Verifiers:** Traced and reproduced by hand. Per pump, lo = emitted+1 and hi = now (SSR_ReplayCursor.mqh:52-58), so at 1x/40ms both bar_lo and bar_hi (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:377-378) floor to the SAME current M1 open; ReadBars returns nb==1, the skip loop at 388-389 leaves first==0 (the bar's time equals bar_lo, not `<`), and the loop at 437 runs once. `bars_used++` at 472 is unconditional — it sits BEFORE the `if(keep > 0)` block at 474 — so it increments even on a pump whose ticks were all already emitted. Line 531 m_cursor.Advance(consumed_to, emitted, bars_used) does `bar_count += bars` (SSR_ReplayCursor.mqh:60-66) and Publish() copies it to m_state.bars_consumed (185). Magnitudes check out: 1500 pumps/replay-minute at 1x/40ms, ~15 at 100x (4000ms per pump). All three consequences verified: the panel prints it raw (SSR_Panel.mqh:1686-1688 `T(SSR_S_BARS), (int)m_state.bars_consumed`, fed by SSR_GroupPort.mqh:141 summing BarsConsumed()); RestoreFrom's no-fingerprint fallback (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1856-1863) compares that inflated live count against a saved one, while the JumpTo path counts bulk bars once (1383 m_cursor.Advance(last_bar-1, 0, n)); and CSSRTickSynthesizer::Synthesize calls SpreadFor once per invocation (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:119, incrementing m_bars_recorded/m_bars_fixed at 56-64), which the host prints as 'of the first %d bars' (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2276-2299). Note the average/widest figures are NOT corrupted (same value re-added), only the counts / Provable from source. EmitWindow re-reads the bar containing `now` on every pump: SSR_ReplayController.mqh:377-383 computes bar_lo/bar_hi as SSRBarOpenMsc(lo)/SSRBarOpenMsc(hi) and calls bp.ReadBars over that inclusive bar range, so at 1x with a 40 ms delta both floor to the same minute and nb == 1. `first` stays 0 because ReadBars only returns bars whose open lies in [bar_lo, bar_hi] (SSR_IDataSource.mqh:128-130), so the while at 407-408 never advances. Inside the loop `bars_used++` at line 472 is unconditional — it sits above the `if(keep > 0)` block at 474, so a pump whose 40 ms window contains none of the 8 synthesised ticks still counts the bar. Line 531 `m_cursor.Advance(consumed_to, emitted, bars_used)` adds it to bar_count (SSR_ReplayCursor.mqh:62-67 `bar_count += bars`), and Publish() copies bar_count into m_state.bars_consumed (line 185). SSR_Panel.mqh:1687 renders m_state.bars_consumed, summed across streams by SSR_GroupPort.mqh:142. The spread half also holds: Synthesize (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:117-124) calls SpreadFor unconditionally, and SpreadFor (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:55-67) increments m_bars_recorded/m_bars_fixed and adds to m_spread_sum_pts, so the host's session summary (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2239-2240, 2298-2299) is inflated by the same factor. The 1x/40 ms figure (25 pumps/s x 60 s = ~1500) and the 100x figure (4000 ms of replay per pump = ~15 pumps per bar) both check out. The legacy resume comparison at 1858-1863 does compare this inflated live count against a JumpTo-rebuilt count, whose bulk part is counted once (line 1390 `m_cursor.Advance(last_bar - 1, 0, n)`) / The mechanism is exactly as described and I can add the root cause. EmitWindow reads bars over [SSRBarOpenMsc(lo), SSRBarOpenMsc(hi)] (lines 380-383), so a 40 ms pump inside one minute reads that minute's bar again. The 'skip bars already consumed' loop at lines 407-409 compares SSRToMsc(m_bars[first].time) < bar_lo — but bar_lo is the lower bound of the read itself, so the first element can never be below it and `first` is always 0; the dedup guard is structurally dead (it should compare against m_cursor.bar_msc). bars_used++ then runs unconditionally at line 472, and line 531 folds it into m_cursor.bar_count, which Publish() copies to m_state.bars_consumed (line 185). CSSRTickSynthesizer::Synthesize calls SpreadFor once per call (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:121), so the spread sample counters inflate identically. Where I part from the finder is severity. Everything downstream is a readout: the Stats-tab 'Bars' line (SSR_Panel.mqh:1687 via SSR_GroupPort.mqh:142), the [spread] log line (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2239-2298, whose recorded/fixed RATIO and the average/widest figures are unaffected — only the counts and the 120-sample gate are), and the pre-fingerprint resume warning (line 1858), which current saves never reach because SaveInto always writes a fingerprint when FingerprintUpTo succeeds. No money arithmetic, no future data, no effect on what is emitted or on SL/TP. A visibly nonsensical diagnostic number is LOW, not MEDIUM

#### `core-engine-6` — Warmup 'bars' are calendar minutes: a Monday-morning start seeds almost no warmup and the HTF chart opens empty

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_ReplayTimeline.mqh:80`** · category: boundary

```mql5
      long want = start_msc - bars * SSR_MSC_PER_MIN;
      if(data_first_msc > 0 && want < data_first_msc)
         want = data_first_msc;
      warmup_first_msc = want;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The arithmetic defect is real, but the illustrative consequence is overstated: 1000 M1 bars is only 16h40m even when every minute exists, so an H4 chart gets ~4 candles and a D1 chart ~1 with or without the bug - the bug costs a factor of roughly 2-4 in warmup depth (e.g. ~240 bars for a Monday 02:00 forex start, ~390 for a 6.5h/day CFD, instead of 1000), which shortens indicator context rather than emptying the HTF chart. It is also not fully silent: SeedWarmup logs 'warmup seeded: N bars' at Info level (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:902-904), so the shortfall is visible to anyone reading the log.

**Why:** Line 80 is pure time arithmetic on start_msc; SeedWarmup (SSR_ReplayController.mqh line 880-885) then reads bars whose open lies in [warmup_first, start-1], so every weekend/holiday minute inside that span yields no bar. No caller converts a bar count to a timestamp through the data (grep: SetWarmupBars is called with InpWarmupBars directly at SSReplayStandalone.mq5 line 498 and 1149).

**Verifiers:** SetWarmupBars is pure time arithmetic - `want = start_msc - bars * SSR_MSC_PER_MIN` (SSR_ReplayTimeline.mqh:80) - and SeedWarmup then reads bars whose open lies in [warmup_first_msc, start_msc-1] (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:880-885), so every non-quoting minute inside that span yields no bar. WarmupBarsFor (94-100) and CSSRHistoryCatalog::WarmupFor (SSR_HistoryCatalog.mqh:126-132) use the same minutes-as-bars arithmetic, so the quoted cost is in minutes too. No caller converts a bar count through the data: the host passes InpWarmupBars straight in (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:498, 1149) while it deliberately counts the REPLAY window in real bars via CopyRates for exactly this weekend reason (1078-1085), and floor_msc at 1090 repeats the minute arithmetic. A Monday 02:00 start with InpWarmupBars=1000 therefore seeds only the few hours of bars that exist after the weekend, which is the empty-HTF-chart failure the file header names / The conflation is unambiguous in source: SetWarmupBars is documented 'expressed in M1 bars' and implements start_msc - bars * SSR_MSC_PER_MIN (SSR_ReplayTimeline.mqh:72-84), pure calendar arithmetic; SeedWarmup then reads only bars whose open lies in [warmup_first, start-1] (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:878-885), so every closed minute in that span yields nothing. WarmupBarsFor (94-100) and CSSRHistoryCatalog::WarmupFor (SSR_HistoryCatalog.mqh:126-132) share the same minutes-as-bars reading, and the host passes InpWarmupBars straight through (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:498, 1149) while also using the same minute arithmetic for its floor (line 1090) - even though the very next block counts the REPLAY window in real bars via CopyRates (1078-1085) precisely because minutes lie across gaps. So the requested warmup is under-delivered by the quote-density factor on every Monday start and on every non-24h instrument (e.g. a 6.5h/day CFD gets ~390 bars for 1000 asked) / Line 80 is pure time arithmetic (want = start_msc - bars * SSR_MSC_PER_MIN, SSR_ReplayTimeline.mqh:73-81) and SeedWarmup reads whatever bars exist in [warmup_first, start-1] (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:853-900), so on any instrument that does not quote every minute the seeded warmup is shorter than requested - always ~half on a 6.5h/day CFD, up to ~85% short on a Monday-morning forex start. Confirmed. But the severity is over-stated: the shortfall is reported, not silent (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:900-903 logs 'warmup seeded: %d bars up to %s'), the default InpWarmupBars=1000 is only ~4 H4 candles and 0 D1 candles even when every minute exists, so the 'empty HTF chart' outcome is mostly a consequence of the requested depth rather than of the calendar arithmetic, and no price, fill or guard is affected - only how much context an indicator has at bar one

#### `core-sync-2` — Spread statistics count synthesis calls, not bars: the bar containing the clock is re-synthesised every pump so BarsWithRecordedSpread/BarsWithFixedSpread inflate by ~pumps-per-bar

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:54`** · category: spread

```mql5
   double            SpreadFor(const MqlRates &bar)
     {
      if(m_spread_mode == SSR_SPREAD_RECORDED && bar.spread > 0)
        {
         double pts = (double)bar.spread;
         m_bars_recorded++;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct except that WidestRecordedSpread is a running maximum and so is NOT distorted by the repeated counting; the distortion is in BarsWithRecordedSpread/BarsWithFixedSpread (the 'N of the first M bars' wording) and in AverageRecordedSpread, which becomes a pump-weighted rather than bar-weighted mean.

**Why:** Controller EmitWindow: bar_lo = SSRBarOpenMsc(lo) with lo = emitted+1 (380); ReadBars(bar_lo, bar_hi) returns the bar whose open == bar_lo (383); the skip loop 'while(first < nb && SSRToMsc(m_bars[first].time) < bar_lo)' (408) does not skip it; Synthesize/SynthesizeClose is called for it (441-442) and its ticks are then trimmed to (lo, hi] (461-470). Since the cursor advances to hi each pump (531) and the next pump's lo is hi+1, the same bar is read and synthesised on every pump until the clock leaves the minute. SpreadFor is unconditional inside Synthesize (123) and SynthesizeClose (197). The only test of these counters (QA smoke 2985-3001) calls Synthesize directly three times and so cannot see the inflation. RECOMMENDATION: count only when the bar's open time differs from the last counted open, or count in the controller when a bar is fully consumed.

**Verifiers:** SpreadFor is unconditional inside Synthesize (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:123) and SynthesizeClose (197), and counts a call, not a bar. EmitWindow re-reads and re-synthesises the clock's own bar on every pump: bar_lo=SSRBarOpenMsc(lo) with lo=emitted+1 (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:380), ReadBars returns that bar (383), the skip loop `< bar_lo` does not exclude it (408), Synthesize/SynthesizeClose runs (440-442), ticks are trimmed to (lo,hi] afterwards (461-470) and the cursor advances to hi (523-531) - so at 1x with InpPumpMs=40 the same minute is synthesised ~1500 times. Nothing resets the counters in the host (ResetSpreadCounters has only a QA-smoke caller), so ReportSpreadOnce trips its `total < 120` gate (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2260) within ~5 s of the first bar and prints 'from the data on N of the first M bars' with N/M counting synthesis calls, and SpreadAverage (sum/recorded) is pump-weighted / Confirmed from source. SpreadFor increments m_bars_recorded or m_bars_fixed unconditionally (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:54-67) and is called once per Synthesize (123) / SynthesizeClose (197). In EmitWindow the window is (emitted+1, now], so while the clock stays inside one minute bar_lo == bar_hi == that bar's open, ReadBars returns the same bar, the 'already consumed' skip loop cannot drop it (SSRToMsc(bar.time) == bar_lo, not < bar_lo, SSR_ReplayController.mqh:380-410), and Synthesize is called again on it (441-442) even on pumps whose ticks are all trimmed away by the (lo,hi] filter (461-470). At 40 ms pumps and 1x that is ~1500 counts for one bar. Consequence in the host is exactly as described: ReportSpreadOnce fires at total >= 120 (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2260) after a few seconds inside the first bar or two, and then prints 'N of the first M bars' with N/M in the hundreds; the counters are also exposed as SpreadBarsRecorded/Fixed/Average/Widest on the controller. No behavioural impact beyond the diagnostic, but the diagnostic is the one the author instructs users to send / The inflation is real. After a pump the cursor sits at hi, the next pump's lo=hi+1 is still inside the same minute, bar_lo=SSRBarOpenMsc(lo) is that bar's open, the skip loop at SSR_ReplayController.mqh:408 cannot drop it (its time == bar_lo), and Synthesize/SynthesizeClose call SpreadFor unconditionally (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:123, 197), so the counters advance once per pump rather than once per bar. Consequently ReportSpreadOnce's 120-sample threshold (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2259) is met inside the FIRST M1 bar and the printed 'N of the first M bars' are pump counts. Impact is a wrong diagnostic number only: the spread actually applied to each tick is still that bar's own, so no money arithmetic changes, and the qualitative verdict the line exists to give (does this history carry a spread at all) survives unless the very first bars differ from the rest - MqlRates.spread is normally populated uniformly or not at all. Downgraded to LOW: a misleading support line, not a defect the trader's account can feel

#### `data-4` — warmup_bars is used both as a count of M1 bars and as a span of wall-clock minutes; on any instrument that does not quote every minute this produces false "not enough history" refusals and silently short warmup context

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Data/SSR_HistoryCatalog.mqh:150`** · category: boundary

```mql5
      q.available_bars  = m_range.bar_count;
      q.exceeds_history = (m_range.available && q.total_bars > m_range.bar_count);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: warmup_bars is used as both a bar count and a span of calendar minutes, which produces a false 'not enough history' refusal in the range dialog for deep-timeframe context on instruments that do not quote 24h, plus an over-conservative earliest-start floor. It does not shorten the actual seeded warmup in the shipped host, which takes InpWarmupBars through SSR_ReplayTimeline instead, and the default H1/300 request never trips the refusal.

**Why:** WarmupFor (lines 126-132) returns `visible * (secs/60)`, i.e. the number of MINUTES the requested number of tf-candles spans on a 24 h clock. Quote then compares that number against m_range.bar_count, which CSSRMt5HistoryProvider::Discover fills from SERIES_BARS_COUNT (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:99, 131) — a count of bars that actually exist, not of minutes elapsed. EarliestStart/LatestStart (lines 161-175) multiply the same number by SSR_MSC_PER_MIN, treating it as elapsed time. The two readings are only equal when the instrument quotes every minute of every day; for anything else they diverge by the quote-density factor, and the code has no notion of that factor anywhere. SSR_T6_History.mq5:39-47 asserts only the arithmetic of WarmupFor itself (288000 for D1/200), never that the number is usable as either a bar count or a time span, so the conflation is untested in both directions.

**Verifiers:** The same number is used with two incompatible units, provably. WarmupFor returns visible*(secs/60), i.e. MINUTES of a 24h clock (SSR_HistoryCatalog.mqh:126-132). Quote compares warmup+replay_minutes against m_range.bar_count (150), which CSSRMt5HistoryProvider::Discover fills from SERIES_BARS_COUNT - a count of bars that exist, not minutes elapsed - so on any instrument that does not quote every minute exceeds_history fires spuriously and SSRValidateRange returns 'not enough history: needs N M1 bars, broker has M' (SSR_SessionRange.mqh:89-92), which CSSRRangeDialog surfaces as m_problem (SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:135). The same validator also refuses starts below EarliestStart(warm), and EarliestStart/LatestStart multiply that identical number by SSR_MSC_PER_MIN, i.e. treat it as elapsed time (161-175) - so the floor is first_msc + exactly `visible` calendar days for D1, which on a 5-day week yields ~143 daily candles rather than the 200 requested, with nothing reporting the shortfall. SSR_T6_History.mq5:39-47 only asserts WarmupFor's own arithmetic, so neither reading is tested / Both readings of the same number are in the source and they are incompatible. WarmupFor returns visible * (PeriodSeconds(tf)/60), i.e. a count of MINUTES spanned on a 24h clock (SSR_HistoryCatalog.mqh:126-132). Quote compares warmup+replay against m_range.bar_count (line 149-150), which CSSRMt5HistoryProvider::Discover fills from SeriesInfoInteger(...,SERIES_BARS_COUNT) (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:99,131) - a count of bars that EXIST, standard MQL5 semantics, not elapsed minutes. EarliestStart/LatestStart multiply the same number by SSR_MSC_PER_MIN and add it to first_msc (161-175), treating it as elapsed time. Downstream both wrong outcomes are real: SSRValidateRange returns 'not enough history: needs N M1 bars, broker has M' on q.exceeds_history and also refuses via the start_msc < EarliestStart test (SSR_SessionRange.mqh:78-91), and CSSRRangeDialog::CanStart is false whenever the quote is not feasible or the problem string is not a warning (SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:135-143). The divergence factor is the instrument's quote density, of which the code has no notion anywhere. Note it bites at the defaults too (max_tf=H1, visible_bars=300 -> 18,000 minutes, i.e. ~65 H1 candles of real context on a 6.5h/day instrument) / The conflation is provable: WarmupFor returns visible*(secs/60), i.e. minutes (SSR_HistoryCatalog.mqh:126-132); Quote compares that number to m_range.bar_count, which Discover fills from SERIES_BARS_COUNT (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh SeriesInfoInteger SERIES_BARS_COUNT), while EarliestStart/LatestStart multiply the same number by SSR_MSC_PER_MIN (:161-175). So on a session-limited instrument a D1/200-300 context request trips exceeds_history and CSSRRangeDialog's CanStart refuses it (SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:134-141, SSR_SessionRange.mqh:88-92), and the earliest-start floor is over-conservative. Severity downgraded from MEDIUM for two reasons I checked in the host: the number never reaches the actual seed - the shipped EA sets warmup solely from InpWarmupBars (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:498, 1149) through SSR_ReplayTimeline, and a confirmed dialog range only performs a JumpTo inside the already-loaded session (:3183-3190) - and the default H1/300 = 18,000 minutes trips exceeds_history on no realistic history depth. The blocked configuration is a deliberate non-default choice with an obvious workaround (fewer visible candles, or H4/H1)

#### `mt5-symbol-3` — Create()'s leftover-adopt fallback uses the SYMBOL_DIGITS>0 existence test that Adopt() documents as wrong for whole-point instruments

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Mt5/SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:338`** · category: broker-symbol

```mql5
ResetLastError();
bool exists = (SymbolInfoInteger(m_symbol, SYMBOL_DIGITS) > 0 &&
               GetLastError() == 0);

if(!exists || !SSRIsReplaySymbol(m_symbol))
  {
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct on the defect, wrong on the coverage claim: T3.7 (SSR_T3_CustomSymbol.mq5:415-436) does not exercise this fallback on any instrument. Create() calls Destroy() first (line 318), and with no chart open the CustomSymbolDelete normally succeeds, so the second Create takes the ordinary create path and never reaches line 338. The fallback is uncovered everywhere, not just on digits>0 symbols.

**Why:** The Adopt() comment explicitly records that '<= 0 digits' was misread as 'no such symbol' and replaced it with SYMBOL_EXIST plus SYMBOL_CUSTOM; the same test survived unchanged in Create's fallback 100 lines earlier. Note also the fallback does not check SYMBOL_CUSTOM, so it is both stricter (digits) and looser (ownership) than Adopt.

**Verifiers:** Confirmed by reading both paths in one file. Create()'s post-failure fallback (SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:337-347) tests existence as SymbolInfoInteger(m_symbol, SYMBOL_DIGITS) > 0 && GetLastError()==0, which evaluates false for an existing whole-point instrument clone (digits==0) regardless of the error code, so the branch takes the hard Fail(SSR_ERR_INTERNAL, 'CustomSymbolCreate(...) failed') instead of adopting the leftover the comment at 332-336 says it exists to adopt. Adopt() 100 lines later documents this exact test as wrong and replaced it with SYMBOL_EXIST + SYMBOL_CUSTOM (comment 433-450, checks 452-464) - the author's own record that digits==0 instruments are real and that this misread killed the one-window handover on US30. The secondary observation is also correct: the fallback checks SSRIsReplaySymbol (a name pattern) and never SYMBOL_CUSTOM, so it is simultaneously stricter on digits and looser on ownership than Adopt.
