T3.7 exists (SSR_T3_CustomSymbol.mq5:415-441) and exercises this fallback, so it would only cover the intended behaviour on digits>0 instruments. Reaching the failure needs Destroy() to have left the symbol behind, which is runtime, but the logic error and its consequence are provable here / The predicate at SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:338-339, `exists = (SymbolInfoInteger(m_symbol, SYMBOL_DIGITS) > 0 && GetLastError() == 0)`, is false for a symbol that exists with 0 digits: the first conjunct fails before the error check is even relevant. This is precisely the misreading the file documents as a bug 100 lines later (the Adopt() banner at 434-450: '<= 0 digits' treated as 'no such symbol', which 'on US30 rejected a symbol that was sitting right there'), and Adopt was fixed to SYMBOL_EXIST + SYMBOL_CUSTOM (453-464) while Create's fallback was not. So in Create the leftover-adopt recovery path is dead on any whole-point instrument: Destroy() fails to remove the leftover, CustomSymbolCreate refuses the existing name, exists evaluates false, and Fail(SSR_ERR_INTERNAL, 'CustomSymbolCreate(...) failed') propagates to the sink as SINK_FAILED (SSR_CustomSymbolSink.mqh:191-195). The finder's secondary point also holds: the fallback substitutes SSRIsReplaySymbol - a bare StringFind of the suffix (SSR_SymbolNaming.mqh:74-77) - for the SYMBOL_CUSTOM ownership test Adopt uses. Whether a given broker's index actually carries 0 digits, and whether Destroy fails, are runtime conditions, but the predicate is wrong from source regardless / The inconsistency is real and provable: Create's post-failure existence probe is (SymbolInfoInteger(m_symbol,SYMBOL_DIGITS) > 0 && GetLastError()==0) at SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:337-339, i.e. the exact test Adopt's own comment (lines 434-450) records as wrong for whole-point instruments, while Adopt now uses SYMBOL_EXIST plus SYMBOL_CUSTOM (lines 452-465). On a 0-digit origin, an existing leftover reads exists=false and Create dies with SSR_ERR_INTERNAL instead of adopting. Severity is lower than claimed on reachability grounds: the fallback fires only when Destroy() failed to delete the leftover (line 318 -> 556), and the common "a chart is open on it" case is already routed around Create entirely - the sink uses Adopt when m_adopt_existing is set (SSR_CustomSymbolSink.mqh:175-187), and Destroy's own CloseCharts+50ms pause normally clears the rest. The user-visible effect when it does fire is a refused session start with a printed reason, not silent wrong data. The finder's T3.7 remark is also inaccurate: T3.7 (SSR_T3_CustomSymbol.mq5:415-436) never reaches the fallback on any instrument, because its second Create deletes the leftover successfully first

#### `strategy-integration-report-8` — The class report declares UTF-8 and is written with FILE_ANSI, so non-ASCII student, symbol and session names come out as mojibake

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Report/SSR_ClassReport.mqh:439`** · category: utf8

```mql5
      int h = FileOpen(out_path, FILE_WRITE | FILE_TXT | FILE_ANSI | FILE_SHARE_READ);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate for the student names, which come from FileFindFirst as genuine Unicode and are lossily transcoded on write. The symbol/session fields are a slightly different mechanism worth noting: SSR_Journal.mqh:173/254 also writes the CSV with FILE_ANSI and ReadOne reopens it with FILE_ANSI (line 150), so those bytes round-trip through the same codepage and land in the HTML as codepage bytes rather than as '?' - still mojibake under the utf-8 declaration, but the loss happened at journal-export time, not in the report writer.

**Why:** This is the handle every FileWriteString in Write() uses, and SSR_ReportStyle.mqh:21 writes the charset declaration into it: "<!doctype html><html><head><meta charset=\"utf-8\">". FILE_ANSI makes MQL5 convert each Unicode string to the system ANSI codepage on write - one byte per character - which is the same trap the project already documents for the READ direction (overview lesson 5) and already solved for the language file with FILE_BIN + CharArrayToString(..., CP_UTF8) at SSR_Strings.mqh:686. grep shows CP_UTF8 appears nowhere in the Report layer, so no path writes UTF-8. The names reaching the page are Html(m_s[i].name) at lines 561, 618 and 650, and Html(sym)/Html(sess) at line 521.

**Verifiers:** The encoding contradiction is provable from source: SSR_ReportStyle.mqh:21 writes <meta charset="utf-8"> into the same handle that SSR_ClassReport.mqh:439 opened with FILE_TXT|FILE_ANSI, which makes MQL5 transcode every FileWriteString from UTF-16 to the system ANSI codepage. grep confirms CP_UTF8 appears nowhere in the Report layer (only SSR_Strings.mqh:686, and only on the READ side), so no path writes UTF-8 bytes. Non-ASCII student names reaching the page at 561/618/650 come from FileFindFirst (real Unicode from the OS), so they are the clearest case: cp-1252/1256 bytes or '?' declared as UTF-8. Only pure-ASCII names escape, which is why I am not calling it worse than MEDIUM / Provable from source plus documented MQL5 file semantics. Line 439 opens the page FILE_WRITE|FILE_TXT|FILE_ANSI, and FILE_ANSI makes every FileWriteString on that handle convert the Unicode string down to the system ANSI codepage, one byte per character. SSR_ReportStyle.mqh:21 writes <meta charset="utf-8"> into that same handle, so the declared encoding and the emitted bytes disagree for any character outside ASCII. The non-ASCII fields are exactly the identifying ones: Html(m_s[i].name) at 561/618/650 (the name comes from FileFindFirst, so the coach's folder naming decides it) and Html(sym)/Html(sess) at 521-522. grep confirms CP_UTF8 appears nowhere under Report/ (only Ui/SSR_Strings.mqh:686), so no path writes UTF-8 bytes / Provable from source: SSRWriteReportHead writes `<meta charset=\"utf-8\">` (SSR_ReportStyle.mqh:21) into a handle opened FILE_WRITE|FILE_TXT|FILE_ANSI (line 439), and that handle is the one every FileWriteString in Write() uses. FILE_ANSI makes MQL5 convert each Unicode string to the system ANSI codepage on write, so for any non-ASCII character the bytes on disk cannot be the UTF-8 the document declares - the mismatch is a source-level fact, only the exact glyph the browser shows depends on the terminal codepage. grep confirms CP_UTF8 appears nowhere in the Report layer while SSR_Strings.mqh:686 already uses FILE_BIN + CP_UTF8 for the language file, so the correct pattern exists in-tree. Reachability is real for the stated audience: the student name is the CSV file name taken straight from FileFindFirst as a Unicode string (311-315) and written through Html(m_s[i].name) at 561/618/650, and the project ships MQL5/Files/SSReplay/lang/fa.txt. Severity stays MEDIUM rather than higher: no arithmetic is touched, every number is correct, and an all-ASCII class (the common case for many coaches) is unaffected - but where it does bite, the one field the document exists to put beside each row becomes unreadable, which is not cosmetic

#### `strategy-integration-report-9` — The class KPI row, the ranking sort and the bar scale all include students who ran a different session, which is the comparison the file says it refuses to make

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Report/SSR_ClassReport.mqh:493`** · category: drawdown-calc

```mql5
      for(int i = 0; i < m_count; i++)
        {
         if(!m_s[i].parsed)
            continue;
         total_trades += m_s[i].trades;
         if(!have || m_s[i].net_profit > best)  best  = m_s[i].net_profit;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate. The one framing correction: the category is not a drawdown calculation - it is an aggregate-scope defect (the key filter is applied to labels but not to any of the four aggregates, the bar scale, or the sort key).

**Why:** The filter in this loop is `!m_s[i].parsed`, never a key test, so best, worst and total_trades span every readable file. MedianNet() (lines 373-396) filters on parsed only. peak (lines 597-600) filters on parsed only. Rank() (line 350) returns net_profit with no key term, and the insertion sort at lines 337-344 orders on Rank alone. m_key and m_key_agree are computed at lines 324-334 and are then used only for the caveat (475-481), the odd_row label in the strip (558-564), the "(different session)" span in the table (619) and Axis() (406-412) - never to exclude a row from an aggregate.

**Verifiers:** I looked for a key filter on every aggregate and there is none. The KPI loop (493-501) filters on !parsed only, so best/worst/total_trades span every readable file. MedianNet (378-380) filters on parsed only. peak (598-600) filters on parsed only, so an outsider's magnitude rescales every diverging bar. Rank (350-351) is `parsed ? net_profit : -1e18` with no key term, and the insertion sort (337-344) orders on Rank alone, so a different-session file can sit at the top of the league table. m_key/m_key_agree are used at exactly four places - the caveat (475-481), odd_row (558), the "(different session)" span (619) and Axis (410-412) - none of which excludes a row from a number. The file header's stated refusal (19-23) is therefore implemented only as a label. State is plainly reachable: one stray CSV in the folder / Every aggregate filters on parsed only, never on the key: the KPI loop at 493-501 (best, worst, total_trades), MedianNet() at 378-380, the bar scale `peak` at 598-600, and Rank() at 350 which returns net_profit with no key term and drives the insertion sort at 337-344. m_key/m_key_agree are read only at 456 (symbol/session caption), 475-481 (caveat), 558 (strip label), 606-619 (table label) and 406-412 (Axis). So an out-of-session file sets Best, shifts Median and Trades, compresses every other bar, and can sort to the top of the league table with only an inline span distinguishing it - directly against the file header's stated refusal (lines 19-23). No MQL5 semantics are involved; this is pure control flow / Every cited filter checks parsed only, verified line by line: the KPI loop at 493-501 (best, worst, total_trades), MedianNet at 378-380, peak at 598-600, and Rank at 350-351 which returns net_profit with no key term while the insertion sort at 337-344 orders on Rank alone. m_key/m_key_agree are used only for the caveat (475-481), the strip label (558-564), the table span (619) and Axis (406-412). So the odd file does shift Best/Worst/Median/Trades between them, does rescale every diverging bar via peak, and does sort to the top of the league table. The state is not hypothetical - the file's own comments at 94-96 and 321-323 name it as the case the design anticipates ("a coach who accidentally drops in one file from last week"). What the coach observes is a headline "Best +5000.00" that belongs to a different set of candles and twenty comparable students visually shrunk against it, which is the specific harm the header at 19-23 says the file refuses to produce. Held at MEDIUM rather than upgraded because the mitigations do fire: the caveat is printed above the table and the outsider's row carries "(different session)", so a coach who reads is warned - the numbers are contaminated, not silently so

#### `ui-port-session-8` — Resume warnings are newline-joined and end up in a single 63-character label

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Session/SSR_SessionManager.mqh:89`** · category: string-length

```mql5
      m_warnings += (m_warnings == "" ? "" : "\n") + text;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: On the dialog's Load path the warning block is rendered as one OBJ_LABEL, so only ~40 characters of the first warning are drawn and every warning after the first \n is unreachable; the user is still alerted (warning colour, "BUT:" prefix) and the leading 40 chars of each DiffText variant carry its gist, but there is no log line or other surface on this path where the full text can be read.

**Why:** CSSRGroupPort::LoadSession copies the whole warning block into m_session_error on SUCCESS (`if(m_sessions.HadWarnings()) m_session_error = m_sessions.Warnings();`, SSR_GroupPort.mqh:969-970). The dialog builds `m_message = "resumed \"" + name + "\"" + (warn == "" ? "" : "  BUT: " + warn);` (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:280-281) and draws it with `m_w.Label("msg", x + SSR_PAD, cy, m_message, ...)` (l.214) - one OBJ_LABEL, 63 characters, no newline support. The warnings it carries are full sentences: was.DiffText(now_fp) for revised history (ReplayController.mqh:1851), the bar-count mismatch (l.1860-1863), the stream-count mismatch (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:320-322), the skew message (l.361-363) and the balance-replay mismatch (TradingEngine.mqh:1254-1257). After the 23-character prefix roughly 40 characters of the first warning survive; anything after the first "\n" is unreachable. On this path nothing tells the user the complete text is in the Experts log (only the host's startup path Prints it, Standalone.mq5:1486).

**Verifiers:** Traced end to end and every link holds. SSR_SessionManager.mqh:89 joins warnings with "\n"; Restore can raise several (stream-count l.320-322, per-stream fingerprint/bar-count from ReplayController.mqh:1836-1856, skew l.361-363, balance/orphan-leg from TradingEngine.mqh:1252-1262). CSSRGroupPort::LoadSession copies the whole block into m_session_error (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:969-970) and returns true, and SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:280-281 concatenates it into m_message which is drawn by one m_w.Label("msg", ...) at l.214. CSSRWidgets has no wrapping, ellipsis or length handling (grep for StringLen/63 in SSR_Widgets.mqh finds only name/width helpers), and OBJ_LABEL renders no newline, so text after the first \n is unreachable and the first warning is cut around 40 characters after the ~23-character prefix. On this path nothing Prints: the only Print of ResumeReport() is the host's startup resume (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1483-1487), reached only when InpResume+CfgSession are set at OnInit, not from the dialog's load button / Verified end to end. Warn() joins with "\n" (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:89); CSSRGroupPort::LoadSession copies the whole block into m_session_error (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:969-970); the dialog renders it as one OBJ_LABEL via CSSRWidgets::Label (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:214 -> SSR_Widgets.mqh:249, single ObjectSetString of OBJPROP_TEXT, no fitting/wrapping helper exists anywhere in SSR_Widgets.mqh). With the 63-char draw limit and the 23-char 'resumed "name"  BUT: ' prefix, ~40 chars remain, and every warning text is longer than that: SSRFingerprint::DiffText returns full sentences (SSR_Fingerprint.mqh:66-76), as do the stream-count (l.320-322), skew (l.361-363) and balance-replay warnings (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:1254-1257). I confirmed the mid-session path has no logging escape: the only Print of ResumeReport()/Warnings() is the host startup path (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1486); GroupPort::LoadSession prints nothing / Provable. Warn() joins with \n (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:89); CSSRGroupPort::LoadSession copies the whole block into m_session_error (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:967-970); the dialog concatenates it into m_message and draws it with one m_w.Label("msg", ...) (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:214, 276-281). I grepped SSR_Widgets.mqh - there is no clip/ellipsis helper anywhere, so the string goes to OBJPROP_TEXT whole and MT draws 63 chars. Neither the dialog nor CSSRGroupPort::LoadSession Prints anything, so on this path the full text exists nowhere the user can reach. Downgraded from MEDIUM on impact: the prefix is 23 chars, so ~40 chars of the FIRST warning survive, and I checked what those 40 chars actually are - every SSRFingerprint::DiffText variant (SSR_Fingerprint.mqh:61-77) front-loads its meaning ("1234 bars then, 1230 now (-4) - the brok", "same bar count and range, but the prices"), and the message is drawn in SSR_C_HOLD because StringFind(m_message,"BUT")>=0. So the user is alerted and gets the gist of warning #1; what is genuinely unreachable is any warning after the first \n. No data is harmed - the load already completed either way

#### `spikes-audits-3` — No spike forces SYMBOL_CHART_MODE_BID or sets TICK_FLAG_LAST, so B1 reports the transport broken on an index or futures origin

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:314`** · category: broker-symbol

```mql5
CustomSymbolSetInteger(sym, SYMBOL_SPREAD_FLOAT, true);
CustomSymbolSetInteger(sym, SYMBOL_TRADE_MODE, SYMBOL_TRADE_MODE_DISABLED);
...
CustomSymbolSetInteger(sym, SYMBOL_START_TIME, 0);
CustomSymbolSetInteger(sym, SYMBOL_EXPIRATION_TIME, 0);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct; note D2 does not build ticks inline (grep shows only B2, B3, C4, D3 plus the kit), so the inline-flags list in the evidence is slightly over-broad. Impact is bounded to spike measurement validity, not to the shipping engine.

**Why:** grep -rn SYMBOL_CHART_MODE over MQL5/ returns no hit in any file under a Spike directory; the only writers are Mt5/SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:383 (the product, which forces BID) and QA/SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:473 (which asserts it). Core/SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:157-175 documents the measured failure in exactly this shape - '60 calls offered ticks, the terminal took 481, refused 0, and the M1 series stayed at 139 bars' - and fixes it with both TICK_FLAG_LAST and forced BID mode, calling that 'the belt to that braces'. The spike kit has neither. B3, C4, D2 and D3 build their ticks inline with the same BID|ASK-only flags.

**Verifiers:** Verified by grep and by reading both sides. SSR_MakeSymbol (SpikeKit:301-332) sets SPREAD_FLOAT, TRADE_MODE, START_TIME, EXPIRATION_TIME and the 24/7 sessions - never SYMBOL_CHART_MODE - and grep -rn SYMBOL_CHART_MODE over MQL5/ returns hits only in Mt5/SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:383 (product, forces BID) and QA/SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:473-481 (asserts it); nothing under any Spike directory. SSR_BarToTicks sets flags = TICK_FLAG_BID|TICK_FLAG_ASK at SpikeKit:592, and B3:69, C4:68, D3:105, B2:127 repeat the same flag pair inline. B1 clones from SSR_Origin(InpOrigin="") = the host chart's symbol (SpikeKit:275-298, B1:35), so an index/futures CFD origin yields a CHART_MODE_LAST clone; B1's forming_bar_took_close and wick_expanded_to_extremes verdicts (B1:167-173) then read a series that never builds. The measured shape is documented in SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:154-174, which fixes it with both TICK_FLAG_LAST and forced BID. Severity reduced from HIGH: the failure is confined to Phase-0 spike measurement validity - the product path already carries both fixes / Verified by grep and by reading both sides. `grep -rn SYMBOL_CHART_MODE MQL5/` returns hits only in Mt5/SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh (363-395, the product forcing BID and verifying it took) and QA/SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5 (473-481, asserting it) - nothing under any Spike directory, and SSR_MakeSymbol (SpikeKit:303-335) overrides only SPREAD_FLOAT, TRADE_MODE, START_TIME and EXPIRATION_TIME after CustomSymbolCreate clones the origin. `grep -rn TICK_FLAG_LAST MQL5/` likewise shows the flag only in the product synthesizer (TickSynthesizer:174, 203) and the smoke test; SSR_BarToTicks sets flags = TICK_FLAG_BID | TICK_FLAG_ASK (SpikeKit:592) and merely fills .last without the flag. B1 defaults InpOrigin="" so the origin is whatever chart it is dropped on, and B1's pass criteria are iClose/iHigh/iLow polling with a 250 ms ceiling (B1:112-141) feeding the verdicts forming_bar_took_close and wick_expanded_to_extremes (B1:167-172) - both of which read 0 of 20 if the injected ticks build no bar. The runtime premise (a CHART_MODE_LAST symbol builds bars only from ticks flagged TICK_FLAG_LAST) is the project's own measured finding, documented verbatim at TickSynthesizer:155-175 ('60 calls offered ticks, the terminal took 481, refused 0, and the M1 series stayed at 139 bars'), and matches the established symbol-API semantics. Severity downgraded HIGH -> MEDIUM: the product already carries both the forced BID and the LAST flag, so the defect is confined to the Phase-0 spike harness producing a false BLOCKER verdict if re-run against an index/futures origin / The omission is provable: SSR_MakeSymbol (SpikeKit:301-331) clones the origin and overrides SPREAD_FLOAT, TRADE_MODE, START_TIME and EXPIRATION_TIME but never SYMBOL_CHART_MODE, SSR_BarToTicks sets flags = TICK_FLAG_BID|TICK_FLAG_ASK only (SpikeKit:592) while filling .last, InpOrigin defaults to "" = this chart's symbol, and grep confirms the only SYMBOL_CHART_MODE writers in the tree are the product manager (forces BID, Manager:383) and the QA smoke assertion. The consequence is documented in-tree as measured, in exactly this shape (TickSynthesizer:152-175: ticks accepted, refused 0, M1 series unchanged), and B1's forming_bar_took_close / wick_expanded_to_extremes verdicts (B1:167-172) are what would read 0 of 20. Severity must come down hard, though: this is Phase 0 measurement scaffolding that ships nothing into the engine, the product already has both the forced BID mode and the LAST flag, and no user of SS Replay can reach this state - the worst outcome is a wrong entry in a dev results file. LOW, not HIGH

#### `strategy-integration-report-2` — OnBar is detected from the last tick of a published batch, so its firing point and the resulting fill price are functions of wall-clock pump boundaries

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Strategy/SSR_StrategyHost.mqh:184`** · category: tick-ordering

```mql5
      long now = ticks[count - 1].time_msc;
...
         long bar_open = SSRBarOpenMsc(now, m_tf[i]);
...
         else if(bar_open > m_last_bar[i])
           { m_last_bar[i] = bar_open; m_bar_calls++; m_strat[i].OnBar(); }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The defect is real; the worked arithmetic is not. With the defaults, InpPumpMs=40 caps a starved pump at ceiling = 4*40 = 160 ms of wall time (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2754-2759), so at the ladder's top (SSR_SPEED_MAX = 100000, i.e. 1000x) one FULL_TICK batch covers at most ~160 s of replay. That is enough to contain two or three M1 boundaries - so M1 bar closes are genuinely skipped - but not two M15 boundaries. Skipping an M15 close additionally requires a larger InpPumpMs (e.g. 250 ms, giving a 1000 s ceiling at 1000x).

**Why:** The host reads only ticks[count-1] and derives bar_open from it; every tick before the last in the batch is invisible to the bar-close test. Contrast CSSRTradingEngine::OnTicks, which loops over all count ticks updating m_bid/m_ask/m_now_msc and calling CheckStops per tick - the account is therefore batch-size independent and deterministic, while the strategy layer is not. In the bar-driven path the batch is one M1 bar's ticks (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:506-507), and whether that bar arrives whole or clipped mid-minute depends on where the pump's `hi` landed (ClipBar, SSR_ReplayController.mqh:159). In the FULL_TICK path the batch is the entire window (line 366), so the number of bar boundaries inside one batch is unbounded and m_last_bar jumps over the ones in between, silently.

**Verifiers:** Both halves check out against the source. CSSRStrategyHost::OnTicks derives `now` solely from ticks[count-1] (184), computes bar_open from it (194) and can advance m_last_bar past several boundaries in one step (202-207), so any bar close that is not the last boundary in the batch is silently skipped - and OnTick likewise fires once per batch, not per tick. In FULL_TICK the batch is the whole window (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:366), so the number of boundaries inside one batch is bounded only by the pump ceiling. The wall-clock dependence is also provable: SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2735 computes `delta` from GetMicrosecondCount() and feeds it to Pump, which reaches m_clock.Advance(wall_delta_ms) (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1006), so window boundaries - and in the bar-driven path the ClipBar/tick-trim boundary at 459-470 - move with CPU load, which moves the tick on which OnBar fires and therefore the Bid/Ask the strategy reads and the price the engine fills at. Contrast confirmed: CSSRTradingEngine::OnTicks iterates all ticks, so the account layer is batch-independent while the strategy layer is not. This breaks the explicit contract at SSR_IStrategy.mqh:20-24 / The mechanism is source-provable. The host reads only ticks[count-1] (184) and derives bar_open from it, so every earlier tick in the batch is invisible to the `bar_open > m_last_bar[i]` test (202-207) and m_last_bar jumps straight over any intermediate boundary; contrast CSSRTradingEngine::OnTicks, which iterates all ticks, making the account batch-invariant. Batch size is a wall-clock artefact: Pump takes wall_delta_ms (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:996, whose own comment only promises determinism for 'identical delta sequences'), the FULL_TICK branch publishes the whole window in one PublishTicks (366), and in the bar-driven path the newest bar is ClipBar'd at wherever `hi` fell (159, 280-309), so the replay instant at which OnBar fires - and hence the ctx.market.Bid()/Ask() CSSRRefBreakout tests at RefStrategy:115 and the price the engine fills at - moves with pump boundaries / The mechanism is confirmed. The host reads only ticks[count-1].time_msc (line 184) and derives bar_open from it, so every earlier tick in the batch is invisible to the bar-close test, and OnBar therefore fires at whatever replay instant the batch happens to end on. In the bar-driven path that instant is wall-clock dependent through clipping: the per-M1-bar segments are built from ClipBar against the pump's `hi` (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:~455 and 506-507), so whether the first M1 bar of a new group arrives whole or clipped mid-minute - a function of the wall delta the clock scaled - moves the Bid()/Ask() the strategy sees at OnBar and hence the fill CSSRTradingEngine gives it and whether CSSRRefBreakout's `sl >= Bid()` gate trips. Contrast with CSSRTradingEngine::OnTicks, which loops all count ticks and calls CheckStops per tick, is correct: the account layer is batch-size independent while the strategy layer is not. Downgraded from HIGH: nothing here corrupts money arithmetic or weakens the virtual-only guarantee - the account still prices every tick - the strategy layer is opt-in, and the per-run deviation is a few ticks of entry price. It breaks reproducibility for strategy trades, which is a documented promise, so MEDIUM rather than lower

#### `trading-analytics-4` — Class report and CSV consumers see a different per-trade result than the statement (raw profit vs net)

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_Journal.mqh:82`** · category: commission

```mql5
p.profit, p.commission, p.swap,
         //--- R is left EMPTY, never zero, when it does not exist:
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: A three-column CSV layout (profit / commission / swap) is defensible on its own - MT5's own statement uses it. The actual defect is that the CSV's only consumer classifies with the wrong column: SSR_ClassReport.mqh:259-266 derives won/lost from the raw 'profit' column while the journal and SSR_Statistics define a result as profit + swap - commission, so with a non-zero commission or swap the class report's win rate (and the '# net_profit' header versus the sum of the 'profit' column) contradicts the statement written from the same trades. Either the CSV needs a 'net' column that ReadOne prefers, or ReadOne must combine the three columns it already has.

**Why:** Row() writes p.profit raw (l.82) with no net column; ExportHtml computes net = p.profit + p.swap - p.commission for its Profit column (l.394) and ComputeFor uses the same net for wins/losses (SSR_Statistics.mqh:431-450). ClassReport ReadOne locates only 'open_time', 'profit', 'r' columns (l.243-245) and derives won/lost from 'profit' (l.259, 266). With the default commission 0 and swap 0 all three agree, which is why no test catches it.

**Verifiers:** Traced and provable. SSR_Journal.mqh:72-89 Row() writes p.profit raw into the 'profit' column with commission and swap as separate columns and no net column (header l.218-220). ExportHtml computes 'double net = p.profit + p.swap - p.commission' (l.394) for its Profit column, and CSSRStatsEngine::ComputeFor uses the identical net for wins/losses/gross/net_profit (SSR_Statistics.mqh:429-448) - the comment at 405-427 records exactly this accounting split being fixed for the statement. Commission really is charged twice per round trip (Open l.753, closing leg l.203/218), so at InpCommission=3 a 1-lot +2 trade nets -4 while the CSV 'profit' says +2. SSR_ClassReport.mqh:242-244 locates only open_time/profit/r and 259-266 derives won from that raw 'profit' (pnl > 0.0), so the class report marks it won. '# net_profit' (l.201) is the net sum and no longer equals the sum of the printed 'profit' column. Default commission 0 and swap 0 make all three agree, which is why QA does not catch it / Verified in source. Row() (SSR_Journal.mqh:69-90) writes p.profit, p.commission, p.swap as three raw columns; ExportHtml (l.394) and CSSRStatsEngine::ComputeFor (SSR_Statistics.mqh:430-452, with its own comment about exactly this trap) define the result as profit + swap - commission, and win/loss classification uses that net. SSR_ClassReport.mqh (l.240-266) locates only 'open_time', 'profit', 'r' and sets out.won = (pnl > 0.0) from the raw 'profit' column, so with a non-zero InpCommission/InpSwap a trade that made +2 and cost 3 to place is a loss in the statement and a win in the class report. Latent only because InpCommission, InpSwapLong and InpSwapShort all default to 0.0 (host l.90-93), which is why no test catches it / The mechanics are as described and provable: Row() (l.72-89) writes p.profit raw with commission and swap as separate columns, ExportHtml's Profit column writes net = profit + swap - commission (l.394, 421), ComputeFor uses the same net to decide wins/losses and net_profit (SSR_Statistics.mqh:429-434, 512), and CSSRClassReport::ReadOne locates only open_time/profit/r and sets out.won = (pnl > 0.0) from the raw column (SSR_ClassReport.mqh:243-245, 259-266). But the impact is much smaller than MEDIUM. Points (a) and (b) of the claim are not defects: profit / commission / swap as three separate columns with a net total in the header is exactly the convention MT5's own HTML statement and every broker statement use, so `# net_profit` not equalling the raw sum of the `profit` column is correct labelling, not a contradiction - and the CSV carries both components, so a reader can net. The only real defect is (c): this product reads its OWN file with a different accounting rule than it wrote it with, and out.won drives nothing but the colour of one marker in the class-report timeline (SSR_ClassReport.mqh:573). The class report's profit_factor rendering is even guarded (l.622). Requires InpCommission != 0 or a swap input (all default 0). User-observable effect: a green dot where a red one belongs on a coach's timeline

#### `trading-analytics-12` — A jump forward never runs the evaluation; a jump that completes the window leaves it IN PROGRESS

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_PropEvaluation.mqh:358`** · category: prop-rules

```mql5
virtual void      OnClock(const long now_msc) override
     {
      if(!m_rules.enabled || m_state != SSR_PROP_RUNNING || m_acct == NULL)
         return;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate for the jump-to-end case, with two refinements: (a) when the forward jump does NOT complete the replay, the next Pump's OnClock does judge - RollDay recomputes m_total_days from the new day index, so the deadline fires late rather than never; what is permanently lost is any breach or target reached inside the skipped span (equity is only sampled at the jump's end). (b) The 'review card' does not print the verdict - SSR_Review.mqh has no prop fields; the stuck 'IN PROGRESS' shows in the HTML statement (SSR_Journal.mqh:286-297) and the panel's prop sheet.

**Why:** SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1333-1420 JumpForward calls EmitWindow, m_sink.OnSeek, Publish() and Transition(COMPLETED) but contains no m_obs[i].OnClock loop, unlike Pump (l.1027-1029) and PumpTo (l.1102-1104); Pump early-returns when status != PLAYING (l.998). The evaluation has no OnTicks override, so OnClock is its only judging point.

**Verifiers:** Provable. A full grep of MQL5/ finds exactly two OnClock fan-out sites, SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1027-1029 (Pump) and 1102-1104 (PumpTo); JumpForward (l.1333-1419) does the bulk SeedBars, EmitWindow for the partial bar, m_sink.OnSeek, RepairWarmupIfLost, Publish and Transition(COMPLETED) with no observer OnClock loop and no CheckObserverPause. CSSRPropEvaluation overrides only OnClock/OnRewind/OnSessionStart, so the ticks EmitWindow publishes reach it not at all. JumpForward is user-reachable: JumpTo (l.1484-1519) is the 'unified entry point: the panel and the dialog both call this' and forwards to it. When the jump lands on the timeline end, m_clock.IsCompleted() transitions to COMPLETED, Pump early-returns 0 for any status != PLAYING (l.997-998), and Play() refuses outright after COMPLETED (l.971-976) - so no OnClock ever runs again and State() stays SSR_PROP_RUNNING, which the statement prints as 'IN PROGRESS' (SSR_Journal.mqh:286-297 via StateName(), SSR_PropEvaluation.mqh:57). The per-pump rather than per-tick judging is also correct as described / Confirmed by reading all three paths. CSSRPropEvaluation overrides only OnClock and OnRewind (no OnTicks), so OnClock is its only judging point. Pump (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1027-1029) and PumpTo (l.1102-1104) each run the OnClock loop after EmitWindow; JumpForward (l.1333-1418) does SetHorizon, bulk SeedBars, EmitWindow for the partial bar, m_sink.OnSeek, RepairWarmupIfLost, Publish and Transition(COMPLETED) with no OnClock loop anywhere, and JumpTo forwards every forward jump to it (l.1519). Pump/PumpTo both early-return 0 when status != PLAYING (l.997-998, 1071-1072), so once the jump lands on the timeline end and the clock completes, no OnClock ever fires again and the state stays SSR_PROP_RUNNING -> StateName() 'IN PROGRESS' in both Report() (used by the statement, SSR_Journal.mqh:295) and the panel. The secondary point is also accurate: even in normal play the rules are evaluated once per pump against end-of-pump equity, so an intra-pump breach that recovers is not seen / Verified line by line: JumpForward (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1333-1419) does the bulk SeedBars, EmitWindow for the partial bar, m_sink.OnSeek, RepairWarmupIfLost, Publish and Transition(COMPLETED) - and contains no m_obs[i].OnClock loop, unlike Pump (l.1027-1029) and PumpTo (l.1102-1104); Pump returns 0 immediately when status != PLAYING (l.997-998). CSSRPropEvaluation overrides only OnSessionStart, OnClock, OnRewind and PauseRequested - no OnTicks - so OnClock is its sole judging point, yet the trading engine DOES process the emitted ticks, so stops fill and equity moves through the whole jumped span unjudged. JumpTo is the unified panel/dialog entry point (l.1482-1519), so this is a first-class control, not an edge path. I upgrade from LOW because the consequence is a dishonest verdict, not a cosmetic one: a jump that reaches the window end leaves the exported statement and review card printing 'IN PROGRESS' forever, and - worse - a daily-loss or drawdown breach that occurred inside the jumped span and had recovered by the next pump is never judged at all, so a run that should read FAILED can go on to read PASSED. Only the once-per-pump granularity remark is unavoidable design rather than defect

#### `trading-analytics-2` — Prop evaluation state is not persisted; a resumed or reset run re-bases every rule on current equity

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_PropEvaluation.mqh:366`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
if(!m_started)
        {
         m_started     = true;
         m_first_day   = now_msc / SSR_PROP_DAY_MSC;
         m_day         = m_first_day;
         m_day_open_eq = eq;
         m_peak_eq     = eq;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate, with the sequencing worth stating: after a resume the evaluation is already SSR_PROP_VOID (see trading-analytics-1), so OnClock early-returns at l.360 and the re-basing only takes effect once the user presses Reset - which finding 1 forces them to do. Note also that the omission is a recorded design decision (docs/audit/maps/ui-port-session.md:247-248 'Not stored: ... the prop evaluation'), so this is an acknowledged gap rather than an accidental one; the wrong-verdict consequence is real either way.

**Why:** The class has no SaveInto/RestoreFrom (grep of the file). SSR_SessionManager.mqh:195-200 and 367-375 save/restore only m_acct and m_stats. Reset() (l.327-341) and the first-clock branch (l.366-377) initialise every counter from m_rules.start_balance / current Equity(). The trading engine and the equity curve ARE restored, so the account carries its history while the judge forgets it.

**Verifiers:** Traced and provable. CSSRPropEvaluation has no SaveInto/RestoreFrom (grep of the file: none), and CSSRSessionManager writes only the streams, m_acct and m_stats (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:193-198) and restores only those (l.366-374); docs/audit/maps/ui-port-session.md:247-248 states outright 'Not stored: ... the prop evaluation'. Reset() (l.327-341) re-bases m_peak_eq/m_low_eq/m_day_open_eq on m_rules.start_balance, zeroes m_trading_days and m_total_days, and takes m_last_positions from the restored count; the first-clock branch (l.366-377) then overwrites m_peak_eq/m_day_open_eq with CURRENT equity and sets m_total_days = 1 and m_first_day to the resumed day. So with trailing rules the total floor rebases to 9400-1000 = 8400 instead of 10000-1000 = 9000, the deadline restarts at day 1 of 30, and the minimum-days count restarts at 0 - while the account and its equity curve ARE restored. The verdict genuinely differs from the same trades taken in one sitting, and it differs in the trader's favour / Verified: SSR_PropEvaluation.mqh contains no SaveInto/RestoreFrom (the whole 481-line file was read), and CSSRSessionManager persists only the group streams, m_acct and m_stats (Save l.193-199, Restore l.366-375) - so no prop counter is written to or read from the session file. Reset() (l.327-341) re-bases m_peak_eq/m_low_eq/m_day_open_eq on m_rules.start_balance and zeroes m_trading_days/m_total_days, and the first-clock branch (l.366-377) then overwrites m_peak_eq, m_day_open_eq, m_low_eq with current Equity() and sets m_total_days = 1. With trailing on and equity at 9400 that makes TotalFloor() 8400 rather than 9000, restarts the deadline at day 1 and the min-days count at 0, while the account and the equity curve are restored in full - so the judge forgets a history the account still carries / Confirmed: CSSRPropEvaluation has no SaveInto/RestoreFrom (nothing in the 481-line file), and CSSRSessionManager persists only the streams, m_acct and m_stats (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:194-200) and restores only those (l.361-375). So the account, its trades and the equity curve come back while the judge's peak, day index, day-open equity, trading-day count and elapsed-day count do not. Combined with trading-analytics-1 (resume forces VOID, so the user must press Reset), the first OnClock after the reset takes the m_started branch (l.366-377) and sets m_peak_eq = m_day_open_eq = current restored equity and m_total_days = 1. With trailing rules, a trader who is 600 down gets TotalFloor() re-based to 8400 instead of 9000 - a fresh full 10% allowance carried on an account that has already spent part of it - and the deadline and min-days counters restart. That is a path to a PASSED verdict an unbroken run would have FAILED, with nothing on the panel saying the peak was re-based, which is why MEDIUM is right and I do not downgrade it to a missing feature. One detail in the claim is off: Reset() itself sets m_peak_eq/m_day_open_eq to m_rules.start_balance (l.335-338); the re-basing to current equity happens on the next OnClock, not in Reset

#### `trading-analytics-3` — Closed-only drawdown walks trades in open order, not close order

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_Statistics.mqh:703`** · category: drawdown-calc

```mql5
for(int i = 0; i < total; i++)
        {
         SSRVirtualPosition p;
         if(!m_acct.At(i, p) || !p.IsClosed())
            continue;
         bal += p.profit + p.swap - p.commission;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate for max_drawdown_closed. The secondary remark about win_streak/loss_streak is weaker: counting streaks by entry order is a defensible convention, so only the balance-curve drawdown (and, per trading-analytics-7, revenge) is provably wrong.

**Why:** At(i) returns positions in slot (creation) order (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:1005-1011, m_pos appended in Open() l.719). Nothing sorts by close_msc before accumulating bal/peak/worst. The same slot-order walk also drives win_streak/loss_streak/revenge in ComputeFor (l.406-513). T10.2 (SSR_T10_Statistics.mq5:204) only checks a sequential, non-overlapping case.

**Verifiers:** ClosedDrawdownFor (SSR_Statistics.mqh:694-717) accumulates `bal += p.profit + p.swap - p.commission` while walking i=0..total-1 through m_acct.At(i, p), and At() is a straight slot read (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:1004-1011) with slots appended in Open() order (l.719) and never sorted or compacted - I grepped the engine for ArraySort/ArrayRemove/compaction and there is none. Nothing orders by p.close_msc, and slot order is not even open order for pendings (open_msc is stamped at request time, l.734). So whenever positions overlap the running balance follows a path the account never took and the peak/worst pair is wrong, as the finder's A/B/C example shows. The wrong number is printed as 'Max drawdown, closed only' (SSR_Journal.mqh:1129-1130) and as 'Drawdown / On closed trades' (SSR_Review.mqh:135), and the statement asks the reader to compare it against the equity drawdown. MEDIUM is fair: a user-visible metric is wrong under ordinary multi-position use, with no guard / Provable from source and arithmetic. ClosedDrawdownFor (SSR_Statistics.mqh:697-719) accumulates bal in At(i) order, which is creation/open order (m_pos append-only, SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:719; At :1005; nothing sorts by close_msc anywhere in the engine - I grepped for ArraySort/compaction and found none). A closed-trade balance curve is by definition ordered by close time, so whenever closes are out of open order the walked curve never existed. I checked the example: real path 10000->11000->10500->10400 has worst 600; slot order gives 9900->10900->10400, worst 500. The figure is published, not internal (SSR_Journal.mqh:1130, SSR_Review.mqh:135 'Drawdown / On closed trades'), and T10.2 only covers a sequential case. This is ordinary control-flow, not runtime-dependent / ClosedDrawdownFor (l.697-719) accumulates bal in At(i) slot order with nothing sorting by close_msc, and slot order is creation order (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:1004-1011 At, Open appends at m_count++ l.719). The finder's worked example is arithmetically right (600 real vs 500 reported). Overlap is the normal case here, not an exotic one: the panel opens multiple positions, CloseAll/BreakEvenAll act on all of them, and pendings take their slot at placement, so the balance path walked is not the path the account took. The number is printed as 'Max drawdown, closed only' with copy inviting the reader to compare the gap with the equity drawdown (SSR_Journal.mqh:1128-1131) and is re-parsed into the class report, so a wrong value is read as a conclusion about holding losses. MEDIUM is right: money-shaped reporting error, reachable by default, but no effect on balance, equity, or the virtual-trading guarantee

#### `trading-exec-5` — Ambiguity test uses the whole bar range, so a target hit after a mid-bar entry is booked as a stop loss when the bar's earlier low was below the stop

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:164`** · category: sl-tp-execution

```mql5
if(p.IsLong())
  {
   sl_in = (m_bar.low  <= p.sl);
   tp_in = (m_bar.high >= p.tp);
  }
...
return (sl_in && tp_in);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: BarIsAmbiguousFor (164-174) tests the whole cached M1 range, which includes price printed before a mid-bar entry, so pre-entry excursion is charged to the position. Reachable when the stop sits ABOVE the bar's earlier low for a long (e.g. buy the breakout at tick 3 with a tight stop 10 points under the entry while tick 2 already printed a low 30 points lower) and the target is inside the bar's high: CheckStops 537-541 then books SSR_CLOSE_SL at StopFill and flags the trade ambiguous although every tick from the entry onward was above the stop. The claim's illustration ('SL 5 points below that bar's low') does not trigger it - with sl below the bar low, sl_in is false. The same whole-bar test at 371-384 flags a pending fill against a low printed before the fill. mae (293-300) already holds the post-entry excursion the test should use.

**Why:** m_bar is the bar cached by OnBarContext (593-598); ClipBar in the controller only trims it to the pump window end (controller 446-459), so inside one emitted segment it is the full minute. BarIsAmbiguousFor (141-175) never compares open_msc with the ticks already seen nor uses the running excursion (mae, updated at 293-300, already records the worst move since entry). CheckStops 537-541 calls it whenever either level is touched. This is a stated pessimism policy, but the pessimism is applied to price that occurred before the position existed, which the design comment (9-20) does not claim.

**Verifiers:** The mechanism is provable: BarIsAmbiguousFor (141-175) compares p.sl/p.tp against the whole cached m_bar low/high and never against open_msc or the running mae (293-300); m_bar is the full minute up to the pump-window end (OnBarContext 593-598; ClipBar keeps 'everything in this minute UP TO the window's end - including the part an earlier pump already emitted', SSR_ReplayController.mqh 446-459). CheckStops 537-541 therefore books ClosePosition(StopFill, SSR_CLOSE_SL, true) - a loss plus slippage - even when every tick after the entry stayed above the stop, and the fill-time flag at 371-384 has the same whole-bar test. BUT the claim's own numbers are self-refuting: with 'SL 5 points below that bar's low' the test `m_bar.low <= p.sl` is FALSE and no ambiguity is raised. The defect needs the stop ABOVE the bar's earlier low, which the title states correctly / The finding's core premise is that the engine's own synthesised tick order is evidence about what happened. The file states the opposite at lines 11-20 ('that ORDER IS OUR ASSUMPTION, not the market's behaviour'), and it is true: an M1 bar cannot say whether the minute's low printed before or after the instant the user clicked Buy mid-minute, so for a mid-minute entry whose stop sits under that minute's low the outcome genuinely is unknowable and the pessimistic-plus-flag policy (R11) is exactly what is documented. The one case where unhappened price would be used is already guarded twice: ClipBar (ReplayController 240-279) rebuilds a forming bar from only the ticks up to the pump window end, and BarIsAmbiguousFor 156-158 rejects a cached bar the current tick does not live in. 'Every synthesised tick after the entry was above the stop' is precisely the self-flattery the design refuses, and the trade is flagged rather than silently booked, with AmbiguousPercent reporting the fraction. No defect provable from source; at most a possible refinement (weighting by entry time within the minute), which would itself require inventing intra-minute order / The code path is provable: BarIsAmbiguousFor (141-175) tests p.sl/p.tp against m_bar.low/m_bar.high with no reference to open_msc, to the ticks already seen, or to the running mae (which UpdateExcursions 293-300 already maintains), and CheckStops (537-541) routes any touched level through it, booking ClosePosition(i, StopFill(i), SSR_CLOSE_SL, true). The bar it tests is the whole minute up to the pump window end - ClipBar (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:269-300, called at 459) deliberately rebuilds the shown bar from the bar's own open, 'the window's LOWER bound is deliberately not applied' - so a low printed in an already-emitted segment, before the position existed, still makes a later TP hit book as a stop loss. Reachable in the stated way (SYNTHETIC_TICK is the automatic fidelity whenever the broker has no real ticks, SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1153-1154) and user-visible: the chart the user watched shows price never returning to the stop after their entry, yet the journal records SL and marks the trade ASSUMED. Not upgraded past MEDIUM because the loss is flagged rather than hidden and the mid-bar entry instant itself sits on the synthesised timeline, so some pessimism here is defensible; the defect is its extent, not its existence. The fill-time half of the claim (371-384) only sets an honesty flag and moves no money

#### `trading-exec-6` — No margin check on entry when margin is modelled: an unaffordable order is accepted, charged, then stopped out on the next tick

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:741`** · category: margin

```mql5
bool is_long = SSRIsLong(type);
m_pos[i].state        = SSR_POS_OPEN;
m_pos[i].open_price   = FillPrice(is_long, true);
...
m_pos[i].commission = m_exec.commission_per_lot * volume;
m_balance          -= m_pos[i].commission;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Open() performs no free-margin check before booking a fill, so when margin is modelled an unaffordable order is accepted, charged, and then liquidated by CheckStopout on a later tick instead of being rejected at zero cost. Reachable only when the user sets both InpMarginLot > 0 and InpStopout > 0 (both default 0), so it is an opt-in fidelity gap rather than a default-path defect.

**Why:** Open() 705-756 checks capacity, volume > 0, price > 0 and nothing else; OpenWithRisk/OpenPendingWithRisk (759-801) add only sizing checks. FreeMargin() (946) is defined but a repository-wide grep finds no caller outside the engine. CheckStopout (436-470) is the only margin enforcement and runs after the fill.

**Verifiers:** Open() (705-756) checks capacity, Reserve, volume > 0, m_bid > 0 and, for pendings, price > 0 - nothing else; it then fills at FillPrice and debits commission at 753-754. OpenWithRisk/OpenPendingWithRisk (759-801) add only lot-sizing checks. FreeMargin() (946) and MarginLevel() (949) exist but no caller in the engine or in GroupPort/Publisher/Strategy gates an entry on them, and CheckStopout (436-470) runs from OnTicks 613 only after the fill, so the oversized position is charged round-turn commission (BookExit 203) and slippage (StopFill/FillPrice) before being force-closed as SSR_CLOSE_STOPOUT with m_stopouts++ (464). Margin modelling is opt-in (InpMarginLot default 0.0, SSReplayStandalone.mq5 94-95), which bounds the blast radius but does not refute the path / The absence is provable: Open() 705-756 performs no affordability test, OpenWithRisk/OpenPendingWithRisk 759-801 add only sizing checks, FreeMargin() (946) has no production caller anywhere in the tree (only SSR_T10_Statistics.mq5 461 reads it), and CheckStopout 436-470 runs from OnTicks 613 only after the fill has been booked and its commission taken at 753-754. So with margin modelled, an order that a terminal would refuse with 'not enough money' at zero cost is instead accepted, charged entry commission, then force-closed by CheckStopout at FillPrice(...,false) with a second commission and adverse slippage, incrementing m_stopouts and tripping the SSR_PAUSE_ON_STOPOUT announcement. Severity cut from MEDIUM: the whole path needs two non-default inputs (InpMarginLot > 0 and InpStopout > 0, both default 0.0 at host lines 94-95), and MarginModelled() is explicitly an opt-in model / Confirmed: Open() (705-756) validates capacity, volume > 0 and m_bid > 0 and nothing else; OpenWithRisk/OpenPendingWithRisk add only sizing checks; FreeMargin() (946) has no caller anywhere outside SSR_T10 (repo-wide grep), and CheckStopout (436-470) runs only from OnTicks, i.e. after the fill and after the commission has been taken (754). Reachable without a deliberately silly order: LotForRisk (SSR_RiskEngine.mqh:95-134) sizes from Equity and a stop distance and caps only at vol_max, so a tight-stop entry legitimately produces a lot whose margin exceeds the account whenever margin is modelled. What the user observes is a trade a terminal would have refused at zero cost being accepted, charged commission, then stopped out on the next tick at bid-slip with a second commission, incrementing Stopouts and firing the 'stop out' auto-pause. Kept at MEDIUM rather than higher because both InpMarginLot and InpStopout default to 0, so the harmful cascade is opt-in (or depends on the origin symbol declaring SYMBOL_MARGIN_INITIAL), and the money lost is modelling error rather than a safety-guarantee breach - no real order exists

#### `trading-exec-8` — BreakEven/Modify accept a stop on the wrong side of the market and the engine then closes the trade instead of rejecting the change

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:920`** · category: sl-tp-execution

```mql5
bool              BreakEven(const long ticket)
  {
   int i = Find(ticket);
   if(i < 0 || m_pos[i].state != SSR_POS_OPEN)
     { m_last_error = "no open position"; return false; }
   m_pos[i].sl = m_pos[i].open_price;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: BreakEven sets a long's stop to its ask-side fill price and CheckStops tests it against the bid, so the condition px <= sl is already satisfied when the stop is written: pressing 'be' before price has advanced past the entry spread closes the trade on the very next tick as an SL. This holds even with zero spread and zero slippage, where sl == open_price == bid triggers on equality (a flat 'stop loss' in the statistics). Modify() has the same missing wrong-side check, and neither path marks the close as a rejected modification.

**Why:** BreakEven (920-927) and Modify (844-853) write sl/tp with only a state check; neither compares against m_bid/m_ask. GroupPort.BreakEven/BreakEvenAll (867-915) add no price check either. CheckStops treats any sl on the wrong side as an immediate hit and StopFill (492-511) takes the worse of level and price, so the fill is at the current market minus slippage.

**Verifiers:** Traced end to end with no guard anywhere. BreakEven (920-927) sets sl = open_price after only a state check; for a long open_price = FillPrice(true,true) = ask + slip (743), which is above the bid by the whole spread plus slippage. On the next tick CheckStops 526-527 evaluates `px <= sl` with px = m_bid and fires; with tp == 0 BarIsAmbiguousFor returns false at 160-161, so 543 closes at StopFill = min(sl, bid) - slip = bid - slip, i.e. a guaranteed loss of spread + 2x slippage booked as SSR_CLOSE_SL. Modify (844-853) writes any sl/tp with no side check. No caller filters: GroupPort BreakEven (867-874) and BreakEvenAll (899-914) only null-check m_acct, and the panel wires them raw ('be' button SSR_Panel.mqh:2116, per-row action 2679) with no price comparison / Provable from source and stronger than the finding states. BreakEven (920-927) sets sl = open_price with only a state check, and for a long open_price = FillPrice(true,true) = m_ask + slip (743), while CheckStops compares px = m_bid against it (524-527). Since bid <= ask always, the new stop is at or above the comparison price from the moment it is written, so the very next tick satisfies px <= sl and ClosePosition books reason SL at StopFill = min(sl, bid) - slip (492-511) - a loss of the spread plus slippage, counted as a losing trade and announced by the SSR_PAUSE_ON_SL watcher. No guard exists upstream either: GroupPort.BreakEven 867-874 and BreakEvenAll 899-914 pass the ticket through untouched, and Modify 844-853 writes any sl/tp with no comparison to m_bid/m_ask. A terminal rejects a long SL above bid (invalid stops) and leaves the position running, so nothing about this depends on unsettled runtime behaviour / Confirmed and easy to reach. BreakEven (920-927) writes sl = open_price with only a state check, and open_price for a long is FillPrice(true,true) = ask + slip, i.e. strictly above the current bid; Modify (844-853) writes any sl/tp with no side check; GroupPort.BreakEven / BreakEvenAll (867-915) add none either. CheckStops (526-527) then reads px = bid <= sl as a stop hit and StopFill (492-511) takes min(level, price) - slip, so the position is closed at bid - slip: a certain loss of spread + slippage, booked with reason SL, counted as a losing trade by Statistics, and announced by auto-pause. The worst reachable case is the panel's one-press BreakEvenAll, which moves EVERY open stop to its entry including positions currently in drawdown - each of those has open_price on the wrong side of the market and is force-closed at market on the next tick, so a user who meant 'protect my winners' silently liquidates their losers. A real terminal rejects the modification ('invalid stops') and the position keeps running. MEDIUM is right: reachable through ordinary UI use and it manufactures closed losing trades, but each individual loss is small (spread + 2x slippage) and nothing beyond the virtual account is touched

#### `ui-port-session-9` — Pending orders occupy wire rows that no total on the wire accounts for

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_GroupPort.mqh:372`** · category: boundary

```mql5
               out.pending_count++;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Confirmed; one arithmetic nit in the second scenario: pos_rows is capped at SSR_POS_MAX=12 (SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:29, loop guard at GroupPort:340), so with 20 opens + 4 pendings only 12 rows are ever collected - the line still misreports ("15 not shown" against 19 items actually absent), but 12 rows, not 24, exist to hide.

**Why:** Rows include pendings (`bool pend = (vp.state == SSR_POS_PENDING); if(vp.state != SSR_POS_OPEN && !pend) continue;`, l.354-356) while `out.open_positions = m_acct.OpenCount();` (l.240) does not. pending_count appears only at its declaration (SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:124), its clear (l.261) and this increment - no consumer exists. The panel computes `int shown = (m_state.pos_rows < cap ? m_state.pos_rows : cap);` (SSR_Panel.mqh:1513) and then `if(m_state.open_positions > shown)` ... `m_state.open_positions - shown` (l.1640-1642), with PosCap() = (134-22)/20 = 5 on the normal panel (l.434-443). Scenario: 2 open positions and 4 pending orders - pos_rows 6, shown 5, open_positions 2; `2 > 5` is false, so no indicator is drawn and one pending order is simply absent, which is exactly the failure the "PENDING ORDERS ARE ROWS TOO / an order you cannot see is an order you forget you left there" comment at l.346-353 exists to prevent. With 20 opens and 4 pendings the same line reports "15 not shown" when 19 rows are hidden.

**Verifiers:** Every cited number verifies. GroupPort:354-356 admits pendings into the row array while GroupPort:240 sets out.open_positions = m_acct.OpenCount(), which counts only SSR_POS_OPEN (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:974-980; a separate PendingCount() exists at 981 and is unused here). out.pending_count is incremented at GroupPort:372 and grep over MQL5/ finds it nowhere else except its declaration (SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:124) and its clear (261) - no consumer. SSR_Panel.mqh:1513 computes shown from pos_rows while 1640-1642 gates the "+N more" line on open_positions > shown and prints open_positions - shown. PosCap() = (134-22)/(SSR_ROW_H+1) with SSR_ROW_H=19 (SSR_Theme.mqh:511) = 5 on the normal panel. So 2 opens + 4 pendings gives pos_rows 6, shown 5, open_positions 2, 2 > 5 false - one row vanishes with no indicator, and since the fill loop runs newest-first (GroupPort:340) the casualty is the oldest order, exactly the "order you forget you left there" the comment at 346-353 exists to prevent / Every number in the claim checks out against the files. GroupPort's row loop admits both states (bool pend = (vp.state == SSR_POS_PENDING); if(vp.state != SSR_POS_OPEN && !pend) continue; at l.354-356) and increments the shared pos_rows for each (l.358), while out.open_positions = m_acct.OpenCount() (l.240) counts only SSR_POS_OPEN - confirmed at TradingEngine:974-980, with a separate PendingCount() at 981-987 that the port never calls. pending_count is written at GroupPort:372 and nowhere read: the only other occurrences in the whole tree are its declaration (ReplayPort:124) and its clear (ReplayPort:261). The panel's indicator compares the two mismatched quantities: shown = min(pos_rows, cap) (Panel:1513) against if(m_state.open_positions > shown) ... open_positions - shown (Panel:1637-1642). PosCap() = (PosGroupH() - 22) / (SSR_ROW_H + 1) with PosGroupH() = 134 non-tall (Panel:443) and SSR_ROW_H = 19 (Theme:511), so cap = 112/20 = 5 exactly as claimed. Scenario 1 (2 open + 4 pending): pos_rows 6, shown 5, open_positions 2, so 2 > 5 is false, no 'not shown' text is drawn, and the oldest row - reachable as a pending, since the loop walks newest-first - is simply absent. Scenario 2 (20 open + 4 pending): pos_rows saturates at SSR_POS_MAX = 12 (ReplayPort:29), shown 5, and the line reports 20-5 = '15 not shown' while 19 of the 24 live items are off the sheet. This is precisely the failure the 'PENDING ORDERS ARE ROWS TOO / an order you cannot see is an order you forget you left there' comment at GroupPort:346-353 exists to prevent. refuted=true records that I could not break it / The mismatch is exactly as described and I verified every number: rows include pendings (GroupPort:354-356), out.open_positions = m_acct.OpenCount() counts opens only (l.240), pending_count has no reader anywhere in the tree (declared SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:124, cleared l.261, incremented GroupPort:372, and grep finds nothing else), and the panel gates its only hidden-rows indicator on open_positions vs shown (SSR_Panel.mqh:1640-1642) where shown derives from pos_rows (l.1513). PosCap() is (134-22)/(SSR_ROW_H=19 + 1) = 5 on the normal sheet, so 2 opens + 4 pendings gives pos_rows 6, shown 5, and 2 > 5 is false: one row vanishes with no "+N more", and 20 opens + 4 pendings prints "15 not shown" when 19 rows are hidden. Severity comes down from MEDIUM because the finding's stated harm - "an order you cannot see" - is mitigated elsewhere: the host draws every pending order on the chart as a dotted, named line for exactly this reason (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2933-2939), so the user does have a visible representation. What is left is a missing panel row (always the oldest, since the loop walks newest-first) and a miscounted indicator: no money arithmetic, no future data, no weakening of the virtual-trading guarantee

#### `ui-panel-2` — HideBody(false) runs every frame and Forget()s the property cache for ~44 sized objects, which are then rewritten in full

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:729`** · category: redraw

```mql5
      HideBody(false);

      int cy = y + SSR_HEADER_H + 3;
      cy = DrawClock(x, cy, W);
      cy = DrawTransport(x, cy, W);
      cy = DrawSpeed(x, cy, W);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate as to mechanism and write count (~390, all from Rect/ButtonC; the labels in HideBody's list still take Text()'s early return). The millisecond consequence is an extrapolation from a comment in SSR_Widgets.mqh:31-38 and is unvalidated on a real terminal, so this is a provable cache regression of known write count and an unproven latency figure - hence MEDIUM rather than HIGH.

**Why:** HideBody's id list (:2079-2098) covers 'bar_bg','bar_fill','restart','back10','back','toggle','step','step10','reset','spdn','spdbox','spup','tabline','lines','sessions','fidelity','status','fill_bg','fill_ac' plus `for(int t=0;t<SSR_TAB_MAX;t++) m_w.Hide("tab"+IntegerToString(t), hidden);` and `for(int t=0;t<SSR_SPEED_LADDER_SIZE;t++) m_w.Hide("spdseg"+IntegerToString(t), hidden);` plus 'spdseg_tk' and 'spdseg_th'. CSSRWidgets::Hide (SSR_Widgets.mqh:616-625) writes OBJPROP_TIMEFRAMES when the object exists and then calls Forget(n) UNCONDITIONALLY - the comment at 622-624 says visibility is not in the fingerprint, so the cache entry has to be dropped. Line 729 is reached on every non-collapsed, non-closed frame, and lines 732-734 and 750-751 then redraw exactly those objects, so Same() returns false for each and ButtonC/Rect execute all 9 writes (SSR_Widgets.mqh:377-386, 211-220).

**Verifiers:** CSSRWidgets::Hide calls Forget(n) UNCONDITIONALLY, including for hidden==false (SSR_Widgets.mqh:616-625), and Forget(n)/Same(n) key on the same prefixed name, so the slot really is dropped. Line 729 is on the hot path every frame. The objects in the list that are Rect/Button ARE then rebuilt in the same frame with their full 9 writes because Same() misses: bar_bg/bar_fill via Progress (18), restart/back10/back/toggle/step/step10/reset (63), spdn/spup (18), spdbox (9), tabline (9), lines/sessions/fidelity (27), status (9), tab0..tab(n-1) (36-45), and the Slider's _tk + 20 cells + _th (198). I counted ~387-400 property writes per still frame, matching the claim. The claim does not overstate by including labels - and correctly so, since CSSRPanel::Text() (:194-200) short-circuits on its own m_cache plus m_w.Exists(), which is still true for a merely hidden object, so clock/prog/spdlbl/spdval/spdmean/st* are not rewritten. The project's own invariant this breaks is written down at :2999-3004 and asserted in SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:5045 (cached_labels==0) and SSR_T5_Ui.mq5:236 (spent<=2). Severity MEDIUM rather than HIGH only because the ms cost rests on the project's own unvalidated 0.07 ms/write extrapolation; the defeated cache itself is provable / Mechanism is provable. CSSRWidgets::Hide (SSR_Widgets.mqh:616-625) calls Forget(n) UNCONDITIONALLY, and the comment at 622-624 states why (visibility is not in the fingerprint). Line :729 HideBody(false) is reached on every non-collapsed, non-closed frame and forgets bar_bg/bar_fill, the seven transport buttons, spdn/spdbox/spup, spdseg0..19 + spdseg_tk + spdseg_th, tabline, lines/sessions/fidelity, status, tab0..4, so Same() (SSR_Widgets.mqh:80-89) returns false for each and Rect/ButtonC execute their 9 writes (211-220, 377-386). My own count of the objects redrawn later in the same frame is ~43 x 9 = ~390 property writes, so the ~400 figure is right. The label ids in the same list (clock, prog, spdlbl, spdval, spdmean, stbal...) are NOT rewritten - CSSRPanel::Text (:194-207) early-returns on m_cache + m_w.Exists and only writes OBJPROP_COLOR - which the finding does not claim otherwise / Mechanism is provable. CSSRWidgets::Hide calls Forget(n) unconditionally (SSR_Widgets.mqh:616-625), HideBody(false) at :729 runs on every non-collapsed non-closed frame, and the objects it names are redrawn immediately after (:732-734, :750-753), so Same() misses and Rect/ButtonC execute all nine writes. Downgraded from HIGH: the consequence is paint cost only, and the quantified user-visible effect (a whole repaint budget, stutter) is a runtime extrapolation the author cannot validate - the 561-writes/39.05 ms figure is a whole-frame measurement, not a per-write cost, so 'roughly 400 writes = 39 ms' does not follow. Nothing is displayed wrongly and no state is corrupted
