#### `ui-panel-1` — DrawSheet deletes and recreates the entire visible sheet on every repaint, defeating both caches

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:1270`** · category: redraw

```mql5
   void              DrawSheet(const int x, const int y, const int w)
     {
      HideSheets();
      switch(m_tab)
        {
         case SSR_TAB_TRADE:     SheetTrade(x, y, w);     break;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate. Two notes: the 39.05 ms figure is quoted from SSR_Widgets.mqh:31-38 and is not a measurement of this path, so the latency claim is unvalidated (hence MEDIUM); and there is an additional, unprovable-from-source risk the finding does not raise - the sheet's OBJ_BUTTONs (buy, sell, openln, px0.., flat, be, stmt) are deleted and recreated 10 times a second, and the panel learns about clicks by polling the latched OBJPROP_STATE (SSR_Widgets.mqh:338-347), so a press that latches while Render() is executing is destroyed with the object.

**Why:** HideSheets() (:1284) is called unconditionally as the first statement of DrawSheet, and its id list at :1301-1316 contains the ids of EVERY sheet including the one about to be drawn ('risklbl','riskval','riskmon','slrow','tprow','rrrow','sizerow','hintrow','setuprow','openln','flipbtn','enbtn','clrbtn','buy','sell','g1_fr','g1_lb','g1_lg','g2_fr','g2_lb','g2_lg'...). CSSRWidgets::Remove (SSR_Widgets.mqh:627-633) does ObjectDelete then Forget. DrawSheet is called from Render() at :752 (rail) / :757 (fallback) on every frame, i.e. every 100 ms (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3063). Per frame the cost is 70 named Remove() + 4*3 meter Remove() + SSR_POS_MAX(12)*6 row Remove() = 154 ObjectFind+Forget, plus ~20-25 ObjectCreate and their full property writes to rebuild the sheet. The project's own measurement is 561 property writes = 39.05 ms mean repaint (SSR_Widgets.mqh:31-38), which is the whole reason the cache exists.

**Verifiers:** HideSheets() is the unconditional first statement of DrawSheet (:1270-1272), and its id list (:1301-1316) names the objects of EVERY sheet including the one about to be drawn on this frame - risklbl, riskval, riskmon, slrow, tprow, rrrow, sizerow, hintrow, setuprow, openln, flipbtn, enbtn, clrbtn, buy, sell, g1_fr/_lb/_lg, g2_fr/_lb/_lg, taglbl - plus the four meters (3 objects each) and SSR_POS_MAX=12 rows of 6 objects each. CSSRWidgets::Remove does ObjectDelete then Forget (SSR_Widgets.mqh:627-633), which invalidates both caches: CSSRPanel::Text()'s early return requires m_w.Exists(id) (:194-198) and CSSRWidgets::Same() requires ObjectFind>=0 (:90). DrawSheet is reached from Render() at :752/:757 on every non-compact frame, i.e. every 100 ms per SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3062-3066. The tagbox is the one exception (Hidden, not Removed). This does break the project's own written invariant at :2999-3004 and the assertions I read at SSR_T5_Ui.mq5:236 (spent<=2) and SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:5045 (cached_labels==0) - neither of which can have been run, since MT5 is not installed here / Provable. DrawSheet (:1270) calls HideSheets() as its first statement, HideSheets's id list (:1301-1316) contains the ids of every sheet including the one about to be drawn, and CSSRWidgets::Remove (SSR_Widgets.mqh:627-633) is ObjectDelete + Forget. DrawSheet is reached every non-compact frame (:752/:757), and Render() runs on the host's 100 ms gate (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3063-3067). Both caches are therefore defeated by construction: CSSRPanel::Text requires m_w.Exists(id) (:198) and Same() requires ObjectFind >= 0 (SSR_Widgets.mqh:86-88). I checked the two cited assertions: on the default Trade tab with lines unarmed, risklbl/riskmon/riskval/hintrow alone give Writes() >= 4 on a still frame, so SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:5045-5046 (cached_labels == 0) and SSR_T5_Ui.mq5:236 (spent <= 2 over 20 renders) cannot pass, and the invariant documented at :2994-2998 does not hold / Provable. HideSheets() is the unconditional first statement of DrawSheet (:1270-1284) and its id list (:1300-1316) contains the ids of the sheet about to be drawn ('risklbl','riskval','riskmon','slrow','tprow','g1_fr','g1_lb','g1_lg','g2_fr',...); Remove() does ObjectDelete + Forget, so both caches miss on every frame - the panel's Text() cache because Exists(id) is false and the widget cache because Same() checks ObjectFind. That provably breaks the documented still-frame invariant at :2994-2998 and the assertions in SSR_T5_Ui.mq5:236 (spent <= 2 over 20 identical renders) and SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:5046 (cached_labels == 0). Downgraded from HIGH on impact: the user-visible consequence is paint cost, which is unvalidated, and the 561-writes = 39.05 ms arithmetic conflates a whole-repaint time with a per-write cost. No wrong value is shown and no state is corrupted - there is no moment where a stale or missing sheet is visible, because ChartRedraw only runs at the end of Render()

#### `ui-panel-5` — Two cache slots (12 and 17) write the same object 'setuprow' at the same coordinates, so order_why is visible for one frame only

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:1432`** · category: redraw

```mql5
         if(m_state.order_why != "")
            Text(17, "setuprow", x + 8, gy + 12, m_state.order_why,
                 SSR_C_STOP, SSR_FS_SMALL);
```

**What goes wrong:** With lines armed and an illegal geometry (order_why = e.g. 'drag the stop below the price'), the reason is drawn on the frame the string first changes and is overwritten by 'LONG setup'/'SHORT setup' on every subsequent frame - so at 10 fps it is on screen for ~100 ms and then gone for good. The user is told only 'cannot place' on the openln button and never why, which is the opposite of what the comment at :1424-1430 claims the slot split achieved.

**Why:** Both Text(12, "setuprow", x + 8, gy + 12, ...) at :1402 and Text(17, "setuprow", x + 8, gy + 12, ...) at :1432 target the SAME object id at the SAME x,y. Text() (:194-207) keys its early return on (slot, text, x, y, Exists(id)) and returns after only setting OBJPROP_COLOR. Because HideSheets() deletes 'setuprow' at the top of every DrawSheet (:1270, id listed at :1306), Text(12) always finds Exists==false and writes the side text; Text(17) then finds the object present with m_cache[17] == order_why and x,y unchanged, takes the early return, and leaves the side text in place. Only on the frame where order_why's value differs from m_cache[17] does the second call actually write.

**Verifiers:** Traced both calls. Text(12,"setuprow",x+8,gy+12,...) at :1402 and Text(17,"setuprow",x+8,gy+12,...) at :1432 write the SAME object id at the SAME coordinates; I grepped the slot numbers and 17 is used nowhere else, so m_cache[17] holds order_why persistently. Because HideSheets() deleted "setuprow" at :1270 (id listed at :1306), Text(12) can never take its early return (m_w.Exists is false) and always writes the LONG/SHORT side text; Text(17) then finds the object present with m_cache[17]==order_why and x,y unchanged, takes the early return at :196-200 and only sets OBJPROP_COLOR. So the reason survives exactly one frame - the frame its string value changes - and thereafter the side text stands in its place. An extra detail the finding does not mention: on those frames the early return paints the side text in SSR_C_STOP, so the row reads as a red "LONG setup" while the openln button says only "cannot place" / Provable by walking the cache. Text(12, "setuprow", x+8, gy+12, ...) at :1402 and Text(17, "setuprow", x+8, gy+12, ...) at :1432 address the same object id at the same coordinates. Text()'s early return (:195-201) keys on (m_cache[slot], m_cache_x, m_cache_y, m_w.Exists(id)) and returns after only ObjectSetInteger(OBJPROP_COLOR). HideSheets() Removes "setuprow" (:1306) at the top of every DrawSheet, so slot 12 always misses on Exists and writes the side text; slot 17 then finds the object present with m_cache[17] == order_why and the same x,y, takes the early return, and leaves "LONG setup"/"SHORT setup" on the chart tinted SSR_C_STOP. order_why is therefore on screen only on the single frame its value differs from m_cache[17] - about 100 ms at the host's paint gate - and the user is left with only T(SSR_S_CANNOT_PLACE) on the openln button / Provable, and the mechanism is exactly as stated. Both :1402 (slot 12) and :1432 (slot 17) write id 'setuprow' at the same (x+8, gy+12). HideSheets() deletes 'setuprow' at the top of every DrawSheet, so Text(12) always misses on Exists(id) and writes the side text, recreating the object; Text(17) then finds m_cache[17]==order_why with x,y unchanged AND Exists(id) now true, takes the early return at :196-200, and only sets OBJPROP_COLOR. The reason is therefore on screen for the single frame its value changes (~100 ms) and never again, including if the same reason recurs after order_why has been empty. Impact is a real loss of actionable feedback at the moment of placing a trade, which is why it stays MEDIUM rather than dropping to LOW - though it is partly mitigated: the surviving 'LONG setup'/'SHORT setup' text is painted in SSR_C_STOP by the early return, so the user does get a red signal plus 'cannot place' on the openln button, just not the reason

#### `ui-panel-7` — Position-row note column collides with the P/L column in the rail layout: 9 px of room for a 9-character note

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:1576`** · category: boundary

```mql5
            Text(104 + r, "pn" + t, x + 120, ry + 3, note,
                 (m_state.pos_no_stop[r] ? SSR_C_STOP : SSR_C_TEXT_FAINT),
                 SSR_FS_SMALL);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Real and understated: the collision is the normal state of the sheet, not the no-stop case only. The note is also set whenever !pos_pending[r] && pos_spread[r] > 0.0 (:1570-1573), i.e. for essentially every filled row, so "  sp 20.0" lands on the P/L figure on ordinary rows too. The precise glyph overlap is font-metric dependent; that 9 characters cannot fit 9 px is not.

**Why:** DrawSheet is called with w = W - 2*SSR_PAD - SSR_RAIL_W - SSR_GAP (:752-753) = 310-16-44-5 = 245. 'pl'+t is anchored at x + w - 116 (:1582, :1585) = x+129; 'pn'+t at x + 120 (:1576). Gap = 9 px. note is T(SSR_S_NO_STOP) = '  no stop' (SSR_Strings.mqh:391) or '  ' + T(SSR_S_SPREAD_SHORT) = '  sp %.1f' (SSR_Strings.mqh:495) - 9 characters either way, which cannot occupy 9 px at SSR_FS_SMALL (7 pt) under any font metric. Persian 'no.stop' is longer still (fa.txt:61).

**Verifiers:** Sheet width confirmed: Render() passes W - 2*SSR_PAD - SSR_RAIL_W - SSR_GAP = 310-16-44-5 = 245 (:752-754, SSR_Theme.mqh:487-488, 510, 514). Within SheetPositions "pn"+t is anchored at x+120 (:1576) and "pl"+t at x + w - 116 = x+129 (:1582, :1585) - a 9 px column. The note is T(SSR_S_NO_STOP) = "  no stop" (SSR_Strings.mqh:391) or "  " + "sp %.1f" (:495), 9 characters either way, which at SSR_FS_SMALL=7 pt occupies roughly 30 px. Even allowing ~8 px for the two leading spaces, "no stop" starts at about x+128 and overprints the signed money figure. Both are OBJ_LABELs with transparent backgrounds so z-order does not rescue it, and the comment at :1563-1574 does indeed do its arithmetic against a 295 px sheet that no longer exists. Persian is worse: spread.short = "اسپرد %.1f" (fa.txt:211) / Verified. DrawSheet is called with w = W - 2*SSR_PAD - SSR_RAIL_W - SSR_GAP = 310-16-44-5 = 245 (:752-753, SSR_Theme.mqh:487-488, 510, 514). "pn"+t is anchored at x+120 (:1576) and "pl"+t at x + w - 116 = x+129 (:1582, :1585), a 9 px column. T(SSR_S_NO_STOP) is "  no stop" and T(SSR_S_SPREAD_SHORT) is "sp %.1f" prefixed with two spaces (SSR_Strings.mqh:391, 495), 9 characters either way, which cannot occupy 9 px at SSR_FS_SMALL 7. Labels do not call Extent so CheckFrame cannot see it either. The comment at :1563-1574 does indeed do its arithmetic on a 295 px sheet that this layout no longer has / Confirmed. DrawSheet is called with w = 310 - 2*8 - 44 - 5 = 245 (:752-753), 'pn'+t is anchored at x+120 (:1576) and 'pl'+t at x+w-116 = x+129 (:1582/:1585) - nine pixels of column for a nine-character note ('  no stop' per SSR_Strings.mqh:391, or '  sp %.1f'), so the note is printed across the P/L figure. The comment at :1563-1574 does its arithmetic for a 295 px sheet, confirming the rail width was never applied. Severity stays MEDIUM and is if anything understated in reach: overlapping glyphs on the money column is not cosmetic - a trader reading a P/L figure with 'no stop' printed through it can misread the number - but it corrupts only the rendering, never the arithmetic, so it does not reach HIGH

#### `ui-panel-6` — Status strip draws the fidelity readout at x+330 in a 310 px panel - permanently outside the frame, on the candles

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:2033`** · category: boundary

```mql5
      Text(53, "stfid", x + 330, y + 4,
           SSRFidelityName(m_state.fidelity_effective) + (degraded ? " !" : ""),
           degraded ? SSR_C_HOLD : SSRFidelityColor(m_state.fidelity_effective),

```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct, with one qualification on where the text lands: the panel's x is clamped to cw - 310 when the chart is at least 310 px wide (:466-469), so on a wide chart the label sits on the candles to the right of the panel as claimed, but with the panel pushed to the right edge part or all of it falls outside the chart window and is simply not drawn. The ~90 px estimate is a font-metric guess. Permanent and visible, but cosmetic - MEDIUM rather than HIGH.

**Why:** The x offsets in DrawStatus (:2015-2036: x+8, x+116, x+218, x+268, x+330) carry no #ifdef SSR_LAYOUT_RAIL, unlike DrawClock (:956-965), DrawTransport (:990-1008), DrawSpeed (:1047-1056) and TabName (:1112-1140), all of which were given rail variants. x+330 fits the 420 px fallback (SSR_Theme.mqh:491) and not the 310 px rail. SSRFidelityName returns 'SYNTHETIC TICK' (SSR_Types.mqh:179). CheckFrame (:3019-3034) compares m_w.MaxRight() against x+W, and MaxRight only sees objects that call Extent() - Rect, Button/ButtonC and Edit (SSR_Widgets.mqh:199, 292, 363); Label does not (SSR_Widgets.mqh:226-253, and the comment at 125-128 says so), so this label is invisible to the instrument and to the LAYOUT OVERFLOW log line.

**Verifiers:** Verified directly: DrawStatus lays out at x+SSR_PAD, x+116, x+218, x+268 and x+330 (:2015, 2018, 2021, 2028, 2033) and I grepped the file's #ifdef SSR_LAYOUT_RAIL sites - they are at :747, :956, :990, :1047 and :1112 only, so DrawStatus has no rail variant while DrawClock, DrawTransport, DrawSpeed and TabName all got one. SSR_LAYOUT_RAIL is unconditionally #defined (SSR_Theme.mqh:484) giving SSR_PANEL_W = 310, so the "stfid" anchor is 20 px outside the frame before any glyph, and SSRFidelityName returns "SYNTHETIC TICK" (SSR_Types.mqh:179), 14-16 chars. It is shown, not hidden, on the resting path (Hide("stfid", false) at :1938). CheckFrame (:3019-3034) compares m_w.MaxRight() against x+W and MaxRight is fed only by Extent(), which Rect, ButtonC and Edit call and Label does not (SSR_Widgets.mqh:125-133, 199, 226-253, 292, 363) - so the instrument genuinely cannot see it. Severity reduced from HIGH to MEDIUM: it is a permanent, always-visible cosmetic fault with no loss of function or of a control / Provable. DrawStatus places "stfid" at x + 330 (:2033) inside a frame of W = SSR_PANEL_W = 310 (SSR_Theme.mqh:487) - 20 px past the right edge before any glyph - and SSRFidelityName returns "SYNTHETIC TICK" (SSR_Types.mqh:179), extending right from ANCHOR_LEFT_UPPER (SSR_Widgets.mqh:239). The other offsets in the same function (x+8, x+116, x+218, x+268) all fit 310, and unlike DrawClock (:956-965), DrawTransport (:1004-1016), DrawSpeed (:1047-1056) and TabName (:1114-1142) this function carries no #ifdef SSR_LAYOUT_RAIL variant, so the offset is a leftover from the 420 px fallback. CheckFrame (:3019-3034) compares m_w.MaxRight(), which only sized objects feed via Extent (SSR_Widgets.mqh:113-131, 199, 292, 363); Label does not, so the instrument is blind to it. It is reached on every frame that is not reset-armed, not showing a trade error or m_pro_why, and not TooNarrow / Provable and permanent: SSR_LAYOUT_RAIL is defined unconditionally (SSR_Theme.mqh:484) making SSR_PANEL_W 310, DrawStatus draws Text(53,'stfid', x+330, ...) with no rail variant while DrawClock/DrawTransport/DrawSpeed/TabName all got one, SSRFidelityName returns up to 'SYNTHETIC TICK !', and a Label never calls Extent() so CheckFrame and the LAYOUT OVERFLOW line cannot see it. Downgraded from HIGH to MEDIUM on impact: it is a mode readout painted outside the frame on the candles on every frame of every session - highly visible and it makes the product look broken - but no information is lost or falsified (the same fidelity is on the caption chip at :887-895, correctly placed), nothing becomes unclickable, and the neighbouring 'stspread' at x+268 still fits. If the panel is dragged to the right edge the label simply falls off the canvas and vanishes

#### `ui-dialogs-7` — The range dialog enables START for any date the broker can serve, but its only caller can only jump inside the already-loaded window

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:139`** · category: boundary

```mql5
   bool              CanStart(void)
     {
      return (m_req.IsComplete() && m_quote.IsFeasible() &&
              (m_problem == "" || StringFind(m_problem, "warning") == 0));
     }
```

**What goes wrong:** Press J, type a date three months before the replay window but well inside broker history, press START. Every check passes (the catalogue has that history), m_confirmed is set and the dialog closes - and then nothing at all happens on screen: the host only jumps when the date lies inside [g_group.StartMsc(), g_group.EndMsc()) and otherwise just prints 'that range needs a fresh session' to the Experts log. The dialog refuses what the BROKER cannot serve and accepts what the SESSION cannot serve, which is the opposite of what its only use needs; the title 'NEW SESSION' and the button 'START' reinforce the wrong expectation.

**Why:** g_dialog.Open() is reached only from RunHostCommand(SSR_CMD_JUMP) (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3096-3103), which seeds start=g_group.Now(), end=g_group.EndMsc(). The confirm handler is SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3181-3192: `if(r.start_msc >= g_group.StartMsc() && r.start_msc < g_group.EndMsc()) g_group.JumpTo(...) else Print(...)`. CanStart's validation comes from SSRValidateRange(m_req, m_cat) (:135), which measures against cat.EarliestStart/cat.LastMsc - the broker's M1 range - and knows nothing about the loaded window. Close() runs before the host's check (:313-318), so there is no surface left to report the refusal on.

**Verifiers:** Traced end to end and every link holds. RunHostCommand(SSR_CMD_JUMP) (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3093-3106) is the ONLY caller of g_dialog.Open(); it seeds r.start_msc = g_group.Now(), r.end_msc = g_group.EndMsc(). Because start_msc > 0 the catalogue-default branch in Open() (SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:105-110) is skipped, so the user's typed start is validated only by SSRValidateRange (SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:135), which measures against cat.EarliestStart(warm) and cat.LastMsc() (SSR_SessionRange.mqh:82-87) - the BROKER's M1 range, with no knowledge of the loaded replay window. CanStart() (:139-143) therefore returns true for any broker-servable date, 'start' sets m_confirmed and calls Close() (:313-318), and only then does RouteEvent check `if(r.start_msc >= g_group.StartMsc() && r.start_msc < g_group.EndMsc())` else Print(...) (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3181-3192). Close() has already destroyed every object (:115-122), so no surface remains to report the refusal; the user gets a closed dialog and an Experts-log line. Title is literally "NEW SESSION" and the button "START" (SSR_Strings.mqh:517-518), reinforcing the wrong expectation. No guard, clamp or earlier check anywhere narrows the accepted set to the loaded window / Verified end to end. CSSRRangeDialog::CanStart (SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:139-143) gates only on m_req.IsComplete(), m_quote.IsFeasible() and m_problem, and m_problem comes from SSRValidateRange (SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:135), which measures exclusively against cat.EarliestStart(warm) and cat.LastMsc() (SSR_SessionRange.mqh:78-92) - the broker's M1 catalogue, never the loaded window. The only caller is RunHostCommand(SSR_CMD_JUMP) (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3095-3106) and the only consumer of the confirmed request is SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3181-3192, which jumps only when r.start_msc is in [g_group.StartMsc(), g_group.EndMsc()) and otherwise only Prints. OnEvent calls Close() (SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:313-318) before the host sees the request, and Close() does m_w.RemoveAll() + ChartRedraw, so every object that could carry a refusal is already gone. No guard, no alternative surface: a broker-servable but session-external date is accepted, closes the dialog, and produces nothing visible / Verified end to end. SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:135 validates only against the catalogue (SSR_SessionRange.mqh:76-90 measures cat.EarliestStart/cat.LastMsc), and CanStart (:139-143) therefore enables START for any broker-servable instant. The sole caller is RunHostCommand(SSR_CMD_JUMP) (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3095-3107) and the confirm handler (:3181-3192) acts only when r.start_msc is inside [g_group.StartMsc(), g_group.EndMsc()); otherwise it Prints to the Experts log. Close() at :316 has already removed every dialog object, so no surface remains for feedback. Reachability is high rather than incidental: the dialog itself prints the BROKER's full range on the 'avail' label (:175-180) as the guide the user types against, so an out-of-window date is the natural thing to enter, and the title 'NEW SESSION' plus the button 'START' promise a reload the host cannot perform. Observable result is a primary command (J) that silently does nothing. No money arithmetic, no future-data exposure, no weakening of the virtual-trading guarantee, so MEDIUM is the ceiling - it is a silent no-op on a reachable path, not corruption

#### `ui-dialogs-5` — The range dialog draws an unbounded validator message into one label, so the 'start too early' refusal loses the date it is about

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:233`** · category: string-length

```mql5
      color pc = (StringFind(m_problem, "warning") == 0) ? SSR_C_HOLD : SSR_C_STOP;
      m_w.Label("problem", x + SSR_PAD, cy, m_problem, pc, SSR_FS_SMALL);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate, with two corrections: the literal part is 49 characters (not 48), giving 77 for PERIOD_D1 and 78 for PERIOD_M15; and the cut is not the only display problem - the dialog is SSR_DLG_W = 248 px wide (SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:25) and the label is drawn at x+SSR_PAD with no width budget, so even a 63-character string overruns the dialog box.

**Why:** The message is built at MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Data/SSR_SessionRange.mqh:84-85 as `StringFormat("start too early - %s context needs history back to %s", EnumToString(r.max_tf), SSRFormatMsc(earliest))`; the literal part is 48 characters, EnumToString of the four offered timeframes is 9-10, and SSRFormatMsc returns TIME_DATE|TIME_MINUTES|TIME_SECONDS = 19 characters (SSR_Time.mqh:62-67). Minimum total 76. The dialog passes it straight to m_w.Label with no length guard, unlike SSRReviewLine which truncates to 60 (SSR_Review.mqh:91-93). A14 cannot measure it because the argument is a variable (tools/ssr_audit.py:944-947).

**Verifiers:** Length arithmetic verified from source. SSR_SessionRange.mqh:84-85 builds StringFormat("start too early - %s context needs history back to %s", EnumToString(r.max_tf), SSRFormatMsc(earliest)). Literal part = 18 + 31 = 49 chars, EnumToString(PERIOD_D1) = 9, SSRFormatMsc returns TimeToString(TIME_DATE|TIME_MINUTES|TIME_SECONDS) = 19 chars (SSR_Time.mqh:61-67), total 77 (78 with PERIOD_M15). The value reaches OBJPROP_TEXT completely unguarded: CSSRRangeDialog::Render passes m_problem straight to m_w.Label (SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:234) and CSSRWidgets::Label (SSR_Widgets.mqh:226-252) performs no Clip/StringSubstr - it writes ObjectSetString(OBJPROP_TEXT, text) verbatim. Cut at 63 the drawn string ends "...history back to 2019." exactly as claimed (49+9 = 58 chars of prose, then 5 date chars). Note the same label also carries "not enough history: " + q.ToString() (SSR_SessionRange.mqh:92), which is a second uncapped path into the same 63-char label. Contrast is real: the panel does Clip(...,62) elsewhere (SSR_Panel.mqh:1890) but this dialog does not / Provable from source with the 63-char OBJPROP_TEXT ground truth. SSR_SessionRange.mqh:81-83 builds StringFormat("start too early - %s context needs history back to %s", EnumToString(r.max_tf), SSRFormatMsc(earliest)); the literal minus the two %s is 49 characters, EnumToString of the four offered timeframes (SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:47-57) is 9-10 ("PERIOD_D1"/"PERIOD_M15"), and SSRFormatMsc uses TIME_DATE|TIME_MINUTES|TIME_SECONDS (SSR_Time.mqh:62-67) = 19 characters. So 77 (D1) to 78 (M15) characters go into one OBJ_LABEL. CSSRWidgets::Label (SSR_Widgets.mqh:226-252) writes OBJPROP_TEXT verbatim with no Clip/StringSubstr guard, unlike the panel's own Clip(...,62) usage (SSR_Panel.mqh:1893). The truncation falls inside the timestamp, so the only actionable part of the refusal is the part that is cut / The arithmetic holds: SSR_SessionRange.mqh:81-83 builds 'start too early - ' (18) + EnumToString(max_tf) (9-10 for the four offered TFs) + ' context needs history back to ' (31) + SSRFormatMsc (19) = 77-78 chars, and CSSRWidgets::Label (SSR_Widgets.mqh:226-253) writes OBJPROP_TEXT verbatim with no Clip, unlike DrawSession which does Clip(...,62) at SSR_Panel.mqh:1893. So the sentence is cut at 63 and ends '...history back to 2019.'. Downgraded on impact: the state is reachable (picking D1 context on a shallow-history broker) but the user is not left without information - START is visibly disabled, the truncated text still says 'start too early' and names the year, and the dialog draws the broker's real first..last instants on the 'avail' label two rows above (:175-180). The loss is the month/day/time of the exact bound, i.e. a degraded message on a refusal path, with nothing corrupted and nothing leaked

#### `ui-dialogs-3` — Recompute() erases the two messages the range dialog sets just before calling it, so a bad date and Load-more's result are never shown

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:268`** · category: event-handling

```mql5
            string txt = ObjectGetString(m_chart, sparam, OBJPROP_TEXT);
            datetime t = StringToTime(txt);
            //--- a typo must not silently become 1970
            if(t > 0)
               m_req.start_msc = SSRToMsc(t);
            else
               m_problem = "could not read that date - use YYYY.MM.DD HH:MM";
            Recompute();
```

**What goes wrong:** Press J, type "march 5" into the start box and press Enter. StringToTime returns 0, the refusal text is assigned to m_problem, and then Recompute() - on the very next line - does `m_problem = ""` and overwrites it with SSRValidateRange's verdict on the UNCHANGED old date, which is usually empty. The user sees no error at all, the field keeps the text they typed, and START stays enabled against the previous start instant - exactly the 'a typo must not silently become 1970' failure the comment claims to prevent. The identical pattern makes LOAD MORE mute: `m_problem = "gained %d more bars" / "the broker has nothing older"` (:304-306) is discarded by the Recompute() on :307.

**Why:** Recompute() begins with `m_problem = "";` (:127) and ends with `m_problem = SSRValidateRange(m_req, m_cat);` (:135), so any value assigned before the call is dead. Render() draws only m_problem into the "problem" label (:234). Both call sites assign m_problem and then call Recompute() immediately (:274-275 and :304-307). There is no other channel - the dialog has no toast and prints nothing to the log.

**Verifiers:** Both call sites are dead-store patterns, provable by ordering alone. Recompute() opens with `m_problem = "";` (SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:127) and closes with `m_problem = SSRValidateRange(m_req, m_cat);` (:135) - the only early return is the m_cat == NULL branch (:129-133), and in both call sites m_cat is non-NULL (the 'more' branch is itself inside `if(m_cat != NULL)`). ENDEDIT path: `m_problem = "could not read that date..."` (:274) then Recompute() (:275) overwrites it, and because m_req.start_msc was NOT updated the verdict is recomputed on the unchanged previous instant, so CanStart() (:141) still returns true. The typed text also survives: Render() sets OBJPROP_TEXT on the edit box only inside `if(ObjectFind(...) < 0)` (:186-196), so the field keeps "march 5" while the engine holds the old date. LOAD MORE path: m_problem = "gained %d more bars" / "the broker has nothing older" (:304-306) is discarded by Recompute() on :307. Render() draws only m_problem into the sole 'problem' label (:233-234); there is no toast and no Print in this file, so both messages are unreachable by any channel. Events do arrive here - the dialog is created on g_panel_chart = ChartID(), the EA's own chart (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1463-1465), so ENDEDIT is genuinely delivered / Straight dead-store, provable from source. Recompute() opens with m_problem = "" (SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:127) and ends by reassigning m_problem = SSRValidateRange(...) (:135). Both writers assign m_problem and then call Recompute() on the next statement: the ENDEDIT branch at :271-275 ('could not read that date...' then Recompute()) and the LOAD MORE branch at :303-307 ('gained %d more bars'/'the broker has nothing older' then Recompute()). Render() draws only m_problem into the single 'problem' label (:233-234), and the dialog has no other channel (no Print, no toast). The typo also survives on screen because Render() sets OBJPROP_TEXT on the OBJ_EDIT only inside the ObjectFind(...) < 0 creation branch (:186-196), so the unparsed text stays in the box while m_req.start_msc keeps the previous instant and CanStart() stays true - exactly the silent-failure the :270 comment claims to prevent / Provable dead-store on both paths. Recompute() opens with m_problem = "" (:127) and closes by overwriting it with SSRValidateRange (:135), and both callers assign m_problem immediately before calling it: the ENDEDIT typo message (:274 then :275) and LOAD MORE's result (:304-306 then :307). Render() draws only m_problem into the 'problem' label (:234) and there is no other channel - no toast, no Print. The typo case is worse than a lost message because the OBJ_EDIT text is set only inside the creation branch (:186-196), so Render() never rewrites it: the box keeps 'march 5' while m_req.start_msc silently still holds the previous instant, START stays enabled, and pressing it commits a date the user never asked for. That is silent substitution of user input on a reachable path, which is exactly what the comment at :270 claims to prevent. Kept at MEDIUM and not raised: the substituted value is the previous, already-valid start, so no money arithmetic is corrupted and no future data is exposed. The LOAD MORE half is partially self-mitigating since the 'avail' label re-renders with the new FirstMsc

#### `ui-dialogs-2` — An open dropdown survives a step change and is redrawn on top of the START button

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:1061`** · category: z-order

```mql5
      if(m_w.Pressed("next"))
        {
         ReadAll();
         m_step++;
         Repaint();
         return "";
        }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: An open dropdown survives Repaint() and every step change (m_menu is never cleared by a step handler), so Render() repaints the preset list over the MODE and START steps, overlapping back/go at m_y+316. Which object consumes a click in that overlap is the one part not settleable from source: the menu buttons and the 'go' button both carry OBJPROP_ZORDER 100 from CSSRWidgets::Common (SSR_Widgets.mqh:164), and MQL5's tie-break between equal-ZORDER objects is runtime behaviour. If the later-created menu item wins, the press applies a preset and the replay does not start; if 'go' wins, the replay starts with a stray list on screen. Either way the stray menu itself is a confirmed paint defect.

**Why:** m_menu is only ever cleared in DrawMenu when the option list is empty (:625), after an option is chosen (:1092) and by the toggling field handlers (:1100-1103). Neither Repaint() (:656-662) nor the step handlers reset it, and Render() unconditionally calls DrawMenu() last (:673 'last, because last is on top'). m_menu_y is written only inside Row() (:221-222), which only runs from RenderSettings, so it keeps the step-1 value. Geometry: Row("pre") is r=10 -> ry = m_y+270 -> fy = m_y+291 (:629), 1+4 default presets x 20 px = 100; RenderStart puts back/go at gy = by+42 = m_y+316 (:930-941). Poll checks the menu items (:1084-1096) and there is no 'go' press to find because the top-most object under the cursor is m1/m2.

**Verifiers:** Traced and reproducible from source. m_menu is cleared only in DrawMenu when the option list is empty (:625), after a choice (:1092) and by the four field toggles (:1100-1103); for "pre" MenuOptions always returns at least 1 (slot 0 "My last"), so it never self-clears. Neither Repaint (:656-662) nor the next/back handlers (:1061-1074) reset m_menu or m_menu_y, m_menu_y is written only by Row() (:221-222) which runs from RenderSettings alone, and Render() calls DrawMenu last on every step (:673). Poll checks 'next' BEFORE the menu block, so a Next press while a list is open is honoured and the list is re-created on the new step. Geometry confirms the harmful overlap on step 3: menu items at x m_x+208..292, y m_y+291..391 (fy = m_menu_y + 20 + 1 with m_menu_y = m_y+270) versus go at x m_x+92..292, y m_y+316..342 (:939-943). DrawMenu runs after RenderStart, so the items are the later-created objects and win the click; Pressed("go") at :1076 is false and the menu block at :1084-1096 then applies a preset instead of starting. Minor nuance: on the MODE step the list falls just below the 288 px frame rather than over its buttons, which is cosmetic; the START-step capture is the real defect / The stale-menu leak is provable. m_menu is assigned at only four sites (:625 empty-option list, :1092 after a choice, :1100-1103 the field toggles); neither Repaint() (:656-662) nor the 'next'/'back'/'md*' handlers clear it, and Render() calls DrawMenu() unconditionally as the last act of every paint (:673). m_menu_y is written only in Row() (:221-222), which runs only from RenderSettings, so it keeps the step-1 value across steps. Geometry checks out: Row("pre") is r = 10, ry = m_y+270, fy = m_y+291 (:629), five entries (1 "My last" + 4 built-ins) x 20 px = m_y+291..391, while RenderStart's back/go sit at gy = by+42 = m_y+316 (:939-941) and RenderMode's frame ends at m_y+288 - so the list is painted over the START row and, on step 2, floating below the panel. Poll does check those items on the later steps (the loop runs whenever m_menu != "", :1084-1096, and MenuOptions("pre") still returns five), so a press that lands on one applies a preset and returns "" instead of starting / I could not refute this and the geometry checks out. m_menu is cleared only in DrawMenu when the option list is empty (:625), after Choose (:1092) and by the field toggles (:1100-1103); neither Repaint() nor the step handlers touch it, and Render() calls DrawMenu() last on every paint (:673). m_menu_y is written only in Row() (:221-222), which runs only from RenderSettings, so it keeps the step-1 value. Row("pre") is r=10 -> ry = m_y+270 -> fy = m_y+291, five default options x 20 px -> items occupy y 291..391 at x m_x+208..292; RenderStart's go spans y 316..342 at x m_x+92..292, so m1 and m2 sit squarely on top of START REPLAY HERE and are created after it. Reachability is the part that makes this MEDIUM rather than LOW: the ONLY way to close a list is to press the same field again (there is no click-outside dismissal, because the panel receives object clicks only), so a user who opens the Preset list to see what is in it and then presses Next leaves it open by default. The observable is bad in a specific way: the click aimed at the irreversible button is consumed by Poll's menu block (:1083-1096) and silently APPLIES A PRESET, rewriting prop_on and the three evaluation numbers (ApplyPreset, :440-450), so the replay does not start and the evaluation rules change under the user. The recap does redraw with the new numbers, which is the only thing keeping this out of HIGH

#### `qa-smoke-2` — Preflight's only tick check counts acceptance and never asks whether a bar was built - the exact signal the project measured as meaningless

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/QA/SSR_QA_Preflight.mq5:397`** · category: zero-empty

```mql5
         int nadd = CustomTicksAdd(test, add);
         if(nadd == ArraySize(add))
            Ok("custom ticks", StringFormat("%d accepted", nadd));
         else
            Limit("custom ticks", ...
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate as a coverage gap: the preflight's only tick assertion is the acceptance count, with no chart-mode set and no post-tick bar read, so the one failure mode the project describes as its longest-lived defect passes the gate. Note the section still proves the bar path independently (500-bar CustomRatesUpdate + CopyRates read-back + M5 derivation at lines 320-379), so the run is not wholly blind - only the tick-to-bar path is.

**Why:** The check reads the return count and nothing else; there is no CopyRates or Bars read after the CustomTicksAdd anywhere in section 4 (lines 383-420), so 'the terminal took every tick and built nothing from them' is indistinguishable from success. The project has measured that exact case: SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:463-467 quotes '60 calls offered ticks, the terminal took 481, refused 0, and the M1 series stayed at 139 bars', and the overview names it as lesson 6. The product itself guards it - CSSRCustomSymbolManager.mqh:383 writes CustomSymbolSetInteger(m_symbol, SYMBOL_CHART_MODE, SYMBOL_CHART_MODE_BID) and then reads it back to check it took - and SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:473-481 asserts it on the replay symbol. The preflight, which runs first and gates everything, asserts neither the chart mode nor a resulting bar.

**Verifiers:** Traced the whole of section 4 (lines 263-438). The tick block at 383-402 writes 100 ticks flagged TICK_FLAG_BID|TICK_FLAG_ASK and judges the result solely on `nadd == ArraySize(add)`. There is no CopyRates, Bars(), SeriesInfoInteger or CopyTicks read after CustomTicksAdd anywhere in the section, and no CustomSymbolSetInteger(test, SYMBOL_CHART_MODE, ...) anywhere in the file (grep confirms the only chart-mode writes in the repo are SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:383 and the assertion at SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:473-481). So on a CHART_MODE_LAST origin the clone keeps LAST, all 100 ticks are accepted, and the preflight prints 'OK custom ticks 100 accepted'. The documented failure (SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:463-467: 'the terminal took 481, refused 0, and the M1 series stayed at 139 bars') is exactly what this check cannot distinguish from success. The omission is provable; the severity is bounded by the fact that the product guards it and the smoke test asserts it, so a user is not shipped a broken engine by this alone - the preflight just fails to fail early / Verified in full: section 4 sets only SYMBOL_TRADE_MODE (line 312) - there is no CustomSymbolSetInteger(SYMBOL_CHART_MODE, ...) anywhere in SSR_QA_Preflight.mq5 - and after `int nadd = CustomTicksAdd(test, add);` the only test is `nadd == ArraySize(add)`; no CopyRates, Bars() or SeriesInfoInteger call follows the tick add before the cleanup block. The ticks carry flags TICK_FLAG_BID|TICK_FLAG_ASK only. The semantics that make acceptance meaningless are established (CustomTicksAdd accepts ticks that build no bar; bars need CHART_MODE_BID for BID/ASK ticks) and the project has its own recorded measurement of exactly that case plus the assertion the preflight is missing (SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:452-481, CSSRCustomSymbolManager.mqh sets and reads back CHART_MODE). The missing assertion is a source-provable defect in the gating QA script / The coverage gap is provable: :397-403 inspects only the CustomTicksAdd return count, and there is no CopyRates/Bars read after it anywhere in section 4, nor any SYMBOL_CHART_MODE assertion on the test symbol. But the impact claim is wrong. The scenario described - replay advances its clock, emits ticks and produces no candle - is not what a GO from this script licenses, because the product does not inherit the origin's chart mode: CSSRCustomSymbolManager (SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:383) forces SYMBOL_CHART_MODE_BID on the replay symbol and reads it back at :387-395, printing an explicit named diagnostic if the terminal refused, and SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:473-481 asserts it on the live replay symbol before every downstream tick check. The preflight's blind spot therefore costs a user a later, noisier diagnosis - not a silent no-candle replay. This is a test-coverage improvement in a diagnostic script, not a defect that reaches money arithmetic, future data or the virtual-trading guarantee. HIGH is not supportable

#### `qa-smoke-1` — The smoke harness aborts the entire run on an unprimed M1 series, and the priming call is the very next line

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/QA/SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:396`** · category: boundary

```mql5
   int have = Bars(origin, PERIOD_M1);
   if(!Check("M1 history present", have >= InpReplayBars + InpWarmupBars,
             StringFormat("%d bars local, %d needed",
                          have, InpReplayBars + InpWarmupBars)))
     {
      ... Done(); return;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate, but two qualifications on blast radius: the abort only occurs where the origin's M1 series is untouched in that terminal session (a script dropped on an M1 chart of the same symbol, or any prior M1 read, primes it), and the damage is confined to the QA harness - it reports a false '0 bars local, 600 needed' failure and stops, it does not affect the product. Severity MEDIUM rather than HIGH for that reason.

**Why:** This is the project's own documented trap (overview lesson 7: 'MetaTrader builds a timeframe series lazily - the first read always returns zero, and zero there means "no series yet", not "no bars". This cost an entire build.'). tools/ssr_audit.py's A21 exempts PERIOD_M1 from its priming rule, but its stated reason is 'M1 is exempt because M1 is what this program WRITES - the writes build that series themselves' - which is true of the replay symbol and NOT true of `origin`, the broker symbol, which this program only reads. The priming read exists nine lines later (line 409, CopyRates(origin, PERIOD_M1, 0, InpReplayBars, back)) but is unreachable because the Check aborts first. SSR_QA_Preflight.mq5:191-201 handles exactly this case for the same symbol with an ask-twice loop and a 15-second wait, so the two harnesses disagree about a known hazard.

**Verifiers:** Traced from OnStart entry: nothing between LogOpen (line 316) and line 396 touches the origin's M1 series - the sweep only walks charts and SymbolsTotal - so `Bars(origin, PERIOD_M1)` at 396 is genuinely the first read of that timeseries, and lazy-build semantics make it 0 on an untouched timeframe. The Check at 397 then runs Done() and returns at 402-403, before the CopyRates at 409 that would have primed it. The cited evidence holds exactly: tools/ssr_audit.py audit_a21 skips `tf == "PERIOD_M1"` with the stated reason "M1 is exempt because M1 is what this program WRITES" (comment at ~1577-1580), which is true of rsym and false of origin; and SSR_QA_Preflight.mq5:190-201 handles the same symbol/timeframe with an ask-twice CopyRates loop and a 15 s wait. No guard, clamp or retry exists on this path / Verified line by line. Line 396 `int have = Bars(origin, PERIOD_M1);` is the first thing after the sweep, and nothing before it touches origin's M1 series (the sweep only calls ChartFirst/ChartSymbol/ChartClose/SymbolsTotal/CustomSymbolDelete). A short count runs Done() and returns (400-405), so the priming read at 409 `CopyRates(origin, PERIOD_M1, 0, InpReplayBars, back)` is unreachable. The lazy-series semantics are established ground truth and the repo's own A21 rule (tools/ssr_audit.py:1553-1580) exempts PERIOD_M1 only on the grounds that 'M1 is what this program WRITES' - which is true of the replay symbol and false for `origin`, a symbol this harness only reads. SSR_QA_Preflight.mq5:190-201 handles the same read with an ask-twice + 15 s wait, so the two harnesses genuinely disagree. Severity lowered from HIGH: the damage is a false FAIL and lost coverage in a QA script, not in shipped product, and the abort prints a remedy line / Provable from source and consistent with the project's established lazy-series behaviour: l.396 reads Bars(origin, PERIOD_M1) with nothing having touched that series, and a zero aborts the entire suite via Done()/return at l.400-405 - while the priming CopyRates sits nine lines later at l.409 and the sibling harness (SSR_QA_Preflight.mq5:187-201) retries the same symbol's M1 for up to 15 seconds. The A21 exemption's stated rationale (M1 is what this program writes) does not cover `origin`, which is only read. Downgraded from HIGH: nothing in the product, the books or the tick stream is affected - the cost is one wasted run and one misleading '0 passed, 1 FAILED' line, the failure detail already prints the correct remedy ('run it once, or press Home on an M1 chart'), and the run passes on the next attempt because the series stays built for the terminal session. A false-negative gate on a diagnostic harness is a MEDIUM, not a HIGH

#### `spikes-audits-4` — A3 has no positive control: on M5..D1 an unbuilt series gives the same answer as 'no future data'

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_A3_FutureIsolation.mq5:48`** · category: future-data-leakage

```mql5
int got = CopyRates(sym, tf, T + 1, far, r);
checks++;
if(got > 0)
  {
   leaks++;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The missing positive control is real and the zero-is-indistinguishable-from-pass argument holds, but 'a terminal that never builds the M5..D1 series' overstates it: path 1's CopyRates(sym, tf, T+1, far, r) is itself a request against that timeframe and will cause the terminal to start building the series, so from the second cut onward the higher timeframes are likely populated. The sound form of the finding is that an unknown subset of the 2800 assertions - at minimum the first pass over each of the six timeframes A3 never primes, plus every pass where the higher-TF series has not yet caught up with the rewrite at line 139 (only M1 is waited on, at line 141) - is vacuously true, and A3 has no assertion that would notice.

**Why:** Path 2 is iTime(sym,tf,0) > T, path 3 is Bars(sym,tf,T+1,far) > 0, path 4 is (datetime)lastbar > T - each of which is false when the value is 0. The lazy-series behaviour that makes 0 ambiguous is the documented reason audit A21 exists (tools/ssr_audit.py:1556-1583) and is handled explicitly by A2 (SSR_A2_RatesAndAggregation.mq5:208-209, 'an unasked-for series answers zero, and this metric is the whole point of the experiment') and by D1 (SSR_D1_SeedPerformance.mq5:117-120, 'TOUCH EACH SERIES BEFORE COUNTING IT'). A3 primes only PERIOD_M1 (line 141) and never touches the other six with a non-future read.

**Verifiers:** Traced AuditCut (lines 35-93) and OnStart (96-169) in full. All four paths only ever increment leaks on a positive finding: got>0, it>T, nb>0, (datetime)lastbar>T. Every one of those is false when the underlying value is 0, and nothing in the file asserts that bars BELOW T exist on any timeframe - there is no Bars()/CopyRates check against the seeded range, and the only wait is SSR_WaitSeries on PERIOD_M1 at line 141. The rest of the suite treats this ambiguity as load-bearing and handles it explicitly (A2:204-209 'an unasked-for series answers zero, and this metric is the whole point of the experiment'; D1:113-120 'TOUCH EACH SERIES BEFORE COUNTING IT'), which makes A3's omission a genuine gap in the suite's strongest verdict rather than a difference of style / AuditCut (lines 35-93) is fully read: all four paths only ever call SSR_Verdict inside an `if(<found something past T>)` branch, and nothing in the file asserts that bars BELOW T exist on any timeframe - the only priming is SSR_WaitSeries(InpTest, PERIOD_M1, 10000) at line 141, M1 only. `got > 0`, `it > T`, `nb > 0` and `(datetime)lastbar > T` are all false when the value is 0, and an all-zero answer increments checks (2800 of them) while leaving total_leaks at 0, which line 163 publishes as zero_future_leaks PASS. With the lazy-series behaviour established, the absence of a positive control means a clean PASS is indistinguishable from a terminal that built no series - and the author already handles this explicitly in A2 (:203-209) and D1 (:112-120). The missing control is structural and provable; only the magnitude is runtime, so MEDIUM rather than the claimed HIGH for a developer spike / The missing positive control is provable: AuditCut (:35-93) records a verdict only on the leak branches, every path is false when the value is 0 (got>0, it>T, nb>0, lastbar>T), and OnStart primes only PERIOD_M1 (:141) - M5..D1 are never touched with a below-T read. The project applies the opposite discipline everywhere else (A2:205-209, D1:113-120, and T3:376-382 which asserts M1/M5/H1 bars > 0 immediately before its own 'no future data in any timeframe' check). Two things cap the impact. First, the product-level guarantee is tested with a positive control: SSR_T3_CustomSymbol.mq5:386-396 is the one that runs against the real engine, and it checks presence before absence. A3 is a Phase-0 spike. Second, the vacuity is not as total as claimed: paths 1-3 themselves request each timeframe, so from the first cut onward the higher series are instantiated as a side effect and iTime/Bars begin returning real values - a terminal that answers 0 on all seven for all 100 cuts is possible but not the default case. Nothing here leaks future data or weakens the runtime guarantee; it weakens the evidence for it in one spike. HIGH is not supportable

#### `spikes-audits-5` — A3's avg_rebuild_time times the SERIES_SYNCHRONIZED flag, then declares it the Reset budget

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_A3_FutureIsolation.mq5:141`** · category: timer

```mql5
SSR_WaitSeries(InpTest, PERIOD_M1, 10000);
t_reset_sum += SSR_ElapsedMs(t0);
...
SSR_Metric("audit", "avg_rebuild_time",  t_reset_sum / MathMax(InpCuts, 1), "ms",
           "cost of a full history rebuild - feeds the Reset budget");
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct on the mechanism. Two refinements: on a 10 s timeout SSR_WaitSeries returns -1.0, which A3 sums as -1 ms rather than flagging the sample as unmeasured, so a terminal where the flag is never set produces an artificially LOW average rather than a high one; and the magnitude of the inflation depends entirely on which of the flag's three observed behaviours the terminal shows.

**Why:** SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:390-404 states the flag 'takes about nine seconds after a custom-symbol rewrite - for 10,000 bars and for 100,000 bars alike. Size-independent, so it is a timer, not work', that CopyRates returns the fresh bars in tens of milliseconds, and that D1 'called the same operation twenty times slower' for exactly this reason. The kit supplies SSR_WaitLastBarBefore for the tail-delete case and SSR_WaitLastBar for the re-seed case; A3 uses neither. Secondary effect: 100 cuts x up to 10 s of flag wait makes A3's own runtime up to ~17 minutes.

**Verifiers:** Traced lines 131-142 and 160-161. t0 opens before CustomRatesDelete, and t_reset_sum accumulates through CustomRatesUpdate and SSR_WaitSeries(InpTest, PERIOD_M1, 10000) - and SSR_WaitSeries (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:338-349) polls nothing but SERIES_SYNCHRONIZED, returning -1.0 on timeout, which A3 adds into the sum as a negative rather than recording it as unmeasured. That total is divided by InpCuts and published as avg_rebuild_time with the note 'cost of a full history rebuild - feeds the Reset budget'. Timing a flag and labelling the result a product budget is provable from source; the specific 9,000-10,000 ms figure is runtime-dependent and I cannot confirm it / The timed region is exactly as described: t0 at line 131, then CustomRatesDelete + CustomTicksDelete + ArrayResize/copy + CustomRatesUpdate + SymbolSelect + SSR_WaitSeries(...,10000), with t_reset_sum += SSR_ElapsedMs(t0) at line 142 and the sum divided out at line 160 under the note 'cost of a full history rebuild - feeds the Reset budget'. SSR_WaitSeries (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:338-349) polls SERIES_SYNCHRONIZED and nothing else, and the kit's own header at 390-417 says that flag is size-independent - 'a timer, not work' - observed at 0.002 ms, 9,200 ms and never, and supplies SSR_WaitLastBar / SSR_WaitLastBarBefore for precisely this case, neither of which A3 uses. It reaches no user, no money arithmetic, no isolation guarantee, and the budget it 'feeds' is a design note, not shipped behaviour. #### `spikes-audits-2` — B1's wick_expanded_to_extremes verdict fails deterministically no matter how MetaTrader behaves

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_B1_TicksAddBroadcast.mq5:139`** · category: boundary

```mql5
if(h0 >= hi - point && l0 <= lo + point)
  { hl_expanded++; break; }
...
SSR_Verdict("wick_expanded_to_extremes", hl_expanded == nbars,
            IntegerToString(nbars), IntegerToString(hl_expanded),
            "high/low grow tick by tick");
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The verdict does fail deterministically with the defaults, but by one bar, not seven. Replaying the exact generators gives hl_expanded = 19/20 for digits 1-5 (not 13/20, and not digits-invariant in the way claimed): the shortfall is 0.0526 x the extreme-to-close leg, and NormalizeDouble's rounding onto the point grid means a wick must exceed ~28.5 points (not 19) to break the one-point tolerance. With this seed exactly one bar (index 17, 39-point up wick, 2.05-point shortfall) does, so hl_expanded == nbars can never hold and the blocker records SPIKE FAIL regardless of terminal behaviour.

**Why:** The chart high can never exceed the highest injected bid. Replaying SSR_Rng/SSR_GenM1 (seed 4242, base 38000, vol 25) and SSR_BarToTicks with cnt=20 for the 20 generated bars gives an achievable high/low within one point on exactly 13 of 20 bars, identically for digits=1, 2 and 5 (the shortfall scales with point, so the symbol does not matter). With cnt=8 it is 3 of 20. The tolerance at line 139 is one point while the model's shortfall is 0.0526 x wick, and SSR_GenM1 draws wicks of |N(0,1)|*15 points, so a wick above 19 points already breaks the tolerance.

**Verifiers:** The mechanism is real but the arithmetic in the evidence is wrong, so I re-derived it. SSR_BarToTicks samples a 3-segment path at u = 3i/(cnt-1) (SpikeKit:579), which for cnt=20 never lands on u=1 or u=2, so the highest injected bid falls short of bar.high by min(0.0526*(H-C), 0.105*(H-L)) and the lowest overshoots bar.low symmetrically. The bar the chart builds cannot exceed the highest injected bid, so the line-139 tolerance of one point is what decides the verdict. I replayed SSR_Rng/SSR_GenM1 (xorshift64, seed 4242, base 38000, vol 25) plus SSR_BarToTicks(cnt=20) exactly: because NormalizeDouble quantises the shortfall onto the point grid, the effective tolerance is ~1.5 points, so 19 of 20 bars pass and bar index 17 (up wick 39 points -> 2.05-point shortfall) fails deterministically for digits 1..5. hl_expanded = 19 != nbars = 20, so SSR_Verdict("wick_expanded_to_extremes", ...) FAILS on a terminal whose broadcast is perfect, and SSR_End then prints SPIKE FAIL for a Tier-B blocker. Conclusion survives; magnitude does not / The mechanism is provable from source and I reproduced the generator independently. In SSR_BarToTicks the path parameter is u = i*3/(cnt-1); with the default cnt=20, (cnt-1)=19 is not divisible by 3, so u never equals exactly 1 or 2 and the two extreme knots k1/k2 are NEVER injected - the closest tick falls short by 5.26% of (extreme - adjacent knot), i.e. 0.0526 x wick, against a tolerance of one point at :139. SSR_GenM1 draws wicks of |N(0,1)|*15 points, so a wick past ~28 points (with NormalizeDouble rounding in the script's favour) breaks the tolerance; with seed 4242 bar 17 has a 38.5-point upper wick (shortfall ~2.0 points) and fails robustly, so hl_expanded < nbars and 'wick_expanded_to_extremes' FAILs on a terminal whose broadcast is perfect. The finder's arithmetic, however, does not reproduce: I get 19/20 at digits 5 and 2 and 15/20 at digits 1, not 13/20, and 'a wick above 19 points' ignores the rounding margin. Severity MEDIUM not HIGH: this is spike harness tooling, and the shortfall depends on the price grid actually being the point grid / The failure is deterministic and I reproduced it. SSR_BarToTicks (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:562-599) samples a 3-segment path at u = i*3/(cnt-1); with cnt=20 no sample lands on the vertices (u=1 at i=6.33, u=2 at i=12.67), so the injected stream falls short of the bar's own high and low by ~5.26% of the wick. Replaying SSR_Rng/SSR_GenM1 (seed 4242, base 38000, vol_points 25) and SSR_BarToTicks in Python, the shortfall is scale-invariant (identical for digits 1, 2 and 5) and at least one of the 20 bars misses the one-point tolerance at line 139 outright, so hl_expanded can never equal nbars and 'wick_expanded_to_extremes' FAILS on a terminal whose broadcast is perfect. Impact is on a developer instrument, not the product: no user sees it, no money arithmetic is touched, and the accompanying metric 'wick_expanded N out of 20' tells a careful reader what happened. The real cost is a Tier-B blocker recorded as SPIKE FAIL against a correct terminal, which could send the author redesigning a transport layer that works - serious for the build process, but MEDIUM, not HIGH

#### `spikes-audits-12` — B3 prints its PASS criterion instead of asserting it, so the session-behaviour spike always reports SPIKE PASS

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_B3_SessionBehavior.mq5:132`** · category: measurement-validity

```mql5
Print("[B3] Compare acceptance_rate between no_sessions and sessions_247.");
Print("[B3] PASS requires sessions_247 acceptance_rate == 100%.");

SSR_End();
```
