The printed summary does read 'PASS=0 FAIL=0', which is the only hint that no assertion ran. MEDIUM - spike harness, not product code.

**Why:** SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:187-189 prints '(g_ssr_fail == 0 ? "SPIKE PASS" : "SPIKE FAIL")', and g_ssr_fail is only incremented inside SSR_Verdict (kit:169). B3 computes accept_rate at line 101 and records it as a metric at line 106, and computes silent_drops at 116-118, but asserts nothing about either. The comparison between the no_sessions and sessions_247 cases - the whole hypothesis at lines 6-9 - is left to a human reading two rows of a CSV.

**Verifiers:** Fully provable. The file's only SSR_Verdict is the creation-failure path inside RunCase (line 33); when both SSR_MakeSymbol calls succeed, nothing else asserts. accept_rate is computed at line 101 and only recorded as a metric at line 106; silent_drops is computed at 116-118 and only recorded as a metric; the no_sessions vs sessions_247 comparison the hypothesis rests on is printed as English prose at lines 132-133. SSR_Verdict is the sole place g_ssr_fail is incremented (SpikeKit:169), and SSR_End prints g_ssr_fail == 0 ? 'SPIKE PASS' : 'SPIKE FAIL' (SpikeKit:187-189), so B3 prints SPIKE PASS even with acceptance_rate 0% - the exact silent-drop defect it exists to detect - and verdicts.csv gets no B3 row at all. Downgraded from HIGH only because this is dev-only instrumentation rather than shipped behaviour / Provable from source: B3's only SSR_Verdict is the symbol-creation failure at :33 inside RunCase, so when both custom symbols are created no verdict is recorded at all; SSR_Verdict is the sole place g_ssr_fail is incremented (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:169) and SSR_End prints 'SPIKE PASS' whenever g_ssr_fail == 0 (:187-189). accept_rate (:101) and the silent_drops delta (:116-118) are recorded as metrics only, and the no_sessions vs sessions_247 comparison - the entire hypothesis - is printed as prose for a human (:132-133). So a run in which sessions_247 reads back 0 of 168 ticks logs SPIKE PASS and contributes no row to verdicts.csv. Severity MEDIUM rather than HIGH: this is spike tooling, the criterion is at least printed in the log, and SSR_End's companion line shows PASS=0 FAIL=0, which flags to a careful reader that nothing was asserted / Provable and exact. SSR_B3_SessionBehavior.mq5's only SSR_Verdict is the creation-failure path at :33; accept_rate (:101) and silent_drops (:116-118) are recorded as metrics and asserted nowhere. SSR_End (SpikeKit:187-189) prints 'SPIKE PASS' whenever g_ssr_fail==0, and g_ssr_fail only moves inside SSR_Verdict (kit:169) - so a run in which sessions_247 acceptance_rate is 0%, the precise silent-drop defect the spike exists to detect, prints SPIKE PASS and writes no B3 row at all to verdicts.csv. Impact is on the author's own evidence trail rather than on any user: the acceptance_rate metric and the two Print lines stating the criterion sit directly above the PASS line, so a careful reader is not deceived, but an automated or hurried reading of verdicts.csv is. Given this is a dev instrument with no product surface, no money arithmetic and no safety guarantee involved, HIGH is too strong; MEDIUM reflects a Tier-B spike that cannot report its own failure

#### `spikes-audits-10` — D1 turns a readable-wait timeout into a zero wait and publishes the flattering write-only rate as 'the number the user waits for'

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_D1_SeedPerformance.mq5:90`** · category: timer

```mql5
double t_total = (t_write + MathMax(t_read, 0)) / 1000.0;
SSR_Metric(c, "total_time", t_total, "s");
...
SSR_Metric(c, "bars_per_sec", (t_total > 0 ? total / t_total : 0), "bars/s",
           "write + readable - this is the number the user waits for");
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: D1:90 collapses SSR_WaitReadable's -1.0 timeout sentinel to 0 via MathMax, so on a readable-wait timeout total_time equals write_time and bars_per_sec (labelled 'the number the user waits for') reports the write-ceiling figure the surrounding comment exists to discredit. Guard needed: treat t_read < 0 as 'not measured' rather than zero, and assert readable_wait >= 0.

**Why:** SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:448-461: the loop runs while SSR_ElapsedMs(t0) < timeout_ms and 'return -1.0' on fall-through. D1:93-105 documents the mistake being guarded against: 'dividing by write_time alone reported 5.5 MILLION bars/second on a seed that took 9.2 seconds to become usable - a number 500x too flattering'. A timeout makes t_total == t_write/1000, i.e. exactly that discarded number, under the label that is supposed to distinguish it from write_call_bars_per_sec. readable_wait does record -1 in the same row, so the evidence is present but the derived headline metric is wrong and no verdict exists to catch it.

**Verifiers:** Traced exactly as described. SSR_SpikeKit.mqh SSR_WaitReadable() loops `while(SSR_ElapsedMs(t0) < timeout_ms)` and falls through to `return -1.0`. D1:90 `double t_total = (t_write + MathMax(t_read, 0)) / 1000.0;` — MathMax(-1.0, 0) is 0.0, so a 60-second timeout collapses to t_total == t_write/1000. D1:101 then divides `total` by that same t_total and labels the result "write + readable - this is the number the user waits for", while D1:103-105 computes the identical quotient and labels it "WRITE CEILING ONLY". On the failure case the two rows are numerically equal and the headline one is the very figure the 93-100 comment says was discarded as "500x too flattering". No guard, clamp or earlier check anywhere prevents this: t_read is used nowhere else, and there is no verdict on t_read < 0. readable_wait does record -1.0 at D1:84 so the raw evidence survives in results.csv, but nothing derives from it. Severity lowered from HIGH: this is a non-shipping diagnostic spike, the contradicting raw value sits one row away in the same case, and no product code path consumes it / SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:448-461 confirms SSR_WaitReadable falls through to `return -1.0` on timeout, and D1:90 does `(t_write + MathMax(t_read, 0)) / 1000.0`. MathMax(-1.0, 0) is 0.0 (double/int promote to double, no ambiguity), so a 60s timeout makes t_total == t_write/1000 and bars_per_sec at :101 becomes numerically identical to write_call_bars_per_sec at :103 while carrying the note 'this is the number the user waits for'. No SSR_Verdict anywhere in the file catches it, and the timeout row is the one that reports the fastest. The -1 sentinel is recorded adjacently as readable_wait (:84), so the evidence survives in results.csv; whether the 60s timeout actually fires is runtime-dependent, but the mishandling of the documented sentinel is settled from source. Severity lowered from HIGH: this is a dev-only measurement script, not shipped product code, and the truth is one row away / Provable from source. SSR_SpikeKit.mqh SSR_WaitReadable falls through to `return -1.0` after the timeout loop, and D1:90 does `MathMax(t_read, 0)`, so a 60 s readable-timeout is folded in as a zero wait: t_total becomes t_write/1000 and D1:101 then publishes the write-call ceiling under the note "this is the number the user waits for" - exactly the 500x-flattering figure the comment at 93-100 says was already corrected once. Impact lens, though, caps this: D1 is a developer measurement script, not product code. Nothing here touches money arithmetic, the future guard, or the virtual-trading path; no end user can reach it (spikes are run by hand and MT5 is not even installed in the build environment). The sentinel also survives one row away as readable_wait = -1, and a timing-out case costs 60 s of wall clock per case, so a reader has two adjacent signals. That is a real sentinel-to-zero arithmetic defect in a derived headline metric, but it misleads one developer reading a CSV rather than a user, so HIGH is too high

#### `spikes-audits-7` — D2's 'live' pass re-sends identical tick timestamps, so 69 of its 70 injections are refused and live == idle

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_D2_TimeframeSwitch.mq5:68`** · category: tick-ordering

```mql5
MqlTick tk[];
SSR_BarToTicks(m1[depth - 1], tk, 50, 20 * point, digits);
CustomTicksAdd(InpTest, tk);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: D2's live pass regenerates ticks from the fixed bar m1[depth-1] on every one of its 70 iterations, so SSR_BarToTicks emits identical time_msc stamps each time and CustomTicksAdd's return is never inspected - the with_ticks=true pass therefore cannot be distinguished from the idle pass, defeating the spike's headline condition. The claim that 69 calls are refused outright rests on the project's own B2 measurement of duplicate-stamp refusal (SSR_B2_TickThroughput.mq5:84-92), which is runtime behaviour the author observed rather than a spec guarantee.

**Why:** SSR_BarToTicks sets time_msc = bar.time*1000 + i*59000/(cnt-1) (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:586), a pure function of the bar - and the bar index depth-1 never changes inside MeasureDepth. B2:84-92 states the rule the code violates: 'CustomTicksAdd only appends: a tick at a stamp the symbol already holds is refused outright... All of them were rejected in full, returned in microseconds'. B2 fixed this by carrying one forward-only t_msc base; D2 did not. Nothing in D2 records accepted counts, so the rejection is invisible in the results file.

**Verifiers:** Traced MeasureDepth (lines 30-106). m1[depth-1] is a fixed element and is re-passed on every iteration of the 10x7 loop at 60-70; SSR_BarToTicks (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:~586) derives time_msc purely as base_msc + i*59000/(cnt-1) with base_msc = bar.time*1000, so all 70 calls in a pass produce byte-identical timestamps. CustomTicksAdd's return is discarded at line 69 and nothing in the file records accepted counts. The project has already diagnosed and fixed this exact mistake elsewhere and documented the rule in SSR_B2_TickThroughput.mq5 ('CustomTicksAdd only appends: a tick at a stamp the symbol already holds is refused outright... All of them were rejected in full, returned in microseconds'), with B2 carrying a single forward-only t_msc base as the fix. D2 was not converted, so the with_ticks=true pass injects a live feed exactly once out of 70 attempts and the 'live' metrics are indistinguishable from the 'idle' ones. Severity is on a measurement spike, not shipped product code / Confirmed from source on both sides. SSR_BarToTicks (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:562-598) derives time and time_msc purely from bar.time (base_msc = bar.time*1000, plus i*59000/(cnt-1)), and D2 calls it with m1[depth-1] - a fixed element, never advanced - inside the rep/i loops, so all 70 live-pass calls in one MeasureDepth submit byte-identical stamps; CustomTicksAdd's return at line 69 is discarded and no accepted-count metric exists, so a total rejection is invisible in the results. The duplicate-stamp refusal rule is the project's own measured lesson (SSR_B2_TickThroughput.mq5:84-92, which corrected exactly this mistake by carrying one forward-only t_msc base) rather than something derivable from the MQL5 spec, so the '69 of 70' number is measurement-backed rather than source-provable - but either way the live pass injects nothing new after its first call, so with_ticks=true is not the condition the spike's header claims to measure / The mechanism is provable. SSR_BarToTicks (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:562-598) derives time and time_msc purely from bar.time, and D2:68 passes m1[depth-1], a fixed element, on every one of the 70 iterations inside a MeasureDepth pass - so all 70 calls submit byte-identical stamps. B2:84-92 records the project's own measurement that CustomTicksAdd refuses stamps the symbol already holds and returns in microseconds, and B2 was fixed by carrying one forward-only t_msc base; D2 was not. The return of CustomTicksAdd at :69 is discarded, so nothing in the results file shows the rejection. The 'live' pass is therefore, after the first injection, the same measurement as the idle pass. Impact is entirely within a Phase-0 developer spike: a comparison the spike claims to make is not actually made, and someone reading tfswitch_*_live would believe switching was measured under load. No product code path, no money arithmetic, no data leakage. HIGH is not supportable for an unmeasured measurement

#### `spikes-audits-6` — D2 measures timeframe-switch cost by polling SERIES_SYNCHRONIZED, so its under-1s gate fails on a flag

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_D2_TimeframeSwitch.mq5:81`** · category: timer

```mql5
if(SeriesInfoInteger(InpTest, g_cycle[i], SERIES_SYNCHRONIZED, sync) && sync != 0)
   if(Bars(InpTest, g_cycle[i]) > 0)
     {
      waited = SSR_ElapsedMs(t0);
      break;
     }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate, with one addition: because line 89 also records the elapsed time on timeout instead of marking the sample unmeasured, a terminal where the flag is never set publishes a flat ~15,000 ms per switch as if it were measured work - the failure is silent in the results file as well as wrong.

**Why:** SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:390-417 documents the flag as size-independent, 'a timer, not work', and 'observed at 0.002ms, at 9,200ms, and never'. D1:74-77 caps its own flag wait at 3 s precisely because 'This run spent 300 of its 315 seconds waiting for a flag that was never going to be set', and D4:64-70 records that the flag 'sat on BOTH sides of this spike's headline ratio'. D2 was not converted: it still polls the flag, on six MeasureDepth passes x 10 repeats x 7 switches = 420 timed waits.

**Verifiers:** Traced lines 72-101. The stopwatch opens at 72, and the exit condition at 81-86 requires SERIES_SYNCHRONIZED to be non-zero before Bars() is even consulted, so the flag is the gate on every one of the 3 depths x 2 modes x 10 repeats x 7 switches = 420 timed waits. On timeout line 89 records the full elapsed time anyway, and that value feeds worst at 90, the per-switch metric at 92-94, and the tfswitch_<d>k_<mode>_under_1s verdict at 100-101. The kit documents that flag as size-independent, 'a timer, not work', observed 'at 0.002ms, at 9,200ms, and never' (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:390-417), and supplies SSR_WaitReadable/SSR_WaitLastBar for data-based waits; D1:74-77 caps its own flag wait at 3 s for precisely this reason and labels the result 'a flag, not a cost'. D2 alone still gates a product requirement on it. The methodology defect is provable; the specific 9,000-15,000 ms figures and the resulting FAIL depend on terminal behaviour I cannot settle from source / Read in full and the mechanism is as described: t0 at line 72 before ChartSetSymbolPeriod, the wait loop at 78-88 breaks only when SERIES_SYNCHRONIZED is set AND Bars() > 0, the loop bound is 15 s, line 89 records the elapsed time anyway on timeout, and worst feeds the tfswitch_<d>k_<mode>_under_1s verdict at 100-101. So the published switch cost is, by construction, time-to-flag rather than time-to-usable, contrary to the kit's 'ask the data, not the flag' rule (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:390-417) that D1 (:70-77, capped at 3 s after losing 300 of 315 seconds to a flag that was never set) and D4 were converted to. Two qualifiers the finding omits: the loop also requires Bars() > 0, so it is flag-AND-data, and g_cycle[0]/[6] are M1, already synchronized from line 46, while later reps reuse series built in rep 0 - so worst is dominated by the first pass over each new timeframe. The 9,000-15,000 ms magnitude and the gate failure are the author's unvalidated flag-timing observation, not source / Provable: D2:78-89 times until SERIES_SYNCHRONIZED is set for the new timeframe (with a Bars()>0 sub-condition that only narrows it further), caps at 15 s, and on timeout records the full elapsed time as the switch cost anyway (:89) - which then sets worst and gates the tfswitch_*_under_1s verdict (:100-101). The kit documents the same flag as size-independent and 'observed at 0.002ms, at 9,200ms, and never' (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:390-417), D1:74-77 was capped at 3 s for that reason and D4 records the same lesson; D2 was never converted. Impact is confined to the spike's own verdicts: a FAIL on a product requirement that would be caused by a flag rather than by the chart being unusable, potentially misdirecting design effort. It changes nothing a user of SS Replay sees, touches no money arithmetic and does not weaken the isolation or virtual-trading guarantees, and the 9 s magnitude is a runtime observation quoted in comments rather than proved by source. HIGH is not supportable

#### `spikes-audits-23` — D3's throughput_stable gate still uses the minute-10 baseline that the same file documents as hiding the finding

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_D3_SustainedRun.mq5:216`** · category: measurement-validity

```mql5
SSR_Verdict("throughput_stable", rate_drop <= 10.0, "<=10% drop",
            StringFormat("%.1f%%", rate_drop), "");
```

**What goes wrong:** rate_drop is computed from rate_first, which is only assigned once minute >= 10 (line 125-129). On the run the file describes - 12,000 ticks/s falling to about 780 before minute 10 - rate_first is already ~780 and rate_last is ~780, so rate_drop is near zero and throughput_stable PASSES on a run that lost 94% of its throughput. The remedy applied was to add rate_peak, rate_at_min1, decay_peak_to_end and decay_min1_to_end as metrics; the gate that produces the PASS/FAIL line and the verdicts.csv row was left on the window the comment says hides the problem.

**Why:** Lines 176-182: 'THE MINUTE-10 BASELINE HIDES THE FINDING... on this terminal most of the decay has already happened by then: the first run fell from ~12,000 to ~780 ticks/s before minute 10, then reported a comfortable PASS on what was left.' rate_drop at line 160 is 100*(rate_first-rate_last)/rate_first with rate_first taken at minute 10, and the else-branch verdict at 216-217 still consumes it. decay_peak_to_end is recorded at lines 187-190 but no verdict reads it.

**Verifiers:** Traced SSR_D3_SustainedRun.mq5 end to end. rate_first is initialised to -1 at line 88 and assigned only inside `if(minute >= 10.0 && mem_first < 0)` at 125-129, so it is the minute-10 sample. rate_drop at line 160 is 100*(rate_first-rate_last)/rate_first, i.e. a minute-10-to-end window, and the else-branch gate at 216-217 -- the line that produces the PASS/FAIL and the verdicts.csv row -- still consumes exactly that. The file's own comment at 176-182 states that on this terminal the run fell from ~12,000 to ~780 ticks/s before minute 10 and 'then reported a comfortable PASS on what was left'. The remediation was metrics only: rate_peak (183), rate_at_min1 (185), decay_peak_to_end (187-190), decay_min1_to_end (191-194) -- I grepped the file and no SSR_Verdict reads any of them. The only other guard is have_baseline (203-211), which handles runs shorter than 10 minutes, not the windowing problem. Nothing clamps or corrects rate_drop before line 216 / Confirmed at SSR_D3_SustainedRun.mq5. rate_first is assigned only inside `if(minute >= 10.0 && mem_first < 0)` (lines 125-129); rate_drop at line 160 is 100*(rate_first-rate_last)/rate_first; the else-branch verdict at 216-217 consumes exactly that. The file's own comment at 176-182 states that this window hides the decay ('fell from ~12,000 to ~780 ticks/s before minute 10, then reported a comfortable PASS'). rate_peak/rate_at_min1/decay_peak_to_end/decay_min1_to_end (183-194) are SSR_Metric calls only — I grepped the file, no SSR_Verdict reads any of them. So the PASS/FAIL line and the verdicts.csv row are still produced by the acknowledged-blind window / Provable from the file. rate_first is assigned only inside `if(minute >= 10.0 && mem_first < 0)` (lines 125-129); rate_drop at line 160 is 100*(rate_first-rate_last)/rate_first; and the else-branch verdict at 216-217 still consumes exactly that. rate_peak, rate_at_min1, decay_peak_to_end and decay_min1_to_end (lines 183-194) are recorded as metrics only - no verdict reads any of them. The file's own comment at 176-182 states that on the observed run throughput fell from ~12,000 to ~780 t/s before minute 10 and then 'reported a comfortable PASS on what was left'. This one keeps MEDIUM under the impact lens because, unlike the other measurement-validity findings in this batch, the false PASS conceals a product behaviour the user does observe: a sustained replay decaying ~94% in throughput, which is a visibly stalling replay after an hour or two

#### `spikes-audits-8` — D4's 1-bar tail-delete timing skips the series rebuild on 16 of 20 trials, inflating the rewind ratio

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_D4_RewindCost.mq5:97`** · category: timer

```mql5
datetime cut = m1[InpDepth - 1 - i].time;
ulong t = SSR_Now();
CustomRatesDelete(InpTest, cut, D'2038.01.01 00:00');
CustomTicksDelete(InpTest, (long)cut * 1000, LONG_MAX);
SSR_WaitLastBarBefore(InpTest, PERIOD_M5, cut, 15000);
double e = SSR_ElapsedMs(t);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: D4's strategy-1 propagation wait (SSR_WaitLastBarBefore on PERIOD_M5) is vacuous on 16 of 20 trials because the M5 bar enclosing the cut minute already opens before the cut whenever the minute index is not a multiple of 5; tail_1bar.avg therefore times only the synchronous CustomRatesDelete/CustomTicksDelete calls and omits the series-rebuild cost on those trials, while the full_rebuild side (strategy 3) waits on exact M1 equality - inflating rebuild_over_tail_ratio and letting step_back_interactive pass on an incomplete measurement. Severity is capped at MEDIUM because this is spike/measurement code, not product runtime, and the delete calls' own cost is still real.

**Why:** D'2023.01.02 00:00' is 1672617600, divisible by 300, and m1[k].time = start + k*60, so the M5 slot of cut is start + (k/5)*300 which is < cut unless k % 5 == 0. For i = 0..19 the cut indices are 99999..99980, of which only 99995, 99990, 99985 and 99980 are multiples of 5 - replaying the comparison gives 16 of 20 waits satisfied before the delete. Strategy 2 (cut 100 bars below the last bar) is unaffected. The spike's own comment at lines 64-70 says every timing here used to include SERIES_SYNCHRONIZED and that the published '317x cheaper' 'was mostly a measurement of a flag' - the data-based wait it was replaced with is vacuous here for a different reason.

**Verifiers:** Traced completely. SSR_GenM1 sets out[i].time = start + i*60 (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:491) and InpStart = D'2023.01.02 00:00' = 1672617600, which is exactly divisible by 300, so the M5 grid is aligned to the seed. SSR_WaitLastBarBefore (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:433-445) returns on the first CopyRates whose r[0].time < cut. For cut = m1[k].time with k not a multiple of 5, the enclosing M5 bar's open is start + (k/5)*300 < start + k*60 = cut BOTH before and after the delete, so the predicate is already satisfied on entry and the loop exits without observing any propagation. For k in 99999..99980 only 99995/99990/99985/99980 are multiples of 5, so 16 of 20 trials are vacuous exactly as claimed. sum1/InpTrials then feeds step_back_interactive (line 149), step_back_usable (line 152) and rebuild_over_tail_ratio (line 146), while strategy 3 uses SSR_WaitLastBar on PERIOD_M1 with exact equality (line 138) and strategy 2's cut indices (99899, 99799, ...) are never multiples of 5 so its pre-delete M5 last bar (start+19999*300) is not below cut and it does wait. No clamp, guard or earlier check prevents this. Downgraded from HIGH only because this is spike measurement scaffolding, not shipped engine code / The MQL5 semantics check out. SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:433-445 returns the moment CopyRates(sym,tf,0,1,r)>0 && r[0].time < cut. SSR_GenM1 (SpikeKit:492) sets time = start + i*60, and InpStart D'2023.01.02 00:00' = 1672617600 is divisible by 300, so with minute-grid alignment (24/7 sessions are set by SSR_Set247Sessions) the M5 bar enclosing m1[k] opens at start + (k/5)*300. That is strictly less than cut = start + k*60 for every k not a multiple of 5 - i.e. the predicate is ALREADY true before CustomRatesDelete has propagated anything, and it is equally true after propagation, so the instrument cannot distinguish the two states. For i=0..19 the cut indices are 99999..99980 and only 99995/99990/99985/99980 are multiples of 5, so 16 of 20 trials skip the propagation wait entirely. Strategy 2 is genuinely unaffected: its cut sits 100 bars below the current last bar, so the pre-delete M5 open (start+19999*300) is well above cut and the wait blocks. This is the same class of measurement-validity defect the file's own comment at lines 64-70 says it was fixing / Arithmetic verified independently. InpStart D'2023.01.02 00:00' = 1672617600, exactly divisible by 300, and SSR_GenM1 writes out[i].time = start + i*60 (SpikeKit:491). The M5 slot containing m1[k] opens at start + (k/5)*300, which is strictly below start + k*60 for every k not a multiple of 5. SSR_WaitLastBarBefore (SpikeKit:433-445) returns the moment CopyRates yields r[0].time < cut, so for cut indices 99999..99980 only 99995/99990/99985/99980 force a real wait - 4 of 20. With InpOpenChart=true the M5 series is already built (chart opened at line 82 + Sleep(2000)), so the first CopyRates in the loop succeeds immediately and the wait is satisfied before CustomRatesDelete has propagated to the M5 series on 16 trials. avg1 therefore gates step_back_interactive (<=500ms) and step_back_usable (<=3000ms) on what is largely call-return time, and inflates rebuild_over_tail_ratio whose numerator (strategy 3) waits properly via SSR_WaitLastBar on M1 with exact equality (line 138). Severity downgraded from HIGH: this is a Tier-D measurement harness, not product code; nothing here touches money arithmetic, future-data leakage or the virtual-trading guarantee. The reachable harm is one-step-removed - a UI design decision ('Previous Candle' as an interactive button) taken on a number biased in the optimistic direction, which the operator would later discover as a stall. The spike has also never been executed, so no wrong number has yet been published

#### `spikes-audits-9` — D4 loses its chart at the strategy-2 restore, so the three rewind strategies are not measured under the same conditions

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_D4_RewindCost.mq5:106`** · category: object-lifecycle

```mql5
//--- restore, then strategy 2: delete a 100-bar tail
SSR_DropSymbol(InpTest);
SSR_MakeSymbol(InpTest, g_origin);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate as to mechanism, but the resulting bias is conservative: the with-chart/without-chart asymmetry lowers rebuild_over_tail_ratio and raises avg1, so it makes the spike's gates harder to pass rather than easier. The substantive defect is the header instructing an indicator-attach step the script structurally prevents, plus the stale cid.

**Why:** SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:213-224 walks ChartFirst/ChartNext and calls ChartClose(cid) for every chart whose ChartSymbol() matches, inside SSR_DropSymbol. Strategy 3 calls SSR_DropSymbol again at line 134 on every trial. The header (lines 9-14) states the spike 'Compares three strategies at a realistic seed depth, with and without indicators on the chart, because indicator recalc is the cost that is easy to forget' and instructs 'Attach MA, RSI and Bollinger to the test chart before running the indicators pass' - but the chart is created by the script at line 82 and there is no input, pause or second pass that would let that happen; ChartIndicatorsTotal(cid,0) at line 85 is read two seconds after ChartOpen.

**Verifiers:** SSR_DropSymbol (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:209-224) walks ChartFirst/ChartNext and ChartClose()s every chart whose ChartSymbol() equals the symbol, and SSR_MakeSymbol (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:303-305) itself begins with SSR_DropSymbol and never opens a chart. So the ChartOpen at D4 line 82 is the only chart creation in the script, it is destroyed at line 106, and strategies 2 (line 114) and 3 (line 131) run with no chart while strategy 1 ran with one - the two halves of rebuild_over_tail_ratio are therefore measured under different conditions. cid is left holding a closed chart id that line 158 still passes to ChartClose (harmless no-op, but stale as described). The header's 'with and without indicators' pass is also unreachable: there is no second pass and no input controlling indicators, and the chart whose indicators the operator is told to attach is created by the script itself two seconds before ChartIndicatorsTotal reads it (lines 82-85) / Provable from source. SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:209-224 (SSR_DropSymbol) walks ChartFirst/ChartNext and ChartCloses every chart whose ChartSymbol() equals the symbol, and SSR_MakeSymbol:305 calls SSR_DropSymbol first as well. So D4:106-107 destroys the PERIOD_M5 chart created at D4:82, and nothing reopens it: strategy 1 (lines 91-101) runs with a chart attached, strategies 2 (114-124) and 3 (131-140) run with none, yet avg1 and avg3 are divided into one published rebuild_over_tail_ratio (line 146) and three verdicts. cid is never zeroed, so the ChartClose(cid) at line 158 acts on a destroyed chart id (harmless, but a no-op). The header's 'with and without indicators' pass is also unreachable as written: the chart is created by the script and ChartIndicatorsTotal(cid,0) is read 2s later (lines 82-86), leaving no point at which an operator can attach MA/RSI/Bollinger to it / Mechanism verified: SSR_DropSymbol (SpikeKit:209-224) walks ChartFirst/ChartNext and ChartCloses every chart whose ChartSymbol() matches, and SSR_MakeSymbol (SpikeKit:303-305) calls SSR_DropSymbol as its first act. So line 106 destroys the M5 chart opened at line 82, line 107 cannot bring it back, and nothing reopens it - strategy 1 runs with a chart, strategies 2 and 3 without. cid is stale from line 106 onward and ChartClose(cid) at line 158 is a no-op. The header's 'Attach MA, RSI and Bollinger to the test chart before running the indicators pass' is unperformable: the chart is created by the script itself, and any pre-existing chart on InpTest is closed by the SSR_MakeSymbol at line 50. Severity downgraded from MEDIUM to LOW on impact: unlike finding 8 the bias here runs in the safe direction - a chart makes the strategy-1 tail delete slower and its absence makes the strategy-3 rebuild faster, so both effects push rebuild_over_tail_ratio and the step_back gates toward pessimism, not toward wrongly approving an interactive button. The stale ChartClose is harmless, and the only real casualty is a documentation promise the script cannot keep

#### `tests-b-1` — T15.7 asserts R still means RESET; the key table maps R to LINES_TOGGLE, so the assertion is false at runtime

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T15_Ux.mq5:402`** · category: tick-ordering

```mql5
      Check("and the other keys still mean what they did",
            SSRKeyToCommand(SSR_VK_SPACE) == SSR_CMD_TOGGLE &&
            SSRKeyToCommand(SSR_VK_J)     == SSR_CMD_JUMP &&
            SSRKeyToCommand(SSR_VK_B)     == SSR_CMD_BOOKMARK &&
            SSRKeyToCommand(SSR_VK_R)     == SSR_CMD_RESET);
```

**What goes wrong:** Run T15 on a terminal: SSRKeyToCommand(SSR_VK_R) returns SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE, not SSR_CMD_RESET, so the conjunction is false and Check records a FAIL. Phase 15 can never print GREEN, and because the harness never aborts, this looks like a product regression rather than a stale assertion.

**Why:** SSR_Keys.mqh binds R with `SSRAddKey(out, i, SSR_VK_R, "R", SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE, "stop and target lines on / off", true);` and binds reset to '0' with `SSRAddKey(out, i, SSR_VK_0, "0", SSR_CMD_RESET, ...)`, under a comment headed "RESET MOVED OFF R, and off every letter". SSRKeyToCommand is a linear scan of that one table and returns the first vk match, so R can only yield SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE. The test was not updated when reset moved; SSRKeyHint()'s trailing literal "R reset" (same file) shows the same stale belief, which is why the mistake reads as plausible.

**Verifiers:** Provable from the two files. SSRKeyBindings binds R at SSR_Keys.mqh:162-164 as SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE and reset at l.200-202 as SSR_VK_0 -> SSR_CMD_RESET, under the comment 'RESET MOVED OFF R, and off every letter' (l.192-199). SSRKeyToCommand (l.224-232) is a first-match linear scan over that one table and R (82) appears exactly once, so it can only return SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE. SSR_T15_Ux.mq5:398-402 ANDs `SSRKeyToCommand(SSR_VK_R) == SSR_CMD_RESET` into its conjunction, so the whole Check is false, g_fail becomes non-zero, and the tail print at l.508-510 makes Phase 15 report RED unconditionally. I checked for any shadowing or alternate table and there is none. Severity downgraded from the finder's HIGH: the product behaviour is correct and only the test is stale, but the phase can never go green, so the suite stops functioning as a regression gate / Settled entirely from source. SSRKeyBindings() adds R exactly once, as SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE (SSR_Keys.mqh:162-164), and RESET is on '0' (:200-202) under the banner 'RESET MOVED OFF R, and off every letter' (:192-199). SSRKeyToCommand is a first-match linear scan over that single table (:224-232), so SSRKeyToCommand(SSR_VK_R) is SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE and the conjunction at SSR_T15_Ux.mq5:398-402 evaluates false. MQL5 compiles the enum comparison fine, so this is a guaranteed runtime FAIL, not a compile error: Phase 15 can never print GREEN. Severity lowered from HIGH because nothing in the product misbehaves - it is a stale assertion producing a permanent false alarm (and the suite has at least one other, tests-b-2, in the same file) / Provable and deterministic: SSR_Keys.mqh:162-164 binds SSR_VK_R to SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE, reset is on SSR_VK_0 (l.200-202), and SSRKeyToCommand is a first-match linear scan (l.224-232) with no other R row, so the T15.7 conjunction is false on every run and Phase 15 can only print RED. The suite's own QA smoke asserts the opposite pair (SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:3205 and 3211-3212: R == LINES_TOGGLE and R != RESET), which settles that the test assertion is the stale side. Downgraded from HIGH: this is test-only, changes no product behaviour, leaks no data and touches no money path; its cost is a permanently red phase whose Check() call passes no detail string, so the log says only 'FAIL and the other keys still mean what they did'

#### `tests-b-2` — T15.9's premise is false: SetCorner does not move the panel, so both of its assertions fail

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T15_Ux.mq5:460`** · category: redraw

```mql5
      panel2.SetCorner(0);                       // top-left
      long x_before = ObjectGetInteger(ChartID(), "T15_move_stbal",
                                       OBJPROP_XDISTANCE);

      panel2.SetCorner(1);                       // top-right
      long x_after = ObjectGetInteger(ChartID(), "T15_move_stbal",
                                      OBJPROP_XDISTANCE);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate. Worth adding that the section's stated purpose is lost either way - because SetCorner cannot move the panel, the position-keyed text-cache regression it was written to guard is no longer exercised by any assertion; a real test would have to call the palette's 'move' action (SnapToCorner) instead of SetCorner.

**Why:** CSSRPanel::SetCorner is `{ m_corner = (c & 3); SavePlace(); Render(); }` (SSR_Panel.mqh:384-385) and m_corner appears nowhere in Render or in any layout computation - its only uses are the constructor, a PrintFormat, SavePlace/RestorePlace and SnapToCorner (grep for m_corner in SSR_Panel.mqh returns lines 121, 287, 350, 385, 386, 543, 579, 598, 606 only). Only SnapToCorner (line 596, reachable from the Move button that v125 removed) recomputes m_x/m_y from the corner. Every object is drawn through CSSRWidgets, whose Common() hard-writes `ObjectSetInteger(m_chart, name, OBJPROP_CORNER, CORNER_LEFT_UPPER)` (SSR_Widgets.mqh:160), so XDISTANCE is always measured from the same chart corner. Two Renders with identical m_x therefore write identical XDISTANCE. (If the object did not exist, ObjectGetInteger would return 0 twice and the first assertion would fail for that reason instead.)

**Verifiers:** Traced the whole path. CSSRPanel::SetCorner is `{ m_corner = (c & 3); SavePlace(); Render(); }` (SSR_Panel.mqh:384-385). I grepped m_corner across SSR_Panel.mqh: lines 121, 287, 350, 385, 386, 543, 579, 598, 606 only - constructor, PrintFormat, SavePlace/RestorePlace, and SnapToCorner. ClampToChart (l.445-481), the only geometry that runs inside Render (called at l.717), never reads m_corner; Render then uses `int x = m_x, y = m_y;` (l.718). 'stbal' is always drawn at `x + SSR_PAD` (l.1929/1947/1988/2001/2015) and CSSRWidgets::Common hard-writes OBJPROP_CORNER = CORNER_LEFT_UPPER (SSR_Widgets.mqh:160), so XDISTANCE is measured from the same chart corner both times. Two SetCorner calls with unchanged m_x therefore write identical XDISTANCE, so both Checks at SSR_T15_Ux.mq5:468-473 fail; if the object were absent, ObjectGetInteger returns 0 twice and the first still fails. Severity MEDIUM rather than HIGH: like tests-b-1 this is a permanently-failing stale test, not a product fault / Verified end to end in source. SetCorner is m_corner = (c&3); SavePlace(); Render(); (SSR_Panel.mqh:384-385). Render takes int x = m_x after ClampToChart (:717-718), and ClampToChart (:445-478) reads only CHART_WIDTH/HEIGHT_IN_PIXELS - m_corner appears nowhere in it. The only place m_corner recomputes m_x is SnapToCorner (:596-618), reachable from the removed [] button and now only from the palette's 'move' action (:2605). 'stbal' is written at x + SSR_PAD on every branch of DrawStatus (:1929, 1947, 1988, 2001, 2015), and SavePlace only writes a file that Render never re-reads, so two SetCorner calls produce identical OBJPROP_XDISTANCE. Both Check calls at SSR_T15_Ux.mq5:468-473 therefore fail. The one alternative path leads to the same place: if m_collapsed was restored true, Render returns before DrawStatus (:702-708) and ObjectGetInteger on a non-existent object returns 0 twice, failing the first assertion anyway. Severity lowered from HIGH: the panel behaves as designed, the test is wrong / Verified independently: SetCorner is `{ m_corner = (c & 3); SavePlace(); Render(); }` (SSR_Panel.mqh:384-385); grep for m_corner in that file returns only 121, 287, 350, 385, 386, 543, 579, 598, 606 - nothing in Render, nothing in ClampToChart (445-481, which reads only chart pixels and m_x/m_y), and the only corner-to-coordinate code is SnapToCorner (596-613), called solely from the palette's 'move' action (2607). Widgets hard-write CORNER_LEFT_UPPER (SSR_Widgets.mqh:160) and stbal is always drawn at x + SSR_PAD in every DrawStatus branch (1929/1947/1988/2001/2015), so two Renders with identical m_x write identical XDISTANCE and both Checks at l.469-474 fail deterministically. Downgraded from HIGH for the same reason as tests-b-1: test-only, no product misbehaviour - but it does mean the text-cache-position regression this section exists to guard is not guarded at all

#### `tests-a-4` — T5.1 asserts R maps to reset; R has been rebound to the SL/TP lines

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T5_Ui.mq5:89`** · category: event-handling

```mql5
      CheckEq("R resets",           SSR_CMD_RESET,          SSRKeyToCommand(SSR_VK_R));
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Confirmed as described. Impact refinement: the user-visible half is limited to a single startup log line (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1694 prints SSRKeyHint()), which advertises a reset key that no longer resets and omits the real one (0); the on-screen key list is table-generated and correct, so nothing destructive is mis-triggered.

**Why:** Ui/SSR_Keys.mqh:162-164 is `SSRAddKey(out, i, SSR_VK_R, "R", SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE, "stop and target lines on / off", true);`, with a boxed comment at lines 192-199 headed "RESET MOVED OFF R, and off every letter". Reset is now `SSRAddKey(out, i, SSR_VK_0, "0", SSR_CMD_RESET, ...)` at line 200. `SSRKeyToCommand` (line 224) is a linear scan of that one table, so R can only return LINES_TOGGLE. Cross-check: `SSRKeyHint()` at SSR_Keys.mqh:273-274 still returns "... F follow  D fidelity  R reset", so the drift this test should have caught is live in production text as well.

**Verifiers:** Traced exactly as described. SSR_Keys.mqh:49 defines SSR_VK_R 82; SSRKeyBindings() binds it once, at the "TRADING" block, to SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE ("stop and target lines on / off"), and the boxed comment "RESET MOVED OFF R, and off every letter" is immediately followed by SSRAddKey(..., SSR_VK_0, "0", SSR_CMD_RESET, ...). SSRKeyToCommand() is a linear scan of that single table and returns on first vk match, so 82 can only yield SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE. In ENUM_SSR_CMD (SSR_Keys.mqh:15-42) SSR_CMD_RESET is ordinal 4 and SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE is ordinal 20 - no accidental aliasing. T5_Ui.mq5:89 therefore fails every run. Cross-check also holds: SSRKeyHint() still emits "... D fidelity  R reset", a live user-visible string that is wrong, and no test in T1..T8 asserts SSR_VK_R or SSR_VK_0 mapping. No guard, clamp or earlier check anywhere intercepts this / Verified end to end. SSR_VK_R is 82 (SSR_Keys.mqh:50) and the single table entry for it is SSRAddKey(..., SSR_VK_R, "R", SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE, ...) (SSR_Keys.mqh:162-164). SSRKeyToCommand (SSR_Keys.mqh:224-232) is a linear scan of that one table and returns the FIRST match, so 82 can only yield SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE. In the enum (SSR_Keys.mqh:15-43) SSR_CMD_RESET is ordinal 4 and SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE is ordinal 19, so CheckEq's long comparison at T5:89 is 4 vs 19 and prints FAIL on every run. Reset is on SSR_VK_0 (SSR_Keys.mqh:200-202) behind the boxed 'RESET MOVED OFF R' comment. I also confirmed the file is compilable (CFakePort implements every '= 0' virtual of CSSRReplayPort, SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:309-327), so 'fails on every run' is reachable rather than moot. The cross-check holds too: SSRKeyHint() (SSR_Keys.mqh:272-276) still ends '...D fidelity  R reset', a live user-visible string naming a binding that no longer exists. No MQL5 semantic rescues this: no duplicate R entry, no second table, no fallback / SSR_Keys.mqh:162-164 binds SSR_VK_R to SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE and SSR_VK_0 to SSR_CMD_RESET (line 200); SSRKeyToCommand is a linear scan of that single table, so the T5.1 assertion at line 89 must fail on every run. Grep confirms no test in T1..T8 asserts R->LINES_TOGGLE or 0->RESET. The secondary production claim also holds: SSRKeyHint() (SSR_Keys.mqh:271-274) still says "R reset" and never mentions 0. Impact is small, though: the stale hint is printed once to the Experts log (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1694) and nowhere on the panel - the in-product key list (H / SSR_CMD_KEYS) is generated from the live table, so the panel is correct. A user who trusts the log line presses R and gets the SL/TP lines - confusing, but the mis-hint points AWAY from a destructive verb, not at one, and reset itself still arms-and-confirms. No money arithmetic, no data leak, no weakening of the virtual-trading guarantee. HIGH/MEDIUM is not warranted for a stale assertion plus one log string

#### `tests-a-2` — T5.2 asserts an 8-stop speed ladder against a 20-stop ladder: four assertions fail

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T5_Ui.mq5:109`** · category: boundary

```mql5
      CheckEq("ladder has eight steps", 8, SSR_SPEED_LADDER_SIZE);
      CheckEq("slowest is 0.25x", SSR_SPEED_025, SSRSpeedLadder(0));
      CheckEq("fastest is 50x",   SSR_SPEED_50,  SSRSpeedLadder(7));
      CheckEq("1x sits at index 2", 2, SSRSpeedLadderIndex(SSR_SPEED_1));
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate as written; severity is test-gate-only (a permanently RED T5), not product behaviour.

**Why:** Common/SSR_Types.mqh:302 is `#define SSR_SPEED_LADDER_SIZE 20`. `SSRSpeedLadder` (line 275) returns 10 for index 0 (the comment says `// 0.1x`) and 300 for index 7; 5000 is index 15 and SSR_SPEED_MAX is index 19. `SSR_SPEED_DEFAULT_IX` is 4 and `SSRSpeedLadder(4)` is 100, so `SSRSpeedLadderIndex(SSR_SPEED_1 /*100*/)` returns 4, not 2. The header comment above the ladder (lines 257-274) explicitly says "Twenty stops, not eight, because the panel now has a trackbar" - the production code documents the change and the test was never brought up to it. The test still compiles because `ENUM_SSR_SPEED` kept its eight named values; only the ladder grew.

**Verifiers:** All four assertions provably fail. SSR_Types.mqh:302 is `#define SSR_SPEED_LADDER_SIZE 20`; SSRSpeedLadder(0)=10 (comment 0.1x) vs SSR_SPEED_025=25; SSRSpeedLadder(7)=300 vs SSR_SPEED_50=5000; SSRSpeedLadderIndex is a nearest-neighbour scan with SSR_SPEED_DEFAULT_IX 4, and SSRSpeedLadder(4)=100=SSR_SPEED_1 exactly, so SSRSpeedLadderIndex(SSR_SPEED_1) returns 4, not 2. The header comment at SSR_Types.mqh:257-274 ("Twenty stops, not eight") confirms the deliberate production change the test was never updated for. The file still compiles: ENUM_SSR_SPEED (lines 118-132) kept its eight names. The two surviving assertions in the block do pass (index 19 = SSR_SPEED_MAX 100000 keeps the ladder strictly ascending; SSRSpeedName returns non-empty for 25 and 5000). CFakePort implements every `= 0` member of CSSRReplayPort (Name, IsConnected, ReadState, Play, Pause, Reset, StepBars, StepBack, JumpTo, Restart, SeekTo, SetSpeedX100, SetFidelity), so the file is not abstract and these failures really do execute. Severity lowered from HIGH: the defect is confined to the test gate, with no runtime or user-facing effect / All four arithmetic claims check out from source. SSR_SPEED_LADDER_SIZE is 20 (SSR_Types.mqh:302) vs asserted 8. SSRSpeedLadder(0) is 10 (SSR_Types.mqh:279) vs SSR_SPEED_025 = 25 (SSR_Types.mqh:120). SSRSpeedLadder(7) is 300 (SSR_Types.mqh:286) vs SSR_SPEED_50 = 5000 (SSR_Types.mqh:127). SSRSpeedLadderIndex(100): the nearest-stop loop (SSR_Types.mqh:313-326) uses strict 'd < gap', so the first exact hit wins - index 4, where SSRSpeedLadder(4)==100 - not the asserted 2. Four guaranteed FAILs, so T5 can never print GREEN. I confirmed the remaining two assertions in the section do pass (the ladder is strictly ascending 10..100000, and SSRSpeedName returns non-empty for both), so the section is red purely from these four. Severity lowered from HIGH: this is a permanently-red test gate with no runtime effect on the product; the ladder itself is coherent and deliberately documented as twenty stops (SSR_Types.mqh:257-273) / Verified line by line against SSR_Types.mqh: SSR_SPEED_LADDER_SIZE is 20 (line 302), SSRSpeedLadder(0)=10 vs SSR_SPEED_025=25, SSRSpeedLadder(7)=300 vs SSR_SPEED_50=5000, and SSRSpeedLadderIndex(100)=4 (SSR_SPEED_DEFAULT_IX, nearest-stop scan) vs the asserted 2. Four guaranteed failures; the file therefore can never print GREEN. But the impact is confined to the test gate: the production ladder and its documented 20-stop rationale are internally consistent, the surviving assertions ("ladder ascends", "names render") still exercise real invariants, and nothing a user touches is wrong. Severity HIGH overstates a stale-constant test that has never been executed (MT5 is not installed in this environment), and it corrupts no money arithmetic, leaks no future data, and does not weaken virtual trading

#### `tests-a-3` — T5.5's three speed-clamp assertions fail against the 20-stop ladder

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T5_Ui.mq5:174`** · category: boundary

```mql5
      CheckEq("one step faster", SSR_SPEED_2, port.last_speed);
...
      CheckEq("cannot exceed the top", SSR_SPEED_50, port.last_speed);
...
      CheckEq("cannot go below the bottom", SSR_SPEED_025, port.last_speed);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Confirmed factually; severity downgraded to LOW. Production clamping at both ends is correct, so the real cost is the lost coverage of the index 0 / index 19 boundaries plus the file staying red.

**Why:** `CSSRPanel::ExecuteInner` case SSR_CMD_SPEED_UP (SSR_Panel.mqh:2317-2322) is `int i = SSRSpeedLadderIndex(m_state.speed_x100); if(i < SSR_SPEED_LADDER_SIZE - 1) i++; return m_port.SetSpeedX100(SSRSpeedLadder(i));`. SSRSpeedLadderIndex(100)=4 -> 5 -> SSRSpeedLadder(5)=150. SSRSpeedLadderIndex(5000)=15 -> 16 -> 7500. SPEED_DOWN (2324-2328) does `if(i > 0) i--`: SSRSpeedLadderIndex(25)=1 -> 0 -> 10. `CFakePort::SetSpeedX100` records the argument verbatim into `last_speed`, and each Execute is preceded by `panel.Render()` so `m_state.speed_x100` is the value the test just set.

**Verifiers:** Traced end to end. CSSRPanel::Render() (SSR_Panel.mqh:631-637) calls m_port.ReadState(m_state), and CFakePort::ReadState copies its test-controlled `state`, so m_state.speed_x100 is exactly what the test set. ExecuteInner case SSR_CMD_SPEED_UP is `i = SSRSpeedLadderIndex(m_state.speed_x100); if(i < SSR_SPEED_LADDER_SIZE - 1) i++; return m_port.SetSpeedX100(SSRSpeedLadder(i));` and SPEED_DOWN mirrors it with `if(i > 0) i--`. SSRSpeedLadderIndex(100)=4 -> 5 -> 150 (test expects 200); SSRSpeedLadderIndex(5000)=15 -> 16 -> 7500 (test expects a clamp at 5000); SSRSpeedLadderIndex(25)=1 -> 0 -> 10 (test expects a clamp at 25). CFakePort::SetSpeedX100 records the argument verbatim into last_speed. Execute() only calls DisarmReset() before ExecuteInner for non-RESET commands, so nothing intercepts. The secondary point is also correct: 50x is index 15 of 20, so the middle assertion exercises no clamp at all and the true boundaries (index 0 = 10, index 19 = SSR_SPEED_MAX) are untested. Severity lowered from HIGH for the same reason as tests-a-2 / Traced the whole path rather than trusting the quote. Render() calls m_port.ReadState(m_state) (SSR_Panel.mqh:631-637), and CFakePort::ReadState copies its public `state` verbatim (T5:58), so m_state.speed_x100 is exactly what the test assigned. Execute() (SSR_Panel.mqh:2183) forwards to ExecuteInner, whose SSR_CMD_SPEED_UP body (SSR_Panel.mqh:2317-2322) is index-then-clamp-at-19, and SPEED_DOWN (2324-2328) clamps at 0. With SSRSpeedLadderIndex exact-hit-first: 100 -> 4 -> 5 -> 150 (asserted 200, FAIL); 5000 -> 15 -> 16 -> 7500 (asserted 5000, FAIL); 25 -> 1 -> 0 -> 10 (asserted 25, FAIL). CFakePort::SetSpeedX100 records the argument verbatim into last_speed (T5:70-71), so the observed value is the ladder value and no rounding or truncation intervenes. The secondary point is also right: 5000 is index 15 of 20, so the middle assertion exercises no clamp at all, and the real ceiling (index 19, SSR_SPEED_MAX = 100000) and floor (index 0, 10) are untested. Severity lowered from HIGH for the same reason as tests-a-2 - stale test, not a product defect / Traced the whole path: Render() calls m_port.ReadState(m_state) (SSR_Panel.mqh:635-636) so m_state.speed_x100 is the value the test just wrote, and ExecuteInner SPEED_UP/DOWN (2317-2328) steps the nearest ladder index. 100->index 4->5->150 (asserted 200), 5000->15->16->7500 (asserted a clamp at 5000), 25->1->0->10 (asserted 25). Three guaranteed failures, and the observation that the true clamps (index 0 = 10, index 19 = SSR_SPEED_MAX) are now uncovered is correct. Impact is nevertheless test-only: the production clamp logic is provably correct at both ends (i < SIZE-1, i > 0), so no user can drive the speed off the ladder. Test-suite damage, not product damage

#### `tests-a-1` — T5.6's click assertion cannot pass: the panel does not handle CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_CLICK at all

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T5_Ui.mq5:208`** · category: event-handling

```mql5
      string s = "SSRT5_play";
      panel.OnEvent(CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_CLICK, l, d, s);
      CheckEq("click reached the port", 1, port.play);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct; note the panel's behaviour is deliberate and right - the defect is entirely in the test, which should press OBJPROP_STATE on SSRT5_play and call panel.PollClicks().

**Why:** `grep -n CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_CLICK MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh` returns exactly one hit, line 2878, and it is a comment inside OnEvent: "//--- CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_CLICK is deliberately not handled here. //--- PollClicks is the ONE mechanism". OnEvent's branches are CHARTEVENT_KEYDOWN (palette, Ctrl+K, tag box, key table), CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_ENDEDIT and CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE; every other id falls through to `return false` at the end (line 2971). Clicks are instead consumed by `PollClicks()` (SSR_Panel.mqh:2445), which scans `ObjectsTotal(m_chart, -1, OBJ_BUTTON)` for names starting with `m_prefix` whose `OBJPROP_STATE` is set. A test of the click path would have to set OBJPROP_STATE on `SSRT5_play` and then call `panel.PollClicks()`.

**Verifiers:** CSSRPanel::OnEvent (SSR_Panel.mqh:2783-2971) was read in full. Its branches are: CHARTEVENT_KEYDOWN with palette up; CHARTEVENT_KEYDOWN + SSR_VK_K with Ctrl; CHARTEVENT_KEYDOWN with m_tag_focus; CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_ENDEDIT on the tagbox; CHARTEVENT_KEYDOWN generally (SSRKeyToCommand -> Owns -> Execute); and CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE. CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_CLICK appears exactly once in the file, as the comment at 2878 ("deliberately not handled here ... PollClicks is the ONE mechanism"), so an object-click event falls straight through to `return false` at 2971. Render() does not poll clicks either, and the test never sets OBJPROP_STATE on SSRT5_play nor calls PollClicks(). port.play therefore stays 0 and line 209 prints FAIL expected=1 actual=0. The suggested fix in the evidence (set OBJPROP_STATE then call PollClicks, which filters on StringFind(name, m_prefix) != 0) matches the real mechanism. Severity lowered from HIGH: test-gate only / I read CSSRPanel::OnEvent in full (SSR_Panel.mqh:2783-2971). Its branches are: palette keydown, Ctrl+K, tag-box keydown, CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_ENDEDIT, generic CHARTEVENT_KEYDOWN, CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE - then `return false` at 2971. CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_CLICK appears nowhere except the comment at 2878-2882 stating PollClicks is the one mechanism. So the call at T5:208 cannot touch the port: port.play stays 0 against an expected 1 and the assertion FAILs on every run. I checked the two ways this could have been wrong and neither holds - Render() does not call PollClicks (it only reads state and writes widgets), and CFakePort::Play is reached from nowhere else in that block. This is consistent with the established ground truth that the panel lives on a chart the EA is not attached to and consumes clicks by scanning OBJPROP_STATE in PollClicks (SSR_Panel.mqh:2445+), so the production design is right and the test is the stale side. Severity lowered from HIGH: one always-red assertion plus a genuine coverage hole, no user-facing fault / CSSRPanel::OnEvent (SSR_Panel.mqh:2783-2971) branches only on CHARTEVENT_KEYDOWN (palette, Ctrl+K, tag box, key table), CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_ENDEDIT and CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE; the only occurrence of CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_CLICK is the comment at 2878 explaining that PollClicks is the one mechanism. So the call at T5:208 is a no-op, port.play stays 0, and the assertion fails on every run. Kept at MEDIUM rather than downgraded with the other stale assertions because grep shows PollClicks is referenced by no test file at all: the click path is the product's PRIMARY input path (the panel lives on the replay chart, which the EA is not attached to, so buttons are the only reliable control surface) and it has zero automated coverage on a codebase the author has never run on a terminal. Still test-only in effect - no money, data or safety impact - so not HIGH

#### `spikes-audits-21` — C2's restart detector false-fails on every run after the first, and the event it hunts destroys its own evidence file

**`MQL5/Services/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_C2_ServicePersistence.mq5:58`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
double prev_marker = GlobalVariableGet("SSR.c2.launches");
GlobalVariableSet("SSR.c2.launches", prev_marker + 1);
...
SSR_Verdict("no_restart", GlobalVariableGet("SSR.c2.launches") <= 1.0, "1",
            DoubleToString(GlobalVariableGet("SSR.c2.launches"), 0),
            "more than 1 means the service was restarted");
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate, with one nuance: a truncated CSV starting again at seq=1 with a low uptime is itself circumstantial evidence that a relaunch happened; what is irrecoverably lost is the pre-restart row history (and the pre-restart gap statistics), which is what the PASS criterion needs to demonstrate the counter reset.

**Why:** No GlobalVariableDel("SSR.c2.launches") appears anywhere in the file, and line 127 prints 'reset the launch counter before the next run: delete GV SSR.c2.launches'. Line 63 opens SSR_Spike\c2_heartbeat.csv with FILE_WRITE|FILE_TXT|FILE_ANSI (no FILE_READ, no seek to end), so OnStart running a second time rewrites the header at offset 0. seq is a local initialised to 0 at line 51, so the only cross-restart evidence is the GV that is already unreliable. Header line 21: 'PASS: heartbeat counter never resets and no gap exceeds 3000ms.'

**Verifiers:** Both halves verified. (a) Line 58 reads GlobalVariableGet("SSR.c2.launches") (0 when absent), line 59 writes prev+1, and line 115 fails the no_restart verdict whenever that value exceeds 1. A repo-wide grep shows no GlobalVariableDel for this name anywhere - sibling spikes C1 (SSR_C1_ServiceApiAccess.mq5:157) and C3 (SSR_C3_IpcChannel.mq5:127-132) do delete their own GVs, so the omission is an oversight, and line 127 pushes the reset onto the operator. MT5 global variables are persisted by the terminal, so run 2 onward reports launch_count 2+ and FAILs no_restart with no restart having occurred: the verdict measures 'has this spike run before'. (b) Line 63 opens SSR_Spike\c2_heartbeat.csv with FILE_WRITE|FILE_TXT|FILE_ANSI - no FILE_READ and no seek - so a second OnStart truncates and rewrites the header at offset 0, erasing the pre-restart rows that are the only durable evidence for the header's PASS criterion (line 21, 'heartbeat counter never resets'); seq is a local initialised to 0 (line 51) and GV SSR.c2.seq is overwritten every beat (line 94) / Both MQL5 semantics used by the finding are correct. (1) GlobalVariableGet on a missing name returns 0.0, and MT5 global variables are persisted by the terminal between runs and survive terminal restarts; C2 never deletes SSR.c2.launches (no GlobalVariableDel anywhere in the file) and line 127 asks the operator to delete it by hand AFTER the run, so run 2 starts from prev_marker=1, sets 2, and the no_restart verdict at line 115 (<= 1.0) fails although nothing restarted - the verdict measures 'has this spike been run before'. (2) FileOpen(..., FILE_WRITE|FILE_TXT|FILE_ANSI) without FILE_READ truncates the file to zero length, and there is no FileSeek(FILE_END), so a genuine mid-run relaunch rewrites c2_heartbeat.csv from offset 0 (line 63-65). Since a relaunched OnStart never reached SSR_Metric/SSR_Verdict on its first pass, the CSV was the only record of the pre-restart rows, and seq is a local reinitialised to 0 at line 51 - so the evidence for the header's PASS criterion ('heartbeat counter never resets', line 21) is the one thing the restart destroys / Both mechanisms check out. GlobalVariableGet returns 0 for a missing GV, so run 1 stores 1 and passes no_restart, run 2 stores 2 and fails it, with no GlobalVariableDel anywhere in the file. FileOpen at line 63 uses FILE_WRITE|FILE_TXT|FILE_ANSI with no FILE_READ and no seek, so a relaunch truncates c2_heartbeat.csv and rewrites the header at offset 0, destroying the pre-restart rows that would evidence the seq reset the header names as the PASS criterion. But the first half is substantially over-stated: the persistence is the detection mechanism, not an oversight - auto-resetting the GV in OnStart would make an accidental relaunch indistinguishable from a first launch and defeat the check entirely - and line 127 prints the reset as an explicit operator step in a spike whose header already prescribes eight manual steps. Severity downgraded from MEDIUM to LOW: this is a dev-facing spike, the false FAIL is self-diagnosing (results.csv carries launch_count with the note 'must stay 1 for the whole procedure', and the printed remedy names the GV), and nothing here touches money, future data or the virtual-trading guarantee

### LOW (105)

#### `host-expert-10` — Extra streams and the session-settings block bypass the Cfg*() accessors, so the setup panel's spread and chart timeframe apply to the primary instrument only

**`MQL5/Experts/SSReplay/SSReplayStandalone.mq5:495`** · category: input-validation

```mql5
      g_ctrl2[built].SetSpreadPoints(InpSpreadPoints);
...
      g_charts2[built].OpenChart(InpChartTf);          // line 511
...
      out.spread_points = InpSpreadPoints;             // line 424
      out.chart_tf      = InpChartTf;                  // line 425
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The list of misrecorded settings is too long. Of the six named, only blind, spread and chart_tf have a Cfg*() accessor to bypass; pause mode (out.session_mode=InpPauseSession), slot (InpSlot) and ticks per bar (InpTicksPerBar) have no setup-panel field and no accessor anywhere, so recording the input is the only thing those three could record and is not a defect. Likewise the extra streams' only bypasses are the spread at 495 and the chart timeframe at 511 - the extra-timeframe list does come through CfgExtraTfs() (1288) and anonymity through g_blind.Anonymous() (491).

**Why:** CfgSpread() (line 218) and CfgChartTf() (221) exist precisely so that 'every reader goes through these, so there is one place that knows which of the two wins - rather than thirty call sites each remembering, and one of them forgetting' (comment 206-213). OpenExtraStreams uses the raw inputs at 495 and 511 while the primary path uses the accessors at 1145 and 1275; CollectSettings uses them at 419-425. Impact is bounded because nothing reads the session file's settings block back at resume time - the host reads only ReadWindow and ReadSymbols (1066, 1325), and SSRSessionSettings/ReadSettings has a single caller, the T12 test (Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T12_Session.mq5:497).

**Verifiers:** Verified at the exact lines: OpenExtraStreams uses the raw inputs (495 SetSpreadPoints(InpSpreadPoints), 511 OpenChart(InpChartTf)) while the primary path uses the accessors (1146 SetSpreadPoints(CfgSpread()), 1276 OpenChart(CfgChartTf())); CollectSettings (415-426) writes InpBlind, InpPauseSession, InpSlot, InpTicksPerBar, InpSpreadPoints, InpChartTf even though CfgBlind/CfgSpread/CfgChartTf exist. So with a setup form that changed spread or chart tf, the extra streams and the saved settings block disagree with the session that actually ran. Impact is bounded as the finder states: grep shows ReadSettings' only caller is Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T12_Session.mq5:497, and the host reads back only ReadWindow/ReadSymbols / Line-verified: OpenExtraStreams uses SetSpreadPoints(InpSpreadPoints) at 495 and OpenChart(InpChartTf) at 511 while the primary path uses CfgSpread() at 1145 and CfgChartTf() at 1275; CollectSettings writes out.blind=(int)InpBlind at 419, out.spread_points=InpSpreadPoints at 424 and out.chart_tf=InpChartTf at 425 although CfgBlind/CfgSpread/CfgChartTf all exist (219-224). The accessors' own comment (206-213) states the rule these three sites break. The bounded-impact argument checks out: the only ReadSettings caller is Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T12_Session.mq5:497, and the host's resume path reads only ReadWindow (1066) and ReadSymbols (1325) / The bypass is real: OpenExtraStreams uses InpSpreadPoints (495) and InpChartTf (511) while the primary path uses CfgSpread() (1145) and CfgChartTf() (1275), and CollectSettings writes raw inputs at 419-425. But the impact is narrower than the title suggests. The session-settings half has no user-observable effect at all: grep shows SSRSessionManager::ReadSettings has exactly one caller, the T12 test (Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T12_Session.mq5:497), so nothing ever reads those fields back and no setting 'silently resets on resume'. The extra-streams half needs InpAlsoSymbols non-empty AND a setup form that changed spread or timeframe, and the money arithmetic is untouched because the virtual account observes only the primary controller (g_acct is Watch()ed on g_ctrl; g_publisher2 is attached with a NULL account), so the mismatched spread only colours ticks on secondary charts. Observable residue: secondary windows open on a different timeframe than the one the user chose. LOW confirmed

#### `host-expert-11` — EnsureHistory's 60-second budget is computed with a ulong subtraction of a 32-bit tick count and breaks at the GetTickCount wrap

**`MQL5/Experts/SSReplay/SSReplayStandalone.mq5:651`** · category: boundary

```mql5
   ulong t0 = GetTickCount();
   int   stalls = 0;
   while(have < want_bars && (GetTickCount() - t0) < 60000 && !IsStopped())
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The mechanism is real but the claim's probability framing is wrong. The wrap must fall inside the loop's own lifetime, not merely on a terminal that has been up for weeks - roughly a 60 s window in each 49.7 days, i.e. ~1e-5 per download - and the realistic effect is an early exit part-way through a download (EnsureHistory falls out of the loop and prints its normal tail line), not a systematic no-op that never enters the body.

**Why:** The same pattern is written correctly elsewhere in this file with uint arithmetic - line 2894 `uint now_ms = GetTickCount(); if(!modal && now_ms - g_panel_paint >= 100)` and line 3145 `(GetTickCount() - g_ready_at_ms) > 1500` where g_ready_at_ms is uint (declared 345). Only t0 at 651 is widened to ulong. GetTickCount64() is the wrap-free primitive. Failure needs a 49.7-day-uptime coincidence, hence LOW, but the outcome is a silent no-op on the one code path whose whole job is fetching history.

**Verifiers:** The arithmetic defect is provable. t0 is declared ulong at 651 while GetTickCount() returns uint, so at 653 the fresh uint is widened rather than wrapped and `GetTickCount() - t0` after a wrap underflows in 64-bit to a value near 2^64, which is >= 60000 and ends the loop. The same pattern is wrap-safe elsewhere in the file because both operands are uint (2894 with `uint now_ms`, 3145 with g_ready_at_ms declared uint at 345). GetTickCount64() is the wrap-free primitive and is not used here / The type error is real and provable. GetTickCount() returns uint and wraps every 2^32 ms (~49.7 days of terminal uptime); t0 is declared ulong at 651, so in `(GetTickCount() - t0) < 60000` at 653 the fresh uint is zero-extended to ulong before the subtraction instead of wrapping modulo 2^32. The file writes the same idiom correctly everywhere else with uint operands - `uint now_ms = GetTickCount(); ... now_ms - g_panel_paint >= 100` at 3062-3063 and `(GetTickCount() - g_ready_at_ms) > 1500` at 3146 with g_ready_at_ms declared uint at 350 - so 651 is the lone outlier, and GetTickCount64() is the wrap-free primitive. Consequence as described: if the wrap falls between t0 and a loop test the budget evaluates as exhausted, the download is abandoned, and BuildSession's SHORT ON HISTORY cascade at 1109-1123 then blames the broker. Severity stays LOW - the vulnerable window is roughly the 60 s preceding a wrap that occurs once per 49.7 days of uptime / The arithmetic defect is real: `ulong t0 = GetTickCount()` widens a uint, so after the 49.7-day wrap `GetTickCount() - t0` is evaluated in ulong and yields ~2^32 rather than a small elapsed time, and the file itself writes the same pattern correctly with uint at 2894 and 3145. Downgraded from LOW on the impact lens because the reachable window is vanishingly small: a terminal already past the wrap simply gets a mid-range t0 and behaves normally, so the bug fires only when the wrap lands between t0 and a later loop test - roughly a 60-second window per 49.7 days of uptime, about 1 in 7x10^4 attaches. The consequence is also bounded: EnsureHistory returns the Bars() it already had, and the SHORT ON HISTORY block at 1128-1145 then prints the true situation including 'the broker HAS more - back to <date>' and the Home-key remedy, so the user is not misled about what to do. GetTickCount64() is the correct primitive; worth fixing, not worth ranking above the reachable defects in this batch


CSSRFirstRun::MarkSeen() is therefore never reached in one-window mode, so seen.txt is never written and the card is not merely consumed - it never exists. Severity reduced from MEDIUM: the dead feature is an onboarding card, with no effect on session correctness, data or money / Same mechanism as host-expert-3 and equally provable: `!one_chart_ok` is false on both passes when InpOneChart is true, and g_panel_chart is non-zero on pass 2 (1449), so with both defaults the card is shown on neither pass and seen.txt is never written. Downgraded from MEDIUM on impact: it is latent behind host-expert-1 exactly as #3 is, and even once reachable the whole consequence is that an onboarding card does not appear - no state is corrupted, nothing is consumed (the marker is never written, so the card is not lost), and every command it advertises is reachable from the panel and the key hint printed at 1694

#### `host-expert-2` — On the replay chart InpSymbol is ignored, so the recovery the failure message tells the user to perform cannot work

**`MQL5/Experts/SSReplay/SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1903`** · category: input-validation

```mql5
   string origin = (on_replay ? stashed
                              : (InpSymbol == "" ? _Symbol : InpSymbol));
...
      Print("[host] this chart is already a replay symbol and I do not know "
            "which instrument it came from. Attach SS Replay to a normal "
            "chart, or set InpSymbol to the origin, and try again.");
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate, but only one of the two remedies the message offers is impossible: the other ('Attach SS Replay to a normal chart') works, and the chart can be switched back by hand, so the user is not without a route out. Harm is a misleading log line plus a lost workaround for host-expert-1, not a dead end.

**Why:** The ternary at 1903-1904 uses `stashed` unconditionally when on_replay is true; InpSymbol appears only in the else branch. The guard at 1911 tests `origin == ""` after that assignment, and the message at 1915-1917 names InpSymbol as a remedy. A one-line change (on_replay ? (stashed != "" ? stashed : InpSymbol) : ...) would make the advice true and would also give host-expert-1 a manual workaround.

**Verifiers:** The ternary at 1903-1904 is `on_replay ? stashed : (InpSymbol == "" ? _Symbol : InpSymbol)` - InpSymbol appears only in the non-replay branch, so on a replay chart it is unreachable. The guard at 1911 tests origin=="" and the message at 1915-1917 explicitly offers "set InpSymbol to the origin" as a remedy. Re-attaching with InpSymbol set (REASON_PARAMETERS, same chart, still a replay symbol) takes the same branch and fails identically. Confirmed from source / The ternary at 1903-1904 is exactly as quoted: `string origin = (on_replay ? stashed : (InpSymbol == "" ? _Symbol : InpSymbol));`. InpSymbol appears only in the non-replay branch, so on the replay chart it is unreachable, while the failure message at 1915-1917 names it as one of two remedies. Nothing downstream re-consults InpSymbol before the 1911 guard. This is a provable message/behaviour mismatch, not an MQL5 semantics question. Severity lowered from MEDIUM: the defect is a misdirecting diagnostic with a manual escape (switch the chart symbol back by hand) that the message also offers; the stranding itself is host-expert-1's impact, not this one's / The ternary at 1903-1904 is exactly as quoted: when on_replay is true, origin is unconditionally `stashed` and InpSymbol is consulted only in the else branch, while the message at 1915-1917 offers InpSymbol as the remedy. So the printed advice is provably false. Downgraded from MEDIUM on impact: the same sentence's FIRST remedy ('Attach SS Replay to a normal chart') does work, and switching the chart's symbol back by hand is a two-click operation, so the user is not trapped - they are sent down one dead end before finding the live one. No money, data or safety consequence; the defect is a misleading message plus a missed one-line workaround for host-expert-1

#### `host-expert-14` — The setup panel's drag handler can never fire, because mouse-move events are not enabled until the main panel is created

**`MQL5/Experts/SSReplay/SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2069`** · category: event-handling

```mql5
         g_setup_ui.Create(ChartID(), sv);

         g_picking = true;
         EventSetMillisecondTimer(200);
         return INIT_SUCCEEDED;
```

**What goes wrong:** The setup window that accompanies the start picker is written to be dragged by its caption, and that is its only event-driven behaviour, but during the picking phase nothing has turned CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE on for the chart. CSSRSetupPanel::OnChartEvent returns immediately for every event id except CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE, so the host's forwarding call at 3110 always returns false and the window cannot be moved. Its buttons still work because the timer polls OBJPROP_STATE, so the failure is silent: a window that looks draggable and is not.

**Why:** The only writer of that chart property in the whole Include tree is the main panel: `ChartSetInteger(m_chart, CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE, true)` at Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:324, inside Create, which the host calls at 1453 - i.e. after the picker has finished (grep -rn CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE returns only Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:318, 324, 370). CSSRSetupPanel::OnChartEvent starts `if(!m_open || id != CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE) return false;` (Ui/SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:958-959). Line 370 of the panel restores the property to whatever it was before, so a previous session does not leave it enabled either.

**Verifiers:** Traced both halves. CSSRSetupPanel::OnChartEvent opens `if(!m_open || id != CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE) return false;` (SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:958-959), so the drag handler is the only event-driven behaviour and it needs mouse-move events. A grep of the whole tree finds CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE written only in Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh (318 save, 324 set true inside Create, 370 restore), and Create is reached from the host at 1467 - after the picker has returned INIT_SUCCEEDED at 2073. CSSRSetupPanel::Create (SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:481-) touches only CHART_MOUSE_SCROLL (970, 983), never the event property. Panel.Destroy restores the prior value, so an earlier session does not leave it enabled. The host's forwarding call at 3117 is correctly placed ahead of the g_ready gate, so forwarding is not what blocks it - the chart simply never emits the event. Buttons still work because the timer polls OBJPROP_STATE, so the failure is silent as described / Verified against MQL5 event semantics. CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE is delivered only when CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE is enabled on the chart, and it defaults to false. A repo-wide grep finds that property written in exactly one place: Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:324 inside CSSRPanel::Create (with the prior value saved at 318 and restored at 370 in Destroy), and the host calls Create at 1453 - after the picker has returned INIT_SUCCEEDED at 2073. CSSRSetupPanel::OnChartEvent opens `if(!m_open || id != CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE) return false;` (Ui/SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:957-958), so the host's forward at 3110 can only ever return false and the caption drag is dead during the one phase the window exists. Its buttons still work because the 200 ms timer polls OBJPROP_STATE via g_setup_ui.Poll() (2513), so the failure is silent. Minor caveat that does not change the verdict: CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE is persisted chart state, so a chart left true by a run that died without OnDeinit could make the drag work by accident - on a clean chart it cannot / Confirmed from source. grep over MQL5/ finds CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE written in exactly one place, CSSRPanel::Create (Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:318-324), with Destroy restoring the previous value at 370; CSSRSetupPanel::Create does not set it, and OnInit returns INIT_SUCCEEDED at 2073 long before g_panel.Create at 1453. CSSRSetupPanel::OnChartEvent returns false for every id except CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE (Ui/SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:956-959), so the host's forwarding call at 3106 can never succeed and the caption drag is dead during the picking phase. Impact is exactly LOW: the form's buttons work (the 200 ms timer polls OBJPROP_STATE at 2494) so nothing is unreachable, and the cost is a window the user cannot move off the candles they are choosing a start from. One caveat that does not rescue the code: the property is per-chart and only ever restored to its prior value, so a chart where some other program left mouse-move enabled would happen to be draggable - that is luck, not a guard

#### `host-expert-9` — The one-shot spread diagnostic is consumed by the start-picker phase and then permanently suppressed for the real session

**`MQL5/Experts/SSReplay/SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2260`** · category: spread

```mql5
   if(total < 120 && g_timer_ticks < 500)
      return;

   g_spread_reported = true;
```

**What goes wrong:** ReportSpreadOnce() is called as the second statement of OnTimer (2455), before the g_picking guard, and while the picker is up the timer runs at 200 ms (2072). A user who spends more than 100 seconds scrolling to choose a start - which is the entire purpose of the picker - reaches g_timer_ticks == 500 with the controller not yet loaded, so total == 0, the latch g_spread_reported is set to true and the log gets 'no bar has been replayed yet - press Play, or raise the speed, and this line will answer itself'. BuildSession resets g_timer_ticks (1577) but never resets g_spread_reported, so the line the comment block at 2213-2222 calls 'the one that says whether this history can show you a release at all' is never printed for that session.

**Why:** g_spread_reported is declared at 2222 and assigned only at 2263; no reset exists anywhere (grep shows two occurrences in the file). g_ctrl.SpreadBarsRecorded()/SpreadBarsFixed() return 0 before Load (Core/SSR_ReplayController.mqh:657-658 forward to the synthesizer's counters), and EffectiveFidelity() defaults to SYNTHETIC_TICK (Core/SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh:69-73), so the 'no bar has been replayed yet' branch is the one taken. 500 ticks x 200 ms = 100 s; the comment beside the condition reasons about 'twenty seconds' at the 40 ms engine interval and did not account for the picker's slower timer.

**Verifiers:** Trace holds. ReportSpreadOnce() is called at 2458, before the g_picking block at ~2470, and g_timer_ticks++ is the first statement of OnTimer, so it counts picker ticks; the picker arms a 200 ms timer at 2072. At 500 ticks (100 s) the condition `total < 120 && g_timer_ticks < 500` fails, g_spread_reported is set true at 2263, and with the controller unloaded total==0 and EffectiveFidelity defaults to SSR_FIDELITY_SYNTHETIC_TICK (SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh:69-73, and SpreadBarsRecorded/Fixed forward to the synthesizer counters via SSR_ReplayController.mqh:657-658) so the 'no bar has been replayed yet' branch prints. grep shows g_spread_reported has exactly two occurrences (2232 declaration, 2263 assignment) - no reset anywhere, and BuildSession resets only g_timer_ticks (1577). The build happens in the same program instance, so the latch is already spent when the real session starts / Verified by grep: g_spread_reported occurs exactly twice in the file - declared at 2232, set true at 2263 - with no reset anywhere, while BuildSession resets g_timer_ticks at 1577.
