ReportSpreadOnce() is the second statement of OnTimer (2458), before the g_picking block at 2470, and g_timer_ticks is incremented unconditionally at 2454. The picker arms a 200 ms timer at 2072, so g_timer_ticks reaches 500 after 100 s of picking with the controller unloaded (SpreadBarsRecorded/Fixed forward to the synthesizer's zeroed counters, Core/SSR_ReplayController.mqh:657-658), total==0, the latch is set and the session's one spread diagnostic is spent on a message about a replay that has not started. Which of the two total==0 branches prints depends on the unloaded controller's EffectiveFidelity(), but the latch is set either way, so the outcome does not turn on that / Confirmed: ReportSpreadOnce() is OnTimer's second statement (2456), ahead of the g_picking block at 2465; the picker runs the timer at 200 ms (2072); the latch condition at 2260 releases once g_timer_ticks reaches 500, i.e. 100 s of picking, with the controller unloaded so recorded+fixed==0; g_spread_reported is assigned at 2263 and, per grep, is never reset anywhere, while BuildSession resets only g_timer_ticks (1577). So a user who takes more than 100 s to choose a start loses that session's spread line and gets the misleading 'no bar has been replayed yet' instead. Impact is confined to one diagnostic log line: nothing the engine does changes, the spread actually used is unaffected, and the same facts are still visible on the panel's fidelity indicator. LOW is right (borderline IMPROVEMENT); the reason it is not dismissed is that this line is the one the file itself designates as the evidence to send when reporting a fault, so losing it costs a support round trip

#### `host-expert-13` — The picker path rebuilds with InpOneChart, discarding the one_chart_ok poison OnInit had just read

**`MQL5/Experts/SSReplay/SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2555`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
         if(!BuildSession(g_origin, false, InpOneChart))
           {
            Print("[host] the session could not be built from that start. "
                  "Remove SS Replay and try another point.");
            return;
           }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Doubly latent, narrower than the finding allows: in the shipped build the poison can never be written at all, because FailInit only stashes '!'+g_origin when g_on_replay_chart && g_origin != "", and today's pass-2 failure happens precisely with g_origin == "" (host-expert-1). one_chart_ok therefore always equals InpOneChart and the discarded flag never differs until host-expert-1 is fixed.

**Why:** one_chart_ok is a function-local in OnInit, never stored in a global, and the timer path at 2555 has no access to it. Reachability is narrow: the poison is only written when a second pass failed (825), and on such a recovery run the SSR_PICK_HANDOFF object usually still supplies g_pick_msc so the picker is skipped and the correct one_chart_ok reaches BuildSession at 2080. It is reachable when no pick stash is present (InpPickStart turned on after the fact, or the stash cleared by a REASON_REMOVE deinit at 2114 while the handoff survived). Low severity for that reason, but the discarded flag is unambiguous.

**Verifiers:** Confirmed: one_chart_ok is a function-local declared at 1893 inside OnInit, is never stored in a global, and the picker's build site at 2555 passes InpOneChart instead. So a run whose poison had just forced two-window mode would attempt the handover again if it reached the build through the picker rather than through 2080 / The code defect is provable: one_chart_ok is a function-local in OnInit (1893), is stored in no global, and the picker's deferred build at 2555 passes InpOneChart instead, so a run the poison told not to attempt the handover would attempt it. Kept as a finding because the discarded flag is unambiguous and the divergence matters the moment host-expert-1 is fixed. Severity dropped to LOW because the failure the claim describes is not reachable in the shipped build / The discarded flag is unambiguous: one_chart_ok is a function-local in OnInit (1893-1901), the picker returns INIT_SUCCEEDED at 2073, and the deferred build at 2555 passes InpOneChart, which no longer carries the poison. But the state is currently unreachable twice over. The poison is only ever written by FailInit, whose branch at 825 requires g_on_replay_chart && g_origin != "" - and host-expert-1 guarantees g_origin is "" on every pass 2, so the '!' prefix is never written at all in this build. Even after host-expert-1 is fixed, reaching line 2555 with a live poison also requires the SSR_PICK_HANDOFF stash to be absent while the handoff object survived. Downgraded from LOW to IMPROVEMENT accordingly: a correctness cleanup (hoist the flag into a global), not a defect any user can currently meet

#### `chart-6` — Bookmark vertical lines are never deleted and survive on the user's own chart after the session

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Chart/SSR_ChartManager.mqh:170`** · category: object-lifecycle

```mql5
      string base = "SSR_MARK_" + IntegerToString((int)when);
      for(int i = 0; i < m_count; i++)
        {
         long id = m_charts[i].id;
         if(id == 0)
            continue;
```

**What goes wrong:** State: one-chart mode (default), user presses B a few times during a session (GroupPort:490 -> MarkTime), then removes the EA. CSSRChartManager offers no ClearMarks(); grep for SSR_MARK_ finds exactly one hit in the whole tree - the creation site - and ObjectsDeleteAll is called only for SSR_NEWS_ (CalendarLines:116) and the widget prefix (SSR_Widgets.mqh:639). At teardown the host does not close this chart: it hands it back to the origin symbol (Expert:2194, ChartSetSymbolPeriod(ChartID(), g_origin, _Period)), and chart objects survive a symbol change and are saved with the chart. Outcome: dashed vertical lines at the bookmarked timestamps stay on the user's live origin chart indefinitely, one set per session, removable only by hand (they are HIDDEN=true, so they do not even appear in the terminal's object list where a user would look for them).

**Why:** MarkTime creates OBJ_VLINE objects with OBJPROP_HIDDEN=true (line 184) and no deletion path exists in this class or any caller. OnDeinit deletes the panel (g_panel.Destroy()), the calendar lines (g_cal_lines.Clear(), Expert:2120) and the trade lines (CSSRTradeLines destructor -> Clear()), but nothing named SSR_MARK_.

**Verifiers:** Traced and confirmed. MarkTime (SSR_ChartManager.mqh:166-190) is the only writer of the SSR_MARK_ prefix in the tree (grep: one hit, the creation site). CSSRChartManager exposes no deletion entry point - the only ClearMarks() in the tree is CSSRSnapshotStore::ClearMarks (SSR_SnapshotStore.mqh:191), which clears a counter, not objects. ObjectsDeleteAll is called for exactly two prefixes (SSR_CalendarLines.mqh:116, SSR_Widgets.mqh:639), neither of which matches. In one-chart mode the marks land on the EA's own chart (Sync matches on symbol alone, line 344, and g_replay_chart == ChartID() at SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1276); OnDeinit hands that chart back with ChartSetSymbolPeriod(ChartID(), g_origin, _Period) at 2192 rather than closing it, and chart objects outlive a symbol change, so the OBJ_VLINEs persist on the user's live chart. OBJPROP_HIDDEN=true (line 184) does suppress them from the terminal's object list. Severity reduced to LOW: the residue is cosmetic and carries no data or trading consequence / Verified by exhaustive grep: "SSR_MARK_" appears at exactly one place in the tree, the creation site (SSR_ChartManager.mqh:170); there is no ClearMarks/ObjectDelete path for it. The only ObjectsDeleteAll calls in production are SSR_CAL_PREFIX (SSR_CalendarLines.mqh:116) and CSSRWidgets::RemoveAll with each component's own prefix - the panel's is "SSRP_" (SSR_Panel.mqh:276), which does not prefix-match "SSR_MARK_", so no purge reaches these objects. In one-chart mode Sync matches on symbol alone (line 344), so the host chart is registered and MarkTime draws on it; teardown hands that same chart back to the origin symbol (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2194 ChartSetSymbolPeriod(ChartID(), g_origin, _Period)) rather than closing it, and MQL5 graphical objects are chart-bound, not symbol-bound, so the OBJ_VLINEs survive the symbol change and are saved with the chart. The object-property detail also holds and makes it worse: OBJPROP_HIDDEN=true (line 184) suppresses the object from the terminal's Object List, and OBJPROP_SELECTABLE=false (line 183) prevents clicking it on the chart, so manual removal is genuinely awkward / Provable from source. MarkTime (166-190) is the only SSR_MARK_ site in the tree; ObjectsDeleteAll exists only for SSR_CAL_PREFIX (SSR_CalendarLines.mqh:116) and the widget prefix (SSR_Widgets.mqh:639); OnDeinit hands the host chart back with ChartSetSymbolPeriod(ChartID(), g_origin, _Period) rather than closing it. So the OBJ_VLINEs survive on the user's own chart. Reachable in the default one-chart mode via GroupPort::Bookmark (490). Aggravating: OBJPROP_HIDDEN=true plus SELECTABLE=false means they do not appear in the terminal's Object List, so normal UI removal is awkward. Downgraded to LOW under the impact lens: this is inert visual residue - no money arithmetic, no future-data exposure, no effect on the virtual-trading guarantee - accumulating at one line per bookmark press per session at historical timestamps the user must scroll back to even see

#### `chart-17` — MarkTime puts the registry index in the object name, so bookmarks duplicate after a chart closes

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Chart/SSR_ChartManager.mqh:176`** · category: object-lifecycle

```mql5
         string n = base + "_" + IntegerToString(i);
         if(ObjectFind(id, n) < 0 && !ObjectCreate(id, n, OBJ_VLINE, 0, when, 0))
            continue;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: MarkTime derives the object-name suffix from the registry loop index, which Remove() reassigns when a chart closes, so re-marking an already-marked timestamp can create a second OBJ_VLINE on a chart that already carries one. Both lines sit at the identical datetime with identical style, so they render exactly coincident and are not visually distinguishable - the cost is duplicate, undeletable chart objects and a name that is not a stable per-chart identity, not two visible lines.

**Why:** Remove() renumbers by copying entries down, and the name is rebuilt from the current loop index on every call. Combined with chart-6 (nothing deletes SSR_MARK_*), the duplicates are permanent.

**Verifiers:** The mechanism is provable. The object name is rebuilt from the live loop index each call (line 176, `base + "_" + IntegerToString(i)`), and Remove() compacts the array by copying entries down (lines 102-105), so a chart's index is not stable across a chart close. With two charts marked at time t and the first closed, the second holds SSR_MARK_t_1 and the ObjectFind guard on line 177 then misses when the same t is re-marked under its new index 0, creating a second OBJ_VLINE. Combined with the absence of any deletion path (chart-6) the duplicates accumulate. The visible outcome is overstated, which is why this stays LOW rather than a user-facing defect / The mechanism is provable: the name suffix is the current loop index (line 176), Remove() renumbers by copying entries down (98-106), and ObjectFind is per-chart, so after a registry shift the same chart is asked for a name it does not yet own and ObjectCreate succeeds, leaving both SSR_MARK_<t>_1 and SSR_MARK_<t>_0 on one chart. The index is indeed not a chart identity. The stated user-visible consequence is overstated, though: two OBJ_VLINEs at the same datetime with identical colour, STYLE_DASH and width are exactly coincident, so the user sees one line, not two - the real cost is silent object accumulation, which only matters because of chart-6 / The mechanism is real (Remove() at 98-106 renumbers, the suffix is rebuilt from the loop index) but the state is both hard to reach and unobservable. Reaching it needs: two or more registered replay charts, one of them closed mid-session, and then the SAME datetime bookmarked again - which means rewinding to the identical second, since base is IntegerToString((int)when). And the product of the duplication is a second OBJ_VLINE with identical time, colour, STYLE_DASH, width and BACK on the same chart: it renders exactly on top of the first, so the user observes nothing at all. No count, no arithmetic and no guarantee is affected; the only cost is an extra hidden object. This is a naming-hygiene nit, not a defect the user can see

#### `chart-14` — OpenLayout counts each chart twice against SSR_MAX_CHARTS

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Chart/SSR_ChartManager.mqh:234`** · category: boundary

```mql5
         if(m_count + opened >= SSR_MAX_CHARTS)
            break;                        // the ceiling, honoured quietly
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The double count holds regardless of how synchronously ChartOpen publishes the chart to ChartFirst/ChartSymbol: if the new chart is visible to its own Sync, m_count = 1+opened; if it lags by one pass, m_count = opened - either way m_count grows in step with `opened`, so the effective ceiling is ~SSR_MAX_CHARTS/2 (8 opens) rather than 16. The finder's POTENTIAL_RISK hedge on ChartOpen timing is unnecessary.

**Why:** OpenChart calls Sync() at line 213 before returning the id, and Sync's discovery branch (346-370) Adds any chart whose symbol matches, incrementing m_count. Whether the newly opened chart is visible to ChartSymbol() on that same Sync depends on how synchronously the terminal completes ChartOpen, which cannot be settled from source - hence POTENTIAL_RISK rather than confirmed; if it is visible (the likely case, since ChartOpen returns the id), the ceiling is halved.

**Verifiers:** Confirmed and in fact less runtime-dependent than the finder allowed. OpenChart (204-215) calls Sync() at line 213, whose discovery branch Adds any chart carrying m_symbol and increments m_count (lines 344-350, Add at 80-96). OpenLayout's guard then adds `opened` on top of the m_count that already includes those charts (line 234). The sum therefore grows by 2 per chart opened, so with one pre-existing registered chart the break fires after 8 opens instead of 15 - silently, as the comment concedes. Add() at line 82 is the real ceiling, so nothing is corrupted; only charts the user asked for are quietly not opened. LOW: requires an InpExtraTfs list of 8+ supported timeframes to be observable / The double count is provable from source and does not depend on how synchronously ChartOpen completes. OpenChart (204-215) ends with Sync(), whose discovery branch Adds any chart whose ChartSymbol matches m_symbol (344-349), incrementing m_count; OpenLayout then also increments its own `opened`. Even if the just-opened chart is not yet enumerable by ChartFirst/ChartNext on that same Sync, the previously opened ones are, so m_count tracks `opened` to within one and the guard `m_count + opened >= SSR_MAX_CHARTS` (line 234) trips at roughly half the ceiling. Concretely, with the primary chart already registered, the break fires after about 7-8 extra charts instead of 15, silently and with no message. SSR_MAX_CHARTS is 16 (SSR_ChartTypes.mqh:33). Low severity only because it needs an InpExtraTfs list of eight or more supported timeframes / The double count is visible in source (OpenChart:204-215 calls Sync() before returning, and Sync's discovery branch Adds by symbol match, so m_count already carries the new chart when 'opened' is incremented). But the impact is negligible. With the host chart registered, the guard breaks after 7 successful opens instead of 15 - and the file's own comment calls 16 'well above any sane multi-chart layout'. Opening 8+ charts of ONE replay symbol is outside normal use of a 310px-rail single-instrument replay desk; Add() independently enforces the real ceiling so nothing overflows, no array is written past its end, and no state is corrupted. The worst observable outcome is that a user who lists more than seven extra timeframes quietly gets seven. Downgraded from LOW to IMPROVEMENT. Kept as POTENTIAL_RISK rather than CONFIRMED only because whether ChartFirst/ChartSymbol see the just-opened chart within the same Sync is terminal behaviour

#### `chart-10` — A detached chart has no user-visible surface: the observer is never wired and charts_detached is never rendered

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Chart/SSR_ChartManager.mqh:464`** · category: event-handling

```mql5
            ChartSetInteger(m_charts[i].id, CHART_AUTOSCROLL, false);
            if(m_observer != NULL)
               m_observer.OnUserScrolled(m_charts[i].id);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: A detached chart has no STATE indicator: the observer hook is wired only in T4, and charts_detached is published into the port state and never rendered. The key hint is not missing - the Session tab prints "F follow" via SSR_S_KEYS_3 - so the defect is the absent detached-state readout, which matters chiefly if a detach ever happens without the user causing it.

**Why:** Confirmed by absence of callers for both channels, plus the deliberate CHART_AUTOSCROLL=false at line 463, which means the terminal will not recover on its own either.

**Verifiers:** Both channels verified dead. SetObserver (line 141) has exactly one caller in the tree and it is a test (Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T4_ChartIntegration.mq5:92), so m_observer is NULL in the EA and the OnUserScrolled call at line 465 - like every other observer call at 369, 400, 419, 487 - is a no-op in production. charts_detached is written once (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:122) and read nowhere: grep finds only the declaration (SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:77), its Init (257) and that assignment. The Follow button that would have carried the count was deliberately removed from both layouts (SSR_Panel.mqh:1189, 1241, and the comment at 1237-1240), leaving only the static 'F follow' line in the Session tab keyboard help (SSR_Strings.mqh:503). So a latched detach - with CHART_AUTOSCROLL forced false at line 463, i.e. no terminal-side recovery - produces no state indication anywhere in the panel. Severity reduced to LOW: this is a missing affordance, not a functional defect, and SSR_S_FOLLOW_N ('Follow %d  F') shows the count was designed for a control that no longer exists / Both absences are verified by grep. SetObserver has exactly one caller in the tree, SSR_T4_ChartIntegration.mq5:92, so m_observer is NULL in the EA and every observer callback is a no-op (all call sites are NULL-guarded, so this is a dead channel, not a crash). charts_detached appears only at its declaration (SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:77), its Init (257) and the assignment in GroupPort:122 - no panel code reads it. CHART_AUTOSCROLL=false at line 463 does mean the terminal will not recover on its own. The claim overreaches on one point: the Session tab that would carry the indicator already prints the keyboard group, and SSR_S_KEYS_3 is "S sessions   F follow   D fidelity   L lines   X flip" (SSR_Strings.mqh:502-503), so the F binding is on screen; what is missing is any indication that a chart is CURRENTLY detached. Also, in the normal case the detach is user-initiated, so the user knows why the chart stopped following; the gap only bites after a false detach (chart-1), which is itself unproven / The absence is provable: SetObserver has one caller in the whole tree and it is Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T4_ChartIntegration.mq5, so m_observer is NULL in production and OnUserScrolled is a no-op; charts_detached is assigned at SSR_GroupPort.mqh:122 and read nowhere; SSR_S_FOLLOW_N ('Follow %d  F') and SSR_S_ACT_FOLLOW_N exist but have zero consumers, and SSR_Panel.mqh:2717 records that the follow button was removed. So no live indicator of detach exists. But the claim 'the interface says nothing at all; the user has to already know that F re-attaches' overstates it: the Session sheet renders SSR_S_KEYS_3 = 'S sessions   F follow   D fidelity   L lines   X flip', so the remedy is printed in the panel, and any pause-play re-attaches via the host's FollowAll. Under the impact lens this is a missing status indicator on a dangling wire - informational only, no arithmetic, no leak, no safety consequence - so MEDIUM drops to LOW

#### `chart-7` — LeakGuard::Advice is written far longer than the 49 characters its only consumer can display, so the actionable half never reaches the user

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Chart/SSR_LeakGuard.mqh:93`** · category: string-length

```mql5
      if(m_report.origin_charts > 0)
         return StringFormat("%s is open on %d chart(s) - close it or your backtest is not blind",
                             m_origin, m_report.origin_charts);
      return StringFormat("%s is in Market Watch - its live price is visible", m_origin);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Advice() is 69 chars (chart case) and 53 chars (Market-Watch case) against 48 usable characters after the 13-char 'Charts' prefix and Clip's 62-char cap, so both lines are truncated with a trailing '~'. The cut falls after 'close it or your' - the actionable verb survives and the rationale is lost - not before 'close it' as the finding states, so the defect is a cosmetically truncated sentence rather than an unreachable instruction.

**Why:** Plain arithmetic on the format strings plus the 62-character Clip. The panel's own comment (SSR_Panel.mqh:1888-1891) records that this line 'has been ending "- close it or your" on the chart, mid-sentence' and clips it deliberately; clipping stopped the ragged cut, it did not make the sentence fit. The wording lives here, so this is where a 49-character message has to be authored.

**Verifiers:** The truncation is confirmed by arithmetic on the actual code. Clip (SSR_Panel.mqh:242-248) caps at 62 and, when over, returns StringSubstr(s,0,61)+"~". '%-12s ' on 'Charts' (SSR_Strings.mqh:433) is a 13-char prefix, so the advice gets 48 characters plus a tilde. The chart variant is 69 chars (not 68) and renders as 'Charts       EURUSD is open on 1 chart(s) - close it or your ~'; the Market-Watch variant is 53 chars (not 54) and renders as 'Charts       EURUSD is in Market Watch - its live price is vi~'. Both are cut, and the panel's own comment at 1888-1891 records that the clip was added to stop a ragged cut rather than to make the sentence fit, so the wording does need to be authored to fit 48 chars here in SSR_LeakGuard.mqh:93-96. Downgraded to LOW because the impact claim does not survive: in the chart case the actionable instruction 'close it' is fully visible and what is lost is the justification 'or your backtest is not blind'; only the Market-Watch case loses a load-bearing word ('visible'). The class's 'never silent when dirty' contract is met - the line is present and coloured SSR_C_HOLD - it just ends in a tilde / The arithmetic is settleable from source and holds. Advice() returns a 69-character string for the origin-charts case and 53 for the Market-Watch case; the sole consumer composes StringFormat("%-12s %s", T(SSR_S_CHARTS), advice) with T(SSR_S_CHARTS)=="Charts" (SSR_Strings.mqh:433) padded to 12 plus a space, giving 82 and 66 characters, and Clip(...,62) (SSR_Panel.mqh:242-248) caps at 62 by returning StringSubstr(s,0,61)+"~". Both variants are therefore truncated, leaving 48 characters of advice plus the "~" marker, and nothing else renders the leak state (other_live_charts and ToString() are log-only). The finding mis-states the cut point and the impact: the rendered text is "Charts       EURUSD is open on 1 chart(s) - close it or your ~", so the actionable instruction "close it" DOES survive - what is lost is the justification clause "backtest is not blind", and the "~" signals the truncation rather than looking like a broken sentence. Hence LOW, not MEDIUM / I redid the arithmetic and it refutes the claim. Clip (SSR_Panel.mqh:242-248) returns StringSubstr(s,0,61)+"~", and the prefix '%-12s ' with T(SSR_S_CHARTS)='Charts' is 13 chars, so 48 advice characters survive - not 49, but more importantly 'close it' occupies advice chars 36-43, well inside 48. The rendered line is 'Charts       EURUSD is open on 1 chart(s) - close it or your~': the symbol, the count, the dirty colour (SSR_C_HOLD) and the instruction all reach the user; only the justification tail 'backtest is not blind' is elided, with the '~' announcing the elision. The Market-Watch variant is 52 chars and renders '...its live price is vis~', which loses nothing operative either. So the class contract 'never silent when dirty' is met, the actionable half is NOT lost, and what remains is a wording-length nit on one line of the Session sheet - no arithmetic, no leak, no safety consequence

#### `chart-12` — Every word the Chart layer draws or hands to the panel is a hardcoded English literal, outside audit A19's reach

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Chart/SSR_TradeLines.mqh:134`** · category: utf8

```mql5
      ObjectSetString (m_chart, n, OBJPROP_TEXT, tip);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The Chart layer's on-chart wording is hardcoded English with no SSR_Strings entries and no T() in scope, so a translated session still shows English on the draggable lines, position levels and history tooltips. A19 misses it for two reasons, not one: the "/Ui/" path filter (tools/ssr_audit.py:1458) and, more fundamentally, that A19 only inspects widget draw calls by argument position (:1449) and never raw ObjectSetString(OBJPROP_TEXT) writes.

**Why:** The literals are in the source at the lines quoted; SSR_Strings.mqh has no entries for them; the audit's directory filter is explicit.

**Verifiers:** The literals are all present as cited: "STOP - drag me"/"TARGET - drag me" (:243-244, :277-278, :298-299), "ENTRY - drag me" (:200, :315), written to OBJPROP_TEXT at :134 with CHART_SHOW_OBJECT_DESCR forced on at :183; "BUY"/"SELL"/"SL"/"TP" and the "stop of %s ..." tooltips (:412-438); "#%d %s %s in at %s" (:546-564); and the English leak advice that reaches the panel (SSR_LeakGuard.mqh:94-96). grep confirms SSR_Strings.mqh has no entries for any of them, and this file includes only Common + Ui/SSR_Theme.mqh, so T() is not even in scope. The audit-mechanism explanation is however inaccurate: A19's literal scan matches widget draw calls (`(m_w.)?(Label|ButtonC|Button|Chip|Group|Toast|Edit|Text)(`, tools/ssr_audit.py:1449) and takes the text by argument position, so a raw ObjectSetString(..., OBJPROP_TEXT, ...) in the Chart layer would be invisible to it even if the "/Ui/" filter at :1458 were removed. No functional failure follows - this is an i18n coverage gap / Every literal and every audit fact checks out. The planning-line captions are English literals passed as `tip` and written to OBJPROP_TEXT at :134 - "STOP - drag me" / "TARGET - drag me" (:200, :243-244, :277-278, :298-299) and "ENTRY - drag me" (:190, :315) - with CHART_SHOW_OBJECT_DESCR forced on in Attach (:183), so they are painted on the chart rather than merely hovered. Position levels are likewise literal: side = "BUY"/"SELL" or the caller's `kind`, the "%s %s at %s" tip and the "SL"/"TP" labels (:411-438), and closed trades print "#%d %s %s in at %s" / "#%d out at %s" (:546-564). grep of SSR_Strings.mqh finds no catalogue entry for any of them, and the leak advice really does reach the panel in English (SSR_LeakGuard.mqh:94-96). The audit blindness is exact: A19's literal pass does `if "/Ui/" not in path.replace("\\", "/"): continue` (tools/ssr_audit.py:1458) before it looks at anything, and its vacuous-pass guard is `if seen[1] < 100` (:1491) counted over the Ui files alone, so it still passes. Two notes that widen rather than narrow the point: A19 also matches only Label/Button/ButtonC/Chip/Group/Toast/Edit/Text call sites, so a direct ObjectSetString(OBJPROP_TEXT) like :134 would be invisible to it even under /Ui/; and these captions are 15-16 characters, comfortably inside MetaTrader's 63-char OBJPROP_TEXT limit, so nothing is truncated. Severity is an i18n completeness gap, not a runtime fault / Verified directly: grep for T(SSR_S over MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Chart/ returns nothing at all, the literals are in the source at the quoted lines ("STOP - drag me" :243, "TARGET - drag me" :244, "ENTRY - drag me" :200/:315, BUY/SELL/SL/TP and the "stop of %s ..." / "#%d %s %s in at %s" tooltips :411-438, :546-564), Attach forces CHART_SHOW_OBJECT_DESCR on (:183) so the descriptions really are painted, SSR_Strings.mqh has no entry for them, and CSSRLeakGuard::Advice returns English prose to the panel (SSR_LeakGuard.mqh:94-96). The audit blind spot is also exactly as stated - tools/ssr_audit.py:1458 skips every path without '/Ui/' (and its call regex only matches m_w.Label/Button/... so raw ObjectSetString(OBJPROP_TEXT) would escape it even without the filter). Impact is a genuine localisation gap for the product's own target audience - the translated panel sits beside English captions on the only objects the user is asked to drag - but it is text-only: no arithmetic, no leaked future data, no trading-safety consequence. LOW confirmed

#### `chart-13` — The flight recorder truncates chart_id to 32 bits, corrupting the column that identifies which chart a fault happened on

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Common/SSR_FlightRecorder.mqh:256`** · category: boundary

```mql5
              s.chart_count, (int)s.chart_id,
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Confirmed for chart_id. The emit_ticks/emit_calls half is far weaker: those would need to exceed 2.1 billion in one recording (over 268 million bars at the default 8 ticks/bar) before wrapping, so in practice chart_id is the only column that truncates.

**Why:** The truncation is in the source; the %d specifier forces a 32-bit argument, so the fix is %I64d or IntegerToString rather than the cast. The claim that real chart ids exceed 32 bits is an inference from MT5's id scheme, not from this tree - but the cast is lossy for any id above 2^31 regardless.

**Verifiers:** The truncation is plainly in the source. SSRFlightSample.chart_id is a long (SSR_FlightRecorder.mqh:58), filled from g_charts.IdAt(0) at Standalone.mq5:2408-2409, and Write formats it as (int)s.chart_id against a %d specifier (SSR_FlightRecorder.mqh:243, 256), so any id above 2^31-1 is written wrapped and often negative into the chart_id column of the diagnostic artefact. The finding is honest that "real chart ids exceed 2^31" is an inference about MT5's id scheme rather than evidence from this tree - but the cast is lossy for such ids regardless, and the fix (%I64d or IntegerToString) needs no runtime knowledge. The same (int) cast is applied to emit_calls/emit_ticks/seed_bars/truncations at 254-255 / The cast is real and lossy: FlightRecorder:256 passes (int)s.chart_id into a %d slot of the sample row, and s.chart_id is filled from g_charts.IdAt(0), a long chart id (Standalone:2407-2409). MQL5's StringFormat %d takes a 32-bit argument, so a long id has to be widened to %I64d or routed through IntegerToString rather than truncated; C-style truncation of a value above 2^31 yields a wrong and frequently negative number in the one column that says which window a fault occurred on, in the artefact the author depends on for remote diagnosis. The one thing I cannot settle from this tree is the magnitude of real MT5 chart ids - that comes from MT5's time-derived id scheme, not from source - but the cast is unconditionally lossy above 2^31 whatever the distribution, and no other code in the tree ever narrows a chart id this way. I do refute the extension of the claim to the instrumentation counters: (int)s.emit_ticks and (int)s.emit_calls would need 2^31 ticks in one recording (at 8 ticks per bar that is ~268M bars), which SSR_FLIGHT_MAX_ROWS-capped sessions cannot plausibly reach, so those casts are not a live defect. Severity LOW: a diagnostic column, not behaviour / The cast is in the source and is lossy: the host fills s.chart_id from g_charts.IdAt(0), a long (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2407-2409), and Write() passes (int)s.chart_id against a %d specifier (SSR_FlightRecorder.mqh:243, 256). But the consequence the finding claims - a recording that "carries a chart id that matches nothing the user or the log can show" - does not survive checking: nothing in this tree ever prints a full chart id for the reader to correlate against (no %I64d chart-id line in the host or the chart manager), MT5 does not show chart ids in its UI either, and the truncation is deterministic, so the column still distinguishes one chart from another within a recording, which is all the diagnostic uses it for. The companion claim about emit_ticks/emit_calls is not reachable: overflowing a 32-bit int needs >2.1e9 emitted ticks in one recording, against a synthesiser default of 8 ticks per M1 bar and a recorder capped at 5000 rows (~40 minutes). Diagnostic-file cosmetics, no user-facing effect - IMPROVEMENT

#### `core-sync-3` — Generic CSSRBarProvider::NextBarOpen reads through the guarded ReadBars: it is useless inside a gap and raises up to four 'future access' violations per call

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_IDataSource.mqh:160`** · category: future-data-leakage

```mql5
      for(int i = 0; i < 4; i++)
        {
         int n = ReadBars(symbol, after_msc + 1, after_msc + spans[i], tmp);
         if(n > 0)
            return SSRToMsc(tmp[0].time);
        }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Same defect, two corrections. (1) The ClampRange snippet is at SSR_FutureGuard.mqh:90-106 (Violation at 80-84), not 312-316 - that file is only 156 lines. (2) The blast radius is narrower than the claim implies and should be read as test-harness-only: CSSRMt5BarProvider overrides NextBarOpen with a times-only CopyTime walk (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:302+), and grep shows CSSRMemoryDataSource is referenced only by MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/* - no production path uses the generic implementation, so the inflated Violations() count cannot reach real UI vitals today and the 'indistinguishable from a real leak' alarm applies to the test/QA harness. The functional half is also masked in tests: StepBars falls back to base = SSRNextBarOpenMsc(now, PERIOD_M1) (line 1165), so dense in-memory data still advances correctly - only gap-skipping is silently lost. Design smell plus a latent trap for any future non-MT5 source; not a live product bug.

**Why:** SSR_FutureGuard.mqh:312-316: 'if(from_msc > m_horizon_msc) { Violation(from_msc); return false; }'. Memory provider ReadBars (SSR_MemoryDataSource.mqh:122-127) calls GuardRange first and returns 0 when it is false. The comment at lines 143-148 acknowledges the INVALID answer ('correctly, and uselessly') but not the violation count. Production is unaffected because CSSRMt5BarProvider overrides with CopyTime (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:302). RECOMMENDATION: make the generic implementation a times-only walk that bypasses the guard (a bar's open time is schedule, not price — the Mt5 override already argues this) or have it not count violations.

**Verifiers:** The mechanism is provable and I could not find a guard that prevents it. CSSRBarProvider::NextBarOpen (SSR_IDataSource.mqh:150-165) calls the virtual ReadBars with from = after_msc + 1 for all four spans. CSSRMemoryBarProvider::ReadBars (Sources/SSR_MemoryDataSource.mqh:113-125) calls GuardRange first and returns 0 when it is false; GuardRange forwards to CSSRFutureGuard::ClampRange, which does `if(from_msc > m_horizon_msc) { Violation(from_msc); return false; }`. The callers do pass after_msc == now: CSSRReplayController::StepBars calls NextTimelineBarOpen(m_clock.now_msc) at line 1166 BEFORE m_guard.SetHorizon(m_clock.now_msc) at 1175, and the horizon is already == now from the previous pump/step (SetHorizon at 1009/1084/1175/1244/1361) or == timeline.start_msc at READY (Arm at 832). So from = now + 1 > horizon for all four spans: four Violation() increments and SSR_INVALID_TIME every time, whether or not there is a gap. The guard does reach this provider - the generic CSSRDataSource::SetGuard (SSR_IDataSource.mqh:244-253) is invoked from the controller at line 573. Two corrections below / Every link verified. CSSRBarProvider::NextBarOpen (SSR_IDataSource.mqh:150-165) calls ReadBars(symbol, after_msc + 1, after_msc + spans[i], tmp) four times. CSSRMemoryBarProvider::ReadBars (Core/Sources/SSR_MemoryDataSource.mqh:115-124) calls GuardRange first and returns 0 when it is false, and CSSRFutureGuard::ClampRange refuses with Violation(from_msc) whenever from_msc > m_horizon_msc (SSR_FutureGuard.mqh:90-96). The horizon equals the clock: SetHorizon(now) on every Pump (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1009) and PumpTo (:1084). StepBars calls NextTimelineBarOpen(m_clock.now_msc) at :1166 BEFORE SetHorizon at :1175, so from_msc = now + 1 > horizon for all four spans - four violations, answer always SSR_INVALID_TIME. CSSRMasterClock multiplies it: NextBarAcross loops every member calling NextTimelineBarOpen (SSR_MasterClock.mqh:329-334) from StepBars (:348) and the idle path (:211), so it is 4 x member count per call, and those counters are summed into the UI vitals (SSR_GroupPort.mqh: out.guard_violations += m.Violations()). Severity LOW and latent, as the finding itself says: CSSRMt5BarProvider overrides with a times-only CopyTime walk (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:302-320), so production never takes this path - the exposure is the memory provider used in tests and any future CSV/external source / The mechanism is accurately described but the state is unreachable in the product, and the base class already specifies the contract. SSR_IDataSource.mqh:143-148 states in terms that 'a provider whose ReadBars is clamped by the future guard will answer INVALID from inside a gap - correctly, and uselessly. Such a provider must override this with a TIMES-ONLY read', and the shipped provider does exactly that: CSSRMt5BarProvider::NextBarOpen uses CopyTime and bypasses the guard (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:302-322). The only class that inherits the generic body is CSSRMemoryBarProvider, and CSSRMemoryDataSource is referenced solely from MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/* - never from MQL5/Experts (the host holds CSSRMt5DataSource g_src / g_src2 at SSReplayStandalone.mq5:189,298) and never from QA/SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5. So the UI-vitals leak alarm the finding worries about is fed only by the MT5 provider and cannot be inflated by this path, and the guard is never falsely tripped in normal use. In the test harness the consequence is also nil: StepBars falls back to base = SSRNextBarOpenMsc (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1165-1168), which is correct for the dense synthetic data those tests use. The residual value is a hardening/hygiene suggestion for a hypothetical future CSV source, not a defect. Note the cited guard lines are wrong - ClampRange is SSR_FutureGuard.mqh:90-106, not 312-316

#### `core-sync-4` — CSSRMetrics accumulates the controller's cumulative bar counter as if it were per-pump, so m_bars / SSRPerfSnapshot.bars grow quadratically

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_Metrics.mqh:108`** · category: cpu

```mql5
      m_pumps++;
      m_ticks += ticks;
      m_bars  += bars;
      if(deferred)
         m_deferred++;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: RecordPump sums the controller's cumulative bar_count as if it were per-pump, so m_bars grows quadratically - but SSRPerfSnapshot.bars has no reader anywhere in the tree and is not printed by ToString, so the wrong value is never observed. Latent inconsistency only; no overflow risk (m_bars is long).

**Why:** SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1033-1035 'm_metrics.RecordPump((double)(GetMicrosecondCount() - t_pump), m_pump_emit_us, emitted, (int)m_cursor.bar_count, m_pump_deferred);' and identically at 1108-1110. `emitted` in the same call IS per-pump, so the two arguments are inconsistent. RECOMMENDATION: pass bars_used from EmitWindow (or the delta of bar_count) and, separately, fix bar_count to count distinct bars.

**Verifiers:** Both call sites pass the cumulative counter: SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1033-1035 and 1108-1110 pass `(int)m_cursor.bar_count`, while `emitted` in the same argument list is per-pump. SSRReplayCursor::Advance does `bar_count += bars` (SSR_ReplayCursor.mqh:65), so bar_count is a running total, and CSSRMetrics::RecordPump does `m_bars += bars` (SSR_Metrics.mqh:108) - i.e. m_bars accumulates a sum of running totals, growing quadratically in pumps. bar_count is itself inflated because EmitWindow re-consumes the clock's own bar every pump (bars_used=1 per pump, 437-472/531). Confirmed harmless today: grep finds no consumer of SSRPerfSnapshot.bars (GroupPort reads only calibrated/us_per_tick/p95-style fields), and m_bars is a long, so it goes absurd rather than negative / Provable: RecordPump sums its `bars` argument into m_bars (SSR_Metrics.mqh:102-108, documented 'One pump completed'), and both call sites pass the cumulative (int)m_cursor.bar_count (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1033-1035 and 1108-1110) alongside a per-pump `emitted`. SSRReplayCursor::Advance only ever adds to bar_count (SSR_ReplayCursor.mqh:60-66), so after P pumps m_bars is the sum of running totals - quadratic, and inconsistent with m_ticks in the same struct. Snapshot copies it to SSRPerfSnapshot.bars (line 182). No overflow to negative is possible (m_bars is long; the int cast only matters above 2^31 bar_count). Impact is confined because no consumer reads perf.bars: CSSRGroupPort reads only calibrated/us_per_tick/pump_p95_ms (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:130-134), SSRPerfSnapshot::ToString omits bars, and T7 only checks percentiles/calibration / The code does exactly what is claimed: both call sites pass the cumulative counter (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1033-1035 and :1108-1110 pass (int)m_cursor.bar_count) into an accumulator (SSR_Metrics.mqh:108 m_bars += bars), while the neighbouring `emitted` argument is per-pump, so m_bars is a sum of running totals. But the impact is nil, not LOW: I grepped every consumer and SSRPerfSnapshot.bars has no reader anywhere - GroupPort takes only calibrated/us_per_tick/pump_p95 (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:130-135) and gets its displayed bar figure from m.BarsConsumed() instead (:143), SSRPerfSnapshot::ToString does not print bars (SSR_Metrics.mqh:55-58), and T7 never reads p.bars. m_bars is a long, so the quoted 2^31 argument does not apply either. Nothing a user or any code path can observe is wrong; this is a latent inconsistency in dead telemetry

#### `core-engine-9` — RecordPump is handed the cumulative bar_count as the per-pump bar figure, so CSSRMetrics.m_bars grows quadratically

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1035`** · category: cpu

```mql5
      m_metrics.RecordPump((double)(GetMicrosecondCount() - t_pump),
                           m_pump_emit_us, emitted,
                           (int)m_cursor.bar_count, m_pump_deferred);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate except the line citation: `m_bars += bars` is SSR_Metrics.mqh:108 (inside RecordPump at 103-110), not line 476 — that file is only 199 lines.

**Why:** Line 1035/1110 pass `(int)m_cursor.bar_count`; SSR_Metrics.mqh line 470-476 accumulates it. Contrast `emitted`, which is correctly per-pump.

**Verifiers:** Confirmed mechanically. Both call sites pass the cursor's running total as the per-pump figure: SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1035 and 1110 pass `(int)m_cursor.bar_count`, while CSSRMetrics::RecordPump accumulates `m_bars += bars` (SSR_Metrics.mqh:108) — contrast `m_ticks += ticks` on the line above, which is correctly fed the per-pump `emitted`. So m_bars becomes the sum of prefix totals, i.e. quadratic in pump count. Snapshot copies it to out.bars (SSR_Metrics.mqh:182) and PerfInto exposes it (1687... ctrl:687). I verified the blast radius claim too: the only PerfInto consumer outside tests is SSR_GroupPort.mqh:130-134, which reads only calibrated/us_per_tick/pump_p95_ms, and SSRPerfSnapshot::ToString (SSR_Metrics.mqh:52-58) omits bars — so it is diagnostics-only today, as claimed / Provable. CSSRMetrics::RecordPump does `m_bars += bars` (SSR_Metrics.mqh:102-108), and both call sites pass the cursor's running total: SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1035 and 1110 both hand it `(int)m_cursor.bar_count`, whereas `emitted` on the same lines is genuinely per-pump. So after k pumps m_bars is the sum of the k running totals, and Snapshot copies it into SSRPerfSnapshot.bars (SSR_Metrics.mqh:182). The impact bound is right too: SSRPerfSnapshot::ToString (SSR_Metrics.mqh:46-55) never prints bars, SSR_GroupPort.mqh:130-134 copies only perf.calibrated/us_per_tick/pump_p95_ms into SSRUiState, and grep finds no other reader, so today it is flight-recorder/diagnostic-only. Note it compounds with core-engine-5: bar_count is itself already inflated per pump, and the `(int)` cast at 1035/1110 truncates a long, so a long session can also overflow the parameter / The code fact holds: CSSRMetrics::RecordPump does m_bars += bars (SSR_Metrics.mqh:104-108) and both call sites pass (int)m_cursor.bar_count, the running total (controller lines 1035 and 1110), so m_bars becomes a sum of prefixes. But nothing reads it. SSRPerfSnapshot.bars is written at SSR_Metrics.mqh:182 and the only consumer of PerfInto in the product is SSR_GroupPort.mqh:130-134, which copies just calibrated, us_per_tick and pump_p95_ms; a repo-wide grep finds no other read of perf.bars outside SSR_T7_Performance.mq5, which does not assert on it. It also feeds no decision: UsPerTick uses m_emit_ticks_total, IsCalibrated uses m_filled. Zero user-observable effect and zero influence on emitted data, so under an impact lens this is below LOW

#### `core-engine-8` — RestoreSnapshot overwrites live settings (fidelity, speed, status, last_error) with the checkpoint's copy, desynchronising m_state from the policy object

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1630`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
      m_state    = snap.state;
      m_clock    = snap.clock;
      m_cursor   = snap.cursor;
      m_timeline = snap.timeline;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct, with one precision: m_clock IS restored (line 1631), so speed is not desynchronised between m_state and the clock — the speed issue is only that a per-stream speed the user has since changed is reverted and then persisted by SaveInto. The genuine desynchronisation is fidelity (m_state.fidelity reverted, CSSRFidelityPolicy::m_requested not), plus the reinstated last_error/last_error_text defeating ClearError(). Also worth noting on the same lines: status is assigned directly at 1630/1650-1651 rather than through Transition(), so CSSRReplaySink::OnStateChanged is never fired for a restore.

**Why:** SetFidelity (line 671-676) writes both m_state.fidelity and m_fidelity; RestoreSnapshot restores only m_state. Fidelity() accessor (line 628) returns m_state.fidelity. SSRReplayState fields at SSR_ReplayState.mqh lines 33-40.

**Verifiers:** Verified field by field. SSRReplayState carries status/speed_x100/data_mode/fidelity/last_error/last_error_text (SSR_ReplayState.mqh:32-39), TakeSnapshot copies the whole struct (1600), and RestoreSnapshot does `m_state = snap.state` at 1630 while restoring neither m_fidelity nor the master clock — SetFidelity writes both m_state.fidelity and m_fidelity.SetRequested (671-676), so the two diverge. The display consequence is exact: SSR_GroupPort.mqh:110 reads c.Fidelity() (= m_state.fidelity, controller:628) and :111 c.EffectiveFidelity() (= policy), so the panel shows requested SYNTHETIC / effective BAR with IsDegraded() false, and SaveInto persists the stale m_state.fidelity (1724). ClearError() at 1612 is indeed undone by the struct copy 18 lines later. Correctly LOW: emission still follows m_fidelity.Decide(), so playback fidelity itself is unaffected — only what is shown and saved / Provable. SSRReplayState carries fidelity, speed_x100, status, last_error and last_error_text (SSR_ReplayState.mqh), TakeSnapshot copies the whole struct (out.state = m_state, line 1598), and RestoreSnapshot assigns it back wholesale at 1630 — after ClearError() at the top of the method, so a snapshotted last_error is reinstated. CSSRFidelityPolicy is a separate member and is not restored, while SetFidelity writes both (671-676), so m_state.fidelity and m_fidelity.Requested() diverge exactly as claimed; the accessors the UI reads sit on opposite sides of that split (Fidelity() returns m_state.fidelity at line 628, EffectiveFidelity() returns the policy's m_effective at 685, and SSR_GroupPort.mqh:110-111 reads both into the panel). Emission keeps using the policy (m_fidelity.Decide at line 328), so the panel's 'requested' is the stale half. SaveInto persists the stale m_state.fidelity (line 1731) and m_clock.speed_x100 (1729). m_clock = snap.clock at 1631 does restore speed, so m_state and m_clock stay mutually consistent on speed — only the write-back to the file is stale / Provable and user-visible, but cosmetic. RestoreSnapshot copies the whole struct back (m_state = snap.state, line 1630) while CSSRFidelityPolicy is untouched; SetFidelity writes both (671-676), so after a mid-session fidelity change plus a back-step the two disagree. SSR_GroupPort.mqh:110-112 publishes out.fidelity = c.Fidelity() (m_state) alongside out.fidelity_effective = c.EffectiveFidelity() (policy), and SSR_Panel.mqh:888-893 / 2032-2035 render any mismatch as a warning-coloured 'degraded' chip with a '!' — so the panel accuses the engine of degrading when it has not. SaveInto persists the stale m_state.fidelity (line 1719), so a session saved after a back-step resumes at the older fidelity. ClearError() at 1612 is indeed undone by the struct copy. What it does NOT affect is emission: EmitWindow decides from the policy (line 331), and the displayed speed comes from the group, not m_state (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:109), so the reverted m_clock.speed_x100 is invisible in the shipped multi-stream host. Misleading indicator plus one persisted setting: LOW is right

#### `core-engine-7` — RestoreSnapshot re-seats the cursor only when the sink cut strictly before the checkpoint; a checkpoint on an M1 open loses that bar's open tick

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1637`** · category: boundary

```mql5
      long actual = m_sink.TruncateFrom(snap.taken_at_msc);
      ...
      m_cursor   = snap.cursor;
      ...
      if(actual < m_cursor.emitted_msc)
         m_cursor.RewindTo(actual);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The off-by-one is real (the test should be `actual <= m_cursor.emitted_msc`, since RewindTo(actual) sets emitted = actual-1 and 'at or after actual ceased to exist'), but the simple fix collides with a second defect on the same line that the finding does not mention: SSRReplayCursor::RewindTo also zeroes tick_count and bar_count (SSR_ReplayCursor.mqh:41-47). So in the COMMON case — a mid-minute checkpoint, where actual < emitted and the branch does fire — RestoreSnapshot already discards the session counters it exists to preserve, which is the opposite of the store's stated purpose (SSR_SnapshotStore.mqh:5-19). The rare boundary case preserves the counters but loses the open tick. Either way RestoreSnapshot needs a rewind that re-seats emitted_msc without resetting the counters.

**Why:** SSR_IReplaySink.mqh line 304-317 defines TruncateFrom as 'Everything at or after from_msc must cease to exist'; SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh line 720-743 deletes rates and ticks from floor(from) and returns floor(from). SSR_ReplayCursor.mqh line 25 defines emitted_msc as the last instant handed to the sink. Pump line 1015-1017 emits PendingRange(now) -> cursor.Advance(hi=now) before the checkpoint at line 1039-1044, so emitted == taken_at. The strict `<` at line 1637 leaves emitted == actual when the two coincide.

**Verifiers:** The strict comparison and the coincidence it fails on are both provable. CSSRCustomSymbolManager::Truncate floors to the M1 open, CustomRatesDelete/CustomTicksDelete from that instant inclusive, and returns bar_open (SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:712-744) — matching the contract text at SSR_IReplaySink.mqh:46-59 ('everything at or after from_msc must cease to exist'). Pump emits PendingRange(now) with hi == now and advances the cursor to it (1015-1017, 531) before TakeSnapshot sets taken_at_msc = m_clock.now_msc (1598), so snap.cursor.emitted_msc == snap.taken_at_msc. When taken_at is exactly an M1 open, actual == emitted and the `if(actual < m_cursor.emitted_msc)` at 1637 does not fire, so the cursor keeps claiming an instant whose tick was just deleted; the next EmitWindow's keep filter (`t < lo`, 462-464) drops the i=0 tick, whose time_msc is exactly base_msc and whose bid is bar.open (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:148-152). The bar therefore rebuilds from the second synthetic tick (base + span/(n-1) = +8571ms at 8 ticks) and its open no longer matches the broker's. Contrast is correct: SeekTo (1233) and Reset (1286) call RewindTo(actual) unconditionally. Reachability is the only soft part — it needs a checkpoint pump to land exactly on a minute boundary, which fixed-delta high-speed pumping produces (4000ms steps at 100x divide 60000 and 300000) and jittery wall deltas make rare / The off-by-one is provable from the two contracts in source. CSSRCustomSymbolManager::Truncate (SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:712-743) floors to bar_open, issues CustomRatesDelete/CustomTicksDelete from that instant inclusive, and returns bar_open; CSSRCustomSymbolSink::TruncateFrom passes it through (SSR_CustomSymbolSink.mqh:340-364). SSRReplayCursor.emitted_msc is documented as the last instant handed to the sink (SSR_ReplayCursor.mqh:25) and Pump advances it to hi == now (line 531 via PendingRange at 1015-1017) before the checkpoint is taken at 1039-1044, so snap.cursor.emitted_msc == snap.taken_at_msc. When taken_at is exactly an M1 open, actual == emitted and the strict `<` at line 1637 skips RewindTo, leaving the cursor claiming an instant whose tick the sink just deleted. The i=0 synthesised tick is stamped exactly at the bar open (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:132-147: time_msc = base_msc + i*span/(n-1)), so that is precisely the tick lost, and the rebuilt bar opens at i=1 (base + 59999/7 = +8571 ms). SeekTo (1236) and Reset (1279-1281) call RewindTo(actual) unconditionally, as the finding says. Kept at LOW: whether a checkpoint ever lands exactly on a minute boundary depends on the wall deltas fed to Pump, which is runtime, but the comparison error itself is static / Every premise checks out. CSSRCustomSymbolManager::Truncate deletes rates and ticks from SSRBarOpenMsc(from) and returns that bar open (SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:713-743); the sink contract is 'at or after' (SSR_IReplaySink.mqh:304-317); emitted_msc is 'the last instant handed to the sink' (SSR_ReplayCursor.mqh:25); Pump emits PendingRange(now) before the checkpoint is taken (controller 1015-1017 vs 1039-1044), so snap.cursor.emitted_msc == snap.taken_at_msc. When the checkpoint instant is itself an M1 open, actual == emitted and the strict `<` at line 1637 skips RewindTo, so the tick stamped exactly at that open — Synthesize writes tick i=0 at base_msc exactly (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:145-147) — is deleted and never re-emitted, since the next window starts at emitted+1 and the trim at 462-466 drops t < lo. The rebuilt candle then opens at the i=1 tick, 3/7 of the way along the first path segment (u = i*3/(n-1) with n=8). Reachability is a coincidence rather than a broker unknown: checkpoints are taken at the first pump at/after a 5-minute boundary (SSR_SnapshotStore.mqh:93-98) with jittery deltas (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2734-2736), so an exact-millisecond landing on a minute open is occasional, and it only matters if the user later back-steps to that checkpoint. Effect is one candle's open on the replay chart; observers are unaffected because ClipBar rebuilds the shown bar from the full synthetic array. LOW is the right ceiling. The contrast with SeekTo (1236) and Reset (1285), which call RewindTo(actual) unconditionally, is also correct

#### `data-9` — SSR_NEWS_ALL ("Everything the calendar has") floors importance at LOW, silently excluding CALENDAR_IMPORTANCE_NONE events such as bank holidays

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Data/SSR_Calendar.mqh:59`** · category: boundary

```mql5
int SSRNewsFloor(const ENUM_SSR_NEWS n)
  {
   if(n == SSR_NEWS_HIGH)     return (int)CALENDAR_IMPORTANCE_HIGH;
   if(n == SSR_NEWS_MODERATE) return (int)CALENDAR_IMPORTANCE_MODERATE;
   return (int)CALENDAR_IMPORTANCE_LOW;
  }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Confirmed as a label/behaviour mismatch provable from source. The consequence — specifically that bank holidays and other non-scheduled entries are the excluded class — rests on MetaTrader publishing those with CALENDAR_IMPORTANCE_NONE, which cannot be settled from this repository; the source-provable part is that SSR_NEWS_ALL is still a filter at LOW.

**Why:** ENUM_CALENDAR_EVENT_IMPORTANCE is NONE=0, LOW=1, MODERATE=2, HIGH=3. The fall-through branch returns LOW, not NONE, so the widest setting the UI offers is still a filter. Nothing downstream re-adds them: Pull (line 152-153 region) is the only insertion point. The fix is one token (return (int)CALENDAR_IMPORTANCE_NONE for the ALL case), and the label "Everything the calendar has" is what makes the current behaviour a defect rather than a choice.

**Verifiers:** SSRNewsFloor (lines 59-64) has no SSR_NEWS_ALL branch: the fall-through returns CALENDAR_IMPORTANCE_LOW, and Pull's only filter is `if((int)ev.importance < min_importance) continue;` (line 147-148), the single insertion point into m_items. ENUM_CALENDAR_EVENT_IMPORTANCE orders NONE(0) < LOW(1) < MODERATE(2) < HIGH(3) and the code relies on that ordering throughout (SSRCalendarItem.Init sets importance=0, CSSRCalendarLines::Colour/Width/Style treat anything below MODERATE as LOW), so the widest setting the UI offers still filters one importance class out, contradicting the enum's own label 'Everything the calendar has' (line 56) and SSRNewsName's 'everything' (line 70). The only call site is SSReplayStandalone.mq5:938 with no compensating logic, and the drawing layer would render NONE items correctly if they arrived / Provable from source. ENUM_SSR_NEWS's widest value is labelled "Everything the calendar has" (SSR_Calendar.mqh:56) and SSRNewsName(ALL) returns "everything" (line 70), yet SSRNewsFloor falls through to CALENDAR_IMPORTANCE_LOW (line 63) for that value. The only filter site is Pull's `if((int)ev.importance < min_importance) continue;` (line 147-148), and MQL5's ENUM_CALENDAR_EVENT_IMPORTANCE has NONE=0 below LOW=1, so importance-0 entries are excluded at the setting that claims to exclude nothing. Nothing re-adds them: Pull is the sole insertion point and the drawing layer would render them fine (CSSRCalendarLines::Colour/Width/Style all have a below-MODERATE branch). The label-versus-behaviour mismatch is settled by source; only the size of the user-visible effect (how many NONE-importance entries a given terminal's calendar actually carries for a given window) is runtime-dependent / Source-provable: SSRNewsFloor falls through to CALENDAR_IMPORTANCE_LOW for SSR_NEWS_ALL, and Pull's `if((int)ev.importance < min_importance) continue;` (line 147-148) then discards every importance-NONE entry. So the setting labelled 'Everything the calendar has' is still a filter, and Pull is the only insertion point — nothing re-adds them. That is a real label/behaviour mismatch. Impact is the smallest kind, though: the user loses a dotted vertical marker on a holiday. No money arithmetic, no future-data leak, no safety-guarantee weakening, and the price action itself is unchanged. Note also that the unfiltered floor would let importance-NONE entries consume the SSR_CAL_MAX=300 ceiling, so 'one token' is not quite the whole fix. Downgraded from LOW to IMPROVEMENT

#### `data-7` — Discover() discards the last COMPLETE M1 bar whenever the market is closed, shortening every available range by one minute

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Data/SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:125`** · category: boundary

```mql5
      if(last_quote > 0 && last_quote <= lastbar_close)
         out.last_msc = lastbar_open - 1;      // the forming bar is not data yet
      else
         out.last_msc = lastbar_close;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct, and broader than the weekend case: the test fires for ANY last_quote at or before the final bar's close, which includes a stale quote that predates the last bar entirely (a symbol whose M1 was downloaded from the server while it is not being quoted). So the one-minute shortfall applies whenever quoting has stopped or lags the series, not only on a Saturday; the else branch that keeps the full last bar is taken only when the quote feed is ahead of the M1 series.

**Why:** The test asks "does the symbol's last quote fall inside the last bar?" as a proxy for "is that bar still forming", but the two differ precisely when quoting has stopped: for a closed market the last quote always falls inside the last bar, and the bar is nevertheless final. There is no check of the symbol's trade/quote session state (SymbolInfoSessionQuote, SYMBOL_TRADE_MODE) or of how far behind the last quote is relative to wall time, either of which would separate the two cases. The cost is bounded at one M1 bar, which is why this is LOW, but it is a systematic off-by-one on the timeline bound that every other class in the subsystem trusts.

**Verifiers:** Traced and it is worse than runtime-contingent - it is deterministic. The last M1 bar exists because a tick landed in it, so for a symbol that has stopped quoting the last quote necessarily satisfies lastbar_open <= last_quote <= lastbar_close, making the test at SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:123 true and out.last_msc = lastbar_open-1, discarding a bar that can never receive another tick. The else branch is only taken when the last quote is NEWER than the final bar's close, i.e. when the M1 series is lagging the quote feed. The consumer chain is as claimed: SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1075 uses range.last_msc directly as win_end, and CSSRHistoryCatalog exposes the same range. No guard, clamp or later correction exists anywhere on that path / The arithmetic is exactly as described and checks out against the constants: SSR_MSC_PER_MIN is 60000 (SSR_Types.mqh:31), lastbar_close = lastbar_open + 59999, and the true branch sets out.last_msc = lastbar_open - 1, which drops the whole final M1 bar (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:117-125). The forming-bar proxy "last quote falls inside the last bar" is indeed unconditionally true on a closed market, because the tick that created the final bar IS the last quote, and the code tests neither SymbolInfoSessionQuote/SYMBOL_TRADE_MODE nor the lag against wall time. It stays POTENTIAL_RISK under this lens because the conclusion rests entirely on the runtime value SymbolInfoInteger(SYMBOL_TIME_MSC) returns for a closed symbol - plausibly the cached Friday quote (giving the off-by-one), but possibly 0 after a terminal restart or symbol re-select, which takes the else branch and is correct - and that cannot be settled without a terminal. Impact is bounded at one M1 bar on win_end (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1076 uses range.last_msc directly), so LOW is right / The logic is exactly as described. SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:121-128 uses 'is the symbol's last quote inside the last bar?' as a proxy for 'is that bar still forming', and on a closed market the last quote is ALWAYS inside the last bar (the last bar is by definition the one the last tick built), so the test fires and out.last_msc = lastbar_open - 1 drops a bar that can never change again. No session-state check (SymbolInfoSessionQuote, SYMBOL_TRADE_MODE) or wall-clock-lag check exists to separate the two cases. But the impact under any realistic use is one M1 bar at the far end of a timeline thousands of bars long, and it errs in the SAFE direction: it withholds data, it never exposes anything beyond the horizon, so no future-data or money-arithmetic consequence. The user-visible consequence is that LatestStart/CanStartAt/win_end land one minute earlier than they could - nothing they can perceive, and every downstream consumer stays self-consistent because they all read the same shortened bound. LOW overstates a one-minute conservative bias; this is a boundary-precision cleanup

#### `data-3` — CSSRRandomPicker draws candidate symbols WITH REPLACEMENT, so a symbol that has enough history can be skipped entirely and the pick reported as impossible

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Data/SSR_RandomPicker.mqh:149`** · category: input-validation

```mql5
      int attempts = (m_pool_count < SSR_PICK_ATTEMPTS
                      ? m_pool_count : SSR_PICK_ATTEMPTS);
      for(int a = 0; a < attempts; a++)
        {
         string sym = m_pool[m_rng.Index(m_pool_count)];
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Pick() draws candidates with replacement from a pool it is bounded by (attempts = min(pool,8)), so a symbol that has the depth can be missed and Pick() reports 'no candidate had enough history' listing the same name repeatedly. Reachable only with InpRandom=true plus a multi-symbol InpAlsoSymbols of mixed history depth; the consequence is a silent-but-logged degradation to the non-random window, not incorrect data. AddSymbol's duplicate branch returning true also inflates AddSymbolList's 'added' count.

**Why:** m_rng.Index(m_pool_count) samples uniformly over the whole pool on every iteration; nothing removes or marks a rejected candidate, and the loop body only appends to m_skipped and continues. attempts is capped at min(m_pool_count, 8), so for any pool of size n the loop makes at most n draws from n with replacement — by construction it cannot guarantee coverage of the pool it is bounded by. The bound's own comment ("how many symbols to try before giving up. Bounded, because a list where nothing has enough history must produce an error the user can read, not a loop", lines 31-34) shows the intent was to try each candidate; the code tries n random candidates instead. A secondary effect: AddSymbol returns true for a duplicate without adding it (lines 78-82), so AddSymbolList's "added" count and the duplicate entries in m_skipped both overstate what happened.

**Verifiers:** Traced Pick() at SSR_RandomPicker.mqh:145-186. attempts = min(m_pool_count, 8) and each iteration re-draws `m_pool[m_rng.Index(m_pool_count)]` over the WHOLE pool (SSR_Random.mqh:60-65 is a plain modulo over count); nothing removes, swaps out or marks a rejected candidate, and both failure arms only append to m_skipped and `continue`. So n draws with replacement from n, and the probability math (8/27 for one good symbol out of three) is right. No guard upstream: host SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1019-1046 calls Pick once and on false prints "random pick failed, using the normal window" and proceeds with the non-random window. The duplicate side-note also holds: AddSymbol returns true without adding on a duplicate (lines 78-82) so AddSymbolList's `added` count overstates, and m_skipped can name the same symbol several times. Severity reduced from MEDIUM: the failure needs a multi-symbol pool (InpAlsoSymbols non-empty; the default pool is just the origin, attempts=1) with at least one too-shallow member, and the outcome is a logged fallback to the ordinary window rather than wrong data / Source-provable and no MQL5 semantics rescue it. SSRRandom::Index (SSR_Random.mqh:60-64) is `Next() % count` over the whole pool on every iteration; SSR_RandomPicker.mqh:145-149 caps the loop at min(m_pool_count, 8) and draws WITH REPLACEMENT, so for a pool of n it makes at most n independent draws from n and cannot guarantee coverage. Nothing marks or removes a rejected candidate - the rejection paths (153, 161-164) only append to m_skipped and `continue`. The host really does build a 3-symbol pool and fall back on failure: SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1021-1022 (AddSymbolList(InpAlsoSymbols) then AddSymbol(origin)), 1044-1046 ("random pick failed, using the normal window"). The (2/3)^3 = 29.6% arithmetic is right. Severity lowered to LOW because harm needs a mixed-depth pool (if every pool symbol qualifies the first draw succeeds), the failure is printed, and the session still starts on the non-random window. The secondary point is also true: AddSymbol returns true for a duplicate without adding it (78-82), so AddSymbolList's "added" count overstates, and the same name can repeat in m_skipped / Source confirms sampling WITH replacement: line 149 draws m_pool[m_rng.Index(m_pool_count)] on every iteration and the loop is bounded by attempts = min(m_pool_count, 8) (145-146); nothing marks or removes a rejected candidate, so coverage of the pool is impossible by construction. AddSymbol also returns true for a duplicate without adding it (78-82), so AddSymbolList's count and the repeated names in m_skipped both overstate. Impact is smaller than MEDIUM, though: the path needs a non-default configuration (InpRandom=true AND a multi-symbol InpAlsoSymbols) AND a pool of mixed history depth - with the default empty list the pool is just the origin (host:1021-1022), attempts=1, deterministic. When it does fail the host does not corrupt anything: it prints '[host] random pick failed, using the normal window: <reason>' and runs the ordinary window (host:1042-1045). No money arithmetic, no future data, no weakening of the virtual-trading guarantee - the user loses the randomisation for that run and is told so, in a message whose repeated symbol name makes the cause unreadable. Downgraded MEDIUM -> LOW

#### `strategy-integration-report-12` — SetSlot is unbounded while Discover only scans 1..SSR_MAX_SLOTS, so extra-stream publishers can be invisible to clients or collide with a second session

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Integration/SSR_Publisher.mqh:121`** · category: boundary

```mql5
   void              SetSlot(const int slot)     { m_slot = slot; }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Two corrections to the magnitudes. (1) Extra streams are capped at SSR_EXTRA_STREAMS = SSR_MAX_STREAMS - 1 = 3 (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:255, SSR_MasterClock.mqh:34), so the highest slot a session can ever occupy is InpSlot+3 and a stream only becomes undiscoverable when InpSlot >= 6, not merely at InpSlot=8. (2) The second-session collision is partially detected, not entirely silent: the host scans existing replay symbols for its own '.SSR<slot>' suffix and prints '[host] NOTE: <sym> is OPEN and also on replay slot N ... set Replay slot to N+1 on one of them' (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1604-1628), which fires when a second session's primary slot collides with an earlier session's extra-stream slot. It is a Print, not a refusal, and it covers only the custom-symbol namespace — nothing checks the publisher's global-variable namespace or an existing heartbeat, so the finding's core point stands.

**Why:** SetSlot accepts any int with no range check and no error path; SSRGvName (SSR_Contract.mqh:124-127) happily builds "SSR.11.now". CSSRClient::Discover loops `for(int s = 1; s <= SSR_MAX_SLOTS; s++)` (SSR_Client.mqh:64), and SSR_MAX_SLOTS is 8 (SSR_Contract.mqh:156). Nothing in the contract reserves a range for a session's extra streams or lets a publisher claim more than one slot atomically, and Begin() does not check whether the slot is already occupied by a live heartbeat.

**Verifiers:** Traced end to end and every cited fact holds. SetSlot (line 121) takes any int with no clamp and no error path; SSRGvName (SSR_Contract.mqh:124-127) concatenates whatever it is given; CSSRClient::Discover scans only 1..SSR_MAX_SLOTS with SSR_MAX_SLOTS 8 (SSR_Client.mqh:64, SSR_Contract.mqh:156); the host assigns extra streams InpSlot+1+i (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1396, and the custom-symbol sink the same way at :490); InpSlot is an unvalidated input (:74) copied into the session file (:422) and never range-checked anywhere in the tree. Begin() (lines 134-147) writes the slot's variables unconditionally, with no check for a live heartbeat already on that slot, so overlapping slots silently share one namespace — exactly what SSR_Contract.mqh:47-49 says slots exist to prevent / Every cited fact checks out. SetSlot (SSR_Publisher.mqh:121) is an unchecked assignment; SSRGvName (SSR_Contract.mqh:124-127) concatenates any integer; SSR_MAX_SLOTS is 8 (SSR_Contract.mqh:156) and is used in exactly one place, CSSRClient::Discover's `for(int s = 1; s <= SSR_MAX_SLOTS; s++)` (SSR_Client.mqh:64); the host assigns extra-stream publishers InpSlot+1+i (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1396) and InpSlot (line 74) has no validation anywhere in the file. So InpSlot=8 with extra symbols publishes to slots 9-11 that no conforming client scans, and Begin() has no occupancy check. The second half is sharper than the finder states: the host's own collision advice at line 1628 tells the user to move the other session to InpSlot+1, which is precisely the slot this session's first extra stream already occupies. Conditional on InpAlsoSymbols being non-empty (line 455-462), so LOW / Every mechanical claim checks out: SetSlot takes any int with no range test (line 121), Begin() never checks whether the slot already carries a live heartbeat (134-147), SSRGvName builds any slot name (SSR_Contract.mqh:124-127), Discover scans 1..SSR_MAX_SLOTS=8 (SSR_Client.mqh:64, SSR_Contract.mqh:156), and the host hands each extra stream InpSlot+1+i (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1396, mirroring the sink at :490). The collision case is actually slightly worse than described and worth recording: the extra publishers are attached to the SAME group with control permission (1395-1398), so a second session started at slot 2 shares SSR.2.cmd.* with session 1's first extra publisher and a Play/Pause/Jump aimed at one would be executed by both. Still LOW on impact: it needs a deliberate multi-symbol session plus a second concurrent session on an adjacent slot, the extra publishers carry no trade permission (SetPermissions(InpAllowControl, false)), no OrderSend exists on any path, and the primary session — the one Discover exists to find — is always inside 1..8 when InpSlot is

#### `strategy-integration-report-7` — The publisher rejects a command whose sequence equals the last one it executed, so a restarted client has one command silently dropped and reported as a timeout

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Integration/SSR_Publisher.mqh:229`** · category: input-validation

```mql5
      long seq = (long)Get(SSR_GV_CMD_SEQ, 0.0);
      if(seq == 0 || seq == m_last_seq)
         return false;                  // nothing new
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The mechanism is confirmed but the scenario is wrong: m_last_seq is advanced to each executed sequence (line 243), so after a restart the collision is not the third command but at most one command, and only when the incoming sequence exactly equals the last one executed. In the finding's own example (previous client reached seq 3, restarted client sends 1,2,3) nothing is dropped — 1 != 3 executes and sets m_last_seq to 1, then 2 and 3 follow normally. The reachable cases are: a restarted client whose first command is seq 1 while the publisher's last executed sequence happened to be 1 (the previous client had executed exactly one command), and two clients sharing one slot whose independent counters momentarily coincide. Hence LOW rather than MEDIUM.

**Why:** The guard is a plain equality against the last executed sequence, not a monotonic comparison and not a comparison against the published cmd.ack; m_last_seq is only reset by Begin() (line 138), which runs on the replay side, never on client restart. CSSRClient initialises m_seq to 0 (SSR_Client.mqh:46), increments it at line 192, and Refresh() (lines 81-131) never reads cmd.ack, so a fresh client has no way to learn where the publisher's counter already is. When Poll() returns early, nothing is written to cmd.ack or cmd.rc, so the client's wait loop at lines 202-213 can only fall out on the timeout branch at 215-218.

**Verifiers:** The mechanism is real: `seq == m_last_seq` (line 229) is a plain equality against the last EXECUTED sequence, not against the published cmd.ack and not a monotonic test; on the early return nothing is written to SSR_GV_CMD_RC or SSR_GV_CMD_ACK (compare lines 241-243), so CSSRClient::Send's wait loop (SSR_Client.mqh:202-213) can only exit through the timeout branch at 215-218 and reports SSR_RC_REFUSED with 'the replay session did not answer within 500ms' — a duplicate rejection diagnosed as a dead session. m_seq is initialised to 0 in the client constructor (SSR_Client.mqh:46), only incremented (:192), Refresh() never reads cmd.ack, and m_last_seq is reset only by Begin() on the replay side (line 138), all as stated / The mechanism is provable: CSSRClient::m_seq is initialised to 0 (SSR_Client.mqh:46) and only incremented (line 192), so a reconstructed client restarts at 1; CSSRPublisher::m_last_seq is reset only in Begin() (SSR_Publisher.mqh:138), which runs on the replay side; and Poll's `if(seq == 0 || seq == m_last_seq) return false;` (229-230) exits before writing cmd.rc or cmd.ack (241-242), so the client's wait loop (SSR_Client.mqh:202-213) can only fall through to the timeout branch at 215-218 and report SSR_RC_REFUSED with "the replay session did not answer within 500ms". Refresh() (81-131) never reads cmd.ack, so a fresh client cannot resynchronise. But the finder's worked example is wrong, which is why the severity drops: m_last_seq is overwritten with each executed sequence (line 243), so 1,2,3 after a restart all execute / The ABA weakness is real — `seq == m_last_seq` is plain equality against the last EXECUTED sequence rather than a monotonic test or a comparison with the published cmd.ack, m_last_seq survives client restarts (only Begin() clears it, line 138), CSSRClient::m_seq starts at 0 (SSR_Client.mqh:46) and Refresh() never reads cmd.ack, and on the early return nothing is written to cmd.ack or cmd.rc so the client can only fall out of the spin loop on the timeout branch (SSR_Client.mqh:201-219) with the wrong diagnosis. BUT the finder's scenario is arithmetically wrong: line 243 assigns m_last_seq = seq on every execution, so after a restart from m_last_seq=3 the new client's seq 1 executes (m_last_seq becomes 1), seq 2 executes (becomes 2), and seq 3 executes against m_last_seq=2. Nothing is dropped. The collision needs m_last_seq to already equal the restarted client's first sequence — i.e. the previous client sent exactly ONE command in its lifetime. That narrows it a great deal, and the user-visible cost is a single ignored button press with a misleading message; the client's next command works. MEDIUM downgraded to LOW

#### `mt5-symbol-8` — Destroy() reports SSR_OK and marks the symbol gone even when CustomSymbolDelete fails

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Mt5/SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:557`** · category: object-lifecycle

```mql5
bool ok = CustomSymbolDelete(m_symbol);
m_created = false;
...
//--- a symbol that was never there is not a failure
Succeed();
return ok;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Destroy() masks a failed CustomSymbolDelete: m_created goes false and Succeed() clears the error before the result is known (SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:557-585), and both callers ignore the return value, so a symbol that could not be deleted survives with nothing logged.
