Accumulation is bounded in practice - the next Create()'s Destroy retry or its adopt fallback reclaims the same slot, and the seed manifest is invalidated beforehand - so this is a lost diagnostic, not unbounded orphan growth.

**Why:** The Succeed() call is unconditional and the comment justifies it only for the never-existed case; GetLastError() after the failed delete is discarded (ResetLastError at 556 precedes it, nothing reads it after).

**Verifiers:** Confirmed line by line (SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:544-586): m_created = false is set immediately after CustomSymbolDelete regardless of its result (557-558), the SSRPause(120) is skipped on failure, Succeed() is unconditional (584) so LastError()/LastErrorText() report OK even though the comment justifies that only for the never-existed case, and the GetLastError() that ResetLastError() at 556 set up is never read. Callers discard the bool: sink Release() (SSR_CustomSymbolSink.mqh:389), sink destructor (73-74) and Create()'s pre-emptive Destroy() (318). So after a refused delete, IsCreated() reports the symbol gone while it remains in the terminal, and no log line records it - the orphan accumulation the file header (10-15) warns about. Only the missing-diagnostic consequence is provable; whether CustomSymbolDelete actually refuses is runtime, which is why LOW is right / Provable from source. Destroy() (SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:544-586) captures ok = CustomSymbolDelete(m_symbol), sets m_created = false unconditionally on the next line, calls Succeed() unconditionally at 584 - clearing m_last_error to SSR_OK and m_last_error_text to "" (91-94) - and returns ok. The GetLastError() that the ResetLastError() at 556 was staged for is never read. Every caller discards the bool: the sink destructor (SSR_CustomSymbolSink.mqh:73-74), Release() (385-391), Create()'s own pre-teardown (318), and the T3 tests. So after a refused delete IsCreated() reports false while the symbol is still in the terminal, and LastError()/LastErrorText() report success, which is the opposite of the read-back-and-report discipline the rest of this file applies (chart mode at 387-395, sessions at 195-215). The consequence is bounded - the symbol was already deselected from Market Watch at 553 and the next run's Create()/Destroy() or adopt fallback usually sweeps it - so LOW / Provable as written: Destroy sets m_created=false before knowing the outcome and calls Succeed() unconditionally (SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:557-585), so LastError()/LastErrorText() report OK after a refused CustomSymbolDelete, GetLastError() from that call is discarded, and both callers ignore the returned bool (CSSRCustomSymbolSink::Release lines 383-391 and its destructor lines 69-75). The consequence is a silently surviving symbol. Impact is limited, which supports LOW rather than anything higher: the survivor is bounded per slot and is reclaimed on the next run - Create() sets m_symbol then calls Destroy() again (CloseCharts + retry), and failing that the adopt fallback takes it over - and the seed manifest was already invalidated before Destroy, so no stale history can be presented as this session's. Nothing here touches trade accounting or leaks future data; the real loss is the diagnostic line the file header's "forty dead symbols" warning would want

#### `mt5-symbol-7` — Seed-cache version guard compares against a constant that has never changed, so it never rejects a manifest

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Mt5/SSR_SeedCache.mqh:176`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
if(m.version != SSR_VERSION)
  { m_last_reason = "written by another version"; m_misses++; return false; }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The manifest's version field is a constant that has never changed, so CanReuse's version guard is inert. Its bite is on the adopt path: Adopt() re-applies the other symbol overrides but not SYMBOL_CHART_MODE, so a leftover pre-fix LAST-mode replay symbol can be reused by v125 and then build no candles from the engine's ticks.

**Why:** git log -S on the SSR_VERSION definition returns only the initial Phase 1 commit dc87fa3; SSR_Build.mqh carries the per-build stamp separately and SSRSeedManifest.Init() copies only SSR_VERSION.

**Verifiers:** Verified: SSR_VERSION is "0.1.0" (SSR_Types.mqh:35) and `git log -S'#define SSR_VERSION'` on that file returns only the initial commit dc87fa3, while the changing stamp lives separately in SSR_Build.mqh:22 ("v125") and never enters the manifest — SSRSeedManifest::Init copies SSR_VERSION only (SSR_SeedCache.mqh:43) and Save writes that (line 112). So the guard at line 176 can never fire across builds, and CanReuse accepts any earlier build's manifest once origin and range agree. The consequence has teeth: the reuse path adopts the existing symbol (SSR_CustomSymbolSink.mqh:162-172 -> CSSRCustomSymbolManager::Adopt), and Adopt re-applies SPREAD_FLOAT, TRADE_MODE, sessions and money properties (SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:476-482) but NOT SYMBOL_CHART_MODE, which is set only in Create (line 383) — so a stale LAST-mode symbol from an older build is reused without the BID fix. Severity stays LOW: the bar-level evidence checks (lines 188-199) still gate reuse, and a bad adoption degrades to an empty/odd series rather than corrupting numbers / Provable from source and history. SSR_Types.mqh:35 defines SSR_VERSION as "0.1.0" and `git log -S'define SSR_VERSION'` on that file returns only the initial commit dc87fa3, while the stamp that actually moves lives separately (SSR_Build.mqh:22, "v125 2026-09-12") and never enters the manifest - SSRSeedManifest::Init copies only SSR_VERSION (SSR_SeedCache.mqh:43) and Save writes only that (112). So the guard at 176-177 can never fire for any build of this program, and a manifest plus custom symbol written by any earlier build is honoured whenever origin and range agree. Severity LOW rather than higher because CanReuse still demands origin equality (178), range coverage (184) and live bar evidence from SeriesInfoInteger (188-199); what it cannot detect is a symbol built with different symbol PROPERTIES (e.g. a pre-v120 LAST-mode index CFD), which is exactly the class of change a version guard exists for / Provable: SSR_VERSION is a fixed "0.1.0" (SSR_Types.mqh:35) while the stamp that actually moves lives elsewhere and is not in the manifest (SSR_Build.mqh:22, SSR_BUILD "v125"); SSRSeedManifest.Init copies only SSR_VERSION (SSR_SeedCache.mqh:43), so the guard at 176 can never fire and 'written by another version' is dead text. The concrete hazard is sharper than stated and I verified it: the cache-hit path adopts rather than creates (SSR_CustomSymbolSink.mqh:165-173 -> CSSRCustomSymbolManager::Adopt), and Adopt re-applies SPREAD_FLOAT, TRADE_MODE, Apply247Sessions and CloneMoneyProperties but NOT SYMBOL_CHART_MODE (SSR_CustomSymbolManager.mqh:476-497) - the BID forcing exists only in Create (383). So a replay symbol left behind by a pre-BID-fix build on an index/futures CFD can be reused by v125 in LAST mode, where CustomTicksAdd accepts ticks and builds no bars. Severity stays LOW because reaching it needs a stale symbol AND its manifest surviving from an older build on a LAST-mode instrument; within one build the reuse path is safe (coverage check plus Truncate(warmup_to+1) prevents a cached future), and no money arithmetic or trade safety is touched

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Report/SSR_ClassReport.mqh:279`** · category: zero-empty

Verified the companion script too: MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/SSR_ClassReport.mq5:61-64 prints s.problem only in the `if(!s.parsed)` branch and gates its odd-session note on `rep.Key() != ""`, so the reason never reaches the Experts log either. No guard or clamp anywhere prevents this / Source-provable. SSR_ClassReport.mqh:279-283 sets out.problem for a keyless export and then out.parsed=true, returning true, so the file counts in `readable` (450-452). The caveat at 475-481 fires on m_key_agree < readable, which 0 < N satisfies, while odd_row (558) and odd (606) are both gated on m_key != "" and so mark nothing when no file carries a key. problem is emitted only inside `if(!m_s[i].parsed)` (648-652), and the companion script SSR_ClassReport.mq5:61-62 prints s.problem only in its own !s.parsed branch, so the recorded reason reaches neither the page nor the Experts log. No MQL5 semantic saves this: StringFormat with (0, 20) prints the contradictory sentence verbatim / The code path is exactly as described and I verified every cited line. ReadOne sets out.problem then out.parsed=true (279-283), so the file counts toward `readable` (450-452); the key loop at 324-334 skips key=="" so m_key stays "" and m_key_agree stays 0; the caveat at 475-481 fires on 0<20; odd_row (558) and odd (606) are both gated on m_key != "", so nothing is marked; problem is only emitted inside `if(!m_s[i].parsed)` at 648-652, so it is never rendered for these files. The page is genuinely self-contradictory. Impact is what pulls the severity down: SessionKey() (SSR_Journal.mqh:125-129) always formats a non-empty string, so key=="" is only reachable from a pre-v76 export - a legacy-file case, not normal use. And nothing numeric is wrong: every metric, rank and bar is correct, the students are all present and correctly named, and the caveat errs toward distrust rather than toward a false comparison. The coach loses time hunting for markings, not money or a correct conclusion. That is a presentational defect on a legacy input, not MEDIUM

#### `ui-port-session-11` — List() strips the extension by first match, so a name containing ".ssr" is listed wrong

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Session/SSR_SessionManager.mqh:142`** · category: boundary

```mql5
         int    dot  = StringFind(name, SSR_SESSION_EXT);
         if(dot > 0)
            name = StringSubstr(name, 0, dot);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct as described, with one narrowing: the dialog's "del" button does not delete anything (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:288-296), so only Peek (the list row) and Restore (Load) mis-address the file.

**Why:** StringFind returns the FIRST occurrence. A file `notes.ssr copy.ssr` yields name "notes"; the dialog shows "notes", SessionSummary calls Peek("notes") which fails with "cannot read ...\\notes.ssr" (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:937-938), and pressing Load calls Restore("notes") - which fails, or silently loads a genuinely different session called "notes" if one exists. The `dot > 0` guard also lets a file named exactly `.ssr` through as the name ".ssr", whose Path() is then `...\.ssr.ssr`. Low because it requires the literal ".ssr" inside a user-typed name, but the name is free text from the panel's edit box and nothing rejects it.

**Verifiers:** SSR_SessionManager.mqh:142-144 uses StringFind, which returns the FIRST occurrence, so "monday.ssr.ssr" (the file produced when a user types "monday.ssr" into the name box) is listed as "monday", and ".ssr" itself survives the dot>0 guard and is listed as ".ssr" whose Path() is "...\.ssr.ssr". Nothing sanitises the name: Path() (l.92-99) strips only \ / and :, SaveSession only rejects "" (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:944-945), and CSSRSetupPanel::Str (SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:300-307) only trims. The listed name then feeds Peek via SessionSummary (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:932-938) and Restore via the load button (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:272-273), so the row reads "(unreadable: cannot read ...)" or, if a genuine "monday.ssr" exists, resumes a different session. Only the dialog's Delete is not affected (it is a no-op message, SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:288-296) / StringFind returns the first occurrence, and the code takes StringSubstr(name,0,dot) rather than trimming StringLen-4 from the end, so 'notes.ssr copy.ssr' lists as 'notes' and Path("notes") addresses a different (or missing) file for Peek/Restore/Delete. The dot>0 guard also leaves a file literally named '.ssr' unstripped, giving Path()=='...\\.ssr.ssr'. The 'continue' inside the do/while is correct MQL5/C semantics (it jumps to the FileFindNext condition), so the loop itself is sound; only the stripping is wrong. Reachability holds: the name is free text / The code is as quoted: StringFind returns the first occurrence, so `notes.ssr copy.ssr` lists as "notes", and `dot > 0` lets a file literally named `.ssr` through as the name ".ssr" (Path() then yields ...\.ssr.ssr). Two parts of the claimed impact are wrong, which is why it stays LOW rather than rising. (a) The dialog's "del" button does NOT delete: SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:290-296 only sets m_message = "deleting is done from the session folder - this dialog does not remove files"; CSSRSessionManager::Delete has no UI caller. (b) The failure is visible, not silent: SessionSummary falls back to `name + "  (unreadable: " + LastError() + ")"` (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:937-938), so the row announces itself as broken before the user presses Load. The silent-wrong-session case needs a second file genuinely named "notes" to coexist, and the name itself is not free panel text - it comes from InpSession / g_setup.session_name (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:109, 231); CSSRSessionDialog::RequestSave, the only name-entry path in the dialog, has no callers at all

#### `ui-port-session-7` — Peek's session summary is roughly twice what its only consumer can draw

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Session/SSR_SessionManager.mqh:238`** · category: string-length

```mql5
      summary = StringFormat("%s  |  %s  |  at %s (%.0f%% of %s..%s)  |  saved %s",
                             name, syms, SSRFormatMsc(now), pct,
                             SSRFormatMsc(start), SSRFormatMsc(end), when);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Real, but the loss is smaller than "never sees what distinguishes two files": name, symbols, the instant reached and the percentage all fit inside 63 characters; what is always cut is the window start/end and the saved-at timestamp. Hence LOW, not MEDIUM.

**Why:** SSRFormatMsc returns 19 characters (TIME_DATE|TIME_MINUTES|TIME_SECONDS, SSR_Time.mqh:62-67) and `written` is another 19 (TIME_DATE|TIME_SECONDS, l.174): 3x19 + 19 + the literals = 111 fixed characters. The only call site is CSSRGroupPort::SessionSummary (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:932-940), whose only caller draws it as OBJPROP_TEXT: `m_w.Button(id, x + 6, ry - 2, SSR_SD_W - 12, SSR_ROW_H - 3, m_port.SessionSummary(idx), idx == m_selected);` (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:176-177). With name "friday" and symbol "US30.U26" the row stops mid-way through the window start: `friday  |  US30.U26  |  at 2026.08.24 19:06:00 (48% of 2026`. The unreadable-file fallback at l.938 (`name + "  (unreadable: " + LastError() + ")"`) is cut the same way, and the loader's own messages are long sentences. Audit A19 cannot see this because the string is assembled at runtime, not a literal.

**Verifiers:** Arithmetic checks out: SSRFormatMsc is TIME_DATE|TIME_MINUTES|TIME_SECONDS (SSR_Time.mqh:61-67) = 19 chars, "written" is TIME_DATE|TIME_SECONDS (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:174) = 19, and the literals in the l.238-240 format add 34, giving ~112 fixed characters before name and symbols. The only consumer is CSSRGroupPort::SessionSummary (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:932-939), whose only caller draws it as a single button caption (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:176-177) with no truncation or wrapping anywhere in CSSRWidgets, so at the 63-character OBJPROP_TEXT limit the row ends inside the window start. The unreadable fallback (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:938) is cut the same way / The fixed literals in the format string total 34 chars, plus three SSRFormatMsc values at 19 each (SSR_Time.mqh:62-67, TIME_DATE|TIME_MINUTES|TIME_SECONDS) and `written` at 19 (TimeToString(TIME_DATE|TIME_SECONDS), l.174) = 110, plus the percentage, the name and the symbol list. The only consumer is CSSRGroupPort::SessionSummary (l.932-940), drawn by SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:176-177 as an OBJ_BUTTON's OBJPROP_TEXT via ButtonC (SSR_Widgets.mqh:385) with no fitting or truncation helper anywhere in the widget layer, so at 63 drawn chars the tail is unreachable. The unreadable-file fallback at GroupPort l.938 is cut the same way / Arithmetic checks out: the format literals total 34 chars, SSRFormatMsc is 19 (SSR_Time.mqh:62-67) x3, `written` is TIME_DATE|TIME_SECONDS = 19 (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:174), plus pct - ~112 fixed chars before name and symbols. The only consumer is CSSRGroupPort::SessionSummary (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:932-939), whose only caller draws it as a button caption (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:176-177), and there is no clipping helper in SSR_Widgets.mqh. So the tail is genuinely never drawn. Downgraded from MEDIUM on impact: the fields are ordered most-distinguishing-first, so name, symbol, the instant reached and the percentage - the facts a user actually picks a session by - all survive inside 63 chars. What is lost is the window start/end and the saved-at date, which are secondary. Nothing is corrupted and no decision about money is made from this row; the worst outcome is loading the wrong one of two files whose name, symbol and instant are all identical

#### `spikes-audits-25` — The spike CSVs are opened without FILE_SHARE_WRITE while three spikes require a concurrent probe writing the same file

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:97`** · category: measurement-validity

```mql5
int h = FileOpen(file, FILE_READ | FILE_WRITE | FILE_TXT | FILE_ANSI | FILE_SHARE_READ);
if(h == INVALID_HANDLE)
  {
   PrintFormat("[SSR] cannot open %s err=%d", file, GetLastError());
   return;
  }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct as a mechanism, but the exposure is narrow: each open holds the file for well under a millisecond and the probe writes four rows per 10s, so collisions are rare per run (D3's hours-long window is where they accumulate). Scope is Phase-0 measurement harness only - no product code path uses SSR_Append.

**Why:** SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:93-108 is the single writer for env.csv, results.csv and verdicts.csv; FILE_SHARE_WRITE is absent from the flag set. SSR_B2_TickThroughput.mq5:10-12 ('Run SSR_Probe_UIJitter on a SEPARATE chart at the same time. Throughput without a responsiveness number is meaningless'), SSR_D1_SeedPerformance.mq5:11-12 and SSR_D3_SustainedRun.mq5:10 all mandate the concurrency. SSR_Probe_UIJitter.mq5:69-73 calls Report() every InpReportSec=10, and Report emits four SSR_Metric rows (lines 92-95). The failure is a Print, not a verdict, so a dropped row leaves no trace in the artefacts.

**Verifiers:** Every fact checks out. SSR_Append (SpikeKit:93-107) is the single writer for env.csv, results.csv and verdicts.csv, opens with FILE_READ|FILE_WRITE|FILE_TXT|FILE_ANSI|FILE_SHARE_READ - FILE_SHARE_WRITE is absent - and on INVALID_HANDLE prints and returns with no retry and no verdict. SSR_Metric (129-153) and SSR_Verdict (155-179) both route through it. B2:10-12, D1:11-12 and D3:10-11 each instruct the operator to run SSR_Probe_UIJitter concurrently, and the probe's Report() (Probe_UIJitter:78-99) emits four SSR_Metric rows into the same results.csv every InpReportSec. Two writers, neither granting write sharing, so an overlapping open fails. I downgrade severity: the open/seek/write/close window is sub-millisecond against a 10s reporting cadence, this is Phase-0 harness code that never ships, and a dropped row degrades a sample series rather than corrupting product behaviour / Source facts hold. SSR_Append (SpikeKit:93-108) is the single writer for env.csv, results.csv and verdicts.csv - SSR_Metric (line 160) and SSR_Verdict (line 174) both funnel through it - and its flag set FILE_READ|FILE_WRITE|FILE_TXT|FILE_ANSI|FILE_SHARE_READ omits FILE_SHARE_WRITE, so a second concurrent FileOpen requesting FILE_WRITE on the same path is denied by MQL5's documented sharing rules and returns INVALID_HANDLE. The mandated concurrency is real: B2:10-12, D1:11-12 and D3:10 all instruct the operator to run SSR_Probe_UIJitter simultaneously, and the probe's OnTimer calls Report() every InpReportSec, emitting four SSR_Metric rows (Probe:79-95, four SSR_Metric calls = four separate open/append/close cycles). The failure path is a bare PrintFormat with an early return, so a lost row leaves no artefact trace. I downgrade severity from MEDIUM to LOW: this is Phase-0 measurement harness explicitly marked 'NOT product code. Nothing here survives into the engine' (SpikeKit:6), the collision window per row is one open-seek-write-close, and the consequence is at most a few missing rows out of thousands in a jitter series, not a wrong product behaviour / The code fact is exact: SSR_Append (SpikeKit:93-108) is the single writer for env.csv, results.csv and verdicts.csv and asks for FILE_WRITE while granting only FILE_SHARE_READ, and the concurrency is genuinely mandated (B2:10-12, D1, D3) with SSR_Probe_UIJitter emitting four SSR_Metric rows every InpReportSec=10 (Probe:68-72, 91-95). So an overlapping open does return INVALID_HANDLE and the row is lost. But the impact lens strips most of the severity: this file is self-declared dev harness ("NOT product code. Nothing here survives into the engine", SpikeKit:6), no user of SS Replay observes anything, the collision window is one open-append-close of a single line (sub-millisecond) against a writer that fires every 10 seconds, so holes are improbable rather than "most likely", and the failure does leave a trace - a PrintFormat in the Experts log naming the file and the error. LOW, not MEDIUM

#### `strategy-integration-report-3` — OnTick fires once per published batch, not once per tick, so the documented intrabar-management use is unreachable and TickCalls() under-reports

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Strategy/SSR_IStrategy.mqh:266`** · category: tick-ordering

```mql5
   //--- every tick, for strategies that manage intrabar. Whatever this
   //--- does on synthetic ticks rests on an invented order of prices,
   //--- and ctx.IsSynthetic() is how it can know.
   virtual void      OnTick(void) {}
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Whenever a published batch carries more than one tick — MAX/very high speed, catch-up, or a candle step — CSSRMarketView retains only the last tick's bid/ask, so the strategy's parameterless OnTick cannot see the intrabar excursion in that batch, and TickCalls() counts batches rather than ticks. At ordinary speeds each batch holds one tick, so the per-tick hook does fire per synthetic tick and the intrabar prices are visible.

**Why:** The hook takes no parameters, so the only tick data a strategy can reach is CSSRMarketView's m_bid/m_ask, which SSR_MarketView.mqh:191-198 sets from ticks[count-1] alone - the rest of the batch is discarded on arrival. CSSRStrategyHost::OnTicks calls m_strat[i].OnTick() once per OnTicks invocation (line 210) and increments m_tick_calls once (line 209). SSR_ReplayController.mqh:506-507 publishes one segment per M1 bar, whose length is m_synth.TicksForBar(fid) = InpTicksPerBar, so one OnTick per 8 ticks at the default. TickSynthesizer places those ticks across the whole minute (base_msc + i*59999/(n-1)) with the last pinned to the bar close, so the high and low genuinely exist in the batch and are genuinely dropped before the strategy is asked.

**Verifiers:** The mechanism is real but the stated frequency is wrong. Confirmed from source: CSSRMarketView::OnTicks keeps only ticks[count-1] (SSR_MarketView.mqh:191-198), the OnTick hook takes no parameters (SSR_IStrategy.mqh:266), and CSSRStrategyHost calls OnTick once per OnTicks per strategy while incrementing m_tick_calls once there too — so everything but the last tick of a batch is invisible and TickCalls() counts invocations, not ticks. What is NOT true is "exactly one OnTick per M1 bar at the default". The controller publishes only the ticks that fall inside the pump window (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:459-492: ticks outside lo..hi are dropped before the segment is formed, and a segment is only published when keep > 0), and the synthesizer spreads InpTicksPerBar ticks evenly across the minute (SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:147). At 40 ms pumps (InpPumpMs=40) the window is 40 ms x speed, so below roughly 200x it is shorter than the ~8.5 s tick spacing and each batch carries a single tick — the strategy does see each synthetic price, including the bar's high and low. The collapse to one price per bar happens only where a batch spans a whole minute: MAX speed / catch-up, or a step-a-candle command / The delivery granularity is provable. SSR_ReplayController.mqh:502-511 publishes one bar context then one tick SEGMENT per M1 bar, each segment being that bar's synthesised ticks; CSSRStrategyHost::OnTicks calls m_strat[i].OnTick() exactly once per OnTicks invocation (SSR_StrategyHost.mqh:209-210). So with InpTicksPerBar=8 a strategy gets one OnTick per M1 bar, not per tick, and CSSRMarketView::OnTicks (SSR_MarketView.mqh:190-197) keeps only ticks[count-1], so the intrabar SEQUENCE is unrecoverable. Two parts of the evidence are wrong, hence the correction: the bar's high and low are NOT lost - PublishBar runs before PublishSegment and CSSRMarketView::OnBarContext (172-188) stores the full MqlRates, so ctx.market.High/Low(PERIOD_M1, 0) reaches them; and m_tick_calls is incremented inside the per-strategy loop, so it counts batches x enabled strategies, not batches / The mechanism is confirmed in source: the controller publishes one segment per M1 bar (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:503-511), CSSRStrategyHost calls m_strat[i].OnTick() once per OnTicks and increments m_tick_calls once (SSR_StrategyHost.mqh:209-210), and CSSRMarketView::OnTicks keeps only ticks[count-1] (SSR_MarketView.mqh:191-198). So OnTick fires per batch, ctx.market.Bid() is the batch's last price, and TickCalls()/"ticks=%I64d" names batches. But the impact statement overreaches: the bar context is published BEFORE its ticks and clipped to the window (ClipBar, 461), and CSSRMarketView exposes High()/Low()/Bar() at M1 shift 0 (SSR_MarketView.mqh:224-256), so the minute's high and low ARE readable inside OnTick - only the tick-by-tick ordering is not. Per-tick stop/target/trailing work is done by CSSRTradingEngine, which does receive the full batch, so a strategy using broker.Trail() still gets tick-granular trailing. No money corruption, no future leak, no weakening of the virtual guarantee: what is left is a contract/naming mismatch against the header comment. Downgraded MEDIUM -> LOW

#### `strategy-integration-report-13` — Group() returns shift 0 with a wrong open and an un-aggregated spread when the group began before the buffer did

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Strategy/SSR_MarketView.mqh:136`** · category: boundary

```mql5
      if(seen == shift && open_set)
        {
         if(shift == 0)
            return true;                // forming, and known to be so
         m_refusals++;
         return false;
        }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Same defect, two corrections. (1) The trigger is not merely "the group began before the buffer did" and it is not reachable via MakeRoom: MakeRoom keeps 2048 M1 bars (~34 hours), which always spans several H4 groups, so the early-return at line 104 fires first. It needs the entire buffer to lie inside the requested shift-0 group, which happens early in a session or just after a rewind that trimmed almost everything. (2) high and low are NOT right either - they are folded only over the minutes present in the buffer, so the group's true extreme can be in the missing earlier part. Only close is certainly correct. out.spread being the newest minute's value (copied once at line 110) is arguably the intended reading of a forming bar's spread rather than a defect.

**Why:** out.open is overwritten on every backward step inside the group (line 125), so when the loop terminates by exhausting the buffer rather than by crossing a group boundary, the last write came from m_m1[0] - not from the group's first bar. The shift==0 early return skips both the m_refusals++ and the false that the shift>0 case gets. out.spread has the same shape: it is copied once from the group's newest M1 bar at line 110 and never aggregated, so it describes one minute rather than the group. Available() does count such a group (it returns 1 when groups==1), so a strategy checking availability first is not protected.

**Verifiers:** Group() does return an un-refused, half-built shift-0 group, but only on a narrower precondition than the finder describes, and more fields are wrong than claimed. Trace: crossing into an older group hits the early `if(seen == shift && open_set) return true;` at 104-105, so a complete group is returned there. Line 136 is reached only when the backward walk never leaves group 0 - i.e. EVERY bar in the buffer belongs to the requested shift-0 group. In that case out.open was last overwritten at 125 from m_m1[0] (the oldest bar the buffer happens to hold), the shift==0 branch returns true, and neither m_refusals++ nor the false that the shift>0 case gets is executed. Available() returns 1 for that single group (372), so an availability check does not protect a caller. Reachable: early in a bar-driven session (OnSessionStart zeroes m_count, the first pump publishes a handful of M1 bars), or right after session start where PrimeView clamps `from` to StartMsc (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:396-397), or after a deep OnRewind - any moment when the whole buffer fits inside one H1/H4/D1 group / The control flow is as described. Group() walks newest-to-oldest; reaching line 136 with seen==0 means no bar from an earlier group was ever seen, i.e. the whole buffer lies inside group 0, so out.open is m_m1[0].open (the oldest buffered M1) rather than the group's true open, and the shift==0 early return at 138-139 skips both m_refusals++ and the refusal that shift>0 receives (140-141). out.spread is whatever line 110's `out = m_m1[i]` copied from the group's NEWEST M1 and is never aggregated. Available() returns 1 for groups==1 (line 372), so an availability check does not protect the caller. tick_volume/real_volume are correctly aggregated (no double count), so the defect is confined to open and spread / The asymmetry is real and provable. When the backward walk exhausts the buffer while still inside group 0, the post-loop block at 132-138 returns true for shift 0 while shift>0 gets m_refusals++ and false, and out.open was last written at line 125 from m_m1[0] - the oldest bar the buffer happens to hold, not the group's first M1 bar. Available() returns 1 for that state (`groups == 1 ? 1 : 0`), so the group is advertised as usable. Impact keeps this LOW: the state needs the entire M1 buffer to lie inside one tf group, which in practice means the first minutes of a session or just after a jump+prime with few bars, and it only ever affects shift 0 - the bar this class already documents as unreliable and tells strategies not to act on. The shipped reference strategy is protected twice over (it requires Available(tf) >= lookback+2 and reads shifts 1 and 2), so nothing in the product observes it today

#### `strategy-integration-report-6` — Prime() and OnRewind() trim on bar-open granularity, so the newest retained bar can hold prices from after the clock

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Strategy/SSR_MarketView.mqh:398`** · category: future-data-leakage

```mql5
         //--- strictly at or before the clock. Never past it.
         if(SSRToMsc(bars[i].time) > now_msc)
            break;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate. Note the contamination is confined to the newest M1 bar, hence to shift 0 on M1 and to the shift-0 group on higher timeframes; the shipped CSSRRefBreakout reads shift 1 and above and is therefore not exposed.

**Why:** SSRToMsc(bars[i].time) is the bar's open instant, and the comparison is `> now_msc`, so a bar opening at exactly now_msc is taken whole; MqlRates carries no intrabar detail that could be clipped here. OnRewind (lines 204-214) uses the same `<= msc` test on bar open times. Core does clip correctly on the live path (ClipBar, SSR_ReplayController.mqh:150-309), and in the bar-driven fidelities the next pump republishes that same minute clipped, which OnBarContext's time-match branch (lines 180-184) overwrites in place - and because PrimeView runs after g_group.Pump() in OnTimer (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2788 then 2797), that overwrite happens before the next strategy hook. So the exposure is confined to FULL_TICK, where no OnBarContext ever arrives to correct it and the contaminated bar remains the newest bar for the rest of the session.

**Verifiers:** Both tests really are on bar-open granularity and nothing downstream clips. Prime (398-399) breaks on `SSRToMsc(bars[i].time) > now_msc`, so the bar whose open is <= now_msc is admitted whole, high/low/close included - and I confirmed the supply side does not pre-clip: CSSRMt5BarProvider::ReadBars (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:252-285) delegates to CSSRBarWindow::Read, which admits every bar with open <= to_msc verbatim (SSR_BarWindow.mqh:272-277). MqlRates carries no intrabar detail, so the minute cannot be clipped here as ClipBar does on the live path. OnRewind (204-214) has the identical `<= msc` shape on open times. The self-correction the finder describes is real: OnBarContext's time-match branch (180-184) overwrites that minute in place with the clipped `shown` bar from SSR_ReplayController.mqh:459/506, and OnTimer runs Pump (2788) before PrimeView (2797) - but in FULL_TICK no OnBarContext ever arrives, so the correction never happens. LOW is right: the exposure lasts only until the clock walks past that minute, and the shipped reference strategy reads shift 1/2 rather than the shift-0 group that carries the contamination / The comparison at 398 is on bars[i].time, the bar's OPEN instant, so the M1 bar containing now_msc is admitted whole. The data path confirms the bar really is whole: CSSRFutureGuard::ClampRange/Allows work on instants (msc <= horizon) and CSSRBarWindow::Read (257-287) selects bars whose open <= to_msc and copies them unmodified, so PrimeView's ReadBars(g_origin, from, now, bars) returns the current minute with its full high/low/close - up to 59.999 s of prices past the clock. OnRewind (204-214) has the identical `<= msc` test on open times. The mitigations the finding names check out: ClipBar clips on the live path, the republished bar carries the same bar-open `time` so OnBarContext's 180-184 branch overwrites in place, and the pump runs before PrimeView in OnTimer (2788 then 2797), so the contamination is corrected before any strategy hook in the bar-driven fidelities and persists only under FULL_TICK / The trim is on bar-open granularity as claimed, and the layer below does not pre-clip: CSSRBarWindow::Read admits every bar whose open <= to_msc (SSR_BarWindow.mqh:274) and CSSRMt5BarProvider::ReadBars passes that straight back, so PrimeView hands Prime a final bar whose open is <= now and whose high/low/close can be up to 60 s past the clock; `> now_msc` at line 398 then keeps it whole. OnRewind (204-214) has the same open-time test. So the method's own header claim that it "TRIMS to" now_msc is not met for the boundary bar. Impact holds it at LOW: the contamination is confined to one M1 bar at the newest end of the buffer - shift 0 on M1 and part of the shift-0 group above - which this class documents as the forming bar a strategy must not act on, and the shipped reference strategy reads only shifts 1 and 2. In the bar-driven fidelities the next pump republishes that minute clipped and OnBarContext's time-match branch (180-184) overwrites it in place before the next strategy hook (Pump at 2788 precedes PrimeView at 2797), so it self-heals. In FULL_TICK it is never corrected, but within 60 s of replay the clock has passed that minute and the prices stop being future - and the bars are frozen anyway, which is finding -1's problem, not this one's

#### `strategy-integration-report-5` — CSSRMarketView::IsSynthetic() can never return false, so the honesty instrument it exists to be is dead

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Strategy/SSR_MarketView.mqh:427`** · category: spread

```mql5
   bool              IsSynthetic(void) { return m_synthetic; }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: One narrow exception to "can never return false" that the finding misses, and it does not rescue the code: if fidelity were LOCKED with FULL_TICK requested and the source had no tick provider, Decide returns FULL_TICK unchanged (SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh:105-110), execution falls through to the bar-driven path, and PublishBar would pass synthetic=false over synthesized ticks - a lie in the unsafe direction. It is unreachable in production: CSSRReplayController::LockFidelity (line 679) has no caller anywhere in MQL5/, so m_locked is always false and IsSynthetic() is indeed a constant true.

**Why:** m_synthetic is set true in the constructor (line 151) and assigned in exactly one place, OnBarContext (line 175). Its only production caller is PublishBar at SSR_ReplayController.mqh:506, which passes `fid != SSR_FIDELITY_FULL_TICK`. That line is only reachable after the FULL_TICK branch has already returned (line 369) or degraded fid via m_fidelity.Decide() to SYNTHETIC_TICK because the source had no tick provider - in both cases fid != FULL_TICK holds, so the argument is always true. OnSessionStart (lines 159-171) does not reset m_synthetic either, so even a hypothetical false would persist across sessions.

**Verifiers:** m_synthetic is initialised true (151), assigned in exactly one place (175), and that assignment's only caller is PublishBar at SSR_ReplayController.mqh:506 with `fid != SSR_FIDELITY_FULL_TICK`. Line 506 is reachable only after the FULL_TICK branch either returned (369) or re-Decided with SetTicksAvailable(false) (344-345), and CSSRFidelityPolicy::Decide demotes requested FULL_TICK to SYNTHETIC_TICK when ticks are unavailable (114-118) - so the argument is always true. OnSessionStart (159-171) does not reset it, so even a hypothetical false would persist. CSSRStrategyContext::IsSynthetic (SSR_IStrategy.mqh:216-217) forwards it, and SSR_RefStrategy.mqh:151-154 appends the caveat unconditionally as a result. Fault direction is conservative, hence LOW / m_synthetic is initialised true in the constructor (151), assigned in exactly one place (175), and OnSessionStart (159-171) does not reset it. The only production caller is PublishBar at SSR_ReplayController.mqh:506 with `fid != SSR_FIDELITY_FULL_TICK`, and that line is reachable only after the FULL_TICK branch either returned (369) or degraded fid. I checked the one path that could make the argument false - Decide() returning m_requested when m_locked is set, so a locked FULL_TICK with no tick provider would fall through with fid still FULL_TICK - and LockFidelity (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:679) has no caller anywhere in the repo, so m_locked is permanently false. Hence IsSynthetic() is a compile-time-constant true, CSSRStrategyContext::IsSynthetic() likewise, and CSSRRefBreakout::Status() always appends the caveat. Fault direction is conservative, as the finding says / Verified: m_synthetic is initialised true in the constructor, assigned in exactly one place (line 175), and its only production feed is PublishBar(seg_bar[sgi], fid != SSR_FIDELITY_FULL_TICK) at SSR_ReplayController.mqh:506 - which is downstream of the FULL_TICK early return at 369, so fid there is never FULL_TICK and the argument is always true. I chased the one path that could produce false (locked policy: SetLocked(true) makes Decide return FULL_TICK even with ticks unavailable, so a missing tick provider would fall through to line 506 with fid == FULL_TICK) and grep shows CSSRReplayController::LockFidelity has no caller outside SSR_T7_Performance.mq5, so it is unreachable in the shipped EA. OnSessionStart does not reset it either. Held at LOW: the fault direction is conservative - it never claims real ticks - and the only observable consequence is CSSRRefBreakout::Status() always appending "  [intrabar order assumed]", plus a dead branch for any future strategy that consults ctx.IsSynthetic(). No trade, price or statistic changes

#### `strategy-integration-report-4` — The per-strategy RNG stream is seeded once at registration and is never re-seeded or rewound, so a strategy that uses it is not reproducible across a step-back

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Strategy/SSR_StrategyHost.mqh:141`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
      m_ctx[k].rng.Seed(m_seed ^ NameHash(s.Name()));
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct on the mechanism, but two overstatements. The strategy is not wholly powerless: rng is a public member of the context it is bound to (SSR_IStrategy.mqh:202), so a strategy can re-seed it deterministically from replay state in its own OnRewind hook; what it cannot obtain is the host's m_seed, so it cannot restore the host-derived stream. And no shipped strategy draws from ctx.rng, so this is a latent gap in the facility rather than a live reproducibility failure.

**Why:** This is the only Seed() call in the host, and it runs inside Add(). CSSRStrategyHost::OnSessionStart (lines 160-169) resets m_last_bar[] and re-Attaches each broker but does not touch m_ctx[i].rng; CSSRStrategyHost::OnRewind (lines 214-222) resets m_last_bar[i] and forwards OnRewind to the strategy but likewise leaves the RNG state alone. SSR_ReplayController.mqh broadcasts OnRewind at lines 1240, 1286, 1644 and 1669 (step back, reset, jump, snapshot restore) and OnSessionStart only at line 837 (Load), which the EA calls once. The seed is held privately in m_seed with no accessor, and CSSRStrategyContext exposes rng but not the seed, so a strategy has no way to re-derive m_seed ^ NameHash(name).

**Verifiers:** The code property is exactly as described: m_ctx[k].rng.Seed(...) at line 141 is the only Seed() call in the host and it runs inside Add(); OnSessionStart (160-169) resets m_last_bar and re-Attaches brokers only; OnRewind (214-222) resets m_last_bar and forwards OnRewind, leaving the stream advanced. m_seed is private with no accessor and CSSRStrategyContext (SSR_IStrategy.mqh:197-218) exposes rng but not the seed. So a step-back, a bookmark jump or a snapshot restore replays the same minutes with a different RNG stream. I am downgrading to LOW rather than refuting because the defect is entirely latent: grep for `rng.` shows no strategy uses ctx.rng at all (only spikes and CSSRRandomPicker draw from SSRRandom), so nothing in the shipped product is non-reproducible today, and the documented promise in SSR_IStrategy.mqh:20-24 - "the same session replays to the same trades" - is literally about replaying a session from its seed, which does hold / Line 141 is the only Seed() call in the host and it runs inside Add(); OnSessionStart (160-169) resets m_last_bar and re-Attaches brokers only, and OnRewind (214-222) resets m_last_bar and forwards the hook, neither touching m_ctx[i].rng. SSRRandom::Seed is the only state-setting entry point and every draw mutates state, so after a step back, jump or snapshot restore (PublishRewind at 1240, 1286, 1644, 1669) the same minutes replay against an advanced stream. m_seed is private with no accessor and CSSRStrategyContext exposes rng but not the seed / The code is as described: line 141 is the only Seed() call in the host and it runs inside Add(); OnSessionStart (160-169) and OnRewind (214-222) both leave m_ctx[i].rng untouched; m_seed is private with no accessor and CSSRStrategyContext exposes rng but not the seed. So a step back, a bookmark jump or a snapshot restore replays the same minutes with the stream already advanced, and the contract's "the same session replays to the same trades" (SSR_IStrategy.mqh:20-24) does not hold across a rewind for a strategy that draws from it. Downgraded to LOW on impact: no shipped strategy touches ctx.rng - CSSRRefBreakout is fully deterministic - so there is nothing a user can observe today, and the headline reproducibility promise the academy actually trades on (same seed, same candles, twenty students) is unaffected because the stream is seeded identically at registration in every terminal. This is a latent gap in a documented guarantee, waiting for the first strategy that uses the facility

#### `trading-analytics-6` — Undefined profit factor is printed as 0.00 in the KPI grid, by-setup table, CSV header and Summary

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_Journal.mqh:304`** · category: zero-empty

```mql5
FileWriteString(h, StringFormat("<div><span>Profit factor</span><b>%.2f</b></div>\r\n", st.profit_factor));
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct, with one narrowing: the class report INGESTS the 0 (SSR_ClassReport.mqh:224) but does not misprint it - l.622-623 renders '-' when profit_factor <= 0. The four wrong printers are the KPI grid (l.304), the by-setup table (l.357-363), the CSV header (l.200) and SSRStatistics::ToString (SSR_Statistics.mqh:164-170) behind Summary().

**Why:** SSR_Statistics.mqh:529-530 leaves profit_factor at 0.0 when gross_loss == 0 by design ('the caller reads trades/losses to know why'); only WriteAllMeasures honours that contract, the other four printers format the raw double.

**Verifiers:** Provable. SSR_Statistics.mqh:527-530 leaves profit_factor at its Init() value of 0.0 when gross_loss == 0.0 (comment: 'it stays 0 and the caller reads trades/losses to know why'). Four printers format that raw double: the KPI grid (SSR_Journal.mqh:304, '%.2f' -> '0.00'), the by-setup row (l.357-363, ts.profit_factor '%.2f'), the CSV header (l.200, '# profit_factor,0.0000'), and SSRStatistics::ToString (SSR_Statistics.mqh:164-170, 'PF %.2f') which Summary() (l.1241-1250) returns to the panel/log. Only WriteAllMeasures honours the contract (l.1099-1103, prints 'undefined'), so the same page says 0.00 at the top and undefined at the bottom. I checked the one consumer the finding names as harmed: SSR_ClassReport.mqh:224 does ingest the 0, but its own renderer guards it (l.622-623 prints '-' when profit_factor <= 0), so the class report's display is not wrong - only the four printers above are / Provable from source. SSR_Statistics.mqh:527-530 leaves profit_factor at its Init() value 0.0 when gross_loss == 0.0 by documented design ('the caller reads trades/losses to know why'). Only WriteAllMeasures honours that sentinel (SSR_Journal.mqh:1099-1103 prints 'undefined' when profit_factor <= 0.0). The KPI grid (l.304 '%.2f'), the by-setup row (l.363 ts.profit_factor via '%.2f'), the CSV header (l.200 '# profit_factor,%.4f') and SSRStatistics::ToString (SSR_Statistics.mqh:166-168 'PF %.2f', reached by Summary() at SSR_Journal.mqh:1241-1249) all format the raw double, so a session with wins and no loser prints 0.00 - the reading a losing session would get - on the same page that says 'undefined'. SSR_ClassReport.mqh:224 ingests it as the number 0 with no way to tell it apart / Provable: SSR_Statistics.mqh:524-527 leaves profit_factor at 0.0 when gross_loss == 0 by design, and the KPI grid (l.304), the by-setup row (l.363) and SSRStatistics::ToString used by Summary() (SSR_Statistics.mqh:164-170, 'PF %.2f') all format the raw double, while WriteAllMeasures (l.1099-1103) alone honours the contract and prints 'undefined'. An all-winners session is entirely reachable. The user observes one page that says 'Profit factor 0.00' at the top and 'undefined' at the bottom - a document that contradicts itself, which is the harm. But nothing downstream is corrupted: the class report GUARDS its rendering (SSR_ClassReport.mqh:622 prints '-' when profit_factor <= 0.0) and does not rank on it, so the claim that it 'ingests as 0' overstates the consequence. Display-only, no money arithmetic affected - LOW

#### `trading-analytics-8` — Average MAE/MFE printed at two decimals in price units and compared with money

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_Journal.mqh:1142`** · category: boundary

```mql5
Measure(h, "Average MAE", DoubleToString(st.avg_mae, 2),
              "maximum adverse excursion: how far the average trade went the wrong "
              "way before it ended");
      Measure(h, "Average MFE", DoubleToString(st.avg_mfe, 2),
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate. Worth adding a third printer with the same two-decimal truncation: SSR_Review.mqh:142 ('Average MAE', StringFormat("%.2f", st.avg_mae)).
