CSSRTradingEngine::UpdateExcursions (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:293-300) records 'move = price - open_price', a PRICE distance, and SSR_TradeTypes.mqh:209 documents the field as '// worst adverse move, in price'. ComputeFor sums them unchanged (SSR_Statistics.mqh:466-468) and averages them unchanged (l.538-542); no MoneyFor/RiskOf conversion touches them anywhere. SSR_Journal.mqh:1142-1147 then prints both with DoubleToString(...,2), so an average MAE of -0.00150 on a 5-digit symbol renders '-0.00' and MFE '0.00', while the CSV prints the per-trade values at %.5f (format string l.72, args l.87) - the two exports genuinely disagree in readability. The MFE caption 'Much larger than the average win means profit was given back' does compare a price distance with the money figure printed at l.1085 / Units and formatting both check out in source. SSRVirtualPosition::mae is declared 'worst adverse move, in price' (SSR_TradeTypes.mqh:209) and CSSRTradingEngine::UpdateExcursions (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:293-300) stores move = price - open_price, a raw price delta; SSR_Statistics.mqh:466-468 and 539-541 average it unchanged, and nothing calls MoneyFor on it (MoneyFor is used only in SSR_GroupPort.mqh:321-322, 390). SSR_Journal.mqh:1142-1147 then prints DoubleToString(avg_mae, 2) / DoubleToString(avg_mfe, 2), which on a 5-digit FX symbol renders a 15-pip excursion as '-0.00' / '0.00' (SSR_Review.mqh:142 has the same '%.2f'), while the same values go out at %.5f in the CSV (l.87). The caption 'Much larger than the average win means profit was given back' does compare a price distance with a money figure. No runtime assumption needed: DoubleToString truncation of a sub-0.005 value is deterministic / Confirmed from source: CSSRTradingEngine::UpdateExcursions (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:294-300) stores mae/mfe as a raw price move (price - open_price, unscaled by point or money), SSR_Statistics.mqh averages them unchanged, and WriteAllMeasures prints DoubleToString(st.avg_mae, 2) / avg_mfe at l.1142-1147. On any 5-digit FX symbol both rows therefore read '-0.00' and '0.00' - the two rows carry no information at all - while the CSV writes the same fields %.5f (l.87), so the two exports of one session disagree on legibility. The MFE caption inviting a comparison with the average win (a money figure) is also a genuine unit mismatch. Purely a presentation defect in an optional bottom-of-page table; no stored value is wrong and nothing downstream consumes it. LOW

#### `trading-analytics-5` — A day on which only a pending order was placed (even if cancelled) counts as a trading day

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_PropEvaluation.mqh:383`** · category: prop-rules

```mql5
int pos = Positions();
      if(pos > m_last_positions)
        {
         m_last_positions = pos;
         m_day_traded     = true;
        }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate. One reachability nuance: the count is only re-read inside OnClock, so an order placed and cancelled while the replay is PAUSED is attributed to whatever day is open at the next pump, not to the day it was placed.

**Why:** Positions() returns m_acct.Total() (l.134-135) = m_count, and CSSRTradingEngine::Open() does m_count++ for a pending order at placement (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:719-738) before any fill; cancellation does not decrement m_count. The evaluation only compares the count, never the state.

**Verifiers:** Provable. Positions() returns m_acct.Total() (l.134-135) which is m_count (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:973), and Open() does 'int i = m_count++;' (l.719) BEFORE the pending branch (l.728-737), which returns with state SSR_POS_PENDING and no fill. Cancellation only sets m_pos[i].state = SSR_POS_CANCELLED (l.872) and never decrements m_count - the only m_count-- in the file is the immediate rollback for a pending with no price (l.732). OnClock (l.383-388) compares the count alone and never inspects state, so the next clock after a placement sets m_day_traded = true for the day then open. Three days of place-and-cancel therefore satisfy min_trading_days with zero fills, and a pending placed on day 1 and filled on day 2 credits day 1 - which contradicts the accessor's own comment at l.131-133 ('a trade was OPENED that day'). No QA stage covers it: the evaluation harness (SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:5437-5452) only ever uses market Open/Close / Provable from source. Positions() returns m_acct.Total() (SSR_PropEvaluation.mqh:134-135), and Total() returns m_count (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:973). Open() does 'int i = m_count++' before branching, and for a pending order returns after setting state = SSR_POS_PENDING (l.719-738) - the counter rose at placement, with no fill. Close() on a pending sets state = SSR_POS_CANCELLED and never decrements m_count (l.865-876; the only m_count-- in the file is the 'pending order needs a price' bail-out at l.732). OnClock (l.383-388) compares only the count and never the state, so a placed-and-cancelled pending sets m_day_traded = true - satisfying min_trading_days with zero fills - and a pending placed on day 1 and filled on day 2 credits day 1, contradicting the class comment 'a trade was OPENED that day' (l.131-133) / Provable from source: Positions() returns m_acct.Total() (l.134-135) which is m_count (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:973), CSSRTradingEngine::Open() increments m_count and returns at the SSR_POS_PENDING branch before any fill (l.719-738), and Close() on a pending merely stamps SSR_POS_CANCELLED without decrementing m_count (l.868-876) - PendingCount/ClosedCount exist but the evaluation never consults state. So OnClock's `pos > m_last_positions` (l.383-388) does credit a trading day for a pending order that was placed and cancelled, and credits the placement day rather than the fill day - which contradicts the file's own comment at l.131-133 ('a trade was OPENED that day'). Impact is small and confined to a self-imposed training rule: min_trading_days can be satisfied without a fill, but gaming it takes deliberate effort across separate replay days, no money arithmetic is touched, and the header already states that firms differ on this definition and that it is an input. LOW

#### `trading-analytics-9` — Equity ring drops its oldest half, so the live max drawdown can shrink and the peak be lost

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_Statistics.mqh:272`** · category: drawdown-calc

```mql5
if(m_eq_count >= SSR_EQUITY_SAMPLES)
        {
         int keep = SSR_EQUITY_SAMPLES / 2;
         for(int i = 0; i < keep; i++)
           {
            m_eq_msc[i] = m_eq_msc[i + keep];
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct in substance, wrong in one detail: with a peak at minute 100 and a trough at minute 3000, the ring drop keeps samples [2048..4095], so the TROUGH survives and the PEAK is discarded - the statement's SVG and its drawdown number therefore stay mutually consistent, but both silently lose the earlier peak and the reported max drawdown shrinks as the session runs on. Reachable only when the replay span exceeds 4096 sampled minutes (default 2000 M1 bars does not).

**Why:** EquityDrawdown (l.347-364) starts peak at m_eq_val[0] of whatever survived; the drop keeps only samples [2048..4095]. The comment admits it ('drop the oldest half rather than the whole curve') but the peak-carrying half is the one discarded. SaveInto/RestoreFrom likewise cap at 4096.

**Verifiers:** Traced Sample() at SSR_Statistics.mqh:263-288. With SSR_EQUITY_SAMPLES=4096 (l.33) and SSR_EQUITY_INTERVAL_MSC=60000 (l.34), the ring fills after 4096 replay minutes; the truncation copies m_eq_[2048..4095] down to [0..2047] and sets m_eq_count=2048, so the OLDER half - the one that can hold the peak - is what is discarded. EquityDrawdown (l.347-364) seeds peak=m_eq_val[0] of whatever survived and there is no persisted running maximum: out.max_drawdown is recomputed from the live ring on every ComputeFor (l.596). So the reported max drawdown genuinely can decrease mid-session, and the in-code comment at l.272-274 ('losing every early sample would hide the drawdown that produced the peak we are still measuring against') describes the opposite of what the loop does. No guard, clamp or earlier check anywhere prevents it: OnRewind (l.325-338) only prunes forward, SaveInto/RestoreFrom (l.623-659) also cap at 4096. Reachability is real but requires a replay window > 4096 M1 minutes (~68h); InpReplayBars defaults to 2000 (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:71) so the default configuration never reaches it, which is why LOW is the right severity rather than higher / Source-provable: Sample() (SSR_Statistics.mqh:271-282) discards samples [0..2047] when m_eq_count reaches 4096, and EquityDrawdown (:347-364) re-derives peak from m_eq_val[0] of whatever survives. No running max is kept anywhere, so max_drawdown is recomputed over a truncated window and can DECREASE mid-session - a 'max' statistic that is not monotone. Nothing in MQL5 semantics rescues this; ArrayResize/EnsureRing and the copy loop are correct, so the only question is reachability, which is configuration, not opaque runtime: bars are M1 (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1076-1078), so >4096 replay minutes (~68h) is needed. Default InpReplayBars=2000 is safe, a week-long range or a custom range dialog span is not / Sample() (SSR_Statistics.mqh:268-281) keeps samples [2048..4095] on overflow and EquityDrawdown (l.347) restarts peak at m_eq_val[0] of the survivors, so a peak in the discarded half is lost and the reported max drawdown can DECREASE mid-session. Reachability is better than the finder allows: it needs >4096 replay minutes (~68 market hours), not reachable at the default InpReplayBars=2000, but InpStart (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:70) lets a user set any start date against range.last_msc, so 'replay last week' is a normal-use path with no cap in the range code. The wrong number is not cosmetic: max_drawdown/max_drawdown_pct feed the statement (SSR_Journal.mqh:1124-1131), the review card, and the instructor-side class report (SSR_ClassReport.mqh:228), and the error direction always UNDERSTATES risk, which is the dangerous one. Upgraded from LOW on that basis; not higher because it never touches balance/P&L arithmetic and only bites on multi-day windows

#### `trading-analytics-10` — R multiple excludes commission while every money measure includes it

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_Statistics.mqh:455`** · category: r-multiple

```mql5
if(p.HasR())
           {
            out.total_r += p.RMultiple();
            out.r_trades++;
           }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Same defect also affects SSRBucket (l.221-225 Bucket()), so the per-weekday/per-hour AverageR() rows carry the commission-free R while their net column does not.

**Why:** RMultiple() = (profit + swap)/risk_at_entry (SSR_TradeTypes.mqh:269-270) has no commission term; ComputeFor's net (l.431) and the CSV/HTML rows do. The file's own header comment (l.15-20) defines R as the result over the risk taken, and the commission is part of the result per l.415-430.

**Verifiers:** Verified both halves. SSRVirtualPosition::RMultiple() is `(risk_at_entry > 0.0 ? (profit + swap) / risk_at_entry : 0.0)` at SSR_TradeTypes.mqh:269-270 - no commission term. ComputeFor's money path is `double net = p.profit + p.swap - p.commission;` (SSR_Statistics.mqh:431) and drives wins/losses/gross_profit/gross_loss/expectancy; Bucket() (l.252) and ClosedDrawdownFor (l.706) subtract commission too. So out.average_r and out.expectancy can carry opposite signs for the same trade set. Commission is charged twice per round trip (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:753 on open, l.355 on close) at exec.commission_per_lot, which is user-set from InpCommission (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1161); it defaults to 0.0 (SSR_TradeTypes.mqh:307), so the two rules agree unless the user sets a commission - exactly the divergence the file's own l.415-430 comment says it exists to prevent. Inconsistency is provable from source; LOW because it is a reporting-consistency defect gated behind a non-default input / Provable from source: RMultiple() = (profit + swap) / risk_at_entry (SSR_TradeTypes.mqh:269-270) omits commission, while ComputeFor's net (SSR_Statistics.mqh:431), Bucket's net (:252) and ClosedDrawdownFor (:708) all use profit + swap - commission. Commission is charged twice (open SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:753, close :355), so the worked example (5/lot/side, 1 lot, +3 gross, risk 50) gives net -7 counted as a loss while R = +0.06 - arithmetic, not runtime. The file's own banner at :415-430 ('COMMISSION IS PART OF THE RESULT ... a statement that contradicts itself is worse than none') makes this the same inconsistency it was written to end. Kept LOW because InpCommission defaults to 0.0 (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:90), so the divergence only appears once a user sets a commission / Provable: RMultiple() = (profit + swap)/risk_at_entry (SSR_TradeTypes.mqh:269-270) with no commission term, while ComputeFor's net at l.431 is profit + swap - commission and decides win/loss/gross_profit/gross_loss. Entry commission is charged per lot per side from InpCommission (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:753, host input l.90), so the divergence is one input away from live - and the whole point of that input existing is that a user sets it. The same file's own l.415-430 comment ruled that 'commission is part of the result' and fixed net for exactly this reason; R was left behind. Observable effect: the statement prints 'Average R' and 'Expectancy in R' next to a negative money expectancy over the same trades, i.e. a self-contradicting document - the failure mode that comment says is worse than none. Upgraded from LOW: it is a money-derived metric that can flip sign, not a cosmetic one

#### `trading-analytics-7` — Revenge detection compares only with the previously OPENED trade, not the most recent loss

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_Statistics.mqh:497`** · category: prop-rules

```mql5
if(prev_was_loss && prev_close != SSR_INVALID_TIME &&
            p.open_msc >= prev_close &&
            (p.open_msc - prev_close) <= SSR_REVENGE_WINDOW_MSC)
            out.revenge_trades++;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Also drifts for pending orders, whose slot is allocated at placement rather than at fill, so 'previous in slot order' can differ from 'previously opened' even with strictly non-overlapping trades.

**Why:** prev_close/prev_was_loss are overwritten unconditionally per slot (l.511-512) so 'previous' means previous in creation order, not the latest close before this open. With a tag filter the same applies within the tag. QA smoke only asserts revenge_trades >= 1 in a sequential scenario (SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:2911).

**Verifiers:** Traced the single pass in ComputeFor, SSR_Statistics.mqh:405-513. prev_close/prev_was_loss are set unconditionally at the tail of every closed-trade iteration (l.511-512), and At(i) hands back positions in slot order with no sort anywhere (CSSRTradingEngine::At, SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:1004-1011; m_pos appended at Open(), l.719; I grepped the engine for ArraySort/ArrayRemove/compaction and found none), so 'previous' is previous-in-creation-order. I ran the finder's scenario through the loop by hand: A(loss, close 10:30) sets prev_was_loss=true; B(open 10:01) is rejected by the `p.open_msc >= prev_close` guard at l.498 and then overwrites prev with its own winning close; C(open 10:31) sees prev_was_loss==false, so revenge_trades stays 0. The Journal text at SSR_Journal.mqh:1196-1198 does promise the broader definition ('trades opened within two replay minutes of the previous trade closing at a loss'). No guard prevents this. LOW: an undercount in a coaching metric, only when trades overlap / Source-provable. prev_close/prev_was_loss are assigned unconditionally at the tail of every iteration (SSR_Statistics.mqh:511-512) of a loop that walks m_acct.At(i) in slot order, and m_pos is append-only with no sort or compaction (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:719, At at :1005), so 'previous' means previous-in-open-order. I traced the stated scenario: A (loss, closes 10:30) sets prev; B (opens 10:01) is rejected by the p.open_msc >= prev_close guard, then overwrites prev with a win; C (opens 10:31) sees prev_was_loss == false and is not counted. revenge_trades stays 0 for a trade the printed definition claims it covers - SSR_Journal.mqh:1196-1198 reads 'trades opened within two replay minutes of the previous trade closing at a loss'. The guard silently converts out-of-order closes into missed detections rather than negative windows, so the bias is always under-count. No MQL5 semantics are misread (msc arithmetic is long, SSR_REVENGE_WINDOW_MSC is cast to long) / prev_close/prev_was_loss are assigned unconditionally at the end of every kept slot (l.511-512) and the walk is m_acct.At(i) in slot order, so 'previous' is the previously-created trade, not the most recent close before this open - the finder's A/B/C case misses a textbook revenge entry. Worse than described in one respect: slots are created at REQUEST time (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:705-730, pendings get a slot when placed and are filled later), so the order can diverge even without overlapping market trades. Impact stays LOW: revenge_trades is one coaching counter in the statement (SSR_Journal.mqh:1196-1198) and the review; it gates nothing, corrupts no money arithmetic, and the guard p.open_msc >= prev_close keeps it from inventing positives, so the failure mode is an undercount a reader can still verify against the printed trade list

#### `trading-exec-10` — SSRPendingFor classifies a long entry inside the spread as BUY_STOP, which fills on the next tick like a market order

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_TradeTypes.mqh:120`** · category: input-validation

```mql5
bool is_long = (stop < entry);
if(is_long)
   out = (entry < bid ? SSR_ORDER_BUY_LIMIT : SSR_ORDER_BUY_STOP);
else
   out = (entry > bid ? SSR_ORDER_SELL_LIMIT : SSR_ORDER_SELL_STOP);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: For longs the limit/stop decision and the 'entry is on the price' guard both use bid while CheckPendings triggers BUY_STOP on ask, so any long entry in (bid + min_dist, ask] - up to 18 points wide at min_dist 2 and a 20-point spread - is accepted as a pending and fills at ask + slippage on the next tick. Because the size came from the entry line but the fill is at ask, the position also carries slightly more money at risk than the requested percent (risk_at_entry is recomputed from the fill, so reporting stays honest). The short side is unaffected.

**Why:** The limit-vs-stop decision (122) and the on-the-price guard (113) both use bid for the long side; the long trigger price in CheckPendings is the ask. Any entry in (bid + min_dist, ask) is therefore an immediately-triggering stop. With min_dist of 2 points and a 20-point spread the window is 18 points wide.

**Verifiers:** Traced end to end. SSRPendingFor's on-the-price refusal and the limit/stop split both measure against bid (SSR_TradeTypes.mqh:113, 120-124), while the long pending's trigger in CheckPendings is the ask (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:337: BUY_STOP hit when m_ask >= request_price). The only caller passes min_dist = pt * 2.0 (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:1010), and neither OpenPendingWithRisk (:783-801) nor Open (:705-739) imposes any minimum distance from the market on a pending — Open just stores request_price and returns. So any long entry in (bid + 2 points, ask) is classified BUY_STOP and is already triggered when placed; with the default InpSpreadPoints = 20 (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:76) that is an 18-point window, and the fill takes FillPrice(long, true) = ask + slippage, worse than the drawn line — exactly what the refusal text claims to prevent. The short side is not symmetric: entry above bid becomes SELL_LIMIT (triggers on bid >= entry), which does not fire immediately. LOW is right: the position is virtual and bounded by the spread / Both halves check out in source. SSRPendingFor uses bid for the on-the-price refusal (SSR_TradeTypes.mqh:113) and for the limit-vs-stop decision (120-124), while CSSRTradingEngine::CheckPendings triggers BUY_STOP on ask >= request_price (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:337) and fills at FillPrice(long,true) (345). Both call sites pass min_dist = pt*2.0 (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:296 and 1010), so any long entry in (bid + 2 points, ask) passes the guard, is classified BUY_STOP, and is triggered by the very next tick - and the engine's ask is bid + spread whenever the bar has none (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:604-607, InpSpreadPoints default 20). The window is 18 points wide at that default, and the asymmetry is right: BUY_LIMIT (ask <= price), SELL_LIMIT (bid >= price) and SELL_STOP (bid <= price) cannot trigger immediately from inside the spread. LOW because the fill is at the correct ask and only mislabels a market order as pending; it closes if the guard is widened to cover the ask side / The asymmetry is provable and I verified both halves. SSRPendingFor compares the entry against bid for the on-the-price refusal (113) and for the limit/stop split (120-124), while CheckPendings triggers a BUY_STOP on ask >= request_price (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:337). Callers pass min_dist = pt*2.0 (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:295, 1010), and the default synthetic spread is 20 points (host:76), so any long entry in (bid + 2pt, ask] is classified BUY_STOP and satisfies its own trigger on the very next tick - exactly what the refusal text at 113-118 claims to prevent. Money angle, which the finding does not spell out: the lot was sized from the entry LINE to the stop (OpenPendingWithRisk with en), yet the fill is FillPrice(is_long,true) at ask plus adverse slippage, so the realised stop distance - and therefore the money at risk - exceeds the requested risk percent by up to the spread; risk_at_entry is recomputed from the actual fill (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:351-354), so the statement's R stays honest. The short side is sound (SELL_STOP triggers on bid <= price and is classified off bid, so it cannot self-trigger). Kept at LOW: the band is only spread-wide, it needs the entry line dragged into the spread, and the trade is still executed and accounted correctly

#### `trading-exec-9` — Limit-order entries receive adverse slippage and can fill worse than their limit price

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:345`** · category: slippage

```mql5
bool is_long = m_pos[i].IsLong();
m_pos[i].type       = (is_long ? SSR_ORDER_BUY : SSR_ORDER_SELL);
m_pos[i].open_price = FillPrice(is_long, true);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: FillPrice (120-126) applies adverse slippage to pending-order fills of every type, so BUY_LIMIT/SELL_LIMIT entries (CheckPendings 335-336, 345) can fill worse than their limit price - inconsistent with the TP treatment the file argues for at 486-490 and implements at 544. Only with InpSlippage > 0 (default 0). risk_at_entry is NOT affected: it is recomputed from the real open_price at 350-353; the residual inaccuracy is only the lot size, sized off the request price in OpenPendingWithRisk 797.

**Why:** FillPrice (120-126) adds slip unconditionally; CheckPendings (345) uses it for all four pending types. Only with InpSlippage > 0 (default 0).

**Verifiers:** FillPrice (120-126) adds m_exec.slippage_points unconditionally on the opening side, and CheckPendings 345 uses it for all four pending types including BUY_LIMIT/SELL_LIMIT whose triggers are `m_ask <= request_price` / `m_bid >= request_price` (335-336). When the trigger tick is at or near the limit the fill exceeds the limit by up to the slippage distance, while the file's own reasoning at 486-490 fills TP exactly at its level (544) because a limit fills at its price or better - so the two limit cases really are modelled inconsistently. Effective only with InpSlippage > 0 (default 0.0, SSReplayStandalone.mq5:91), and a gapped trigger can still fill better than the limit, hence LOW. One sub-claim is wrong: risk_at_entry is recomputed at fill from the actual open_price (350-353), so it does not understate the risk; only the lot size from OpenPendingWithRisk/LotForRisk (797) is computed off the requested price / The code path is as described: FillPrice (120-126) adds slippage_points unconditionally and its own comment calls it 'the price a market order actually gets'; CheckPendings 345 uses it for all four pending types, so a BUY_LIMIT triggered at m_ask <= request_price is filled at m_ask + slip, which can land above the limit. That contradicts the file's own reasoning at 486-490, where a take profit is treated as a limit that fills at its level precisely because a limit does not suffer adverse slippage - and it is a divergence from terminal semantics, where deviation applies to market orders, not to limit entries. Kept only as an improvement: exec.slippage_points comes from InpSlippage, default 0.0 (host line 91), so the path is inert unless the user opts in / Confirmed from source: FillPrice (120-126) adds slippage unconditionally and CheckPendings (345) uses it for all four pending types, so a BUY_LIMIT triggered at m_ask == request_price fills at request_price + slip - worse than its own limit. For BUY_STOP/SELL_STOP that is correct (a stop is a market order, as StopFill's comment argues), but the same file argues at 486-490 that a limit fills at its price or better and honours that for take-profit (544), so the treatment of entry limits is internally inconsistent, and risk_at_entry computed off the slipped fill no longer matches the risk the user sized at the request price. Impact is genuinely small: InpSlippage defaults to 0 (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:91), so nothing happens unless the user opts into slippage, and then the error is bounded by the configured points on limit entries only. LOW stands - no upgrade, since the direction is pessimistic (it never flatters a result) and it cannot leak future data or corrupt the balance beyond that bounded amount

#### `trading-exec-12` — Open() accepts any positive volume from the external path without step/min/max validation

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:714`** · category: input-validation

```mql5
if(volume <= 0.0)
  { m_last_error = "volume must be positive"; return 0; }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate as a validation gap, but reachable only via the Publisher external path (gated by InpAllowTrade, default false) and hand-written strategies; all panel paths are already rounded by LotForRisk, and the account arithmetic stays self-consistent at any positive volume, so the effect is presentational fidelity rather than incorrect money.

**Why:** Open() 705-756 validates only volume > 0; m_risk.VolMin()/VolMax() and the step are unused on this path. The UI paths go through LotForRisk which rounds, so only the Publisher and Strategy raw paths are affected.

**Verifiers:** Open() (705-756) validates only volume > 0 (714) and never consults the lot step, min or max, although the engine owns them: CSSRRiskEngine reads SYMBOL_VOLUME_MIN/MAX/STEP (SSR_RiskEngine.mqh 49-56), exposes VolMin/VolMax (73-74) and has a step-rounding helper (29-31) used by LotForRisk only. Both raw callers are real and unguarded: SSR_Publisher.mqh:301/307 passes the wire's a1 straight into Open(), and CSSRStrategy::Send (SSR_IStrategy.mqh:185) passes a strategy's volume unrounded. The downstream inconsistency is also real - GroupPort.ClosePartial rounds to SYMBOL_VOLUME_STEP and refuses 'too small to split at this lot step' (SSR_GroupPort.mqh 852-858). Impact is cosmetic/statistical in a virtual engine, so LOW is right / Provable: Open() 705-756 validates capacity, volume > 0, a price for pendings and nothing else; the risk engine already holds m_vol_min/m_vol_max/m_vol_step and a rounding helper (SSR_RiskEngine.mqh 23-31, 49-56, 73-74) that only the LotForRisk paths use. Both raw callers are real - Publisher 301/309 passes the external client's a1 straight through as volume, and CSSRStrategyBroker::Send (SSR_IStrategy.mqh 185) does the same for strategy code - and neither clamps or rounds first. The divergence from terminal semantics is genuine (OrderSend answers an off-step or out-of-range volume with 10014 invalid volume), but the harm is confined to fidelity of the journal, R and margin figures plus a later ClosePartial refusal, hence LOW rather than a correctness failure of the account / The code fact holds: Open() (705-756) checks only volume > 0, and m_risk.VolMin()/VolMax() plus the step (SSR_RiskEngine.mqh:73-74, RoundToStep at 29-31) are consulted only inside LotForRisk, which the Publisher external path (SSR_Publisher.mqh:301/309) and the strategy raw path (SSR_IStrategy.mqh:185) bypass. But the impact is close to nil under this lens: every UI path (Market/MarketAt -> OpenWithRisk, the dragged-line orders, PreviewLot) goes through LotForRisk and is already rounded and clamped, the external path additionally requires InpAllowTrade, which defaults to false (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:124), and nothing downstream miscomputes - the P/L, commission, swap, margin and R arithmetic is all linear in volume and stays self-consistent at 0.003 or 7.777 lots. The only observable consequence is a fidelity blemish (a size no venue would accept appears in the journal) and GroupPort.ClosePartial later refusing to split it. Downgraded from LOW to IMPROVEMENT: no money is corrupted, no future data leaks, and the safety guarantee is untouched

#### `trading-exec-11` — AmbiguousPercent divides ambiguous fills of still-open positions by the closed count

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:999`** · category: zero-empty

```mql5
double            AmbiguousPercent(void)
  {
   int closed = ClosedCount();
   return (closed > 0 ? 100.0 * (double)m_ambiguous_count / (double)closed : 0.0);
  }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Real numerator/denominator mismatch, but it affects only CSSRTradingEngine::ToString()'s log line and the SSR_GV_AMBIGUOUS global export (SSR_Publisher.mqh:206). The statement, CSV and HTML honesty figure is computed independently in SSR_Statistics.mqh over closed trades only and is correct, so no user-facing report shows the inflated percentage.

**Why:** m_ambiguous_count is incremented in CheckPendings (382) for OPEN positions and in BookExit (239) for closes, but the denominator counts only state == CLOSED (988-994).

**Verifiers:** Numerator and denominator genuinely count different populations. m_ambiguous_count is incremented both for still-OPEN positions at fill time (CheckPendings 381-382) and for closes (BookExit 236-240), while AmbiguousPercent (999-1003) divides by ClosedCount(), which counts only state == SSR_POS_CLOSED (988-994). One ambiguous open fill with one unambiguous closed trade prints 100%, two print 200%, and with ambiguous fills but zero closes it prints 0%. The figure is not internal: it is published to external clients as SSR_GV_AMBIGUOUS (SSR_Publisher.mqh:206) and rendered by ToString (1266-1274) / Mismatched numerator and denominator, provable from source. m_ambiguous_count is incremented for a still-OPEN position at the pending-fill site (371-383) as well as for closes in BookExit (236-240), and RestoreFrom 1216-1217 counts the flag on positions in any state, while AmbiguousPercent (999-1003) divides by ClosedCount(), which counts only state == SSR_POS_CLOSED (988-994). So one ambiguous open fill against one clean closed trade reports 100%, two report 200%, and the figure is user-visible: ToString 1266-1273 prints it and Publisher 206 exports it to SSR_GV_AMBIGUOUS. The converse also holds - ambiguous open fills with nothing closed yet report 0.0%. LOW, since it misstates an honesty metric rather than the account / The arithmetic mismatch is real: m_ambiguous_count is incremented for OPEN positions at fill (381-383) as well as for closes (236-240), while AmbiguousPercent's denominator is ClosedCount() (988-994), which counts only state == CLOSED - so the ratio can exceed 100%, and conversely reports a flat 0.0 while ambiguous fills are open but nothing has closed yet. But the blast radius is far smaller than the finding implies: the user-facing honesty figure in the statement, the CSV and the HTML report does NOT come from this method. SSR_Statistics.mqh computes ambiguous_trades over closed positions only (loop filter at 407-408 `!p.IsClosed() -> continue`, count at 463-464) and divides by out.trades (544), which is correct. CSSRTradingEngine::AmbiguousPercent has exactly two consumers: ToString() (1271, a log line) and the Publisher's SSR_GV_AMBIGUOUS global export (SSR_Publisher.mqh:206). So nothing a trader reads in the statement is wrong; a debug log line and one integration global can read >100%. Downgraded from LOW to IMPROVEMENT accordingly

#### `ui-dialogs-14` — CSSRFirstRun::Show reports success without checking the card fits, so the once-ever card is consumed unseen on a short chart

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_FirstRun.mqh:89`** · category: boundary

```mql5
      int x = 14, y = SSR_PANEL_H + 40, w = 396, h = 104;
      m_w.Rect("bg", x, y, w, h, SSR_C_PANEL, SSR_C_PANEL_EDGE);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Show() reports success without checking that the card fits, and the host writes the once-ever seen.txt on that return, so on a short chart the onboarding card can be spent unread. Two narrowings: the card is fully off-screen only when the chart is shorter than ~376 px (the 356..376 band the panel warning misses); between 376 and 480 px it is merely clipped. And it is reached only in two-window mode — the host guards the block with `InpFirstCard && !one_chart_ok` (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1736), so with the default InpOneChart=true the card (and the marker write) never happens at all.

**Why:** SSR_PANEL_H = 23+32+27+21+21+SSR_SHEET_H(186)+18+8 = 336 (SSR_Theme.mqh:499), so y = 376 and the card ends at 480. Show() reads no chart dimension and its only failure path is `chart_id == 0` (:82-83) - it returns true unconditionally otherwise (:121). The host writes the marker on that return value: `if(g_first.Show(g_panel_chart)) CSSRFirstRun::MarkSeen();` (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1742-1743). The host's own height warning fires only below SSR_PANEL_H+20 = 356 (:1676-1682), i.e. it does not cover the 356..480 band where the card is invisible but the panel is fine.

**Verifiers:** Arithmetic and control flow check out: SSR_PANEL_H = 23+32+27+21+21+186+18+8 = 336 (SSR_Theme.mqh:449, 499 with SSR_LAYOUT_RAIL active), so the card occupies y 376..480; Show() reads no chart dimension, its only false path is chart_id == 0, and it returns true unconditionally (SSR_FirstRun.mqh:81-122), while the host consumes the once-ever marker on that return (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1742-1743) and its own height warning fires only below 356 (1676-1682). Two scope corrections below keep this at LOW rather than higher / Arithmetic and control flow both verified. SSR_PANEL_H = 23+32+27+21+21+186+18+8 = 336 (SSR_Theme.mqh:449, 499), so the card occupies y 376..480 (SSR_FirstRun.mqh:89), and CSSRWidgets anchors every object CORNER_LEFT_UPPER with OBJPROP_YDISTANCE measured from the top (SSR_Widgets.mqh:160, 211-212, 244-245) - an object at y=376 on a shorter chart is simply not on screen. Show() reads no chart dimension, its only false path is chart_id == 0 (82-83), and it returns true unconditionally at 121; the host consumes the once-ever marker on that value (`if(g_first.Show(g_panel_chart)) CSSRFirstRun::MarkSeen();`, SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1742-1743) while its own height warning fires only below SSR_PANEL_H+20 = 356 (1676-1682), leaving the 356..480 band uncovered. Recovery really is deleting MQL5/Files/SSReplay/seen.txt. Note it also never uses the widgets' own Extent()/CheckFrame() overflow instrument, which exists for exactly this / Arithmetic and control flow both check out. SSR_PANEL_H = 23+32+27+21+21+186+18+8 = 336 (SSR_Theme.mqh:449, 499), so the card occupies y 376..480 at CORNER_LEFT_UPPER (SSR_Widgets.mqh:160), and no widget clamps to the chart - Extent() only records a maximum for instrumentation (SSR_Widgets.mqh:129, 189). Show() reads no chart dimension and returns true on every path past chart_id == 0 (SSR_FirstRun.mqh:81-121), and the host consumes the once-ever marker on that return value: 'if(g_first.Show(g_panel_chart)) CSSRFirstRun::MarkSeen();' (host:1742-1743). The host's own height warning only fires below SSR_PANEL_H+20 = 356 (host:1676-1682), leaving the 356..480 band where the panel is fine and the card is off-screen. Reachable on any chart under ~480 px tall, which an open Toolbox produces routinely, and the only recovery is deleting MQL5/Files/SSReplay/seen.txt - the host does print that hint, but only on later runs. Onboarding text lost, no data or money effect: LOW is right

#### `ui-port-session-12` — The session list re-reads and fully parses every file on every dialog render

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_GroupPort.mqh:937`** · category: cpu

```mql5
      if(!m_sessions.Peek(m_names[i], summary))
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate: Peek fully parses each session file and the dialog calls it once per visible row on every Render(), with no cache and no header-only mode. The per-file parse is capped by SSR_SF_MAX_ENTRIES = 16384 / SSR_SF_MAX_SECTIONS = 1024, so the worst case is roughly 8 x 16k entries per click rather than an unbounded amount.

**Why:** Peek constructs a local CSSRSessionFile and calls f.Load(Path(name)) (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:217-219), which parses the entire file including every `pos`, `leg` and `e` row before any key is read. The dialog calls SessionSummary(idx) inside its row loop with SSR_SD_ROWS = 8 (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:27, 158-178), and Render() runs on Open() and on every up/down/row/load click (l.255, 257, 264, 284). There is no cache and no header-only read mode. With 8 visible sessions of 400 trades each that is roughly 8 x 9,000 parsed entries per click, on a modal drawn over the replay chart while the pump timer is still firing.

**Verifiers:** Trace confirms no cache and no header-only mode anywhere on the path. SessionSummary (GroupPort:932-940) calls m_sessions.Peek, which constructs a local CSSRSessionFile and calls f.Load(Path(name)) (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:215-219); Load (SSR_SessionFile.mqh:206-259) reads the file line by line to EOF, growing m_ent_sec/m_ent_key/m_ent_val for every entry - pos, leg and e rows included - before Peek reads its five keys. The dialog calls m_port.SessionSummary(idx) inside the row loop for SSR_SD_ROWS=8 (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:27, 157-178), and Render() runs from Open() and from the up, down, row, load and del handlers (255, 257, 262-268, 284, 293). Severity stays LOW: it is a modal, user-paced interaction and the cost is a file parse per visible row per click, not a correctness fault / Mechanism verified end to end. CSSRSessionManager::Peek (SessionManager:216-243) constructs a local CSSRSessionFile and calls f.Load(Path(name)); Load (SSR_SessionFile.mqh:206-259) reads the file line by line to EOF, appending every key=value pair - including every pos/leg/e row - into the dynamic m_ent_sec/m_ent_key/m_ent_val arrays before Peek reads its five [session] keys. GroupPort::SessionSummary (l.932-940) calls Peek per index with no cache, and the dialog calls m_port.SessionSummary(idx) inside its row loop bounded by SSR_SD_ROWS = 8 (SessionDialog:27, 172-173), with Render() re-entered on up, down, row click, load and after save (SessionDialog:255-257, 264, 284, and Open() from RequestSave). There is no header-only read mode anywhere. Two corrections to the magnitude rather than the mechanism: the parse is bounded by SSR_SF_MAX_ENTRIES = 16384 and SSR_SF_MAX_SECTIONS = 1024 (SSR_SessionFile.mqh:33-34), so the worst case is ~16k entries per file and ~131k per render, not the unbounded 9,000-per-file figure implied; and the cost is string/array work in a modal that a human clicks a handful of times, so it is an efficiency wart, not a hang. Severity stays LOW / The mechanism is real: Peek constructs a CSSRSessionFile and calls Load (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:213-219), which parses every line of the file into three parallel dynamic arrays before a single key is read (SSR_SessionFile.mqh:206-258), and the dialog calls m_port.SessionSummary(idx) once per visible row inside its render loop with SSR_SD_ROWS = 8 (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:27, 172-174). No cache, no header-only mode. But the cost is far smaller than claimed and the trigger is narrower. Parsing is capped at SSR_SF_MAX_ENTRIES = 16384 entries per file with amortised doubling growth (Reserve, l.64-74), so per-file work is bounded regardless of trade count; and Render() runs only from Open() and from the dialog's own click handler - the host references g_session_dlg only at Create/Destroy/IsOpen/Open/OnEvent (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1466, 2116, 3052, 3091, 3167-3169), so it is not redrawn per pump. The worst a user observes is a brief hitch on a click inside a modal they opened deliberately. That is an efficiency improvement, not a defect with a user-visible failure

#### `ui-port-session-10` — A save made from the panel records settings that are not the session's

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_GroupPort.mqh:949`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
      SSRSessionSettings set;
      set.Init();
      set.slot = (m_sink != NULL ? m_sink.Slot() : 1);
      if(!m_sessions.Save(name, set))
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate. Worth noting the port's comment at GroupPort:947-950 states the limitation explicitly ("the panel knows none of the settings; the host owns them"), so this is a knowingly accepted gap whose only defect is that the written file is indistinguishable from a truthful one.

**Why:** Init() sets seed 0, blind 0, pause_flags 0, session_mode 0, slot 1, ticks_per_bar 8, spread_points 0.0, chart_tf PERIOD_M5 (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:62-67) and Save writes those verbatim (l.184-191). The host's own save path uses the real values (`SSRSessionSettings set; CollectSettings(set);`, Standalone.mq5:2137-2139), so two saves of the same running session disagree depending on which button produced them. The file's own comment at l.182-183 says the seed is written "so a session that was random can be recognised as such months later" - which a panel-made save cannot do. The port's comment acknowledges the gap; the reason it is not already visible is that nothing in the product calls ReadSettings (l.244), so the wrong values are stored and never yet read.

**Verifiers:** Verified on both sides. SaveSession (GroupPort:941-957) does set.Init() then overwrites only set.slot, and Init() (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:62-67) fixes seed 0, blind 0, pause_flags 0, session_mode 0, ticks_per_bar 8, spread_points 0.0, chart_tf PERIOD_M5; Save writes all eight verbatim into [settings] (184-191), under a comment at 182-183 claiming the seed is written "so a session that was random can be recognised as such months later". The host's own path calls CollectSettings (Standalone.mq5:415-426, used at 2137-2139), which fills the real values from g_picker.Seed(), InpBlind, g_autopause.Flags(), InpPauseSession, InpSlot, InpTicksPerBar, InpSpreadPoints, InpChartTf. So two saves of one running session write different, indistinguishable [settings] blocks. grep confirms ReadSettings has no product caller (only SSR_T12_Session.mq5:497), so the impact today is a persisted artefact that lies rather than a functional fault - LOW is right / The data defect is provable. GroupPort::SaveSession (l.942-957) does SSRSessionSettings set; set.Init(); then overwrites only set.slot, and Init() (SessionManager:62-67) hardcodes seed 0, blind 0, pause_flags 0, session_mode 0, ticks_per_bar 8, spread_points 0.0, chart_tf PERIOD_M5. CSSRSessionManager::Save writes all eight fields verbatim into [settings] (SessionManager:181-191), directly under its own comment that the seed is written 'so a session that was random can be recognised as such months later' - which a panel-made save inverts, recording seed 0 for a session that was seeded. The host path does it correctly (CollectSettings at Standalone:415, called at 2138), so two saves of the same live session disagree by which button produced them, and SaveSession is reachable from the dialog at SessionDialog:242 and 318. I confirmed the mitigation the finding itself concedes: ReadSettings (SessionManager:244-259) has exactly one caller in the tree, SSR_T12_Session.mq5:497, so nothing in the product reads the falsified values today. Against confirming: GroupPort:950-952 explicitly documents the limitation ('the panel knows none of the settings; the host owns them ... A host that wants the full set calls the manager directly'). I still let it stand because a documented limitation that writes concrete wrong values into a persisted artefact is a latent data bug rather than a design note, but severity stays LOW / The facts are right: SaveSession calls set.Init() and overrides only slot (GroupPort:945-951), Init() plants seed 0, blind 0, pause_flags 0, session_mode 0, ticks_per_bar 8, spread_points 0.0, chart_tf PERIOD_M5 (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:62-67), and Save writes those verbatim into [settings] (l.180-191), while the host's own deinit save uses CollectSettings (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2136-2138). So two saves of one running session disagree. But the impact lens empties this out almost entirely: ReadSettings is called from nowhere in the product - only from SSR_T12_Session.mq5:497 - and Restore() never touches the [settings] section, so no load, no resume and no panel readout is affected. Nothing a user does today produces a wrong behaviour; what is wrong is metadata in a file only a human reading it could be misled by. IMPROVEMENT rather than LOW, on the ground that the effect is unreachable through any product path

#### `ui-plumbing-1` — The key card - the product's primary discovery surface - is the one user-facing screen that is never translated

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_KeyCard.mqh:92`** · category: utf8

```mql5
         m_w.Label(id + "a", x + 12, ry, b[i].label, SSR_C_HOLD, SSR_FS_SMALL);
         m_w.Label(id + "b", x + 86, ry, b[i].what, SSR_C_TEXT, SSR_FS_SMALL);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The key card's 18 listed rows draw untranslated English because SSRKeyBindings() stores raw literals in .label/.what with no string-catalogue ids, so a translated install shows an English body under a translated title and footer (and audit A19 cannot see it, since the drawing argument is a variable). Effect is confined to help text - the key labels are language-neutral and every shortcut still works.

**Why:** SSRKeyBindings() stores raw English literals in SSRKeyBinding.label and .what; there is no ENUM_SSR_STR id for any of them (the catalogue has only SSR_S_KEYCARD_TITLE, SSR_S_KEYCARD_CLOSE, SSR_S_ALL_VIRTUAL for this card). Audit A19 cannot see it: tools/ssr_audit.py:1456 skips SSR_Strings/SSR_Widgets/SSR_Layout and otherwise only reports LITERALS appearing directly in a drawing call's text argument - here the argument is the variable b[i].label, so the audit finds nothing and passes.

**Verifiers:** Verified directly. CSSRKeyCard draws its chrome through T() (SSR_KeyCard.mqh:79-81, 97-99, and the catalogue holds only keycard.title/keycard.close/all-virtual, SSR_Strings.mqh:197-198, 507-508) but the body rows pass b[i].label and b[i].what (lines 92-93), which SSRKeyBindings() fills with raw English literals (SSR_Keys.mqh:125-218; counted 18 rows with listed=true, i.e. 36 untranslated cells) with no string id anywhere. A19 cannot catch it: tools/ssr_audit.py:1450-1470 only matches string literals appearing directly as a draw call's text argument, and here the argument is a variable. So with InpLanguage=fa (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:187, 1804) a Persian user pressing H gets a Persian frame around an English list. Severity reduced from MEDIUM: this is an i18n coverage gap with no functional failure — every key still works and the labels (Space, PgDn, R, Tab) are the physical keys / Directly readable. SSRKeyBindings() stores raw English literals in .label/.what (SSR_Keys.mqh:130-218: "play / pause", "one candle forward", "TAKE THE TRADE the lines describe", ...), and CSSRKeyCard draws them straight from those fields (SSR_KeyCard.mqh:92-93) while only the title, close hint and footer pass through T() (79-81, 97-99). The translation path can only override catalogue entries by name (SSR_Strings.mqh:619-720, T() at 728-742), and the catalogue holds no ids for any binding text (only SSR_S_KEYCARD_TITLE/_CLOSE/SSR_S_ALL_VIRTUAL, 197-199, 507-509), so no lang file can reach them. Counting listed==true gives exactly 18 rows, i.e. 36 untranslated cells, as claimed. Severity lowered to LOW: the card remains legible English rather than broken, and nothing malfunctions - but for a Persian install the H card is mixed-script, and audit A19 cannot see it because the drawing argument is a variable / Verified end to end: every binding's .label and .what is an English literal inside SSRKeyBindings (SSR_Keys.mqh:131-218, e.g. 'play / pause', 'TAKE THE TRADE the lines describe'), the card draws them straight through m_w.Label (SSR_KeyCard.mqh:92-93) with no ENUM_SSR_STR id in the catalogue for any of them, and only title/close/footer pass through T() (79-81, 97-99). I counted 18 listed rows, matching the claim, and SSRLoadLanguage does translate everything that goes through T() from a real MQL5/Files/SSReplay/lang/<code>.txt, so the mixed-language screen is genuinely what a translated install shows. But the observable is a help overlay in the wrong language, on a list whose left column ('Space', 'R', 'PgDn') is language-neutral anyway; the keys themselves keep working, nothing is truncated, no arithmetic, data or trade-safety guarantee is touched. That is a localisation completeness gap, not a MEDIUM product fault: downgraded MEDIUM -> LOW

Meanwhile SSR_Panel.mqh:928 still states 'K the command palette had no key and is now unreachable'. No MQL5 semantics are misread; impact is discoverability only, hence LOW / All three legs check out: the panel opens the palette on Ctrl+K ahead of the key table (SSR_Panel.mqh:2827-2832), the caption K button is not merely undrawn but removed (m_w.Remove("palette") at 936, with the comment at 929 stating the palette 'had no key and is now unreachable'), SSR_VK_K appears in no SSRAddKey call, and the key card is generated purely from SSRKeyBindings() (SSR_KeyCard.mqh:61-95), so the product's only key list cannot mention it. LOW confirmed, not higher

#### `ui-panel-12` — The key card is torn down and rebuilt from scratch by the panel ten times a second while it is up

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:776`** · category: cpu

```mql5
      if(m_keys.IsUp())
         m_keys.Show(m_chart);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct in mechanism and cadence, but the per-frame cost is smaller than stated. SSRKeyBindings declares 22 bindings of which 18 carry listed==true (minus/Num+/Num-/L are listed==false), so Show() rebuilds 1 OBJ_RECTANGLE_LABEL + 39 OBJ_LABELs (title, subtitle, 2 per listed binding, foot) = 40 objects: one ObjectsDeleteAll, ~40 ObjectCreate, and 9 + 39*6 = 243 property writes per frame, not ~41+41 objects and ~450 writes.

**Why:** CSSRKeyCard::Show (SSR_KeyCard.mqh:55-104) calls SSRKeyBindings(b) (:61), m_w.Attach (:71) and m_w.RemoveAll() (:72) before drawing 'bg', 't', 't2', two labels per listed binding (:92-93) and 'foot' (:97). m_w.RemoveAll is ObjectsDeleteAll + ForgetAll (SSR_Widgets.mqh:637-643), so nothing can be cached between frames by construction. SSRKeyBindings itself rebuilds a 22-entry array with ArrayResize per call (SSR_Keys.mqh:112-122, 125-222). The panel invokes it unconditionally whenever m_keys.IsUp(), and the comment at :767-775 explains why the redraw is needed for z-order - the redraw is right, the destructive implementation is the cost.

**Verifiers:** Render() :776-777 calls m_keys.Show(m_chart) unconditionally whenever the card is up, and Show() (SSR_KeyCard.mqh:55-104) begins with SSRKeyBindings(b) (which does ArrayResize(out,0) then rebuilds the whole table, SSR_Keys.mqh:125-222) and m_w.RemoveAll() = ObjectsDeleteAll(chart,"SSRK_") + ForgetAll (SSR_Widgets.mqh:637-643), then recreates every object. Nothing can be cached across frames by construction. Cadence confirmed: SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3062-3066 repaints when now_ms - g_panel_paint >= 100, on the same OnTimer as the InpPumpMs=40 engine pump (:78, :1574). The mechanism is exactly as described; only the arithmetic is inflated / Provable. SSR_Panel.mqh:776-777 calls m_keys.Show(m_chart) on every frame while IsUp(), and CSSRKeyCard::Show (SSR_KeyCard.mqh:55-104) calls SSRKeyBindings(b) then m_w.Attach + m_w.RemoveAll() (:71-72) before recreating bg/t/t2/two labels per listed binding/foot. RemoveAll is ObjectsDeleteAll + ForgetAll (SSR_Widgets.mqh:637-643) so the 512-slot cache cannot help by construction, and SSRKeyBindings rebuilds the array with ArrayResize on every call (SSR_Keys.mqh:113-122). I counted 22 SSRAddKey calls with 18 listed, i.e. 18*2+4 = 40 objects / Provable: Render() calls m_keys.Show(m_chart) whenever m_keys.IsUp() (:776-777), and Show() begins with m_w.RemoveAll() = ObjectsDeleteAll + ForgetAll (SSR_KeyCard.mqh:72) before recreating bg/t/t2, two labels for each of the 18 listed bindings and foot, and rebuilding the binding array (SSRKeyBindings ArrayResize per call). So ~40 delete + ~40 create + a few hundred property writes + an extra ChartRedraw, ten times a second. Downgraded from MEDIUM on impact: this is bounded to the seconds a user holds a help overlay open, is dismissed with one keypress, touches no state, and the most likely observable is the card flickering rather than a measurable replay stutter - the stutter figure is extrapolated from an unrelated whole-frame measurement on a terminal nobody has run

#### `ui-panel-13` — Caption chip row overruns the collapse button by 6 px when fidelity is degraded and both BLIND and PROP chips are shown

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:887`** · category: boundary

```mql5
      int cx = x + 140;
      bool degraded = (m_state.fidelity_effective != m_state.fidelity);
      cx += m_w.Chip("chfid", cx, y + 4,
                     SSRFidelityShort(m_state.fidelity_effective) +
                     (degraded ? " !" : ""),
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct for the shipping English catalogue only. In Persian the row clears: blind = "کور" (3 chars = 28) and prop = "پراپ" (4 chars = 34) give 140+52+4 = 196, +28+4 = 228, 228+34 = 262 < 268 (fa.txt:124-125). The collision is therefore English-specific, and it also needs SSRFidelityShort to be the 5-char "SYNTH" - a degraded "BAR !" or "FULL !" also clears.

**Why:** CSSRWidgets::Chip returns w = 10 + StringLen(text) * 6 (SSR_Widgets.mqh:529) and each caller adds 4 (:894, :898, :904). SSRFidelityShort returns 'SYNTH' (SSR_Types.mqh:198) and the degraded suffix is ' !' (:891), giving 7 characters -> 52 px. T(SSR_S_BLIND) is 'BLIND' -> 40 px and T(SSR_S_PROP) is 'PROP' -> 34 px (SSR_Strings.mqh:458-459). 140+52+4 = 196; 196+40+4 = 240; 240+34 = 274 > 268. The collapse button is at x + W - 42 with W = SSR_PANEL_W = 310 (:936, SSR_Theme.mqh:487). The chips' plates are Rects so CheckFrame sees them, but they end inside the 310 px frame and the instrument only reports overflow past the frame, not collisions inside it.

**Verifiers:** Verified every input. Chip returns w = 10 + StringLen(text)*6 (SSR_Widgets.mqh:529) and each of the three call sites adds 4 (:894, :898, :904) from cx = x+140 (:887). SSRFidelityShort(SYNTHETIC_TICK) = "SYNTH" (SSR_Types.mqh:198) plus the " !" suffix at :891 = 7 chars = 52; SSR_S_BLIND = "BLIND" = 40, SSR_S_PROP = "PROP" = 34 (SSR_Strings.mqh:458-459). 140+52+4 = 196, +40+4 = 240, 240+34 = 274. The collapse button is created AFTER the chips in DrawCaption at x + W - 42 = x+268, y+3, 18x15, and the chip plates sit at y+4..y+17, so they overlap vertically too - and because creation order is the only z-order, the button paints over the chip's last 6 px / Arithmetic verified against source. Chip returns w = 10 + StringLen(text)*6 (SSR_Widgets.mqh:529) and each caller adds 4 (:894, :898, :904). SSRFidelityShort returns "SYNTH" (SSR_Types.mqh:198), the degraded suffix is " !" (:891) = 7 chars -> 52; T(SSR_S_BLIND)="BLIND" (SSR_Strings.mqh:458) -> 40; T(SSR_S_PROP)="PROP" (:459) -> 34. 140+52+4=196, 196+40+4=240, plate 240..274 against the collapse button at x + W - 42 = x+268 (:936, SSR_PANEL_W 310). The collapse Button is created after the chips in DrawCaption, so it paints over the plate's last 6 px. Needs SYNTH specifically: "FULL !" lands the plate at exactly 268 (zero margin) and "BAR !" clears it / Arithmetic is exact and the plate width is computed, not estimated: Chip returns 10+StringLen*6 (SSR_Widgets.mqh:529) and each caller adds 4. 'SYNTH !' (7) -> 52, x+140..x+192, cx=x+196; 'BLIND' (5) -> 40, x+196..x+236, cx=x+240; 'PROP' (4) -> 34, x+240..x+274 against the collapse button at x+W-42 = x+268 with W=310. Confirmed 6 px overlap, and confirmed the non-degraded case clears by only 6 px. Severity stays LOW: the collapse button is created after the chips (:936 vs :887-906) so it paints over the plate's last 6 px, which hides a plate edge and at most a glyph of 'PROP'; the mode itself is still legible and duplicated nowhere else that matters. It also needs InpBlind + InpProp + a degraded fidelity together

#### `ui-panel-8` — Speed 'meaning' text at MAX is 21 unclipped characters drawn into a 56 px reserve at the panel's right edge

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Panel.mqh:1091`** · category: string-length

In the rail branch mw = 52 (:1053) so the anchor is x + 310 - 8 - 52 + 4 = x+254, leaving 56 px to the frame edge; 21 glyphs at SSR_FS_SMALL = 7 pt cannot occupy 56 px under any plausible metric (that would need <=2.7 px/char). No Clip() is applied, unlike "prog" (:960), "pp_rules", "pp_head" and "ses3", and CSSRWidgets::Label never calls Extent() so CheckFrame is blind to it (SSR_Widgets.mqh:125-133, 226-253) / Verified. mw = 52 in the rail branch (:1053), so the anchor is x + 310 - 8 - 52 + 4 = x+254 with 56 px to the frame edge. SSRSpeedMeaning returns the 21-character "as fast as ticks feed" for speed_x100 >= SSR_SPEED_MAX (SSR_Types.mqh:350-353), and SSR_SPEED_MAX is ladder rung 19 (SSR_Types.mqh:298) so it is reachable by "+" and by the last groove cell. Label creation sets ANCHOR_LEFT_UPPER (SSR_Widgets.mqh:239) so the text extends right, and Label never calls Extent (the comment at 117-124 says so) so CheckFrame (:3019-3034) is blind to it. 21 glyphs at SSR_FS_SMALL (7) cannot occupy 56 px under any plausible metric; the exact overrun in pixels is font-dependent, which is why the "30 to 50 px" figure is an estimate, not a measurement / Confirmed: mw=52 in the rail branch (:1053), so the anchor is x+310-8-52+4 = x+254 with 56 px to the frame edge, SSRSpeedMeaning returns the 21-character 'as fast as ticks feed' at speed_x100 >= SSR_SPEED_MAX (SSR_Types.mqh:352-353), rung 19 of the ladder IS SSR_SPEED_MAX (SSR_Types.mqh:298) so it is reachable with '+' or by clicking the last groove cell, no Clip() is applied, and a label is invisible to Extent()/CheckFrame. Downgraded from MEDIUM: the effect is text spilling onto the candles beside the panel at one ladder rung. HideBody solved the identical problem with a loop and documented it at :2088-2090: 'SSR_TAB_MAX, not four written out. This list said tab0..tab3 and Phase 8 added a fifth tab, so closing the panel left a lone "Prop" button sitting on the candles.' HideSheetArea was not updated with it.

So the code defect exists exactly as described. I reduce it to LOW and add a correction because it has no independently observable effect today / Literally true at :2042-2048: the ids array is {"tab0","tab1","tab2","tab3","tabline","lines","sessions","fidelity"} while SSR_TAB_MAX is 5 (SSR_Theme.mqh:544), TabCount() returns SSR_TAB_MAX when prop_on (:1106-1107) and DrawRail draws tab0..tab(TabCount()-1) (:1214-1223). HideBody solved the same drift with a loop and documented it at :2088-2090. So the list is genuinely one entry short / The code fact is provable - HideSheetArea's list (:2044) stops at 'tab3' while SSR_TAB_MAX is 5 and DrawRail draws tab0..tab4 when prop_on - and the finder is honest that this only bites once ui-panel-3 is fixed. Downgraded from MEDIUM to LOW precisely on that: today it produces NO observable effect that ui-panel-3 does not already produce, because HideBody(false) re-shows tab0..tab4 anyway, so a user cannot distinguish a five-tab panel from a four-tab one in compact mode. It is a latent trap for whoever fixes ui-panel-3, not a defect the user can reach as a separate symptom

```mql5
                            "Reset deletes %d closed and %d open - press again",
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct on the defect, wrong on "the only user-facing panel string not routed through T()". There are therefore at least two such strings in this file.

**Why:** m_reset_warning is drawn on the status strip at :1929 `Text(50, "stbal", x + SSR_PAD, y + 4, m_reset_warning, SSR_C_STOP, SSR_FS_SMALL);` - the top rung of the ladder documented at :1956-1972. Every other drawn string on this panel goes through T(SSR_S_*) (e.g. :1037 T(SSR_S_RESET_ASK), which IS translated at fa.txt:23 'reset.ask'). Audit A19's stated rule is that no text may be drawn from a literal; it passes here because the literal is assigned to a member at :2253 and drawn from that member at :1929, not written at the draw site.

**Verifiers:** Confirmed at source: m_reset_warning is built from the English literal "Reset deletes %d closed and %d open - press again" at :2252-2255 and drawn on the status strip at :1929 via Text(50,"stbal",...), the top rung of the documented ladder at :1956-1972. fa.txt has reset ("شروع دوباره") and reset.ask ("مطمئنی؟") at lines 22-23 but no key for this sentence, so a Persian user sees the destructive question in English. And I confirmed why the audit misses it: A19 (tools/ssr_audit.py:1449-1482) only inspects string LITERALS appearing in the text argument at the draw call, so a literal assigned to a member and drawn from that member is invisible to it / The literal is there at :2253-2255 and is drawn at :1929 as the top rung of the status-strip ladder, and the evidence about why A19 misses it is correct: the literal scan only inspects the text argument of draw calls in /Ui/ files (tools/ssr_audit.py:1455-1487), and here a member is passed, not a literal. So an otherwise fully translated panel asks its one destructive question in English / Provable: the literal is assigned at :2253 and drawn at :1929 through the status-strip ladder, while reset.ask is translated (fa.txt:23). Severity stays LOW on impact: the destructive semantics are still conveyed in the user's language - the button itself relabels to T(SSR_S_RESET_ASK) ('مطمئنی؟') and ResetWithConfirm still requires the second press within SSR_CONFIRM_MS regardless of what the strip says - so what a Persian user loses is the count of what would be destroyed, not the confirmation guarantee. No arithmetic, no safety mechanism, and no trade state is affected

The comment at :290-292 argues about confirming a deletion that never occurs. Truncation is real, but the finding's character count is wrong / Confirmed from source. The DELETE button is created enabled at :210-211 (m_w.Button(..., T(SSR_S_SD_DELETE), false, m_selected >= 0); SSR_Strings.mqh:539 maps that to the label "DELETE"), and the handler at :288-297 performs no deletion - it only sets m_message and re-renders. CSSRSessionManager::Delete exists (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:403) and is unreachable from here; CSSRReplayPort (SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:404-405) exposes SessionCount/SessionName and no delete, so the dialog has no route to one. Worth adding: Button's `enabled` argument only selects a text colour (:261-271), so even a greyed DELETE stays clickable / Reachable in normal use and verified end to end. The dialog is opened by the host on SSR_CMD_SESSIONS (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3091), the DELETE button is drawn enabled whenever a row is selected (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:210-211, enabled = m_selected >= 0) with the literal label "DELETE" (SSR_Strings.mqh:539), and the handler at :288-297 only sets m_message and re-renders. #### `ui-dialogs-12` — RequestSave and the whole overwrite-confirm mode are unreachable, and would discard their own outcome message if reached

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:318`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
      bool ok = m_port.SaveSession(name);
      m_message = (ok ? "saved \"" + name + "\""
                      : "could not save: " + m_port.SessionError());
      m_pending = "";
      Open();
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate that RequestSave and the whole confirm-save mode are unreachable and that Open() would destroy the save-outcome message if they were reached - but every runtime consequence (message loss, the two screens drawn over each other) is therefore unobservable today. The one live defect is documentary: the header promises "IT ASKS BEFORE IT OVERWRITES", which the shipped tool does not do. The host's OnDeinit write of CfgSession() is the intended session-resume autosave, not a silent overwrite defect. IMPROVEMENT.

**Why:** grep over MQL5/** finds RequestSave defined at :304 and called nowhere; CSSRReplayPort::SaveSession is called only from :242 and :318, both inside these dead paths. Open() sets `m_message = (m_count == 0 ? "no saved sessions yet" : "");` (:94) after refreshing m_count from the port, so after a successful save the message is always "". Named sessions are actually written by the host: SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2135-2143 calls g_session_mgr.Save(CfgSession(), set) with no existence check. A second consequence of the early `return` at :153: the CONFIRM render never hides the list rows, up/down/load/del or msg, and the PICK render never removes q1/q2/yes/no - so if the mode is ever reached the two screens are drawn on top of each other, with the later-created yes/no buttons floating over the list after the confirm is answered.

**Verifiers:** Every limb checks out. (1) `grep -rn RequestSave MQL5/` returns exactly one hit — the definition at SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:304 — and m_mode is set to SSR_SD_CONFIRM_SAVE only at :313 inside it, so the entire overwrite-confirm mode is unreachable and the header's "IT ASKS BEFORE IT OVERWRITES" (:12-13) is not a shipped behaviour. (2) Named sessions are written by the host with no existence check: SSReplayStandalone.mq5 OnDeinit calls g_session_mgr.Save(CfgSession(), set) unconditionally when CfgSession() != "". (3) Message destruction is real: :318-322 sets m_message then immediately calls Open(), whose :94 assigns `m_message = (m_count == 0 ? "no saved sessions yet" : "")` after refreshing m_count — so both "saved <name>" and "could not save: <err>" are gone before Render() draws the msg label at :214. The yes/no handlers at :243-246 and :250 have the identical defect ("kept the existing one" is likewise erased). (4) The overlap is structural: the CONFIRM branch returns at :153 before drawing rows/up/down/load/del/msg, and the PICK path never hides q1/q2/yes/no, and CSSRWidgets objects persist until RemoveAll — so the two screens would stack if the mode were ever entered. Severity LOW is right: the primary consequence is dead code plus an inaccurate header promise, and the message bug is only observable on paths nothing can reach / Every sub-claim checks out. grep over MQL5/** finds RequestSave only at its definition (SSR_SessionDialog.mqh:304) - no caller - and m_mode is private with SSR_SD_CONFIRM_SAVE set only there, so the whole confirm-overwrite mode and the header's 'IT ASKS BEFORE IT OVERWRITES' promise are unreachable in the shipped tool; named sessions are written unconditionally by the host at SSReplayStandalone.mq5 OnDeinit (g_session_mgr.Save(CfgSession(), set) with no existence check). Open() at :91-96 sets m_message = (m_count == 0 ? "no saved sessions yet" : "") before Render(), so the Open() call at :322 (and at :246/:250) discards the outcome string assigned one line earlier - the save result can never be displayed. And the early `return` at :153 means the CONFIRM render never hides row*/up/down/load/del/msg while the PICK render never hides q1/q2/yes/no, and ObjectCreate-based widgets persist until RemoveAll, so the two screens would overlay each other if the mode were ever reached. Severity LOW because all of it sits in dead code today / The facts hold: RequestSave is defined at :304 and called nowhere, so SSR_SD_CONFIRM_SAVE is unreachable, and with it the yes/no handlers (:240-251) and the overlapping-render consequence of the early return at :153. Open() does clobber the outcome message (:94 sets m_message to "" or "no saved sessions yet") on both the :246 and :322 paths. But the impact lens flattens this: nothing a user can do reaches any of it, so there is no observable behaviour to misjudge - the only live artefact is the header's claim at :12-13 that the dialog asks before overwriting. The finding's framing of the host's save as the counterpart risk is also weaker than stated: SSReplayStandalone.mq5's deinit save writes the user's own configured session name (CfgSession()) on every deinit reason, which is the deliberate resume mechanism the surrounding comment argues for, not an unguarded overwrite of a name the user chose in a dialog. Dead code plus a stale header promise: IMPROVEMENT

#### `ui-dialogs-13` — SetStartText draws the orange-line caption at the START step's coordinates while another step is on screen

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:548`** · category: object-lifecycle

```mql5

     {
      if(t == m_start_text)
```
