```mql5
      m_start_text = t;
      if(m_open && m_start_y > 0)
         m_w.Label("startlbl", m_x + 12, m_start_y, t, SSR_C_HOLD, SSR_FS_SMALL);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: m_start_y (set only in RenderStart, :933) is never invalidated on a step change, so SetStartText (:548) paints the orange-line caption at the START step's y while another step is displayed. On step 2 it lands just below the 288 px frame, on candles; on step 1 the 482 px frame means it lands inside the panel and overprints an evaluation-row label. Cleared by the next Repaint.

**Why:** m_start_y is written only in RenderStart (:933) and never reset by Repaint/Render/step changes. The host calls g_setup_ui.SetStartText(...) on every timer beat while picking, whenever the pick line exists and regardless of the panel's step (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2489-2491). Only Repaint()'s RemoveAll (:658) clears the stray. The overflow instrument cannot see it either: CSSRWidgets::Label never calls Extent() (SSR_Widgets.mqh:226-253) and the setup panel never calls CheckFrame at all.

**Verifiers:** Reachable exactly as described. m_start_y is written only in RenderStart (:933) and never cleared by Repaint (:656-662) or by the step handlers (:1061-1074). SetStartText's only guards are m_open and m_start_y > 0 (:555) - nothing checks m_step - and the early-out at :550 means it draws whenever the caption text changes, i.e. whenever the user drags the pick line. The host calls it on every 200 ms beat during picking, regardless of step (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2489-2491). Geometry checks out: on step 3 with random off, by = m_y+274 and m_start_y = m_y+300, while the MODE frame is h = 34+4*48+62 = 288 (:828), so a label lands 12 px below the frame on the candles and survives until the next Repaint's RemoveAll. The widget cache cannot suppress it either (RemoveAll calls ForgetAll) / Reachable and provable. m_start_y is written only in RenderStart (`m_start_y = by + 26`, :933) and is never reset by Repaint() (:656-662), Render() (:664-673) or any step handler; SetStartText (:541-550) draws SSRS_startlbl at (m_x+12, m_start_y) whenever m_open && m_start_y > 0, with no step test. The host calls it on every 200 ms beat during picking whenever the pick line exists, regardless of step (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2489-2491), and the `t == m_start_text` early-out is defeated precisely by dragging the line, which changes the text. Geometry checks out: on step 3 (non-random) r reaches 10, by = m_y+274, so m_start_y = m_y+300, while RenderMode's frame is h = 34 + 4*48 + 62 = 288 - the label lands 12 px below the MODE panel, on the candles, and only the next Repaint's RemoveAll clears it. The instrument genuinely cannot see it: CSSRWidgets::Label does not call Extent() (it is the one sized-object-less widget) and this file never calls CheckFrame / The mechanism is exactly as stated: m_start_y is written only in RenderStart (:933), is never invalidated by Repaint() or a step change, and SetStartText (:548) draws "startlbl" at that stale y whenever m_open && m_start_y > 0. The host calls it on every 200 ms beat while the pick line exists, regardless of step (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2489-2491), and CSSRWidgets::Label never calls Extent() so the overflow instrument cannot see it. Reachable (step 3 -> Back -> drag the orange line) but the observable is one small amber caption at a wrong y, showing genuinely correct information, cleared by the next button press. Purely cosmetic - no data, no arithmetic, no trading guarantee touched. One detail of the claim is off: on step 2 the stray lands ~12 px below the frame as described, but on step 1 (step3 -> Back -> Back, then drag) the frame is 482 px tall so it lands INSIDE the panel, overprinting the evaluation-row label at the same x+12 gutter, which is the more visible of the two variants. LOW is right

#### `ui-dialogs-15` — MenuClear only sweeps 32 menu items while presets.ini is unbounded, so a longer preset file orphans buttons on the chart

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:611`** · category: object-lifecycle

```mql5
   void              MenuClear(void)
     {
      m_w.Remove("mbg");
      for(int i = 0; i < 32; i++)
         m_w.Remove("m" + IntegerToString(i));
     }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: MenuClear's hardcoded 32-slot sweep leaves m32.. on the chart when presets.ini holds more than 31 readable rows; they are stale, latched-looking buttons that persist until the next step change (Repaint/RemoveAll), and they are inert rather than still applying presets, since Poll only checks menu items for the currently open field's option range.

**Why:** LoadPresets sizes m_presets from `int rows = f.Count("p")` with no cap (:331-371); PresetNames returns all of them (:426-433) and DrawMenu creates one button per option (:639-641). Only Repaint()'s RemoveAll (:658, ObjectsDeleteAll by prefix) can clear the surplus, and Render() calls MenuClear - not RemoveAll - on every paint (:668). DrawMenu's off-screen defence is a single flip-upwards test (:633-635), which cannot help a list taller than the chart.

**Verifiers:** The count mismatch is real: LoadPresets applies no cap (rows = f.Count("p"), :331-371, and SSR_SessionFile caps only at 16384 entries), PresetNames returns all 1+n (:426-433), DrawMenu creates one button per option (:639-641), and MenuClear sweeps a hardcoded m0..m31 (:611-616). Render() calls MenuClear, not RemoveAll (:668), so with more than 31 file presets the surplus buttons persist across paints until a step change or Destroy. The 820 px list and the single flip-up test (:633-635) are also as described. One consequence is mis-stated: an orphan m32+ is NOT still applying a preset, because Poll only inspects menu items while m_menu != "" and only for i < n of the CURRENT field's option list (:1084-1096) - an orphan press is simply ignored, leaving a latched-down ghost button / The bound mismatch is real and provable: LoadPresets sizes from `int rows = f.Count("p")` with no cap (:344-346), PresetNames returns all 1+n names (:426-433), DrawMenu creates one button per option (:639-641), and MenuClear (:611-616) sweeps only mbg and m0..m31, so with more than 32 presets the surplus SSRS_m32.. survive every Render() (which calls MenuClear, not RemoveAll, :668) until a step change happens to call Repaint()/RemoveAll. The 820 px list and the single flip-upwards test (:632-635), which can only move an over-tall list off the top instead of the bottom, are also as described. One part of the claim is wrong, and it is the part that carries the severity / The 32-slot sweep in MenuClear (:611-616) really does not match DrawMenu's unbounded creation (:639-641), so the code smell is real - but the described user-visible defect is not reachable and the described click behaviour is wrong. (a) Reachability: presets.ini ships with 4 rows and each row is one challenge SHAPE (the file's own comment says so, :356-363); reaching 32+ requires a hand-edited file of over thirty presets, which is not normal use of this product. (b) The orphans are transient, not persistent: every 'next', 'back', mode press and quick-step press calls Repaint() -> m_w.RemoveAll() (:658), which is ObjectsDeleteAll by prefix and takes m32+ with it, and Destroy() does the same - so nothing survives the panel or even the next step change. (c) The claim that each orphan 'still applies a preset when pressed' is false: Poll only inspects m0..m(n-1) inside `if(m_menu != "")` (:1083-1096), and MenuClear runs precisely when the list has been closed (m_menu == ""), so a click on an orphan is inert - it merely latches the button. The residual worth fixing is the magic 32 itself (sweep ArraySize(m_presets) slots, or cap the list and page it), not a live defect a user can hit

#### `ui-dialogs-10` — The quick step builds the session path itself and skips the session manager's sanitiser, so 'Continue' disappears for names containing a slash or colon

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:712`** · category: input-validation

```mql5
      bool have_last    = FileIsExist(SSR_SETUP_FILE);
      bool have_session = (m_v.session_name != "" &&
                           FileIsExist("SSReplay\\sessions\\" +
                                       m_v.session_name + ".ssr"));
```

**What goes wrong:** Name a session "eu/us open" or "NY:2024-03-05". CSSRSessionManager stores it as sessions\eu_us open.ssr (it replaces \ / : with _), but the quick step tests for sessions\eu/us open.ssr, which cannot exist - so the "Continue ..." button is never offered and the user concludes the session was not saved. The same unsanitised name is what the panel writes to setup.ini and hands to the host.

**Why:** CSSRSessionManager::Path sanitises before use: `StringReplace(safe, "\\", "_"); StringReplace(safe, "/", "_"); StringReplace(safe, ":", "_");` (MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Session/SSR_SessionManager.mqh:92-97), and both Save (:167) and Exists (:117) go through it. SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:713-715 is the only place in the subsystem that concatenates a session path by hand, and Str("ses", ...) (:1175) applies no character validation or length limit of any kind.

**Verifiers:** Verified both sides. SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:713-715 concatenates "SSReplay\\sessions\\" + m_v.session_name + ".ssr" by hand, while CSSRSessionManager::Path (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:92-99) replaces \\, / and : with _ before building SSR_SESSION_DIR + "\\" + safe + SSR_SESSION_EXT (:43-44), and both Save (:167) and Exists (:117) go through Path. So a name containing / \\ or : is saved under a different filename than the quick step probes, have_session is false and the "Continue ..." button (:744) is never offered. Str("ses") (:1175) applies no character filter or length limit, so such a name is accepted. The host's own resume check uses the manager (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2015-2016), which is what makes the panel the odd one out / Verified on both sides. CSSRSessionManager::Path sanitises the name before any file call - StringReplace of \\, / and : with _ (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:92-98) - and Exists (:116-117) and Save (:167) both go through it, with SSR_SESSION_DIR = "SSReplay\\sessions" and SSR_SESSION_EXT = ".ssr" (:43-44). RenderQuick (:712-715) is the one place that concatenates that path by hand, from the raw m_v.session_name, so for a name containing / or : the FileIsExist test addresses a path the manager never writes (with / MQL5 treats it as a directory separator, so it looks inside a folder that does not exist) and have_session is false - the Continue button is never offered even though the .ssr file is on disk under the sanitised name. Str("ses", ...) (:1175) applies no character filter or length limit, so the unsanitised name is also what Save() writes to setup.ini (:1226). Cosmetic-severity: no data is lost, the resume path through the Session dialog still works / Provable inconsistency: RenderQuick concatenates "SSReplay\\sessions\\" + m_v.session_name + ".ssr" by hand (:712-715) while every real access goes through CSSRSessionManager::Path, which replaces backslash, forward slash and colon with underscore (SSR_SessionManager.mqh:92-98, used by Save :167 and Exists :117). A name containing / \ or : therefore tests a path that can never exist. Impact is narrow, though: it requires the user to type a separator character into 'Save as', and the only consequence is that the quick step omits the optional "Continue ..." shortcut. The session itself still saves under the sanitised name and InpResume + Exists still resumes it correctly, because both of those go through the sanitiser - so no data is lost and no state is corrupted; one convenience button is missing and a user could misread that as 'not saved'. Cosmetic-to-minor -> LOW confirmed

#### `ui-dialogs-6` — The setup panel's drag handler can never fire: nothing enables CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE on the chart it lives on

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:955`** · category: event-handling

```mql5
   bool              OnChartEvent(const int id, const long lparam,
                                  const double dparam, const string sparam)
     {
      if(!m_open || id != CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE)
         return false;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Dead code rather than a malfunction: the caption-drag handler and SavePlace/m_placed path are unreachable because no code enables CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE on the host chart while the setup panel exists, so the panel cannot be moved and always re-centres - the picking workflow itself still works because the vertical pick line is grabbable outside the panel's footprint.

**Why:** The only writer of that property in the whole tree is CSSRPanel::Create - `ChartSetInteger(m_chart, CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE, true)` (SSR_Panel.mqh:324) - and CSSRPanel::Destroy puts the saved value back (:370), so nothing leaks across runs. The host creates the setup panel during the picking phase at SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2069 and only calls g_panel.Create() later, inside BuildSession (:1467), after the setup panel has already been destroyed (:2522). grep over MQL5/** for CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE returns exactly the four SSR_Panel.mqh lines. The property's platform default is off, and MT5 exposes no user setting for it. Secondary: the drag exit also writes CHART_MOUSE_SCROLL = true unconditionally (:970) instead of restoring what the chart had, unlike SSR_Panel.mqh:371.

**Verifiers:** The unreachability is provable from source. OnChartEvent returns immediately for anything but CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE (:958), and a grep over MQL5/** shows the only writer of CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE is CSSRPanel::Create (SSR_Panel.mqh:324), with CSSRPanel::Destroy restoring the saved value (:370) - so nothing leaks. The setup panel is created on ChartID() during the picking phase (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2069, inside OnInit before BuildSession) and destroyed on 'go' (:2522), while g_panel.Create runs later at :1467; the two lifetimes do not overlap, so the property is off (platform default) for the whole life of the setup panel and no CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE is delivered. Consequently SavePlace() (:1006-1011) never runs, SSR_SETUP_X/Y never exist, m_placed stays false and Create re-centres every run (:531-541). The secondary point is also correct but moot: :970 would set CHART_MOUSE_SCROLL true unconditionally, in code that cannot execute. Severity downgraded from MEDIUM: the pick line is a full-height OBJ_VLINE that can be grabbed above or below the panel, so this is a dead feature and a lost preference, not a blocked workflow / The MQL5 semantics are right and the grep is exhaustive. CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE is delivered only to programs on a chart whose CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE property is true, and that property defaults to false and has no terminal UI. The only writers in the tree are CSSRPanel::Create (SSR_Panel.mqh:324) and its restore in Destroy (:370) - grep over MQL5/** returns those lines and nothing else. The setup panel is created on the host chart during picking (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2069, ChartID(), so OnChartEvent does reach it at :3117), but g_panel.Create() runs only inside BuildSession (:1467) after the setup panel has been destroyed (:2522), and a prior run's Destroy put the saved value back, so nothing leaks the flag forward. OnChartEvent (:955-998) therefore returns at its first line on every event the chart does deliver: the drag, the CHART_MOUSE_SCROLL suspend/restore pair and SavePlace() are all unreachable, SSR_SETUP_X/Y are never written, LoadPlace never finds them, m_placed stays false and the panel re-centres every run. The secondary point is right too - the exit writes CHART_MOUSE_SCROLL = true unconditionally (:970) rather than restoring, unlike SSR_Panel.mqh:371 / The unreachability is provable from source. The setup panel is created on the EA's own chart in OnInit (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:2069, ChartID()) and events are forwarded to it (:3117), so the handler is wired - but a grep of MQL5/** shows CHART_EVENT_MOUSE_MOVE is written in exactly one place, CSSRPanel::Create (SSR_Panel.mqh:324), which runs only later inside BuildSession (:1467) after the setup panel has been destroyed (:2522), and CSSRPanel::Destroy restores the saved value (:370) so nothing leaks into a later run. MT5 emits CHARTEVENT_MOUSE_MOVE only when that property is on and its default is off, so every branch of CSSRSetupPanel::OnChartEvent (:955-996) is dead on a fresh chart. Where I part company with the finder is severity. What the user loses is one convenience - the setup window cannot be dragged - and the fallback is benign: Create() already centres the panel on the tallest step (:531-544), m_placed simply stays false, and the two GlobalVariables are never written, which is not a corruption of anything. Every control is a click, so nothing is out of reach; the orange line can still be dragged wherever the panel is not, or fetched with the 'bring line here' button. No money arithmetic, no future-data leak, no weakening of the virtual-trading guarantee. The secondary point (CHART_MOUSE_SCROLL forced true at :970 instead of restored) sits inside the same dead code and has no effect at all. A documented-but-absent feature plus a small amount of dead code -> LOW, not MEDIUM

#### `ui-dialogs-9` — ReadAll clamps balance, risk, spread and speed but not the three prop numbers, so a typed 0 silently deletes a prop rule

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_SetupPanel.mqh:1194`** · category: prop-rules

```mql5
      if(m_v.balance      <= 0.0)   m_v.balance      = 10000.0;
      if(m_v.risk_percent <= 0.0)   m_v.risk_percent = 0.5;
      if(m_v.risk_percent >  100.0) m_v.risk_percent = 100.0;
      if(m_v.spread_points < 0.0)   m_v.spread_points = 0.0;
      if(m_v.speed        <= 0.0)   m_v.speed        = 1.0;
      if(m_v.speed        > 10000.0)m_v.speed        = 10000.0;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: ReadAll validates balance/risk/spread/speed but not the three prop numbers, so a 0 or negative typed into a prop box passes through and the corresponding > 0.0-gated rule is dropped - notably prop_on with profit target <= 0, an unpassable challenge that the panel's own presets.ini loader rejects (:342). The numbers are not hidden (step-3 recap :886-890 and the host's prop.ToString() log), and 0 meaning "no limit" matches the max_days convention, so the defect is the missing keyboard-side validation, not a silent deletion.

**Why:** ReadAll assigns prop_target/prop_daily/prop_total from Num() at :1176-1178 and the clamp block at :1191-1199 never mentions them. Downstream: SSR_PropEvaluation.mqh:391 `if(m_rules.max_daily_loss_pct > 0.0 && eq <= DailyFloor())`, :401 the same for max_total_loss_pct, :429 the same for profit_target_pct, :207 `if(!m_started || m_rules.profit_target_pct <= 0.0) return 0`. The values reach the rules via the host's CfgPropTgt/Dly/Tot accessors (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:239-241).

**Verifiers:** The asymmetry is real and provable. ReadAll assigns prop_target/prop_daily/prop_total from Num() (:1176-1178) and the clamp block (:1191-1199) never mentions them; Num() returns 0.0 for a typed "0" because the unreadable-guard only rejects a zero whose first character is not '0' (:295). The host copies them straight into the rules with no validation (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1189-1191), and every rule is gated on > 0.0 (SSR_PropEvaluation.mqh:391, :401, :429, and TargetProgress at :207), so a typed 0 or negative disables that rule and a zero target makes the challenge unpassable while the prop chips still show. The panel's own preset loader refuses exactly that shape (:342). Two claim details are wrong: the values ARE echoed - step 3 recaps "Evaluation %.1f / %.1f / %.1f %%" (:886-890) and the host prints prop.ToString() when the evaluation is on (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1201) - and 0 = "no limit" is this codebase's own convention (max_days > 0 at SSR_PropEvaluation.mqh:419). What survives is the unvalidated prop_on + target <= 0 combination / The mechanics are confirmed: ReadAll assigns prop_target/prop_daily/prop_total from Num() (:1176-1178) and the clamp block (:1191-1199) names only balance, risk, spread and speed; Num() returns 0.0 for a typed "0" (the :295 guard exempts a leading '0') and passes a negative straight through; the values reach the rules via CfgPropTgt/Dly/Tot (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:239-241) and SSR_PropEvaluation.mqh guards each rule with > 0.0 (:391, :401, :429, :207). But the claim over-reads the guards. `max_daily_loss_pct > 0.0` is the deliberate 'this rule is not configured' convention - a challenge with no daily loss limit is a real firm's terms, and LoadPresets accepts exactly that shape (it rejects only `on != 0 && tg <= 0.0`, :342). So a zero daily-loss or drawdown figure is a configuration, not a dropped rule / Code-provable: prop_target/prop_daily/prop_total are assigned from Num() at :1176-1178 and the clamp block at :1191-1199 never mentions them, so 0 and negatives pass through (Num's unreadable-guard lets "0" through explicitly because the first character is '0', :295). Downstream every rule is gated on `> 0.0` (SSR_PropEvaluation.mqh:391, :401, :429, :207), so a zero silently removes the rule, and prop_on + target 0 is a challenge that can never be passed. The panel's own preset loader rejects exactly that shape (:342), so the inconsistency between the file path and the keyboard path is real. Severity stays LOW, and one claim needs correcting: it is not true that 'nothing anywhere says a rule was dropped' - step 3's recap prints the three numbers verbatim, "Evaluation 0.0 / 5.0 / 10.0" (:886-891), on the screen carrying the START button. Nothing here touches money arithmetic or the virtual-trading guarantee; the worst case is a training rule the user believes is armed and is not, on values they can see

#### `ui-plumbing-14` — H, B and X are printed as clickable row captions on the Positions sheet while the same three letters are global hotkeys for unrelated commands

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Strings.mqh:393`** · category: key-table

```mql5
   SSRAddString(out, n, SSR_S_ROW_HINT,        "row.hint",
                "H halves   B stop to entry   X closes");
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Same defect, one overstatement: X -> SSR_CMD_LINES_FLIP flips the PLANNING stop/target lines (long <-> short) and touches no open position or order, so it changes the pending setup rather than 'trade state'. The collision and the missing 'these are buttons' cue are as described.

**Why:** SSR_Keys.mqh:216-218 binds H to SSR_CMD_KEYS, :179-181 binds B to SSR_CMD_BOOKMARK, :168-170 binds X to SSR_CMD_LINES_FLIP. Every other key letter the panel prints (F, L, B, J, S, D, X in SSR_S_FOLLOW, SSR_S_LINES_ON/OFF, SSR_S_BOOKMARK, SSR_S_JUMP, SSR_S_SESSIONS, SSR_S_FIDELITY, SSR_S_FLIP) IS the global hotkey for the control it is printed on, which is what teaches the user that a letter on this panel means a key - so this one line reuses an established convention for the opposite meaning.

**Verifiers:** All three legs verified. SSR_Strings.mqh:392-393 defines SSR_S_ROW_HINT = "H halves   B stop to entry   X closes"; SSR_Panel.mqh:1620-1629 draws it as the 'poshint' text under the rows (shared with TradeError()); SSR_Panel.mqh:1607-1613 creates the per-row buttons "ph"/"pb"/"px" captioned "H", "B", "X" - nothing in the hint says these are click targets. The letters are simultaneously live global hotkeys: SSRKeyBindings binds SSR_VK_H -> SSR_CMD_KEYS, SSR_VK_B -> SSR_CMD_BOOKMARK, SSR_VK_X -> SSR_CMD_LINES_FLIP (SSR_Keys.mqh, the TRADING and NAVIGATION blocks). Keys do reach these commands: the panel lives on g_panel_chart = ChartID() (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1463), the EA's own chart, and RouteEvent dispatches CHARTEVENT_KEYDOWN through SSRKeyToCommand (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3202-3232). The convention claim also checks out - every other letter the panel prints (keys.1/keys.3, SSR_S_FLIP "Flip  X") matches its binding. Only one wording overreach, corrected below / Both halves verified. SSR_Strings.mqh:392-394 defines SSR_S_ROW_HINT as "H halves   B stop to entry   X closes" and SSR_Panel.mqh:1620-1629 draws it as 'poshint' directly beneath the per-row buttons created at SSR_Panel.mqh:1608-1613 with captions "H", "B" and "X". SSR_Keys.mqh binds the same three letters globally to unrelated commands: H -> SSR_CMD_KEYS (:216-218), B -> SSR_CMD_BOOKMARK (:179-181), X -> SSR_CMD_LINES_FLIP (:168-170), and keys are dispatched on the EA's own chart (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3217-3241), which is also g_panel_chart (:1463-1467), so in the one-window case the keypresses do fire. The evidence's convention claim also checks out (SSR_Strings.mqh:330-337: "Follow  F", "Lines on  L", "Bookmark  B", "Jump...  J", "Sessions...  S", "Fidelity  D", and SSR_S_FLIP "Flip  X") / This is a caption-wording preference, and the stated harm does not survive checking. The line is drawn at x+8 (SSR_Panel.mqh:1626-1631) directly beneath rows whose H/B/X buttons are created at x+w-68/-47/-26 (:1608-1613), on the same sheet and with the same three letters - i.e. it reads as a legend for the controls it sits under, which is what it is. The three keystrokes a misreader would try are all harmless and immediately visible: H opens the key card (SSR_Keys.mqh:216-218 -> SSR_Panel.mqh:2313-2315), which is generated from the binding table and therefore CORRECTS the misreading on the spot; B bookmarks (:2298-2299); X calls m_port.FlipLines() (:2304-2305). FlipLines mirrors the planning lines only - it touches no position and no order, so the claim that one of them 'changes trade state' is wrong; nothing is committed and a second X restores it. Additionally keys reach only the chart the EA is attached to (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1457-1463), so on the replay-chart layout the keystrokes do nothing at all. No reachable state in which the user loses anything

#### `ui-plumbing-2` — The panel's on-screen keyboard hint says "R reset"; R is bound to the SL/TP lines and reset is bound to 0

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Strings.mqh:500`** · category: key-table

```mql5
   SSRAddString(out, n, SSR_S_KEYS_2, "keys.2",
                "+ - speed    R reset    J jump    B bookmark");
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate, with the impact stated more precisely: the wrong hint fails safe (R draws lines, it does not reset), and the authoritative list is still correct because SSR_KeyCard is generated from SSRKeyBindings(); the defect is that the panel's hand-written hint line contradicts it and fa.txt has translated the error.

**Why:** SSR_Keys.mqh's own header (lines 79-88) states the reason the binding table exists: "Two lists drift - always, and silently, and the one that drifts is the one the user is reading." The key CARD is generated from the table, but these three hint lines are hand-written strings in the catalogue and nothing cross-checks them against SSRKeyBindings(). SSR_KeyCard.mqh's header even names this exact case: "R moved from reset to the stop-and-target lines in this very build; a second list would already be wrong."

**Verifiers:** Straight contradiction between two lists, both read. SSR_Strings.mqh:499-500 defines SSR_S_KEYS_2 = "+ - speed    R reset    J jump    B bookmark", drawn as the 'keyhint' label on the Session tab (SSR_Panel.mqh:1899-1901, inside the SSR_S_GRP_KEYBOARD group). The binding table says otherwise: SSRKeyBindings adds SSR_VK_R -> SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE ("stop and target lines on / off") in the TRADING block, and SSR_VK_0 -> SSR_CMD_RESET ("start the session over (asks first)") under a comment block headed "RESET MOVED OFF R, and off every letter" (SSR_Keys.mqh). SSRKeyToCommand is a pure table lookup, so pressing R genuinely toggles the lines and 0 is the only reset key - and "0" appears in none of the four SSR_S_KEYS_* strings, which are the panel's only keyboard guide. keys.3 even prints "L lines" for the same command, so the panel contradicts itself. The Persian catalogue faithfully reproduces the error: MQL5/Files/SSReplay/lang/fa.txt:216 reads `keys.2 = + - سرعت    R شروع دوباره    J پرش    B نشانه` ("R start over"). Nothing cross-checks these hand-written strings against SSRKeyBindings() / The mismatch is provable. SSR_Strings.mqh:499-500 sets SSR_S_KEYS_2 to "+ - speed    R reset    J jump    B bookmark" and SSR_Panel.mqh:1902-1904 draws it as the 'keyhint' label on the Session tab. SSR_Keys.mqh:162-164 binds SSR_VK_R to SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE ('stop and target lines on / off') and SSR_Keys.mqh:200-202 binds SSR_CMD_RESET to SSR_VK_0, under a comment block ('RESET MOVED OFF R, and off every letter') that documents the move the hint line was never updated for. Nothing cross-checks these hand-written strings against SSRKeyBindings(). Downgraded to LOW rather than MEDIUM because the consequence is non-destructive in the safe direction - pressing R places the SL/TP lines instead of destroying the session - and the generated key card (H) still lists 0 correctly, so the correct information is reachable in the product / The drift is provable in both directions: SSR_Keys.mqh:162-164 binds SSR_VK_R to SSR_CMD_LINES_TOGGLE and :200-202 binds SSR_CMD_RESET to '0', while SSR_Strings.mqh:499-501 states 'R reset' and SSR_Panel.mqh:1902-1904 draws that string as the 'keyhint' label on the Session tab. Nothing cross-checks the four hand-written SSR_S_KEYS_* lines against SSRKeyBindings(), and fa.txt repeats the error. Downgraded on impact rather than refuted: pressing R runs ArmLines/ClearLines - non-destructive, instantly visible, and undone by pressing R again - and the user who wanted reset has both the panel's own Reset button (SSR_Panel.mqh:1037, which asks for confirmation via ResetWithConfirm at :2292) and the key card, which is generated from the table and therefore names '0  start the session over (asks first)' correctly. So the false statement misleads for one keystroke; it destroys nothing and blocks nothing

#### `ui-plumbing-6` — The property cache has no tombstones: Forget() can silently clear nothing and Keep() then writes a duplicate, leaking slots until the cache stops working

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Widgets.mqh:103`** · category: memory

```mql5
   void              Forget(const string name)
     {
      int k = Slot(name);
      if(k >= 0 && m_ck[k] == name)
        { m_ck[k] = ""; m_cn[k] = 0; m_ct[k] = ""; }
     }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Forget() can resolve to a free earlier slot in the probe chain and clear nothing, after which Keep() stores a duplicate and the off-home entry is orphaned for the life of the cache. Two parts of the finding are wrong, though. (1) The leak is self-limiting, not one slot per Hide at 10 Hz: once the orphan exists it occupies its slot and so removes the free-slot precondition for that chain, and the next Forget/Keep pair re-converges on a stable placement - steady state is a small constant number of orphans per colliding chain, bounded by chain reconfigurations, so the '8-slot probe run fills and the cache switches off' end-state is not established by this code. (2) Contrary to the finding's own evidence, the shadowed duplicate is not always harmless: if another name later occupies the earlier slot, Slot() resolves the name onto the ORPHAN tuple, and Same() then compares the request against a state the object no longer holds - a matching comparison skips the write and leaves the wrong colour/text. That path is neutralised for every Remove()-invalidated name (the object is deleted, so Same()'s ObjectFind check fails) and is only live for Hide()-invalidated names, which are never deleted.

**Why:** Slot() stops at the first slot that is either free or matching, so after any deletion earlier in a probe chain the lookup for a later name resolves to the wrong (free) slot. Forget() guards on m_ck[k] == name and therefore becomes a no-op instead of finding the real entry; Keep() then stores a second copy at the earlier slot. Nothing ever reclaims the old copy: ForgetAll() runs only from Attach() (when chart or prefix changes) and RemoveAll(). The failure is not a wrong pixel - the shadowed duplicate is only reachable once a third name occupies the earlier slot, and by then the object's real state matches the tuple that is found - but the slot is permanently consumed.

**Verifiers:** The mechanism is exactly as coded. Slot() (SSR_Widgets.mqh:58-77) stops at the first free-OR-matching slot, Forget() (101-107) then guards on m_ck[k]==name and silently no-ops when the probe lands on a freed earlier slot, and Keep() (91-99) writes a second copy there; nothing but ForgetAll() (Attach/RemoveAll) ever reclaims the orphan. Home-slot collisions are certain at this load: hashing the panel's real names (SSRP_ prefix, ~264 of them in 512 slots) with the file's own FNV-1a gives 53 colliding home slots (e.g. SSRP_lines/SSRP_pn3, SSRP_riskval/SSRP_spdseg0, SSRP_hintrow/SSRP_palette), and the churn that supplies the freed earlier slot is real - HideSheets() Removes ~100 names and 6x SSR_POS_MAX row names at the top of every repaint, and SSR_Panel.mqh:1937-1940 Hides four status names unconditionally per frame. I could not refute the duplicate-slot leak / The mechanism is fully settled from source. Slot() (SSR_Widgets.mqh:57-76) returns the FIRST free-or-matching slot in an 8-probe run, so once an earlier slot in a probe chain is cleared, a displaced name resolves to it; Forget() (103-108) then guards on m_ck[k]==name and no-ops, and Keep() (86-94) writes a second live copy at the earlier slot, orphaning the later one forever (only ForgetAll(), i.e. Attach-with-changed-chart/prefix or RemoveAll(), reclaims it). Displacement is not hypothetical: I computed FNV-1a exactly as Slot() does over the 107 literal widget ids found in SSR_Panel.mqh under the SSRP_ prefix and 12 of them already land on an occupied home slot - and the real name set is considerably larger (composite widgets add _bg/_fill/_fr/_lb/_lg/_tk/_th/_ac/_lim children, plus 20 spdseg cells, 5 tabs and the list rows). The churn that opens the window is also per-frame and unconditional: Hide() calls Forget() whether or not the object exists (SSR_Widgets.mqh:634-642), and SSR_Panel.mqh:1931-1940 hides stflt/stopen/stspread/stfid on every render while 2085-2099 hides the whole id list, 5 tabs, 20 speed cells and the groove/thumb on every collapse. Two things in the write-up are imprecise rather than wrong, hence the corrected claim / The logic defect is provable: Slot() (Widgets:70-76) returns the FIRST free-or-matching slot in the 8-probe run, so a hole earlier in a chain shadows a real entry further down; Forget() (Widgets:103-108) then guards on m_ck[k]==name and clears nothing, and the next Keep() writes a second copy at the earlier slot, permanently consuming the shadowed one. Nothing reclaims it (ForgetAll only runs from Attach on a chart/prefix change and from RemoveAll). Reachability is in fact far higher than the finder argues: SSR_Panel.mqh:1284-1340 HideSheets() calls m_w.Remove() on ~80 ids plus 6*SSR_POS_MAX row ids at the top of EVERY repaint, so holes are punched and Forget() is called hundreds of times per second. But the impact is bounded and non-visual: the only certain consequence is attrition of a performance cache - and the objects that churn hardest (all sheet labels) are deleted and recreated every frame anyway, so they never benefited from the cache. The claimed end state (an 8-slot run fully saturated, Slot() returning -1, the 561-write/39.05 ms repaint returning) is a projection, not something the source establishes. No money arithmetic, no future data, no virtual-trading guarantee is touched, so MEDIUM is one step too high

#### `spikes-audits-30` — The UIJitter probe divides by its window-size input with no validation

**`MQL5/Indicators/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_Probe_UIJitter.mq5:66`** · category: input-validation

```mql5
g_samp[g_head] = jitter_ms;
g_head = (g_head + 1) % InpWindow;
if(g_count < InpWindow) g_count++;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: InpWindow is used unclamped as an array size (:34) and a modulus (:66). With InpWindow=0 the first OnTimer's g_samp[0] write is an array-out-of-range critical error that kills the indicator before the modulus runs; with a negative value ArrayResize fails, the buffer stays zero-length and the same out-of-range write occurs (no division by zero). Misuse-only, LOW; a MathMax clamp like CSSRTickSynthesizer::SetTicksPerBar would close it.

**Why:** OnInit does `ArrayResize(g_samp, InpWindow); ArrayInitialize(g_samp, 0.0);` with no clamp, and EventSetMillisecondTimer(InpPeriodMs) is likewise unvalidated. OnTimer's first statement after the timing arithmetic is the indexed write at line 65 followed by the modulus at line 66. Report() guards only against too few samples (g_count < 5 at line 79), not against a zero-length buffer. Compare CSSRTickSynthesizer::SetTicksPerBar (Core/SSR_TickSynthesizer.mqh:106), where the product clamps the analogous input: `m_ticks_per_bar = (n < 4 ? 4 : n)`.

**Verifiers:** Traced the whole path and found no clamp. OnInit does ArrayResize(g_samp, InpWindow) then ArrayInitialize with no validation (lines 34-35) and starts the timer (line 42); OnTimer's first buffer statement is the indexed write g_samp[g_head] (line 65) followed immediately by % InpWindow (line 66). With InpWindow=0 the array is length 0, so the write is an array-out-of-range critical error on the first timer beat; a negative value makes ArrayResize fail (size stays 0) with the same outcome, and the modulus is a zero-divide. Report()'s only guard is g_count < 5 (line 79), which is downstream of the fault. The comparison to CSSRTickSynthesizer::SetTicksPerBar's clamp is accurate. LOW is right: it needs a deliberate misconfiguration of a dev-only probe whose default is 2000 / Unvalidated input is real: OnInit does ArrayResize(g_samp, InpWindow)/ArrayInitialize with no clamp (:34-35) and OnTimer indexes g_samp[g_head] then takes % InpWindow (:65-66); Report()'s only guard is g_count < 5 (:79). With InpWindow=0 the indexed write into a zero-length dynamic array is an out-of-range critical error, which is established MQL5 semantics, and it stops the program before the modulus is ever evaluated - so the 'divides by zero' ordering is wrong. The negative case is also mis-described: ArrayResize with a negative size fails and leaves size 0, so the same out-of-range write occurs, but % with a negative modulus is well defined and is not a fault. Reaching any of this requires a deliberately invalid input to a spike probe, and whether the terminal reports or silently removes the indicator is runtime behaviour - hence at most POTENTIAL_RISK, LOW / Missing validation is real and provable: SSR_Probe_UIJitter.mq5 OnInit does ArrayResize(g_samp, InpWindow) with no clamp (:33-34 of the file), and OnTimer writes g_samp[g_head] then takes (g_head+1) % InpWindow (:65-66) - with InpWindow=0 the indexed write hits a zero-length array and a negative value gives ArrayResize a negative size; Report() guards only g_count<5. Compare CSSRTickSynthesizer::SetTicksPerBar, which does clamp. But impact under normal use is nil: the default is 2000, this is a developer-only diagnostic indicator that ships to no trader, and the failure requires someone to deliberately type 0 or a negative into a 'Samples kept for percentiles' input. Nothing is corrupted, nothing is leaked, and the failure is loud and immediate (the probe dies on attach) rather than silent. IMPROVEMENT, not LOW

#### `qa-smoke-7` — Stage 7 increments the pass count with no condition tested

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/QA/SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:724`** · category: zero-empty

```mql5
      Ok("manager redraws on demand", "Redraw(force) returned");
```

**What goes wrong:** This is the only unconditional Ok() in the file and it asserts nothing: reaching the line is the whole of its evidence, which is implied by control flow. It adds one to g_pass on every run, so the headline '=== N passed, 0 FAILED ===' overstates what was measured by one, and a reader scanning the report sees a named property ('the manager redraws on demand') that no code checked. CSSRChartManager::Redraw's return value or Snaps() delta is available two lines above and is what the neighbouring checks use.

**Why:** grep for '^\s*Ok(' in this file returns two hits: this line and 2207, and 2207 is guarded by if(cal.Available()). Every other assertion in 5512 lines goes through Check(what, cond, detail). The file's own rule for an unmeasurable property is Note(), which moves neither counter (lines 262-263, used at 1789, 2212, 4153, 4390, 4464, 4609, 4646, 4731, 4980, 5057, 5075).

**Verifiers:** Line 724 is `Ok("manager redraws on demand", "Redraw(force) returned")`, unconditional inside the `if(Check("a chart can be opened..."))` block, and Ok() at line 226 does `g_pass++` with no predicate. grep '^\s*Ok(' returns exactly two hits, 724 and 2207, and 2207 sits inside `if(!loaded) { if(cal.Available()) ... }`. Note() at 262-263 moves neither counter, which is the file's own idiom for an unmeasurable property. The suggested alternative is also available: CSSRChartManager::Redraw is declared `bool Redraw(const bool force = false)` (SSR_ChartManager.mqh:531) and Snaps() is already read two lines above at 719 / Confirmed from source. `grep '^\s*Ok('` returns exactly two hits (724 and 2207); 2207 sits inside `if(cal.Available())` (2206). Line 724 `Ok("manager redraws on demand", "Redraw(force) returned")` takes no condition, so g_pass is incremented for a property nothing evaluated - reaching the line is implied by the control flow that already ran mgr.Redraw(true) four times above. CSSRChartManager::Redraw does return bool (SSR_ChartManager.mqh:531) and mgr.Snaps() (589) is read two lines above, so an assertable value is in hand; Note() (262) is the file's own device for an unmeasurable property / Verified: l.724 is the only unguarded Ok() in the file (the other, l.2207, sits inside `if(cal.Available())`), it tests nothing, and it adds one to g_pass on every run, so the '=== N passed ===' headline overstates measured properties by one. Downgraded from LOW to IMPROVEMENT under the impact lens: the effect is confined to one line of a self-report the author reads, the detail string is honest about what happened ('Redraw(force) returned'), and mgr.Snaps() two lines above was already asserted by the neighbouring check - so no property the user depends on is left unmeasured. This is test hygiene, not a defect with an observable consequence

#### `qa-smoke-5` — The blind-mode restore check cannot see a chart left with no price scale, and goes vacuous entirely when the template has OHLC off

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/QA/SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:1144`** · category: object-lifecycle

```mql5
      Check("blind mode hides what the chart announces",
            !(bool)ChartGetInteger(probe2, CHART_SHOW_OHLC) &&
            !(bool)ChartGetInteger(probe2, CHART_SHOW_PRICE_SCALE),
      blind.RestoreAll();
      Check("and puts the chart back the way it was",
            (bool)ChartGetInteger(probe2, CHART_SHOW_OHLC) == had_ohlc,
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The coverage gap is real; the stated consequence is not. CSSRBlindMode::Restore (SSR_BlindMode.mqh:174-181) writes CHART_SHOW_DATE_SCALE, CHART_SHOW_OHLC and CHART_SHOW_PRICE_SCALE back from m_had_* unconditionally, and RestoreAll drains every saved chart, so no chart is left without a price axis by today's code. probe2 is also a chart the suite itself opened (ChartOpen at 1121) and closes at 1156, never the user's. The accurate finding is narrower: the restore assertion covers one of the two properties Apply changed, and goes vacuous when had_ohlc is false - capturing had_scale beside had_ohlc at 1130 closes it.

**Why:** The restore comparison names only CHART_SHOW_OHLC, while the Apply comparison names CHART_SHOW_OHLC and CHART_SHOW_PRICE_SCALE; the price scale is the property whose pre-state is never captured and never re-compared. Stage 36 repeats the same asymmetry at lines 3700-3702 (back > 0 && !bl.IsApplied() && CHART_SHOW_OHLC == had_ohlc). Capturing had_scale beside had_ohlc at line 1130 and comparing both would close it; the stage already knows how, because that is what it does for OHLC.

**Verifiers:** The asymmetry is exactly as described: the apply check at 1137-1141 names CHART_SHOW_OHLC and CHART_SHOW_PRICE_SCALE, the restore check at 1144-1146 names only CHART_SHOW_OHLC, and only had_ohlc is captured (1130) - no had_scale exists. When the chart's template has the OHLC line off, had_ohlc is false and the restore comparison is false == false, which holds whatever RestoreAll did. Stage 36 repeats it at 3700-3702. So a future regression in the price-scale restore would be reported as PASS / The asymmetry is provable: the Apply assertion (1140-1143) names CHART_SHOW_OHLC and CHART_SHOW_PRICE_SCALE, the restore assertion (1144-1147) names only CHART_SHOW_OHLC, and had_scale is never captured beside had_ohlc at 1130. Stage 36 repeats it (3700-3702). Two corrections: CSSRBlindMode::Restore (SSR_BlindMode.mqh) writes all three remembered properties back (CHART_SHOW_DATE_SCALE, CHART_SHOW_OHLC, CHART_SHOW_PRICE_SCALE) from a snapshot taken once per chart, so the product does not in fact leave a chart without its price axis - this is a QA coverage gap, not the trap the finding implies. And 'the default in several shipped MetaTrader profiles' is an unverifiable claim about terminal defaults; the vacuous `false == false` case is real but only on a terminal whose template has OHLC off / The asymmetry is real and provable: the Apply assertion at l.1143-1146 names CHART_SHOW_OHLC and CHART_SHOW_PRICE_SCALE, the restore assertion at l.1144-1149 names only OHLC, and had_scale is never captured beside had_ohlc at l.1130; stage 36 repeats it at l.3696-3702. But the finding's stated consequence does not follow. CSSRBlindMode::Restore (SSR_BlindMode.mqh) writes CHART_SHOW_DATE_SCALE, CHART_SHOW_OHLC and CHART_SHOW_PRICE_SCALE back from per-chart saved state unconditionally, so there is no reachable state in which a user's chart is left without a price axis - the product keeps the promise the check only half measures. The second half ('vacuous when the template has OHLC off') is correct in form but its trigger is the terminal's default template, which I cannot settle from source. Net: a coverage gap in one assertion, no user-observable defect, so MEDIUM is wrong

#### `qa-smoke-13` — Stage 18's compact assertion checks tab0 and tabline only, and can never reach the fifth tab that the panel's hide list omits

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/QA/SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:1783`** · category: object-lifecycle

```mql5
            Check("and dropped only what is consulted",

```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: CSSRPanel::HideSheetArea (SSR_Panel.mqh:2044) omits tab4 from its hand-written list, so a panel with an evaluation attached that goes compact leaves the Prop tab visible over the candles until the chart grows again. Stage 18's compact assertion (l.1783-1785) cannot see it - the stage's panel has a NULL port, so no fifth tab is ever created - but its `tabline` term is not vacuous: the active rail layout draws tabline in DrawActions.

**Verifiers:** Both halves check out. SSR_Panel.mqh:2042-2050 HideSheetArea hides the written-out list {"tab0","tab1","tab2","tab3","tabline","lines","sessions","fidelity"} - tab4 is absent - while HideBody at 2077-2100 was fixed to `for(int t = 0; t < SSR_TAB_MAX; t++)` with the comment naming exactly this regression. The removal loop that would sweep tab4 lives in DrawRail (1216-1217) / DrawTabs (1165-1167), and Render skips both under `if(!m_compact)` (735), so a panel that was full with an evaluation (tab4 drawn) and then shrinks leaves tab4 visible with the sheet gone. The QA side is as described: the assertion at 1783-1785 names only tab0 and tabline, and the panel at 1663 is `pnl.Create(pchart, NULL, "SSRQ_")` - a NULL port means Render() calls m_state.Init() (line 635), prop_on is false, TabCount() returns SSR_TAB_COUNT (1103-1104), so tab4 is never created in this stage and nothing can be left behind for the missing list entry to expose / Both halves check out. SSR_Panel.mqh:2044 HideSheetArea writes out {"tab0","tab1","tab2","tab3","tabline","lines","sessions","fidelity"} while HideBody twelve lines later loops `for(int t = 0; t < SSR_TAB_MAX; t++)` (2091-2092) with the comment naming exactly this regression; SSR_TAB_MAX is 5 (SSR_Theme.mqh:544) and tab4 exists whenever m_state.prop_on (TabCount(), 1104). In compact mode Render skips the whole tab strip (735), so nothing else removes tab4 - DrawRail's `for(i = n; i < SSR_TAB_MAX; i++) Remove(...)` (1223-1224) is not reached. The QA check at 1783-1785 names only tab0 and tabline, and the stage's panel is built with a NULL port (1663), so prop_on is false, TabCount() is 4 and no fifth tab can ever exist there. The stranding itself needs a runtime resize across the threshold with an evaluation attached, which source cannot demonstrate; the coverage gap it describes is fully provable / The product defect behind the coverage gap is provable and reachable in normal use. HideSheetArea (SSR_Panel.mqh:2044) hides the written-out list tab0..tab3 + tabline; HideBody twelve lines later loops `for(int t = 0; t < SSR_TAB_MAX; t++)` with the comment recording exactly this bug being paid for once. tab4 exists whenever TabCount() is 5 (l.1104, m_state.prop_on), Render() calls HideSheetArea only on the full->compact transition (l.680-682) and then skips the whole strip while compact (l.735), and nothing else sweeps tab buttons (HideSheets, l.1284-1320, does not). So a trader with an evaluation running who opens the Toolbox is left with a lone 'Prop' button floating on the candles with no sheet behind it - the same photograph stage 43 exists because of. The QA half of the claim also holds: the stage's panel is built with a NULL port (l.1663), so prop_on is false, TabCount() is 4, and there is never a tab4 for the missing list entry to leave behind. One nuance against the finding: `tabline` is NOT vacuous under the active rail layout - DrawActions (l.1260) creates it - so that half of the assertion does bite. LOW is right: the consequence is a stray clickable button, cosmetic, with no effect on the books

#### `qa-smoke-4` — Stage 22d reports two PASSes for calendar lines that were never drawn

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/QA/SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:2316`** · category: zero-empty

