**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Stage 22d has no non-empty-calendar guard, so on any terminal or window where cal.Count() == 0 (no calendar published, or a quiet window with Available() true) both of its conditions reduce to 0 == 0 and two PASSes are counted for properties nothing exercised. The printed details do carry the zeros, so a careful reader can see the vacuity; the pass total cannot.

**Why:** 22d sits after the `else` block closes at line 2306, so it runs whether or not cal.Load() succeeded, unlike 22b and 22c. The file holds itself to the opposite standard four lines above: 'a stage that quietly passed because there was nothing to test would be the most misleading line in the report' (2239-2241), and 22c acts on it with a Note. 22d has no such guard, and Note() exists for exactly this.

**Verifiers:** Structurally verified: the `else` opened at 2214 closes at 2306, so 22d (2308-2326) runs unconditionally, unlike 22b/22c. With no calendar, cal.Count() is 0, and CSSRCalendarLines::Draw (SSR_CalendarLines.mqh:71-105) loops `for(i = 0; i < cal.Count(); i++)` and returns made = 0 without creating anything; Clear (108-119) returns ObjectsDeleteAll's count, which is 0 with nothing to delete. So 2318-2320 reduces to `0 == 0 && 0 == 0` and 2321-2324 to `0 == 0 && ObjectFind(...) < 0` - two PASS lines reading '0 created, 0 on the second pass' and '0 object(s) removed' for idempotence and cleanup that were never exercised. The file states the opposite standard four lines above at 2239-2241 and 22c acts on it with a NOTE; 22d has no such gate / Verified. 22d sits outside the `else` that guards 22b/22c, so it runs whether or not cal.Load() succeeded. With cal.Count() == 0, CSSRCalendarLines::Draw returns 0 without creating anything (its loop is `for(i = 0; i < cal.Count(); i++)`) and Clear returns ObjectsDeleteAll's 0, so `second == 0 && first == cal.Count()` is 0 == 0 && 0 == 0 and `cl.Clear() == first && ObjectFind(cc, SSR_CAL_PREFIX + "0") < 0` is 0 == 0 && true - two PASS lines for untested idempotence and cleanup, against the file's own standard at 2239-2241. Downgraded from MEDIUM because the detail strings do print the numbers ('0 created, 0 on the second pass', '0 object(s) removed'), so the report is thin rather than actively lying, and Note() is the one-line fix / Confirmed on both sides of the call. CSSRCalendarLines::Draw loops cal.Count() times and returns 0 without creating anything; Clear() returns ObjectsDeleteAll, which is 0 when nothing carries the prefix. With Count() == 0 the stage prints 'PASS drawing twice draws each line once  0 created, 0 on the second pass' and 'PASS and clearing takes them all back  0 object(s) removed' - idempotence and cleanup both untested - and 22d sits after the else block closes at l.2306, so unlike 22b/22c it runs regardless. Reachable without any broker excuse: the window is InpReplayBars = 400 M1 bars, under seven hours, filtered to SSRNewsFloor(SSR_NEWS_MODERATE), so a quiet session yields zero events even where the terminal has a full calendar. Downgraded from MEDIUM: the harm is two misleading lines and +2 on the pass count in the author's own report; nothing the user runs is affected, and Note() is the file's own answer (used at 22c four lines above)

#### `qa-smoke-9` — Three stages drive real panels outside the panel.ini stash window, so a smoke run still eats the user's saved tab

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/QA/SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:4194`** · category: object-lifecycle

```mql5
         pp.Dispatch("tab4");
         pp.Render();
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate, and one more field is at risk than the finding names: stage 39's two P presses are not both guaranteed to run - 4558 sits inside the `else` taken only when the chart is tall enough (4446 is unconditional), so on a chart shorter than SSR_PANEL_TALL_H + 24 the panel-size wish is toggled an odd number of times and SavePlace persists the user's `pro` preference inverted as well as the tab.

**Why:** The file opens with the rule it is breaking: 'A TEST MUST NOT EAT THE USER'S SETTINGS ... running the smoke test threw away whatever the user had typed into the setup panel, and told them PASS while doing it' (lines 76-84), and stage 19's own comment repeats it ('a QA run that resets the user's own layout has done more harm than the stage is worth', 1808-1809). Moving the Stash to just before stage 17 and the Unstash to after stage 42 - or stashing once around the whole panel-driving range - would cover it.

**Verifiers:** Every link verified. Stash/Unstash windows are 1344/1886 and 4595/4742 (grep-confirmed line numbers). Outside them: 4194 pp.Dispatch("tab4"), 4400 tp.Dispatch("tab1"), 4446 and 4558 tp.OnEvent(CHARTEVENT_KEYDOWN, SSR_VK_P), 5095 mp.Dispatch("tab2"). Dispatch's tab branch (SSR_Panel.mqh:2568-2584) calls SavePlace() whenever m_tab changes; SSR_CMD_PANEL_SIZE (2349-2357) calls SavePlace() too; SavePlace (527-550) only refuses while !m_place_loaded, and Create() -> RestorePlace() (348, 553-555) sets that flag on every one of these panels. So each of those writes the real MQL5\Files\SSReplay\panel.ini, and the run ends with tab=2 from stage 42 rather than the user's tab - against the rule the file opens with at 76-84 and repeats at 1808-1809 / Fully verified. Stash/Unstash of SSR_PANEL_FILE occur only at 1344/1886 and 4595/4742. Outside those windows: 4194 pp.Dispatch("tab4"), 4400 tp.Dispatch("tab1"), 4446 and 4558 tp.OnEvent(CHARTEVENT_KEYDOWN, SSR_VK_P), 5095 mp.Dispatch("tab2"). CSSRPanel::Dispatch writes the file on any tab change (SSR_Panel.mqh:2569-2583 -> SavePlace) and Execute(SSR_CMD_PANEL_SIZE) does the same (2349-2356), reached from OnEvent's KEYDOWN branch (2854-2875). SavePlace is live for every one of these panels because Create calls RestorePlace (348) which sets m_place_loaded = true before its FileIsExist test (555-556), so the `if(!m_place_loaded) return false` guard (531) never blocks. Nothing after 5095 rewrites the file, so a completed run leaves panel.ini at tab=2 - directly against the rule the file opens with (76-84) and repeats at 1808-1809 / Every link verified. The stash windows are exactly l.1344-1886 and l.4595-4742. Outside them, stage 38 dispatches 'tab4' (l.4194), stage 39 dispatches 'tab1' (l.4400) and sends SSR_VK_P twice (l.4446, l.4558), stage 42 dispatches 'tab2' (l.5095). Dispatch's tab branch calls SavePlace() on any change (SSR_Panel.mqh:2578-2582) and SSR_CMD_PANEL_SIZE calls it unconditionally (2349-2353); SavePlace is live because Create() -> RestorePlace() (348) sets m_place_loaded before anything else (522-528). Stage 40's Unstash then restores the .qabak it took, which by then holds stages 38/39's values rather than the user's, and stage 42 writes last - so a completed run leaves MQL5\Files\SSReplay\panel.ini holding the test's tab, corner, x/y, collapsed and pro, and reports PASS throughout, against the rule stated at l.76-84 and repeated at l.1808-1809. The P-key path writes unconditionally, so the loss is effectively certain on any run where stage 39's chart opens, not merely possible. LOW is the right level: what the user loses is one UI preference, recoverable by setting it again - no trade, statement or history is touched

#### `qa-smoke-10` — SSR_Z_Cleanup closes the chart of any broker symbol whose name starts with SSR

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/QA/SSR_Z_Cleanup.mq5:44`** · category: broker-symbol

```mql5
      if(StringFind(s, "SSR") == 0 || StringFind(s, ".SSR") >= 0)
        {
         ChartClose(id);
         closed++;
        }
```

**What goes wrong:** The first test matches any symbol name beginning with the three letters SSR, not just the fifteen spike symbols the script owns. SSRM is a live exchange ticker (SSR Mining Inc., NASDAQ/TSX) and is carried by brokers that offer share CFDs; SSRT, SSRN and similar prefixed names exist too. A user with an SSRM chart open who runs the cleanup after a test session loses that chart with no warning, and the script reports it as 'charts closed=1' among its own.

**Why:** The spike symbols are enumerated exactly in g_syms[] at lines 15-17 and the product's own names are recognised by SSRIsReplaySymbol / the .SSR suffix (SSR_SymbolNaming.mqh:74-77), so both sets are already addressable precisely. The symbol-DELETE loops are correctly narrow - line 71 requires '.SSR' and line 85 walks g_syms[] by name - which makes the chart loop the only place a name outside the script's ownership is acted on. Testing against g_syms[] membership or requiring the '.SSR' suffix would make the two halves agree.

**Verifiers:** Traced lines 37-50. `StringFind(s, "SSR") == 0` matches any chart whose symbol name merely BEGINS with SSR, with no membership test against g_syms[] (lines 15-17) and no requirement for the '.SSR' suffix, and ChartClose(id) is called unconditionally. The two symbol-DELETE loops in the same file are correctly narrow by contrast - line 71 requires '.SSR' and line 85 walks g_syms[] by exact name - and CustomSymbolDelete cannot touch a broker symbol anyway, so the chart loop is the only place the script acts on a name it does not own. Any broker-supplied ticker with an SSR prefix (SSR Mining trades as SSRM) would have its chart closed and counted in 'charts closed=N'. Data loss is limited to a chart window, hence LOW / The pattern at SSR_Z_Cleanup.mq5:44 is confirmed over-broad relative to the rest of the file: the .SSR delete loop (line 71) and the g_syms name loop (line 85) are exact, and only the chart loop uses a bare three-letter prefix match, so a name outside the script's ownership can be acted on. ChartClose is also not undoable. But whether any user is affected depends entirely on broker symbol naming - most brokers decorate share CFDs (SSRM.US, #SSRM, SSRM.cfd), and a decorated name does not satisfy StringFind(s,"SSR")==0 - and no source in the repo settles that. Impact is a closed chart window, not data loss, hence LOW / Provable from source: SSR_Z_Cleanup.mq5:44 tests StringFind(s,"SSR")==0, which matches any broker symbol whose name begins with those three letters, while the two symbol-DELETE loops immediately below are correctly narrow (:71 requires '.SSR', :85 walks the explicit g_syms[] list). SSRM (SSR Mining) is a real listed ticker carried by share-CFD brokers, and broker suffixing (SSRM.US, SSRM.NAS) still matches a prefix test. The state is reachable in normal use because the engine itself instructs users to run this script when a replay symbol will not delete (:23-27), so it is not developer-only. Impact is bounded to what ChartClose does: an unrelated chart vanishes without warning and is counted as one of 'ours' in the closed= tally and the Comment(). The template can be reopened; drawings and per-chart objects on it are lost. Nothing is deleted, no symbol is touched, no data or money arithmetic is affected - LOW is the right level, as the finder already set

#### `qa-smoke-12` — SSR_Z_Gaps can report more than 100% of minutes present, and InpMinRun=0 turns every bar into a hole

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/QA/SSR_Z_Gaps.mq5:52`** · category: boundary

```mql5
   int span = (int)((InpTo - InpFrom) / 60);
   PrintFormat("  %d bars across %d minutes (%.1f%% present)",
               n, span, span > 0 ? 100.0 * n / span : 0.0);
...
      if(gap < InpMinRun)
         continue;
```

**What goes wrong:** CopyRates with a start_time/stop_time pair is inclusive of both ends, so the default range 11:00..12:00 returns 61 bars for a span computed as 60 minutes and the script prints '61 bars across 60 minutes (101.7% present)'. The whole point of this script is to be the arbiter of whether missing candles are the broker's fault, and a coverage figure above 100% undermines the one number the reader is being asked to trust. Separately, InpMinRun is used unclamped: set it to 0 and every consecutive pair of bars has gap == 0 >= 0, so the script reports n-1 'holes', prints twenty lines reading 'HOLE 0 minute(s) missing after ...', and then concludes 'A replay crossing these will show the clock moving with no new candle' about a range with no holes in it at all.

**Why:** span counts intervals while n counts bars over an inclusive range, so n == span + 1 on a complete range - (InpTo - InpFrom)/60 + 1 is the matching denominator. The gap test at line 63 has no lower bound on InpMinRun, and the input's comment ('Report holes of at least this many minutes') does not describe 0 as a value; a MathMax(1, InpMinRun) at the top of OnStart matches the documented intent.

**Verifiers:** Both halves check out in source. (a) span = (InpTo-InpFrom)/60 counts intervals (line 52) while n counts bars returned by CopyRates(sym, PERIOD_M1, InpFrom, InpTo, r) (line 41), whose start/stop form includes both boundary bars - the repo itself relies on that inclusivity elsewhere (SSR_Mt5Providers.mqh:389-394 documents CopyTicksRange as 'INCLUSIVE at both ends' and compensates by +1ms). A complete 11:00..12:00 range therefore yields n = span+1 = 61 and prints 101.7%. (b) gap = intervals-1 at line 62 and the filter is gap < InpMinRun at line 63, so InpMinRun=0 makes every adjacent pair (gap 0) a hole: holes = n-1, twenty 'HOLE 0 minute(s) missing' lines, then the '... and N more holes' tail and the closing 'A replay crossing these...' conclusion, on a range with no missing minute. Nothing clamps InpMinRun anywhere in OnStart. Cosmetic/reporting only, in a diagnostic script - LOW / The InpMinRun half is airtight from source: gap = (dt/60) - 1 is 0 for adjacent bars and the filter is 'if(gap < InpMinRun) continue' (:62-63), so InpMinRun=0 (or negative) passes every one of the n-1 pairs, prints up to twenty 'HOLE 0 minute(s) missing' lines, skips the NO HOLES branch (:76) and ends with the broker-blame conclusion on a range with no holes. The coverage half rests on documented CopyRates(start_time, stop_time) inclusivity of both ends, which makes n = span + 1 on a complete range and prints 101.7% for the default 11:00..12:00; span counts intervals while n counts bars, so (InpTo-InpFrom)/60 + 1 is the matching denominator. Nothing here contradicts established ground truth. LOW, as claimed - a QA script's cosmetic/threshold nit, not product behaviour / Both halves check out against SSR_Z_Gaps.mq5. (a) span = (InpTo-InpFrom)/60 counts intervals (:52) while CopyRates with a start/stop pair is inclusive of both endpoints, so a complete default range 11:00..12:00 yields 61 bars over span 60 and line 53 prints '101.7% present'. This is the default path - no unusual input needed. (b) The gap filter at :63 is 'if(gap < InpMinRun) continue' with no lower clamp, so InpMinRun=0 admits gap==0, making every consecutive bar pair a 'HOLE 0 minute(s) missing' and driving the script past the 'NO HOLES' branch into the broker-blaming conclusion (:82-84) on a range with no holes. Impact is bounded: this is a QA diagnostic script the author runs by hand, its output is a printed log line, and the hole list itself (times and sizes) is still correct - only the coverage percentage and the InpMinRun=0 degenerate case mislead. No money arithmetic, no user-facing surface. LOW is right

#### `spikes-audits-17` — A1's max_symbol_name_length attributes any CustomSymbolCreate refusal to name length and leaves residue behind on failure

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_A1_SymbolLifecycle.mq5:164`** · category: zero-empty

```mql5
for(int len = 4; len <= 64; len++)
  {
   string probe = "";
   for(int i = 0; i < len; i++)
      probe += "A";
   if(CustomSymbolCreate(probe, "SSReplay\\Spike\\len"))
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The length probe at A1:164-177 breaks on the first CustomSymbolCreate failure without reading GetLastError (despite the ResetLastError at :169), so any refusal reason is recorded as a name-length ceiling and the name_len_usable verdict can FAIL with no diagnosis. Residue is a lesser concern than claimed: the probes are unselected and chart-free so the unchecked delete at :173 normally succeeds, and SSR_Z_Cleanup.mq5:95-100 sweeps them.

**Why:** Lines 169-176: ResetLastError() is called but GetLastError() is never read, and the else branch is a bare break. CustomSymbolDelete(probe) at line 173 is unchecked, so a failed delete leaves AAAA-style symbols under SSReplay\Spike\len that poison every later run of A1. SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:236-246 shows the author already knows deletes fail and need a retry plus a named log line; this loop has neither.

**Verifiers:** Traced lines 162-181. ResetLastError() at 169 is never paired with a GetLastError() read; the else branch at 175-176 is a bare `break`, so every refusal reason (5304 name already exists, a custom-symbol count ceiling, a locked bases folder) is published at line 178 as max_symbol_name_length and fed to the name_len_usable verdict at 180. CustomSymbolDelete(probe) at 173 is unchecked and has no retry, unlike SSR_DropSymbol (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:225-246) which the same file uses at lines 66 and 215 and which deselects, pauses 200 ms, retries after 800 ms and logs by name. If a probe name survives an interrupted run, the next run breaks at that length and reports a smaller or zero metric with nothing in the results explaining why. Mitigation exists but is outside this script: SSR_Z_Cleanup.mq5:95-100 sweeps the AAAA-style names / Every element is on the page at SSR_A1_SymbolLifecycle.mq5:162-181: ResetLastError() at 169 with no matching GetLastError() anywhere in the loop, a bare `break` for every refusal, CustomSymbolDelete(probe) at 173 with its bool return discarded, and max_len published at 178 as 'drives the <SRC>.SSR<slot> naming convention' with name_len_usable gating on >=16. So any refusal cause - pre-existing name from a prior run whose delete failed, a custom-symbol count ceiling, a write-locked bases folder - is recorded indistinguishably as a name-length limit, and residue from an unchecked delete is a self-poisoning input to later runs (partially mopped up only if someone separately runs SSR_Z_Cleanup.mq5:95-100). Contrast SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:225-246, where the author retries and logs by name. Severity stays LOW: this is a developer probe metric, not product behaviour / The narrow code defect is provable: SSR_A1_SymbolLifecycle.mq5:169 calls ResetLastError() and the result is never read, :175-176 is a bare break, so every refusal reason (5304 already-exists, a custom-symbol count limit, a locked bases folder) is published as max_symbol_name_length and fed to the name_len_usable verdict with nothing recording why. But the impact is close to nil and two of the supporting claims are refutable. The probe symbols are created chart-free and never selected, so CustomSymbolDelete(probe) at :173 is the case MT5 does accept - the 5306 chain the kit documents does not apply here - and SSR_Z_Cleanup.mq5:95-100 already sweeps exactly these AAAA-style names, so 'poisons every later run' overstates it. In the normal case (no leftovers) the loop returns the terminal's true name-length ceiling, which is what the metric claims. This is a developer spike's informational metric with no path to product behaviour, money arithmetic or the isolation guarantee

#### `spikes-audits-18` — B4 turns a missing series into '20,700 days of history' and inverts its Load-More-History conclusion

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_B4_BrokerDataAudit.mq5:41`** · category: zero-empty

```mql5
SSR_Metric(sym, "m1_first_local_days_ago",
           (double)(TimeCurrent() - (datetime)first_local) / 86400.0, "days",
           TimeToString((datetime)first_local, TIME_DATE));
...
SSR_Metric(sym, "m1_downloadable_more",
           ((datetime)first_server < (datetime)first_local ? 1 : 0), "bool",
           "Load More History would gain data");
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct; scope is narrower than the claim implies - the affected output is a Tier-B audit metric read by the author, and the adjacent m1_bars_local = 0 and copyrates_week 'NOT MEASURED' notes (lines 40, 112-114) partially contradict the absurd figure in the same report.

**Why:** Lines 35-38 declare `long first_local = 0, first_server = 0, bars_m1 = 0;` and call SeriesInfoInteger three times discarding each bool result. The subtraction at line 42 is unguarded, so first_local == 0 yields TimeCurrent()/86400. The inversion at lines 47-49 follows directly: 0 < 0 is false and real_server_date < 0 is false, so both the 'nothing local' and 'nothing anywhere' cases report the same 0. B4's whole purpose (header lines 6-9) is to decide whether full-tick fidelity is fiction at this broker, so an inverted or absurd history figure changes a roadmap decision.

**Verifiers:** Traced with no guard anywhere on the path. SSR_B4_BrokerDataAudit.mq5:35 declares `long first_local = 0, first_server = 0, bars_m1 = 0;` and lines 36-38 call SeriesInfoInteger three times discarding every bool return, so a symbol whose M1 series does not exist (or a call that fails) leaves both dates at 0. The only thing upstream is CopyRates(...,10,warm) plus SSR_WaitSeries (lines 31-32), neither of which is checked and neither of which can manufacture history that is not there - the wait is published as a metric and execution falls through regardless. Line 41-43 then publishes (TimeCurrent() - 0)/86400 ~= 20,700 days annotated TimeToString(0) = "1970.01.01", and lines 47-49 publish `first_server < first_local` = `0 < 0` = false under the note "Load More History would gain data" - i.e. 'nothing to download' for the one case where everything is downloadable, and identical output for the 'nothing local' and 'nothing anywhere' cases. The unguarded subtraction and the inverted boolean are both provable from source; only the exact day count varies with TimeCurrent() / Source-provable. SSR_B4_BrokerDataAudit.mq5:35 initialises first_local/first_server/bars_m1 to 0 and :36-38 make three SeriesInfoInteger calls whose bool results are discarded; MQL5 leaves the by-reference out parameter untouched on failure, and SERIES_FIRSTDATE on a symbol with no M1 history is 0 either way. Line 41-43 then computes (TimeCurrent() - (datetime)0)/86400.0 unguarded, publishing ~20,700 'days' with the note TimeToString(0, TIME_DATE) = '1970.01.01'. The inversion at :47-49 follows mechanically: with first_local == 0 and any real first_server, first_server < 0 is false, so 'Load More History would gain data' reports 0 in precisely the case where everything is downloadable, and 'nothing local' is indistinguishable from 'nothing anywhere'. Severity LOW, not MEDIUM: this is a developer-run spike script (#property script_show_inputs, SSR_Metric output), not product code - the damage is a misleading diagnostic line, and the same run also prints m1_bars_local = 0 alongside it / Code reads as described: lines 35-38 initialise first_local/first_server to 0 and discard all three SeriesInfoInteger return values, line 42 subtracts unguarded (TimeCurrent()/86400 ~ 20,700 days in 2026), and lines 47-49 compute 0 < 0 = false. The author already knows the correct guard - SSRDataRange::CanExtendBackwards requires server_first_msc > 0 && first_msc > 0 (SSR_IDataSource.mqh:51-54) - so the omission here is a genuine inconsistency, and it cuts both ways: a symbol whose SERIES_SERVER_FIRSTDATE is not yet known (0) but whose local history is present reports m1_downloadable_more = 1 falsely. Downgraded on impact: this is a developer-facing spike report, not product runtime, and the absurd row is self-flagging - the note column prints TimeToString(0) = '1970.01.01', m1_bars_local = 0 is published immediately above at line 40, and m1_first_server_days_ago shows the same sentinel. A reader deciding the F1 roadmap sees three mutually corroborating signals of 'no series', so the 'inverts a roadmap decision' framing is overstated

#### `spikes-audits-19` — B4's tick-depth walk samples the same hour of the day at every depth, so a symbol closed in that hour reports zero tick history

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_B4_BrokerDataAudit.mq5:72`** · category: boundary

```mql5
for(int d = 1; d <= 2048; d *= 2)
  {
   MqlTick tk[];
   datetime from = now - d * 86400;
   int got = CopyTicksRange(sym, tk, COPY_TICKS_INFO,
                            (long)from * 1000, (long)(from + 3600) * 1000);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The code defect (fixed time-of-day sampling, INFO-vs-ALL mismatch) is exactly as described, but the reported consequence is conditional on the instrument's session actually excluding the audit hour - unverifiable from source - and it is partly mitigated: the zero-depth metric already ships the note "no ticks in any sampled day - check copyticksrange_week_return before concluding" (lines 88-91), so a reader is pointed at the contradicting figure rather than left with a bare zero.

**Why:** `from = now - d * 86400` with d doubling keeps the time-of-day of `now` fixed for every d. The comment at lines 53-68 identifies the right cause ('An empty hour is a closed market, not an absent history') and applies a tolerance rather than varying the sampled hour or scanning a session-aware window. A second inconsistency compounds it: the depth walk uses COPY_TICKS_INFO (bid/ask changes) while the ceiling probe at line 98 uses COPY_TICKS_ALL, so the two metrics the note at line 90 asks the reader to compare are not measuring the same tick population.

**Verifiers:** The sampling defect is provable from source: `datetime from = now - d * 86400` with d = 1,2,4,...,2048 (line 72-77) holds the clock time-of-day of `now` fixed for all twelve probes, and the window is only from..from+3600, so every probe interrogates the same one hour on twelve different days. The tolerance the author added (misses/InpEmptyTolerance, lines 84-86) only defers the break - with every sample empty, misses hits 4 at d=8 and the loop exits with deepest_days still 0, publishing tick_history_depth = 0 days. The author's own comment at 53-68 diagnoses the right cause ("An empty hour is a closed market, not an absent history") yet varies neither the hour nor the day-of-week. The COPY_TICKS_INFO (depth walk, line 76) vs COPY_TICKS_ALL (ceiling probe, line 98) mismatch is also exactly as stated, so the two figures line 90 asks the reader to compare count different tick populations. Downgraded to LOW and see the correction: this is a diagnostic spike script with a mitigating note, not product code / The code shape is exactly as described: line 75 computes from = now - d * 86400 with d doubling 1..2048 (no int overflow - 2048*86400 = 176,947,200), so every one of the twelve probes samples the identical clock hour on a different day, and line 77 fetches only [from, from+3600). With InpEmptyTolerance = 4, four consecutive empty probes break the walk (:84-86) leaving deepest_days = 0, published with 'no ticks in any sampled day' (:88-91). The COPY_TICKS_INFO vs COPY_TICKS_ALL mismatch against the ceiling probe at :98 is also real, so the note at :90 asks the reader to compare two different tick populations. What I cannot settle from source is whether any audited instrument's session actually excludes the run hour on four consecutive probe days - that is broker/session-schedule behaviour, and for a 24/5 FX symbol the weekend probes reset misses rather than accumulate. So a genuine sampling weakness, but the zero-depth outcome is conditional; and it is a diagnostic script whose own metric note tells the reader not to conclude before checking copyticksrange_week_return / The arithmetic is unarguable: from = now - d*86400 with d doubling holds the sampled clock hour fixed for all twelve probes (line 75), and the window is only 3600s (line 77), so an instrument whose session excludes the hour the audit happens to be run in - a cash-equity CFD audited outside its ~6.5h session is the clearest case - yields twelve empty samples, trips InpEmptyTolerance at line 85 and publishes tick_history_depth = 0. The COPY_TICKS_INFO vs COPY_TICKS_ALL mismatch between line 76 and line 98 is also real, so the two metrics the note asks the reader to compare count different tick populations. Downgraded on impact: this is a diagnostic script, the zero case prints its own cross-check instruction ('no ticks in any sampled day - check copyticksrange_week_return before concluding', lines 89-90), and no product behaviour, money arithmetic or guard depends on the number. Whether any given broker/symbol actually has a permanently closed hour at the run time is runtime-dependent, but the mechanism is settled from source

#### `spikes-audits-20` — B4 reports the number of ticks in a week as a call ceiling and a page size

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_B4_BrokerDataAudit.mq5:93`** · category: measurement-validity

```mql5
//--- practical ceiling of one CopyTicksRange call
int cap = 0;
...
   int got = CopyTicksRange(sym, tk, COPY_TICKS_ALL,
                            (long)from * 1000, (long)now * 1000);
   cap = got;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Mislabelled measurement confirmed (density, possibly truncated, reported as an API ceiling), plus a real cost hazard where a failed allocation would publish cap = -1; but the number is consumed only by a human reading a spike report and by no shipped code, so LOW.

**Why:** Lines 95-101 issue exactly one call over a fixed seven-day window and assign its return to cap, with no comparison against a known tick count, no second call over a wider window, and no check of GetLastError(). The variable name and the metric note ('sets the page size for TickSource') both assert a ceiling the code cannot distinguish from data volume. Cost note: this is a COPY_TICKS_ALL fetch of a full week per symbol, up to InpMaxAuto=6 symbols, immediately after the comment at lines 60-64 explains that fetching a single day of ticks per probe 'went from seconds to looking hung'.

**Verifiers:** Source supports the measurement-validity claim. Lines 93-103 issue exactly one CopyTicksRange over a fixed seven-day window (from = now - 7*86400 .. now, COPY_TICKS_ALL), assign the return straight to `cap`, and publish it as "copyticksrange_week_return" with the note "sets the page size for TickSource" under the comment "practical ceiling of one CopyTicksRange call". There is no second call over a wider window, no comparison against a known tick count, no GetLastError() check and no truncation test of any kind, so the code provably cannot distinguish 'the API refused to return more' from 'that is how many ticks the week held' - a quiet symbol yields a small number and a busy one a large number for reasons unrelated to any limit. The -1 error return is also published verbatim as a tick count. The cost observation holds too: a full week of COPY_TICKS_ALL per symbol for up to InpMaxAuto=6 symbols, in a file whose own comment at 60-64 says a single day of ticks per probe made the audit look hung. Severity LOW: a mislabelled metric in a spike/audit script that produces a report for a human, with no code consuming the value / The semantics are as the finding states: CopyTicksRange returns the number of ticks it copied for the requested range (or -1 on error), not a per-call limit. SSR_B4_BrokerDataAudit.mq5:93-103 makes exactly one COPY_TICKS_ALL call over a fixed seven-day window, assigns the return straight to cap with no second call over a wider range, no comparison against a known count, and no GetLastError()/negative check - so a -1 error would be published as a tick figure too. The value is then labelled 'practical ceiling of one CopyTicksRange call' and noted as 'sets the page size for TickSource', neither of which the measurement can support: for any symbol whose week of ticks is below whatever real limit exists, cap is pure tick density. Severity LOW because this is spike tooling, not shipped code, but the conclusion it feeds (TickSource page size) is exactly the kind of decision the mislabel would corrupt / Semantics are as claimed: lines 95-101 make exactly one CopyTicksRange over a fixed seven-day window and assign the return to a variable named cap, with no wider second call, no known-count comparison and no GetLastError check, so the published 'practical ceiling of one CopyTicksRange call' is indistinguishable from the broker's tick density over that week - and the note 'sets the page size for TickSource' therefore makes page size a function of symbol liquidity. The cost objection is also real and sits two comments below the author's own note that a single day of ticks per probe made the audit 'look hung' (lines 60-64): a week of COPY_TICKS_ALL per symbol, up to InpMaxAuto=6, and if the allocation fails got is -1 and cap = -1 is published as a tick count. Downgraded on impact: the metric feeds a human roadmap judgement in a spike, not any shipped code path - grep finds no consumer of this value - so nothing in the product mis-sizes itself as a result

#### `spikes-audits-14` — C3's torn-read test runs the writer and the reader sequentially in one thread, so 'race-free' cannot fail

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_C3_IpcChannel.mq5:82`** · category: event-handling

```mql5
GlobalVariableSet("SSR.c3.cmd.a1", (double)(i * 7));
GlobalVariableSet("SSR.c3.cmd.a2", (double)(i * 13));
GlobalVariableSet("SSR.c3.cmd.a3", (double)(i * 29));
GlobalVariableSet("SSR.c3.cmd.seq", (double)i);   // published last

//--- reader side
double seq = GlobalVariableGet("SSR.c3.cmd.seq");
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate as to mechanism, but mis-categorised and over-rated: this is a measurement-validity/test-coverage defect in a diagnostic spike (category 'event-handling' is wrong), with no effect on shipped code. The consequence is only that the spike's 'race-free' claim in its header is uncorroborated; severity LOW, not HIGH.

**Why:** Lines 79-98 are one for-loop in OnStart: writer block then reader block, no Sleep, no second program, no EventSetTimer, and no indicator or service involved. The only way `a1 != seq*7` at line 95 is if GlobalVariableSet/Get lose data within one thread - which section 1 of the same spike already tests separately. The real product shape (a Service writing while an Indicator panel reads, header lines 6-7) is never instantiated anywhere in the spike suite; C1 and C2 each run alone.

**Verifiers:** Traced SSR_C3_IpcChannel.mq5:76-105 in full. The whole 'race' test is one for-loop inside OnStart: lines 82-85 write a1/a2/a3 then seq, lines 88-94 read them back in the same iteration, same thread, no Sleep, no second program, no EventSetTimer, no indicator/service. seq read at 88 always equals i and last_seq always equals i-1, so the `seq != last_seq` branch is always taken and a1/a2/a3 are always exactly i*7/i*13/i*29 -- torn++ at line 96 is unreachable unless GlobalVariableSet/Get corrupts data within one thread, which section 1 (lines 32-51) already tests independently. So SSR_Verdict("seq_last_protocol_safe", torn == 0) at 104 is a constant PASS and carries no information about the seq-last discipline. I also checked the rest of the suite for the two-program shape the header (lines 6-9) claims to validate: the only other programs are MQL5/Services/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_C1_ServiceApiAccess.mq5 and SSR_C2_ServicePersistence.mq5 and the two indicators in MQL5/Indicators/SSReplay/Spike/, and grep shows no GlobalVariable use in either indicator, so no concurrent writer/reader pair exists anywhere. Nothing refutes the finding / Verified at SSR_C3_IpcChannel.mq5:79-98. The writer block (lines 82-85) and reader block (lines 88-96) are consecutive statements inside one for-loop body of a single script; MQL5 script execution is single-threaded and GlobalVariableSet/Get are synchronous, so a1/a2/a3 are always the values just written for the current i, and `seq != last_seq` is true every iteration with seq == i. `torn` is therefore structurally 0 and `seq_last_protocol_safe` cannot fail. I confirmed nothing else in the tree writes SSR.c3.cmd.* (grep across MQL5/ finds only this file), and the suite has no concurrent pair: MQL5/Services/SSReplay/Spike holds C1 and C2 (each standalone) and MQL5/Indicators/SSReplay/Spike holds only the two probes, neither of which touches GlobalVariable*. So the concurrency claim in the header (lines 6-7) is untested anywhere / Mechanically true. SSR_C3_IpcChannel.mq5:79-98 is one for-loop inside a single OnStart: four GlobalVariableSet calls then three GlobalVariableGet calls, no Sleep, no second program, no timer, no indicator/service. `seq` is read immediately after being written, so `a1==seq*7` etc. hold by construction and `torn` is structurally 0; the verdict seq_last_protocol_safe cannot fail, and would still pass with the seq-last ordering removed. No second program is instantiated anywhere in the spike suite. Impact, however, is confined to the developer-facing evidence base: nothing in the product changes, no user observes anything, and the protocol the spike fails to exercise (SSR_GV_CMD_SEQ/A1/A2/A3, Integration/SSR_Contract.mqh:147-151) is logically sound anyway because GlobalVariableSet is a synchronous terminal call issued in program order, and the publisher/client round trip is separately exercised in T14.6/T14.7. A torn read, if it ever happened, would land on a virtual-only command (SSR_CMD_BUY volume/sl/tp) with no real-money path. HIGH overstates it - the finding is a measurement-validity gap in a research spike, not a defect a user can reach

#### `spikes-audits-15` — C4's timecurrent_is_unsafe verdict is a tautology, and C4 never checks that its ticks were accepted

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_C4_ReplayClock.mq5:120`** · category: timer

```mql5
SSR_Verdict("timecurrent_is_unsafe", (long)tc != sym_t, "differs from replay clock",
            StringFormat("tc=%s sym=%s", TimeToString(tc), TimeToString((datetime)sym_t)),
            "confirms the rule: never use TimeCurrent in replay logic");
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct, with two precisions: 'InpStart is required to be in the past' is only an input comment (line 21), not an enforced guard -- the tautology holds regardless because tc would have to equal the injected second exactly. And a wholesale tick rejection does not produce a false green: bad_sec would be 1000 and the verdict symbol_time_equals_injected (line 91) would FAIL loudly. The real defect from the missing acceptance check is therefore diagnostic ambiguity -- a rejected-tick failure is indistinguishable from a genuine 'SYMBOL_TIME does not follow injected ticks' failure -- not a masked pass.

**Why:** tc is TimeCurrent() at line 99 and sym_t is SYMBOL_TIME after injecting ticks stamped from D'2024.01.08'; the inequality is guaranteed by the input contract. The supporting metric symbols_ahead_of_replay (lines 109-119) counts every Market Watch symbol whose SYMBOL_TIME exceeds the 2024 replay base - which is every symbol quoted since then, closed markets included - yet its note claims 'each one can drag TimeCurrent forward'. Line 70 is a bare `CustomTicksAdd(InpTest, tk);` with no accepted count, while SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:351-364 makes checking acceptance the suite's first rule.

**Verifiers:** Both halves check out in SSR_C4_ReplayClock.mq5. (a) Line 98 reads SYMBOL_TIME of SSRC4, which the loop at 55-81 has just stamped at InpStart (D'2024.01.08') + up to 1000 s; line 99 reads TimeCurrent(), the live server time of the chart symbol. The verdict at 120 is `(long)tc != sym_t`, i.e. 'two clocks read differently at one instant' -- it cannot fail for any realistic input and would pass even if TimeCurrent were perfectly safe, so it does not test the counter-claim stated in the header (lines 11-12) that other Market Watch symbols drag TimeCurrent forward. The supporting metric at 109-119 likewise counts every Market Watch symbol whose SYMBOL_TIME exceeds a 2024 timestamp, which is every symbol quoted since then, closed markets included. (b) Line 70 is a bare `CustomTicksAdd(InpTest, tk);` with the accepted count discarded, while the suite's own rule block at MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:351-372 exists precisely to force acceptance accounting, and the sibling spike SSR_D3_SustainedRun.mq5:111-115 does capture it (`int acc = CustomTicksAdd(...)`). SSR_MakeSymbol (SpikeKit.mqh:303-335) never sets SYMBOL_CHART_MODE to BID, so a non-BID origin clone is a live rejection path / Line numbering checks out (verdict at C4:120, CustomTicksAdd at C4:70). `tc` is TimeCurrent() and `sym_t` is SYMBOL_TIME after injecting ticks stamped from InpStart (default D'2024.01.08', documented 'in the past'), so `(long)tc != sym_t` passes on any terminal whose last quote is not that exact 2024 second — it certifies nothing about whether TimeCurrent is dragged forward by other symbols. The symbols_ahead_of_replay metric (lines 109-119) likewise counts every Market Watch symbol quoted since 2024, including permanently closed ones, under a note claiming each 'can drag TimeCurrent forward'. CustomTicksAdd's int return is indeed discarded at line 70 / Both halves check out. (a) InpStart is D'2024.01.08' (SSR_C4_ReplayClock.mq5:20) and sym_t is SYMBOL_TIME after injecting ticks stamped from that base, so `(long)tc != sym_t` at line 119 is true by the input contract on any connected terminal; it demonstrates two clocks reading differently, not the stated counter-claim that other Market Watch symbols drag TimeCurrent forward. symbols_ahead_of_replay (lines 108-118) counts every symbol quoted since 2024, closed markets included, which is effectively total_mw. (b) Line 69 is a bare `CustomTicksAdd(InpTest, tk);` with the accepted count discarded, and SSR_MakeSymbol (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:303-335) never sets SYMBOL_CHART_MODE to BID, so a rejecting clone is a live possibility. Impact is small: a rejection makes bad_sec ~1000 and the symbol_time_equals_injected verdict FAIL loudly - a misdiagnosis to chase, not a false green - and the tautological verdict is harmless noise. MEDIUM is too high for a diagnostic-quality issue in a spike no user runs. Minor mis-citation: SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:351-364 is the SSR_Rate throughput doctrine, not a general acceptance rule, though D3:111 does follow that practice

#### `spikes-audits-11` — D1 computes seed throughput from bars requested, not bars accepted, and never asserts they are equal

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_D1_SeedPerformance.mq5:101`** · category: boundary

```mql5
SSR_Metric(c, "bars_per_sec", (t_total > 0 ? total / t_total : 0), "bars/s",
           "write + readable - this is the number the user waits for");
SSR_Metric(c, "write_call_bars_per_sec",
           (t_write > 0 ? total / (t_write / 1000.0) : 0), "bars/s",
           "WRITE CEILING ONLY - excludes the series build");
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: D1's two bars/s metrics (SSR_D1_SeedPerformance.mq5:101 and :103-104) divide bars *requested* (`total`) rather than bars *accepted* (`written`, accumulated at :62 only for chunks where CustomRatesUpdate returned > 0), and SeedCase issues no verdict comparing the two, so a partially-rejected seed still publishes full throughput. SSR_RateMetric's hardcoded "ticks/s" unit means the kit's SSR_Rate shortfall guard cannot be reused for a bar rate. The finding's claim that A2 has the same defect is WRONG: SSR_A2_RatesAndAggregation.mq5 does assert it — `SSR_Verdict("rates_written", written == n, ...)` immediately after its bars_per_sec metric — and A2's rate divides `written`, not `n`.

**Why:** Line 53-63 accumulates `written` only when w > 0, and lines 80-81 record bars_target and bars_written separately; nothing uses `written` in a rate or a verdict. SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:351-372 mandates routing throughput through SSR_Rate, which returns -1 when accepted < sent - but SSR_RateMetric hardcodes the unit 'ticks/s' (kit:382-387), so a bar-rate cannot use it and the guard is bypassed here and in A2:133 ('bars_per_sec' from `written` over t_write with no check that written == n and no guard against CustomRatesUpdate's -1).

**Verifiers:** The D1 half is provable. D1:53-63 accumulates `written` only when `w > 0` (so a -1 chunk is silently skipped), D1:80-81 record bars_target and bars_written as separate metrics, and both rate metrics (D1:101 and D1:103-104) divide `total`, never `written`. SeedCase issues no SSR_Verdict at all, so nothing compares them. SSR_RateMetric (SpikeKit) does hardcode "ticks/s" in both its branches, so a bar rate genuinely cannot route through it as written. However the finding's secondary evidence about A2 is false and must not be repeated / Verified in source: D1:54-63 accumulates `written` only for chunks where CustomRatesUpdate returned w > 0, :80-81 record bars_target and bars_written separately, and both rate metrics at :101 and :103-104 divide `total` (requested) rather than `written` (accepted). No SSR_Verdict compares them, so a partial or rejected chunk still publishes full throughput. SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:352-372 does mandate routing rates through SSR_Rate (which returns -1 when accepted < sent) and SSR_RateMetric hardcodes unit 'ticks/s' at :382/:387, so a bars/s rate cannot use the wrapper as written. One piece of the evidence is wrong and does not affect the D1 verdict: SSR_A2 at :133 divides `written`, not what was sent, and A2:134-135 does carry SSR_Verdict("rates_written", written == n, ...) - so A2 is not an instance of the same gap / The code fact is provable: D1:101 and D1:103-105 both divide `total` (the request) and nothing in the file consults `written` (accumulated at :62) in a rate or a verdict; SSR_RateMetric (kit:382-387) hardcodes the unit "ticks/s", so a bar rate genuinely cannot route through the SSR_Rate shortfall guard. But whether this ever produces a wrong number depends on terminal behaviour I cannot settle from source: CustomRatesUpdate's return value is a count of changed/added bars, so written != total is not necessarily a shortfall at all, and a partial-acceptance run is unvalidated (the author has never run this on MT5). If it does happen, the harm is one overstated bars/s in a dev CSV that already carries bars_target and bars_written side by side for comparison (:80-81). No user-facing effect, no money or guard impact, so MEDIUM overstates it

#### `spikes-audits-13` — D1 also prints its PASS gate rather than asserting it

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Spike/SSR_D1_SeedPerformance.mq5:161`** · category: measurement-validity

```mql5
Print("[D1] PASS gate: 100k bars in <= 20s, 500k in <= 120s,");
Print("[D1] and ui_jitter_p95 <= 500ms from the UIJitter probe.");

SSR_End();
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct: D1 prints its PASS gate instead of asserting it and contributes no rows to verdicts.csv, so a run where every seed takes 60 s still ends "SPIKE PASS". Confined to developer tooling, with the gate text and the per-case timings printed in the same log, so LOW rather than MEDIUM.

**Why:** The only SSR_Verdict in the file is at line 143 (symbol_ready, inside the early-return branch). SeedCase records eleven metrics and zero verdicts. SSR_End (SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:187) prints SPIKE PASS whenever g_ssr_fail == 0. **Verifiers:** Verified by count and by path. `grep -c SSR_Verdict` on SSR_D1_SeedPerformance.mq5 returns 1, and that single call is D1:143 inside the `if(!SSR_MakeSymbol(...))` early-return branch — so on every successful run the file emits ZERO verdicts. SeedCase computes t_total at :91 and holds the per-case timing, but records eleven metrics and no assertion. The numeric gate is only Print()ed at :161-162. SSR_End (SpikeKit) prints "SPIKE PASS" whenever g_ssr_fail == 0, so a run where every 100k seed took 60 s ends PASS=0 FAIL=0 SPIKE PASS and writes nothing to verdicts.csv. The contrast is real: D2, D4, B2 and D3 all encode their gates as SSR_Verdict (D3 even emits explicit FAIL verdicts for "not measured"). Severity lowered from MEDIUM: part of D1's stated gate (ui_jitter_p95 from a separate probe chart, manual bases\Custom folder size) is genuinely unassertable from inside this script, and this is a developer diagnostic, not shipped code / Confirmed by reading the whole file: the only SSR_Verdict call is at :143, inside the `if(!SSR_MakeSymbol(...))` early-return branch, so a successful run of D1 records zero verdicts. SSR_SpikeKit.mqh:181-191 prints 'SPIKE PASS' whenever g_ssr_fail == 0, so the numeric gate printed as prose at :161-162 can never fail the run. total_time is in hand at :90-91 and unused by any assertion. The comparison spikes do encode their gates with SSR_Verdict - verified at D2:100-101 (tfswitch_under_1s), D4:149-156 (step_back_interactive / usable / tail_beats_rebuild) and B2:177-182 (throughput_target / floor) - so D1 standing outside that pattern is a genuine gap, not a house style. Severity lowered to LOW: dev-only spike instrumentation / Verified: the only SSR_Verdict in the file is the symbol_ready one at :143, inside the early-return branch, so a normal run records eleven metrics per case and zero assertions; SSR_End (kit:181-190) prints "SPIKE PASS" whenever g_ssr_fail == 0, and the numeric gate at :161-163 is only printed. So every completing D1 run reports PASS regardless of how slow the seed was. Impact, however, is confined to a hand-run developer spike whose whole purpose is to produce numbers a human reads; the gate text sits three lines above the PASS line in the same log, and the per-case timings are printed at :126-129. Nothing a user of SS Replay can reach, nothing that corrupts arithmetic or weakens the guard. False assurance in a dev instrument: LOW, not MEDIUM

#### `tests-b-3` — T13.8 claims seed reproducibility and asserts three tautologies instead

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T13_Strategy.mq5:539`** · category: input-validation

```mql5
      CSSRStrategy *s1 = h1.At(0);
      CSSRStrategy *s2 = h2.At(0);
      Check("both hosts registered it", s1 != NULL && s2 != NULL);
      Check("under the same name", s1.Name() == s2.Name(), s1.Name());
      CheckEq("and the second host took both", 2, h2.Count());
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate. Severity is test-coverage rather than product impact: the per-strategy stream mixing is simply unverified, not shown to be wrong.

**Why:** The section draws no random number and never touches a context. s1.Name() and s2.Name() are two instances of CSSRRefBreakout, whose Name() is `return "ref-breakout";` (SSR_RefStrategy.mqh:48), so the comparison is a literal against itself. h2.Count() counts registrations, not streams. The claim the comment makes is about `m_ctx[k].rng.Seed(m_seed ^ NameHash(s.Name()))` (SSR_StrategyHost.mqh:141), and that is observable from a test: SSRRandom rng is a public member of the public CSSRStrategyContext (SSR_IStrategy.mqh:202) and the bound ctx is reachable from a strategy subclass - the file's own CSSRCheater already reads ctx.market and ctx.Now(). A probe strategy exposing ctx.rng.Next() would make the section real; nothing of the sort is done.

**Verifiers:** Traced T13.8 (lines 508-540) in full: it constructs two hosts, calls SetSeed(8143772915) on both, registers strategies, and then asserts only s1 != NULL && s2 != NULL, s1.Name() == s2.Name(), and h2.Count() == 2. No random number is drawn and no context is read. Both s1 and s2 are CSSRRefBreakout whose Name() is the constant "ref-breakout" (SSR_RefStrategy.mqh:48), so the name comparison is a literal against itself, and SetSeed only stores m_seed (SSR_StrategyHost.mqh:77) - the seeding under test is m_ctx[k].rng.Seed(m_seed ^ NameHash(s.Name())) at line 141, which nothing here observes. Different seeds, a no-op SetSeed, or index-based seeding would all pass. The finder is also right that it is observable: SSRRandom rng is a public member of the public CSSRStrategyContext (SSR_IStrategy.mqh:202) and the bound ctx is reachable from a subclass. Severity reduced to LOW: a coverage gap in a test, not a product defect / All three assertions in T13.8 (T13:539-543) are independent of seeding. s1 and s2 are both CSSRRefBreakout instances whose Name() returns the literal "ref-breakout" (SSR_RefStrategy.mqh:48), so s1.Name()==s2.Name() compares a constant with itself; h2.Count() counts registrations, not streams; the non-NULL check only restates that Add succeeded. No random number is drawn and no context is touched, so gutting SetSeed (SSR_StrategyHost.mqh:77) or replacing m_ctx[k].rng.Seed(m_seed ^ NameHash(s.Name())) (line 141) with the registration index leaves the section green, and two hosts given different seeds would pass it too. The claim is testable: CSSRStrategyContext::rng is a public SSRRandom member (SSR_IStrategy.mqh:~202) and the bound ctx is readable from a strategy subclass - the file's own CSSRCheater already reads ctx.market and ctx.Now() (T13:88-95) / The coverage gap is real and provable. T13.8 draws no random number and never touches a context: s1.Name() == s2.Name() compares the constant "ref-breakout" (SSR_RefStrategy.mqh:48) against itself, h2.Count() counts registrations, and the NULL check only proves Add succeeded. A grep for rng across MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests returns nothing, so no test in the build observes m_ctx[k].rng at all, and the section's stated claim - that the stream derives from m_seed ^ NameHash(name) and is therefore independent of registration order (SSR_StrategyHost.mqh:141) - is untested despite being observable (SSRRandom rng is a public member of the public CSSRStrategyContext, and the file's own CSSRCheater already reads ctx.market and ctx.Now()). Severity downgraded from MEDIUM to LOW on impact: I verified the seeding it fails to cover is actually correct - SetSeed coerces 0 to 1, NameHash returns non-zero, and SSRRandom::Seed replaces a zero state with 0x9E3779B97F4A7C15 (SSR_Random.mqh:33-39) - so the weak test is masking nothing today. No money arithmetic, no future-data path, no effect on the virtual-trading guarantee; the exposure is purely that a future regression in per-strategy seeding would ship green

#### `tests-b-4` — T14.8's "the command set is exactly the documented ones" is unfalsifiable by construction

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T14_Integration.mq5:355`** · category: boundary

```mql5
      for(int cmd = 0; cmd <= 11; cmd++)
        {
         if(cmd == SSR_CMD_PLAY || cmd == SSR_CMD_PAUSE ||
            ... || cmd == SSR_CMD_BUY_RISK || cmd == SSR_CMD_SELL_RISK)
            continue;
         read_verbs++;
        }
      CheckEq("the command set is exactly the documented ones", 0, read_verbs);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct, but severity is LOW rather than MEDIUM: nothing in the shipped product misbehaves — the section simply fails to be the tripwire its own comment (lines 348-350) says it is.

**Why:** SSR_Contract.mqh defines exactly twelve verbs occupying 0..11 (SSR_CMD_NONE 0 ... SSR_CMD_CLOSE_ALL 9, SSR_CMD_BUY_RISK 10, SSR_CMD_SELL_RISK 11, lines 92-108) and the `if` enumerates all twelve of those names. Every iteration of 0..11 therefore hits a `continue`, making `read_verbs++` unreachable and the assertion a constant 0 == 0. A thirteenth verb takes value 12, outside the hard-coded bound. The enum's size is never asserted, and there is no SSR_CMD_COUNT/SSR_CMD_MAX to assert against.

**Verifiers:** Verified against the enum. SSR_Contract.mqh:92-108 defines exactly twelve command macros with values 0..11 (NONE 0, PLAY 1, PAUSE 2, STEP 3, STEP_BACK 4, SPEED 5, JUMP 6, BUY 7, SELL 8, CLOSE_ALL 9, BUY_RISK 10, SELL_RISK 11). SSR_T14_Integration.mq5:355 loops `cmd = 0; cmd <= 11` and the if at 360-366 enumerates all twelve of those names, so every iteration hits `continue` and read_verbs++ at 367 is unreachable; CheckEq at 369-370 is a literal 0 == 0. The bound 11 is a hard-coded literal, and I grepped the contract header: there is no SSR_CMD_COUNT/SSR_CMD_MAX and no assertion on the enum's size, so a thirteenth verb at value 12 falls outside the loop and the section's stated purpose (comment at 348-350) is not served. No guard elsewhere covers this / SSR_Contract.mqh:92-108 defines exactly twelve verbs at values 0..11 (NONE 0 … CLOSE_ALL 9, BUY_RISK 10, SELL_RISK 11). The if-chain at SSR_T14_Integration.mq5:359-366 names all twelve, so every iteration of `for(cmd = 0; cmd <= 11; cmd++)` hits `continue`, read_verbs++ is unreachable, and CheckEq compares 0 to a compile-time-constant 0. I grepped the whole tree: no SSR_CMD_COUNT, SSR_CMD_MAX or CMD_LAST exists, so a thirteenth verb would take value 12 and fall outside the hard-coded bound without touching this test / Verified against the contract: SSR_Contract.mqh:92-108 defines exactly twelve verbs at 0..11, and the `if` at T14:360-365 enumerates all twelve names, so every iteration of the 0..11 loop hits `continue`, read_verbs++ is unreachable, and CheckEq asserts a constant 0==0. A thirteenth verb would take value 12 and fall outside the hard-coded bound; there is no SSR_CMD_COUNT/SSR_CMD_MAX to bound against. So the section's own stated purpose ('a future edit that adds one of these verbs should have to delete a test that says why it must not') is not served. Impact is a dormant tripwire, not a live defect: no read verb exists today, nothing leaks, and no user can reach any state through this.
