#### `core-engine-11` — Improvements: stale cursor comment in SaveInto, dead SSR_BAR_READ_CEILING, bulk threshold off by 1 ms for PgDn, degraded FULL_TICK skips the bulk rule

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1707`** · category: input-validation

```mql5
  //  The cursor goes too. Without it a resumed session would re-emit
  //  bars the sink already holds, or skip ones it does not.
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Corrections to two sub-claims. (a) RestoreFrom does read the saved 'bars' (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1856, the no-fingerprint legacy check against m_cursor.bar_count); it is 'emitted' and 'ticks' (written at 1725-1726) that no restore path consumes. The stale-comment point at 1707-1708 stands: position is rebuilt by JumpTo, not by the saved cursor. (c) owed_msc = hi - lo = N*60000 - r - 2, where r is the current instant's offset into its own minute (base = floor+60000, target = base + (N-1)*60000 - 1 at line 1169, lo = emitted+1 per SSR_ReplayCursor.mqh:54). From xx:59.999 a 10-bar step owes 539,999 ms, not 599,999; the 599,999 near-miss occurs on the FIRST step after Load, where cursor.RewindTo(start) leaves emitted = start-1 so lo == start (a bar open) and owed = 10*60000 - 1. Either way the conclusion holds: StepBars(10) can never reach SSR_BULK_THRESHOLD_MSC = 600,000, so PgDn is never a bulk moment while an 11-bar step almost always is — express the rule in bar counts. (d) the harm is also narrower than stated: a 33-hour catch-up does not traverse this path, because JumpForward bulk-writes whole bars with SeedBars (1370-1392) and hands EmitWindow only the target's partial minute; the branch bites on a post-stall pump, where m_budget.MaxBars (398-404) already bounds the work per call — so it is a throughput/fidelity-priority nit, not a burst.

**Why:** RestoreFrom lines 1766-1866 read origin/now/speed/fidelity/auto_pause/bookmarks/fingerprint/bars only. grep shows SSR_BAR_READ_CEILING only at its definition. SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh lines 114-129.

**Verifiers:** Three of four sub-claims are exactly right and the fourth's conclusion holds on corrected arithmetic. (b) grep across MQL5/ shows SSR_BAR_READ_CEILING only at its definition (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:42) — dead, and the constructor uses a bare 8192 literal instead (547). (d) SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh:114-129 is genuinely if/else-if: `m_requested == FULL_TICK && !m_ticks_available` short-circuits, so the `owed_msc >= SSR_BULK_THRESHOLD_MSC` branch is unreachable for that request for the whole session, and a large owed window runs at m_ticks_per_bar (8) instead of 1. (a) is right about the stale comment — RestoreFrom rebuilds position with JumpTo(want_now) at 1826 and never reads 'emitted' or 'ticks' — but wrong that none of the three is read: 'bars' IS read at 1856 as the legacy pre-fingerprint drift check. (c) reaches the right conclusion from a wrong instant. Nothing here is guarded or compensated elsewhere, and the IMPROVEMENT severity is right: none of the four is user-visible on its own / All four items are static facts, two of them mis-stated. (b) is clean: grep shows SSR_BAR_READ_CEILING only at its definition (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:42) — the constructor uses a bare literal ArrayResize(m_ticks, 8192) instead, and the bar read at 383 happens before the cap at 398, so the bound its own comment promises does not exist anywhere. (d) is clean: CSSRFidelityPolicy::Decide (SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh:114-129) chains the NO_TICK_DATA degrade and the bulk rule as if/else-if, and m_requested stays FULL_TICK, so a FULL_TICK session on a tick-less symbol has the bulk rule disabled for its whole life. (a) is right about the stale comment at 1698-1708 but wrong that RestoreFrom reads none of the three fields — it does read `bars` at 1856-1863 for the legacy check; `emitted` and `ticks` are the write-only pair, and position is rebuilt via JumpTo at 1826. (c) reaches the right conclusion by wrong arithmetic: with base = floor(now)+60000 and target = base+(bars-1)*60000-1 (line 1169) and lo = emitted+1, StepBars(10) from an xx:59.999 rest point owes 539,999 ms, not 599,999 — 599,999 is the first step from a freshly loaded bar open (emitted = start-1). Ten-bar steps span 539,999..599,999 and so can never reach SSR_BULK_THRESHOLD_MSC = 600,000; an 11-bar step is bulk except at exactly mm:59.999, and 11 is not reachable from the panel (SSR_Panel.mqh:2293-2296 wires only 1 and 10) / Judged as a bundle of hygiene items with no user-observable effect; two of the four are mis-stated. (a) is right in substance — the SESSION PERSISTENCE comment (1698-1708) claims the saved cursor prevents re-emission, whereas RestoreFrom rebuilds position with JumpTo(want_now) at 1826 — but 'never reads them' is wrong: `bars` IS read at 1858 for the legacy no-fingerprint check; only `emitted` and `ticks` are write-only. (b) is fully confirmed: SSR_BAR_READ_CEILING has exactly one occurrence repo-wide (line 42), the constructor uses a bare 8192 at line 547, and the bar read at 383 happens before the cap at 398-404, so the define's own comment promises a bound that does not exist. (c) is arithmetically wrong: with base = SSRNextBarOpenMsc(now), target = base + (bars-1)*60000 - 1 and lo = emitted+1, owed = 599998 - r for StepBars(10), where r is now's offset into its minute — 539,999 at mm:59.999 and never above 599,998, so PgDn is 1,002 ms short at best, not 1 ms, and an 11-bar step is bulk everywhere except that exact instant. The conclusion (PgDn is never a bulk moment) holds and is arguably the desired behaviour, since degrading a ten-candle step to BAR would strip the intrabar path from exactly the candles the user chose to inspect. (d) is confirmed: the if/else-if at SSR_FidelityPolicy.mqh:114-129 means a FULL_TICK request degraded for NO_TICK_DATA never evaluates the bulk rule at all, for the whole session; the consequence is milder than stated, because a large jump never reaches this path (JumpForward bulk-seeds and sends only the target minute to EmitWindow) and the per-pump cap at 398-404 bounds each call, so the user sees slower stall recovery rather than a 33-hour tick burst. IMPROVEMENT is correct

#### `core-sync-6` — SnapshotStore::DropFrom leaves holes in the ring without adjusting m_count/m_head, so Count() over-reports and the next checkpoints overwrite the oldest survivors instead of refilling the holes

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Core/SSR_SnapshotStore.mqh:140`** · category: boundary

```mql5
   int               DropFrom(const long msc)
     {
      int dropped = 0;
      for(int i = 0; i < m_count; i++)
        {
         if(m_ring[i].taken_at_msc >= msc)
           { m_ring[i].Init(); dropped++; }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Real but overstated in one respect: overwriting the oldest entry is normal full-ring behaviour, so the loss versus an ideal implementation is k checkpoints (k x 5 min) of the oldest history plus a temporary rewind depth of 64-k until the head wraps onto the holes - not '5h of rewindable history destroyed'. The observable wrongness is Count()/the panel's 'checkpoints' figure over-reporting, and the reduced depth.

**Why:** m_count is only incremented in Checkpoint (line 107-108 'if(m_count < SSR_CHECKPOINT_RING) m_count++') and reset in ClearCheckpoints; m_head is only advanced in Checkpoint (106). DropFrom (140-160) touches neither. T8.2 tests DropFrom with 10 entries (no wrap) and asserts only the dropped count and the newest survivor. RECOMMENDATION: on DropFrom, rewind m_head to the slot after the newest survivor and set m_count to the survivor count (entries are written in time order, so dropped entries are always the newest contiguous run behind the head).

**Verifiers:** DropFrom (SSR_SnapshotStore.mqh:140-160) Init()s matching slots and recomputes m_last_msc but touches neither m_count nor m_head; m_count is only raised in Checkpoint (107-108) and zeroed in ClearCheckpoints, m_head only advanced in Checkpoint (106). So after a step back from a full ring, Count() still reports 64 (surfaced as ReplayVitals.checkpoints via SSR_GroupPort.mqh:148) while k slots are empty, and the head keeps writing forward over the oldest live entries instead of reusing the holes, which stay empty until the head wraps. NearestAtOrBefore is unaffected (it skips taken_at<=0, 126) / Provable from source: DropFrom Init()s the matching slots and recomputes m_last_msc but touches neither m_count nor m_head (SSR_SnapshotStore.mqh:140-160), while m_count/m_head are only ever moved in Checkpoint (105-108) and ClearCheckpoints. So after a StepBackward -> DropFrom(now+1) (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:1475) Count() keeps returning the pre-drop total - and that is a user-visible number, GroupPort publishes it as 'checkpoints' (SSR_GroupPort.mqh: out.checkpoints = c.Snapshots().Count()) - and the next Checkpoint writes at m_head, i.e. over the OLDEST live entry, while the freed holes near the head stay empty until the head wraps. Rewind depth therefore degrades to the survivor count for a full lap of the ring. NearestAtOrBefore is unaffected because it skips taken_at <= 0 (119-136), so no wrong restore results - IMPROVEMENT is the right grade, and the suggested fix (rewind m_head/m_count to the newest survivor) is sound since dropped entries are always the newest contiguous run behind the head / Provable: Checkpoint is the only writer of m_head/m_count (SSR_SnapshotStore.mqh:105-108), ClearCheckpoints the only resetter (:69), and DropFrom touches neither (:140-160) - it only Init()s slots and recomputes m_last_msc. So Count() over-reports and the head keeps walking over live slots while holes sit unused. Correctness is untouched (NearestAtOrBefore scans i<m_count and skips t<=0, :123-127; IsDue uses the recomputed m_last_msc), so the only user-visible artefact is the panel's 'checkpoints' figure via GroupPort (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:147) reading 64 when fewer are live. The claim over-states the damage: the next k checkpoints overwrite the k OLDEST slots, costing k*5 minutes of rewind depth prematurely, not '5h of rewindable history'

#### `data-11` — Every data-quality number this subsystem measures is computed on the hot path and then discarded; the only route to a human is a ToString() with no caller

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Data/SSR_Mt5DataSource.mqh:146`** · category: input-validation

```mql5
   string            ToString(void)
     {
      return StringFormat("mt5[%s open=%s ticks=%s %s]",
                          m_symbol, (m_open ? "yes" : "no"),
                          (m_has_ticks ? "yes" : "no"),
                          m_bars.Window().ToString());
     }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: One detail is wrong: the window's own ToString - the string that carries dropped= - is not entirely unreachable; SSR_T2_DataEngine.mq5:256-258 calls mbp.Window().ToString() and prints it. What is unreachable is CSSRMt5DataSource::ToString and every host-side route: nothing in the shipped EA/Ui ever prints m_dropped or any SSRDataReport field, so a live user's gappy or sanitised range produces no diagnostic line.

**Why:** Repo-wide grep confirms the chain is dead at both ends: no caller for CSSRMt5DataSource::ToString, none for CSSRBarWindow::ReportInto or Dropped(), and no occurrence of "dropped", "micro_gap", "session_gap" or "duplicates" anywhere in MQL5/Experts, Ui, Common or Core outside the validator itself. CSSRMt5TickProvider::Pages/TicksRead/ReadTimeMs are equally unread. The instrumentation was written deliberately — SSR_BarWindow.mqh:53 says "instrumentation, so Phase 7 tunes with numbers not opinions" — so this is an unfinished wiring, not an absent idea. It compounds data-2 and data-1: both of those failures are silent precisely because this telemetry never surfaces.

**Verifiers:** The wiring really is dead at the host end. CSSRMt5DataSource::ToString (SSR_Mt5DataSource.mqh:146-152) has no caller anywhere; CSSRBarWindow::ReportInto (SSR_BarWindow.mqh:329) has no caller (the only ReportInto with one is CSSRLeakGuard's); and micro_gaps/session_gaps/duplicates appear nowhere outside SSR_DataValidator.mqh and the T2 test script, i.e. never in MQL5/Experts, Ui, Common or Core. So the per-range validation report and m_dropped (accumulated at SSR_BarWindow.mqh:210) never reach the log, the vitals line or the flight recorder, which is exactly why data-2's failures are silent / The dead-end is verifiable by grep and by reading the files: CSSRBarWindow computes m_last_report (ValidateBars over the exact loaded range: duplicates, out_of_order, micro_gaps, session_gaps, largest_gap_msc) and m_dropped on every miss (SSR_BarWindow.mqh:208-210), exposes them through ReportInto (329) and Dropped() (321), and neither has any caller; CSSRMt5DataSource::ToString - the single expression that formats the window (SSR_Mt5DataSource.mqh:146-152) - has no caller either (the host only ever uses g_src.Open/Close/RangeInto/Bars/History), and no occurrence of 'dropped', 'micro_gap', 'session_gap' or 'duplicates' exists in Experts, Ui, Common or Core outside the validator. So the telemetry never reaches the log, the vitals line, or the flight recorder. This is unfinished wiring rather than incorrect behaviour, hence IMPROVEMENT, and the observation that it is what makes data-2 and data-1 silent is fair / The dead ends are real: grep shows CSSRBarWindow::ReportInto and Dropped() have no caller outside the class, Window() is touched only by SSR_T2_DataEngine.mq5, and CSSRMt5DataSource::ToString (SSR_Mt5DataSource.mqh:146-152) is never invoked - the host never calls g_src.ToString(). PrintVitals composes its line from the sink, charts and clock only, so duplicates/micro_gaps/session_gaps/dropped never reach a human. But this is absent diagnostics, not a defect: nothing the product does is wrong because of it, and the user observes nothing. It is also partly mitigated - the default vitals line does print the replay symbol's M1 bar count and last bar time once a second, which is how 'candles missing in the middle of Tuesday' would first be seen. IMPROVEMENT, not LOW

#### `strategy-integration-report-15` — SSR_CMD_SPEED reports success for a value the clock silently rewrote

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Integration/SSR_Publisher.mqh:283`** · category: input-validation

```mql5
         case SSR_CMD_SPEED:
           {
            m_group.SetSpeedX100((long)a1);
            return SSR_RC_OK;
           }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: SSR_CMD_SPEED returns an unconditional SSR_RC_OK with no bounds test, so a client sending 0 or a negative x100 is told the command succeeded while the clock has clamped the value to 1 (0.01x). The finding's upper-bound half is wrong: SetSpeedX100 has no upper clamp (SSR_ReplayClock.mqh:78) and SSR_SPEED_MAX is only the UI ladder's last stop and a name/meaning threshold, so a request above it is applied verbatim and OK is accurate.

**Why:** The case returns a constant OK with no inspection of a1 and no bounds test. SSR_ReplayClock.mqh:76-80 is `speed_x100 = (s < 1 ? 1 : s);` with no upper bound. SSR_Types.mqh:131 defines SSR_SPEED_MAX = 100000 and the ladder's stop 19 (SSR_Types.mqh:298) is that value, so the contract exposes a range the UI does not. Contrast SSR_CMD_STEP and SSR_CMD_JUMP, which both propagate the engine's bool.

**Verifiers:** The premise 'every other command reports what the engine actually did' is refuted by the file itself: SSR_CMD_JUMP is documented as clamp-and-succeed three lines above the speed case — 'clamped by the engine into its own timeline, so a client asking for an instant outside the session lands at the edge rather than anywhere strange' (lines 289-292) — and SeekTo (SSR_ReplayClock.mqh:104-112) unconditionally returns true after SSRClampMsc, so JUMP also answers OK for a value the engine rewrote. Speed follows the same established pattern. No incorrect state results: SetSpeedX100 clamps to >= 1 (SSR_ReplayClock.mqh:76-80), the group propagates the clamped master value to every stream (SSR_MasterClock.mqh:281-288), Advance saturates at end_msc (SSR_ReplayClock.mqh:99-108) so no overflow or runaway follows a huge value, and every consumer treats speed_x100 >= SSR_SPEED_MAX as 'as fast as ticks feed' (SSR_Types.mqh:241, 352) — so 5,000,000 runs exactly as the caller asked. The authoritative value is republished in SSR_GV_SPEED on the next Publish (line 190) and the shipped client's only speed reading is that field (SSR_Client.mqh:120); nothing in the contract lets a client display its own request. The residual discrepancy — OK for a1 <= 0 which becomes 0.01x — is an API-consistency improvement, not a defect / The source facts hold for the low side: SSR_CMD_SPEED (SSR_Publisher.mqh:283-287) returns a constant SSR_RC_OK with no inspection of a1, while CSSRReplayClock::SetSpeedX100 is `speed_x100 = (s < 1 ? 1 : s);` (SSR_ReplayClock.mqh:76-80), so a client sending 0 or a negative x100 is told OK and the replay runs at 0.01x. CSSRReplayGroup::SetSpeedX100 (SSR_MasterClock.mqh:281-289) is void, so there is genuinely no engine bool to propagate — unlike SSR_CMD_STEP/JUMP, which do. The high-side half of the claim is refuted: there is no upper clamp anywhere, and SSR_SPEED_MAX (SSR_Types.mqh:131) is only the UI ladder's top stop (line 298) and a display threshold (lines 241, 352), so 5,000,000 is honoured rather than rewritten and the OK is truthful. Improvement-grade: a bounds test or a read-back of m_group.SpeedX100() before answering / Verified: the SSR_CMD_SPEED case returns a constant SSR_RC_OK with no inspection of a1, while CSSRReplayClock::SetSpeedX100 is `speed_x100 = (s < 1 ? 1 : s);` (SSR_ReplayClock.mqh:76-80) and CSSRReplayGroup::SetSpeedX100 (SSR_MasterClock.mqh:281-288) just forwards, so 0 or a negative becomes 1 (0.01x) and is still reported as success — inconsistent with STEP/JUMP, which propagate the engine's bool. Correctly filed as IMPROVEMENT, and the impact is smaller still because Publish() writes m_group.SpeedX100() to SSR_GV_SPEED every pump (line 190), so the truth is always on the wire for any client that reads it. One correction: the 5,000,000 example is not a value 'the clock silently rewrote' — there is no upper clamp anywhere, so that speed is accepted verbatim and OK is truthful; it merely exceeds the range the UI ladder exposes

#### `trading-exec-13` — Auto-pause reasons are English literals with a hard-coded 5-digit price format

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_AutoPause.mqh:124`** · category: utf8

```mql5
Raise(StringFormat("entry filled - %s %.2f @ %s",
                   SSROrderName(p.type), p.volume_initial,
                   DoubleToString(p.open_price, 5)));
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: All auto-pause reason strings are untranslatable English literals that the panel repeats verbatim, and the default flags (SL|TP|STOPOUT) make that live out of the box. The hard-coded 5-digit price format additionally applies only to the entry-filled line, which requires SSR_PAUSE_ON_ENTRY to be switched on.

**Why:** Lines 124-126, 137-138, 142, 146, 153 build literals; the position's digits are available via m_acct.Digits() but not used.

**Verifiers:** Read as quoted: every Raise() argument in Scan() is an English literal (SSR_AutoPause.mqh:124-126, 137-138, 142, 146, 153) with no T()/ENUM_SSR_STR route, and the price is DoubleToString(p.open_price, 5) regardless of instrument digits even though CSSRTradingEngine::Digits() exists (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:1026). The text does reach the UI verbatim: CSSRTradeAutoPause -> controller pause reason -> SSR_GroupPort.mqh:127-128 -> SSR_Panel.mqh:954-955 appends it to a panel label, so with InpLanguage=fa (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:187, 1804) an English sentence appears on a translated panel. A19's scan is confined to /Ui/ draw-call literals (tools/ssr_audit.py:1450-1470), so it cannot see this. Only nit: entry pauses need SSR_PAUSE_ON_ENTRY, which is off by default (SSR_PAUSE_DEFAULT), but the SL/TP/stop-out literals carry the same defect, so the finding stands as an IMPROVEMENT / Verifiable by reading: every Raise() argument in SSR_AutoPause.mqh (124-126, 137-138, 142, 146, 153) is an English literal built with StringFormat, none goes through T(ENUM_SSR_STR), and SSR_Strings.mqh's override mechanism (SSRLoadLanguage, 619-720) can only replace catalogue entries - so no lang/fa.txt can ever translate these. The reason does reach the panel: CSSRReplayController stores it (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:156-167, PauseReason() at 726) and CSSRGroupPort copies it into the view model (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:127-128). DoubleToString(p.open_price, 5) is hard-coded although m_acct.Digits() exists (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:1026), so a 2-digit index prints three spurious zeros. Correcting the scope: the digit bug is on the entry line only, which needs SSR_PAUSE_ON_ENTRY (off in SSR_PAUSE_DEFAULT, 41); the untranslated SL/TP/stopout reasons are on by default / Verified: lines 124-126, 137-138, 142, 146 and 153 build English literals that Core repeats verbatim (SSR_ReplayController.mqh:156-167) and the panel prints (SSR_Panel.mqh:954-955), while SSRLoadLanguage genuinely swaps everything that goes through T() (SSR_Strings.mqh:619+). CSSRTradingEngine::Digits() exists (SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:1026) and is not used, so DoubleToString(...,5) is hard-coded. Impact is smaller than even the finder's example suggests: in the active rail layout the whole progress+reason string is clipped to 24 characters (SSR_Panel.mqh:960-961, Clip(pct,24)), so 'entry filled - BUY 0.50 @ 41234.56000' is truncated long before the spurious decimals appear, and SSR_PAUSE_DEFAULT excludes SSR_PAUSE_ON_ENTRY (line 41) so that particular banner is off unless the user turns it on. What a Persian user actually observes is a short English fragment where the rest of the panel is translated. IMPROVEMENT is the right tier

#### `ui-plumbing-5` — SSR_Layout.mqh is dead in production: nothing outside the QA smoke test positions anything through it, so the RTL plan it exists to enable has no backing

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Layout.mqh:67`** · category: rtl

```mql5
int SSRLead(const SSRFrame &f, const int off, const int width)
  {
   if(!f.rtl)
      return f.x + f.pad + off;
   return f.x + f.w - f.pad - off - width;
  }
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate as described. Only the severity is over-stated: nothing misbehaves at runtime; the cost is that the RTL phase will have to rewrite the inline arithmetic the helpers were built to prevent, and the file header asserts a constraint the codebase never adopted.

**Why:** The file's header states the constraint it was built to impose: "The localization plan therefore places one constraint on every phase after this one - position through here, never by hand - and this file is that promise made concrete." The constraint was never adopted; every UI file computes x positions inline (e.g. SSR_KeyCard.mqh:74-93 `int x = 14; ... x + 12 ... x + 86`, SSR_Panel.mqh:889 `int cx = x + 140`). The smoke test's own note (SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:4984) records the file as "written and deliberately OFF", which is true of the mirroring flag but understates it: the helpers are not merely off, they are uncalled.

**Verifiers:** Grep over every .mqh/.mq5 is decisive: SSRLead/SSRTrail/SSRCentre/SSRInner/SSRColW/SSRColX/SSRFrame/SSRRows appear only in SSR_Layout.mqh itself, in SSR_Panel.mqh:44 (a bare #include that uses nothing), and in SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:3354-3386 (the only caller and the only rtl=true). SSRCentre and SSRInner have no call site at all except SSRColW's internal use of SSRInner. Production draw sites do compute pixels inline (SSR_ReviewCard.mqh:112-204 is a full page of m_x+12 / m_x+SSR_RV_W-96 arithmetic, exactly the pattern the header forbids). So flipping SSRFrame.rtl changes nothing on any screen and the header's claim that later phases position 'through here, never by hand' is not honoured anywhere. No runtime defect exists today, hence LOW rather than MEDIUM: this is an unadopted abstraction plus an over-stated header, not a fault a user can hit / Grep over every .mqh/.mq5 confirms the only callers of SSRLead/SSRTrail/SSRColW/SSRColX/SSRFrame/SSRRows are SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:3354-3386; SSR_Panel.mqh:44 only #includes the header. No production draw site positions through it, so flipping SSRFrame.rtl is a no-op on screen - provable from source, no MQL5 semantics involved. But there is no runtime defect: the header itself states 'mirroring is IMPLEMENTED and OFF', RTL is not a shipped feature, and MQL5 compiles uncalled global functions without complaint. The finding is accurate technical debt, not a MEDIUM behavioural bug, and one sub-claim is wrong: SSRInner IS called, at SSR_Layout.mqh:134 inside SSRColW (only SSRCentre is truly callerless) / The grep is accurate (MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Layout.mqh's helpers are referenced only by SSR_Panel.mqh:44's #include and SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:3354-3386), but nothing here is a defect a user can reach or observe. Every draw site positions correctly today; SSRFrame.rtl is never true outside the test, so no pixel is misplaced, no string is cut, no arithmetic is wrong. The file's own header (lines 28-31) states the status the finding reports as a discovery: 'mirroring is IMPLEMENTED and OFF. Nothing calls it with rtl=true yet, because no string has been translated. Phase 10 turns it on.' Pre-built, deliberately unwired scaffolding whose adoption is scheduled for a later phase is a planning observation, not a bug, and MEDIUM misrepresents a zero-impact state as a product fault. The residual point - that the promised 'position through here, never by hand' constraint was not adopted, so Phase 10 will cost a manual rewrite of eight UI files - is worth recording at IMPROVEMENT, no higher

#### `ui-port-session-16` — data_mode is on the wire, set by nobody and read by nobody

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:54`** · category: zero-empty

```mql5
   ENUM_SSR_DATA_MODE data_mode;
```

**What goes wrong:** The one port implementation never assigns this field, so it permanently reports SSR_DATA_MEMORY; the first consumer to trust it will describe a disk-backed session as in-memory.

**Why:** Init() sets `data_mode = SSR_DATA_MEMORY;` (l.250) and CSSRGroupPort::ReadState never touches it, although the value exists on the controller and is written into the session file (`f.SetInt("data_mode", (int)m_state.data_mode);`, ReplayController.mqh:1728). A grep for data_mode across Ui and Integration returns only the declaration and that Init line - no writer, no reader. Either fill it in ReadState beside fidelity (l.110-112) or drop it from the struct: SSRUiState is the IPC contract, and a field that is always a default is a field the promised Ipc port will silently get wrong.

**Verifiers:** Traced every occurrence of data_mode in the tree: SSR_ReplayState.mqh:35/55 (engine field, defaults MEMORY), SSR_ReplayController.mqh:693 SetDataMode and :1728 the session-file write, SSR_PositionFile.mqh:79/119, and in the UI layer only SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:54 (declaration) and :250 (Init sets SSR_DATA_MEMORY). No ReadState implementation assigns it and no panel/dialog reads it. Meanwhile production really does set the engine to a different value (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1150 and :499 call SetDataMode(SSR_DATA_BROKER)), so the wire field is not merely unset, it is provably inconsistent with the controller it is supposed to mirror. Dead field: no current user-visible fault, correctly filed as IMPROVEMENT / Verified: SSRUiState::Init (SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:250) sets data_mode = SSR_DATA_MEMORY and CSSRGroupPort::ReadState (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:100+) calls out.Init() then never touches data_mode, while it does fill fidelity/fidelity_effective/fidelity_note. A repo-wide grep for data_mode returns only SSR_ReplayState.mqh:35/55, SSR_ReplayController.mqh:693/1728, SSR_PositionFile.mqh:79/119 and these two UI lines - no UI writer and no UI reader. So the field is dead on the wire today; the harm is latent (a future Ipc port or panel consumer reading a permanently-default field), exactly as the finder's own IMPROVEMENT severity says / Verified: SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:54 declares data_mode, :250 sets it to SSR_DATA_MEMORY in Init(), and a repo-wide grep for data_mode returns only those two lines plus the controller side (SSR_ReplayState.mqh:35/55, SSR_ReplayController.mqh:693/1728, SSR_PositionFile.mqh:79/119). CSSRGroupPort::ReadState never copies it, so the UI-side field is permanently MEMORY while production sets the controller to SSR_DATA_BROKER (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:1150, :499). Impact today is exactly nil - no panel widget, dialog or card reads SSRUiState.data_mode, so nothing is displayed wrongly and nothing is computed from it. The finding's own severity (IMPROVEMENT) is already the right altitude: an unfilled wire field on the IPC contract, a latent trap for the first consumer, not a live defect

#### `ui-dialogs-16` — Review-card measure rows are OBJ_BUTTONs whose latch nothing consumes, so a clicked statistic stays visually pressed

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_ReviewCard.mqh:162`** · category: event-handling

```mql5
      m_w.List("m", m_x + 12, ly, SSR_RV_W - 24, SSR_RV_ROW_H,
               lines, m_first, SSR_RV_SHOWN, -1);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Real, but the escape hatch named is wrong: paging does NOT clear it. Page()+Render() only rewrite XDISTANCE/text through ButtonC, which never touches OBJPROP_STATE, so the row stays visually latched until Hide()/RemoveAll() deletes the object. The stale pressed look therefore survives every repaint and every page turn for the life of the card.

**Why:** CSSRWidgets::List draws each row with Button()/ButtonC() (SSR_Widgets.mqh:565-587), and ButtonC deliberately does not clear the pressed state on repaint (:388-394, 'THE PRESSED STATE IS NOT CLEARED HERE ANY MORE'). Only Pressed() consumes a latch (:337-346) and CSSRReviewCard::Poll asks about up/down/stmt/close only (:213-222). The card passes selected = -1, so it never intends selection. Nothing else routes the click either: RouteEvent hands unmatched object clicks to g_panel.OnEvent, which filters on the SSRP_ prefix (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3228).

**Verifiers:** Traced every possible clearer and found none. CSSRWidgets::List (SSR_Widgets.mqh:565-589) draws each visible row via Button(), Button() delegates to ButtonC() (:261-272), and ButtonC deliberately never writes OBJPROP_STATE (:388-394). Only Pressed() clears a latch (:337-346) and CSSRReviewCard::Poll asks about up/down/stmt/close only (SSR_ReviewCard.mqh:217-220). The panel's sweep cannot reach it twice over: CSSRPanel::PollClicks skips any object whose name does not start with its own prefix (SSR_Panel.mqh:2480-2481, prefix SSRP_ vs the card's SSRR2_), and while the card is up the host sets modal=true and does not call PollClicks at all (SSReplayStandalone.mq5:3052-3059). No object-click event route exists either, since the card lives on g_panel_chart which the EA is not attached to. Cosmetic-only, so IMPROVEMENT is right / Traceable end to end: CSSRWidgets::List (SSR_Widgets.mqh:563-587) draws every visible row as an OBJ_BUTTON via Button()/ButtonC(); ButtonC deliberately never writes OBJPROP_STATE (:388-394); only Pressed() (:337-346) clears a latch, and CSSRReviewCard::Poll (:213-222) polls just up/down/stmt/close with selected = -1. The panel's own sweep cannot help: CSSRPanel::PollClicks filters object names on m_prefix 'SSRP_' (SSR_Panel.mqh:276, 2480-2488) and the card's widgets use 'SSRR2_'. The card is drawn on the replay chart, so CHARTEVENT_OBJECT_CLICK never reaches the EA either. Cosmetic only - no command fires, no state corrupts - hence IMPROVEMENT. One detail is understated rather than wrong / Mechanism verified end to end: CSSRWidgets::List (SSR_Widgets.mqh:565-587) draws each visible row with Button()/ButtonC(); ButtonC deliberately never writes OBJPROP_STATE on repaint (:388-394); only Pressed() (:337-346) consumes a latch; CSSRReviewCard::Poll (SSR_ReviewCard.mqh:217-220) polls up/down/stmt/close only; selected is passed as -1 (:163) so selection is never intended; and a grep for OBJPROP_STATE shows no sweep anywhere that clears stray latches on 'SSRR2_m*' objects. So a clicked statistic row stays visually depressed. Impact is a false affordance on a read-only modal: nothing is mis-executed, no state changes, no number is wrong. IMPROVEMENT is the correct altitude and the finder already chose it

#### `ui-plumbing-11` — The metrics comment a later designer must read to size a new row is stale in four of its six numbers

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Theme.mqh:437`** · category: boundary

```mql5
//|   caption 23 + clock+progress 32 + transport 27 + speed 33        |
//| + tabs 21 + sheet 222 + status 18 + margin 14 = 390               |
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The metrics comment at SSR_Theme.mqh:437-438 disagrees with the constants it documents in four figures, not four of six: speed 33 vs the built 21, sheet 222 vs SSR_SHEET_H 186 (:449), margin 14 vs 8, and total 390 vs SSR_PANEL_H = 336 (:499). Five of its figures still match — caption 23, clock+progress 32, transport 27, tabs 21 and status 18 — so the finding's "only caption 23, transport 27 and status 18 still match" understates the agreement. The consequence stands: a row sized against the comment's 222 px sheet overflows the real 186 px sheet by 36 px, reproducing the v69 draw-over-the-status-bar failure the same block warns about.

**Why:** SSR_PANEL_H was deliberately turned into a compiler-added expression (line 495-499: "ADDED UP BY THE COMPILER, not by me. The sum above was a comment for eleven builds and the two rows v69 added went straight past the end of it") but the prose sum above SSR_SHEET_H was left as the older set of numbers, so the file now carries two disagreeing totals - the failure mode it was rewritten to prevent, one comment higher up.

**Verifiers:** Arithmetic and line numbers verified. The prose sum at SSR_Theme.mqh:437-438 reads "caption 23 + clock+progress 32 + transport 27 + speed 33 + tabs 21 + sheet 222 + status 18 + margin 14 = 390". The built constants are SSR_SHEET_H 186 (:449) and SSR_PANEL_H = (23 + 32 + 27 + 21 + 21 + SSR_SHEET_H + 18 + 8) (:499), which evaluates to 336. So speed is 21 not 33, sheet 186 not 222, margin 8 not 14, total 336 not 390 — and a row budgeted from the comment's 222 px sheet overruns the real 186 px sheet by exactly 36 px, which is the v69 overflow the same comment block (:426-430) describes. The file does carry two disagreeing totals, and :495-498 explicitly says the compiler-added form exists so there is "no second number to forget" — while the comment one page up is that forgotten number. The count in the title is slightly off / Provable from the file. The prose budget at SSR_Theme.mqh:436-440 reads 'caption 23 + clock+progress 32 + transport 27 + speed 33 + tabs 21 + sheet 222 + status 18 + margin 14 = 390' with a sheet 'rounded to 222', while the built constants are SSR_SHEET_H 186 (:449) and SSR_PANEL_H = (23 + 32 + 27 + 21 + 21 + SSR_SHEET_H + 18 + 8) (:499), which sums to 336 with speed 21 and margin 8. So speed, sheet, margin and the total disagree with what compiles, and the same block's 'Positions now ends at 193, Trade at 218' both exceed the 186 sheet it sits above. The irony is real: :495-498 explains that SSR_PANEL_H was turned into a compiler-added expression precisely because the prose sum went stale, and the prose sum directly above SSR_SHEET_H was left at the pre-rail numbers. Count correction: four values are stale out of the nine quoted, not 'four of six' - caption 23, clock 32, transport 27, tabs 21 and status 18 still match / The two totals genuinely disagree in the shipped file: the prose walk above SSR_SHEET_H (:437-438) budgets speed 33, sheet 222, margin 14 and a 390 px panel, while the built constants are SSR_SHEET_H 186 (:449) and SSR_PANEL_H = (23+32+27+21+21+186+18+8) = 336 (:499). A designer sizing a new row from the comment would over-budget the sheet by 36 px, and CheckFrame (SSR_Panel.mqh:3019-3034) would not catch that class of error because it only measures past the panel's outer frame, not past the sheet - a row spilling onto the status strip stays inside the frame. So the v69 failure mode the block describes is still available to a reader who trusts the prose. It is still only a comment: no user sees it, no rendered pixel changes, no arithmetic or safety guarantee is touched. IMPROVEMENT is the right level, as the finder had it

#### `ui-plumbing-8` — Label()'s fingerprint identifies the font by the LENGTH of its name, so a font change to an equal-length face is skipped

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Widgets.mqh:232`** · category: memory

```mql5
      long   fp = Mix(Mix(Mix(Mix(x, y), (long)col), size),
                      (long)StringLen(font));
      if(Same(n, fp, text))
         return true;
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Label() keys the font by StringLen(font) instead of storing it, so a change between two equal-length face names would be skipped. Unreachable in the shipped build (SSR_FONT and SSR_FONT_MONO are both "Tahoma" and are the only fonts any call site passes), and it stays unreachable even after the theme splits them unless the replacement face happens to have the same name length - so this is a latent cache-key defect with no user-observable effect, not a live LOW.

**Why:** font is the one string argument Label folds into the numeric fingerprint instead of storing whole, unlike text which is kept in m_ct[] precisely so it cannot be confused. Today it cannot bite, because SSR_FONT and SSR_FONT_MONO are both "Tahoma" (SSR_Theme.mqh:424-425) and every call site passes one of them. SSR_FONT_MONO exists only so they can be split later - SSR_Theme.mqh:29-31: "SSR_FONT_MONO is kept as a separate name pointing at the same face, so if a terminal ever proves otherwise it is one line to split them again" - and that one line is the trigger for this.

**Verifiers:** The code fact is as quoted - Label() folds only StringLen(font) into the fingerprint (SSR_Widgets.mqh:230-233) - but the described call is unreachable, and doubly so. SSR_FONT and SSR_FONT_MONO are the SAME literal 'Tahoma' (SSR_Theme.mqh:424-425), and a repo-wide grep shows every Label/Text call site passes one of those two macros and no font string literal is passed anywhere (the only other font write is ObjectSetString(OBJPROP_FONT, SSR_FONT_MONO) in SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:192, which bypasses the cache entirely). So today no caller can even present two different font strings, let alone two of equal length. Splitting the macros is not sufficient to trigger it either: the skip needs the two faces to have name strings of IDENTICAL length, which the plausible splits (Tahoma/6 vs Courier New/11, Consolas/8, Verdana/7) do not. A latent seam worth closing by storing the font whole or mixing its characters, not a defect in this build / Provable from the two lines quoted: Label() folds only (long)StringLen(font) into the fingerprint (SSR_Widgets.mqh:232-233) and Keep() stores only the numbers and the text (86-94), so the font name's identity is never retained. Same() therefore returns true for a request that differs ONLY in font among equal-length faces, and ObjectSetString(OBJPROP_FONT) at line 244 is skipped - the object keeps the old face. This is settled from source and needs no runtime assumption; the asymmetry with text (kept whole in m_ct[] precisely so it cannot be confused) is real. It has no symptom in this build because SSR_FONT and SSR_FONT_MONO are both "Tahoma" (SSR_Theme.mqh:424-425) and grep shows no other face is ever passed - the only non-default call sites are SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:180/222/229 passing SSR_FONT_MONO - so the write would be a no-op anyway. Severity stays LOW: a latent trap in a public primitive, armed by the one-line split the theme header explicitly contemplates / The code reads exactly as quoted (Widgets:232-235): the font is folded into the numeric fingerprint as StringLen(font), so two equal-length face names are indistinguishable and ObjectSetString(OBJPROP_FONT) is skipped. That is a genuine correctness hole in the cache key. But the impact today is exactly zero and cannot be provoked by any user action: SSR_Theme.mqh:424-425 define SSR_FONT and SSR_FONT_MONO both as "Tahoma", and a repo-wide grep shows the only non-default font argument anywhere is SSR_FONT_MONO at SSR_RangeDialog.mqh:180/222/229 - the same string. No call site can currently pass two different faces to one id, and if the two tokens were split to names of different lengths (Tahoma/Consolas, Tahoma/Courier New) the length would differ and the bug still would not fire; it needs an equal-length pair specifically. Latent maintenance hazard, not a LOW user-facing defect

#### `ui-plumbing-9` — SSR_C_THUMB_EDGE and SSR_C_TICK are declared in all three palettes and drawn by nothing; the slider outlines its thumb with SSR_C_TRACK_EDGE instead

**`MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Ui/SSR_Widgets.mqh:492`** · category: boundary

```mql5
      return Rect(id + "_th", tx, y - 3, 4, h + 6,
                  SSR_C_THUMB, SSR_C_TRACK_EDGE);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate as stated, but note the scope: there is no functional or runtime defect - two of 51 tokens per palette are dead declarations, and drawing the thumb with SSR_C_TRACK_EDGE is a defensible choice (the thumb then continues the groove's own outline, which is the line the slider comment says the frame behind supplies). The substance is dead tokens plus an unenforced 'the token named for a part is the token that part draws with' rule, not a visible fault.

**Why:** The palettes carry 51 tokens each and the audit A17 rule is that no colour may be written outside this file - which is satisfied - but there is no audit that a declared token is used, nor that the token named for a part is the token that part draws with. The theme's own thumb argument (SSR_Theme.mqh:409-420) reasons carefully about SSR_C_THUMB against three surfaces and never mentions the edge.

**Verifiers:** Verified exactly. grep over every .mqh/.mq5 returns SSR_C_THUMB_EDGE and SSR_C_TICK only at their three definitions each (SSR_Theme.mqh:119/120, 186/187, 261/262) - no reader anywhere. SSR_C_THUMB has exactly one draw site, Slider()'s thumb at SSR_Widgets.mqh:492-493, which passes SSR_C_TRACK_EDGE as the border. The thumb is 4px wide with BORDER_FLAT and WIDTH 1, so in LIGHT two of its four columns are the 138-grey groove edge over a (20,23,27) fill, as claimed. And the contrast table carries no SSR_CONTRAST line for SSR_C_THUMB_EDGE - only SSR_C_THUMB on TRACK / TRACK_FILL / PANEL (SSR_Theme.mqh:418-420) - so A18 indeed audits nothing for either token. Nothing here refutes the finding / Verified by grep across every .mqh and .mq5: SSR_C_THUMB_EDGE and SSR_C_TICK appear only at their three definitions each (SSR_Theme.mqh:119/120, 186/187, 261/262) and have no readers anywhere. The only thumb draw site is SSR_Widgets.mqh:492-493, which passes SSR_C_TRACK_EDGE as the edge argument of Rect() - and in Rect() the edge argument becomes OBJPROP_COLOR with BORDER_FLAT (SSR_Widgets.mqh:198-201), i.e. the visible 1px border of a 4px-wide object, so the mismatch is not cosmetic-in-theory. The contrast-table observation also holds: the only declared pairs mentioning the thumb are SSR_C_THUMB on TRACK / TRACK_FILL / PANEL (SSR_Theme.mqh:418-420), none for THUMB_EDGE. Two dead palette tokens plus a part drawn with another part's edge colour is confirmed from source; severity is IMPROVEMENT rather than LOW because no wrong-looking pixel is demonstrated - whether a TRACK_EDGE-bordered thumb actually reads badly is a visual judgement the author cannot make without a terminal / The facts check out - grep confirms SSR_C_THUMB_EDGE and SSR_C_TICK appear only at their three definitions each (SSR_Theme.mqh:119/120, 186/187, 261/262) and nothing reads them, and Widgets:492-493 does border the thumb with SSR_C_TRACK_EDGE - but nothing here is a defect. Two unreferenced #defines cost nothing at runtime and are invisible to the user. The colour claim does not survive inspection either: the ACTIVE palette is RAIL, where the thumb is C'230,233,238' (near-white) inside a C'103,111,125' hairline over a C'21,24,28' groove, which reads correctly; the LIGHT case the finding picks is a fallback palette, and a near-black thumb with a 138-grey hairline is a legible design choice, not a rendering fault. The audit argument is self-contradicting: the finding states the contrast table declares no pair for SSR_C_THUMB_EDGE and then says A18 'blesses' it - a token no draw site references is simply not in A18's scope. Dead-token cleanup, not a LOW boundary bug

#### `qa-smoke-15` — Stage 12's closed-trade prices are in whole units, the mistake the comment six lines above says was fixed

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/QA/SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:1073`** · category: broker-symbol

```mql5
         lines.DrawClosed(777, t1 - 600, lpx, t1, lpx + 3.0, true, 0.10, 42.0);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Correct on the substance, with one naming error: the trailing 42.0 is DrawClosed's `net` parameter (net profit, used for the win/loss colour and the exit tooltip at SSR_TradeLines.mqh:521 and 562), not an R value. And the impact is confined to the harness - the objects are created, the check passes, and the only loss is that a stage which quotes a visibility lesson draws its evidence off-screen on any 5-digit symbol while contradicting the points rule stated six lines above it.

**Why:** Lines 1006-1014 in this same stage state the rule and the cost of breaking it - 'OFFSETS IN POINTS, NOT IN WHOLE UNITS. This said lpx - 5.0. On gold at 4438 that is a stop below the entry; on EURUSD at 1.15965 it is MINUS 3.84 ... three stages failed and named the product' - and line 1016 duly uses lpx - 500 * pt and lpx + 1000 * pt. The DrawClosed call seven lines later was not converted. `pt` is already in scope from line 914.

**Verifiers:** Line 1073 and its repeat at 1080 pass `lpx + 3.0` where lpx = acct.Bid() (a 5-digit replay price ~1.16), i.e. a whole-unit offset. CSSRTradeLines::DrawClosed (SSR_TradeLines.mqh:506-583) rejects only `open_price <= 0.0 || close_price <= 0.0` and `time <= 0`, then creates _A, _B and _L unconditionally, so the assertion at 1074-1078 (ObjectFind >= 0 on all three) passes on objects placed thousands of points off the visible range. Lines 1006-1014 in the same block state the points rule and the cost of breaking it, and 1016 obeys it with `lpx - 500 * pt` / `lpx + 1000 * pt`; `pt` is in scope from line 914 and used seven lines earlier. The conversion was simply not applied to this call / The whole-unit offset is real and inconsistent with the rule stated seven lines above: 1006-1014 spell out 'OFFSETS IN POINTS, NOT IN WHOLE UNITS', 1016 duly uses `lpx - 500 * pt` / `lpx + 1000 * pt`, and 1073/1080 still pass `lpx + 3.0` with `pt` in scope since 914. CSSRTradeLines::DrawClosed (SSR_TradeLines.mqh:506-583) rejects only open_price/close_price <= 0 and open_time/close_time <= 0, so the objects are created and the check passes. Two corrections to the claim, though: the 42.0 argument is `net` (used only to pick the win/loss colour at 521 and in the exit tooltip at 562), not an R value, so nothing 'seeds a nonsense R'; and no assertion is weakened today, because the stage's checks are ObjectFind-only and would be blind to placement at any price / Confirmed against both sides. The stage states the rule and the price it paid for breaking it at l.1006-1014 and converts the DrawPosition call at l.1016 to `500 * pt` / `1000 * pt`, then hands DrawClosed a whole-unit `lpx + 3.0` seven lines later (l.1073, repeated l.1081); `pt` is in scope from l.914. CSSRTradeLines::DrawClosed (SSR_TradeLines.mqh:512) rejects only prices <= 0, so on a five-digit symbol the exit arrow and the trend line are created thousands of points off the visible range and the assertion - which is ObjectFind only - passes on geometry nobody could see. The 42.0 R against a 3.0-unit move is likewise inconsistent but is asserted on by nothing. Severity IMPROVEMENT as filed: no production code path takes these numbers, so there is no user-observable effect at all - only a stage that cannot observe the visibility property its own class comment cares about

#### `qa-smoke-8` — The compact-mode assertion in stage 18 re-implements the production expression, so it cannot fail

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/QA/SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:1667`** · category: boundary

```mql5
         bool compact = pnl.IsCompact();
         Check("the panel picked the mode this chart can hold",
               compact == (pch > 0 && pch < SSR_PANEL_H + 24),
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate, with one nuance: it is not literally incapable of failing. pch is sampled once at 1659 before Create/Render, while the panel re-reads the height inside every Render (679), so a freshly opened chart that answers 0 on the first read and a real height later makes the check FAIL while the panel is behaving correctly. Either way the assertion carries no independent content about the threshold.

**Why:** Line 679 of SSR_Panel.mqh and line 1668 here are the same predicate on the same inputs; pch is read at line 1659 from the same CHART_HEIGHT_IN_PIXELS the panel reads in Render(). A check with independent content would be behavioural - e.g. that the tab strip exists in full mode and does not in compact (which the compact branch already does at 1783-1787 for its half), or that PanelH() equals SSR_PANEL_COMPACT_H when compact.

**Verifiers:** Verified the two predicates are the same expression over the same input. SSR_Panel.mqh:678-679 inside Render(): `int chart_h = (int)ChartGetInteger(m_chart, CHART_HEIGHT_IN_PIXELS); m_compact = (chart_h > 0 && chart_h < SSR_PANEL_H + 24);` and IsCompact() at 2984 just returns m_compact. SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:1659 reads pch from the same CHART_HEIGHT_IN_PIXELS of the same chart and 1667-1668 asserts `compact == (pch > 0 && pch < SSR_PANEL_H + 24)`. A wrong threshold, comparison direction or constant would be mirrored on both sides and still pass. The independent alternatives the finding names do exist: PanelH() returns SSR_PANEL_COMPACT_H when m_compact (427-428), and the compact branch already does a behavioural check at 1783 / The duplication is exact: SSR_Panel.mqh:679 `m_compact = (chart_h > 0 && chart_h < SSR_PANEL_H + 24);` and SSR_QA_Smoke.mq5:1668 `compact == (pch > 0 && pch < SSR_PANEL_H + 24)`, with SSR_PANEL_H from SSR_Theme.mqh:499. So the check re-implements the decision instead of observing a consequence, and a wrong threshold, direction or constant would be mirrored. But 'cannot fail' is not accurate: pch is read at 1659 before Create/Render, while the panel re-reads CHART_HEIGHT_IN_PIXELS inside Render (677) - a just-opened chart that reports 0 on the first read and a real height on the second makes the two predicates disagree and produces a spurious FAIL. It is therefore a non-independent and mildly flaky assertion rather than a tautology / Verified character-for-character: SSR_Panel.mqh:679 sets `m_compact = (chart_h > 0 && chart_h < SSR_PANEL_H + 24)` inside Render(), IsCompact() (2984) returns it, and l.1668 compares that against the same predicate over the same chart. The check establishes nothing independent - a wrong threshold, direction or constant would be mirrored. One correction to the claim: it is not literally incapable of failing. `pch` is read at l.1659 immediately after ChartOpen, before Create()/Render(), so if the freshly opened chart answers 0 for its height on the first read and a real height by the time Render() runs, the two expressions disagree and the check FAILS on a working panel. That makes it a flaky no-op rather than a guaranteed pass. Either way the impact is confined to one report line; IMPROVEMENT, not LOW

#### `tests-b-16` — T10.8 leaves its exported CSV in the trader's journal folder

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T10_Statistics.mq5:533`** · category: object-lifecycle

```mql5
      Check("export succeeded", j.ExportCsv("t10", 2), j.LastError());
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate as a test-hygiene issue. The 'any open-my-latest-journal workflow may pick it up' consequence is speculative — no such picker exists in the source I read; the concrete defect is simply that T10 leaves a synthetic three-trade CSV in the user's journal folder while every comparable test cleans up.

**Why:** CSSRJournal::ExportCsv does `FolderCreate(SSR_JOURNAL_DIR)` then writes SSR_JOURNAL_DIR + "\\" + name + ".csv" (SSR_Journal.mqh:171-176) with name = "t10". Section T10.8 opens the file for reading, FileCloses it (line 570) and ends without a FileDelete; no other line of T10 mentions FileDelete.

**Verifiers:** Confirmed from both sides. SSR_T10_Statistics.mq5:533 calls j.ExportCsv("t10", 2); CSSRJournal::ExportCsv (MQL5/Include/SSReplay/Trading/SSR_Journal.mqh:166-176) does FolderCreate(SSR_JOURNAL_DIR) then FileOpen(SSR_JOURNAL_DIR + "\\" + name + ".csv", FILE_WRITE...), with SSR_JOURNAL_DIR defined as "SSReplay\\journal" at line 23 of that header -- so the artefact is MQL5\Files\SSReplay\journal\t10.csv, the same folder real session journals go to. T10.8 reads the file back and FileClose(h) at line 570, and `grep -n "FileDelete" SSR_T10_Statistics.mq5` returns nothing at all, so no cleanup exists anywhere in the file. The contrast is real: SSR_T12 has six FileDelete calls (lines 164-166, 347, 380, 447). No guard, no teardown, no overwrite-only-in-a-temp-dir path / SSR_T10_Statistics.mq5:533 calls j.ExportCsv("t10", 2); SSR_Journal.mqh:171-176 does FolderCreate(SSR_JOURNAL_DIR) then writes SSR_JOURNAL_DIR + "\\t10.csv" with SSR_JOURNAL_DIR = "SSReplay\\journal" (line 23), i.e. MQL5\Files\SSReplay\journal\t10.csv. T10.8 reopens it for reading and FileCloses it; grep for FileDelete/FolderDelete across the whole of T10 returns nothing, while T12 issues six FileDelete calls. The file therefore persists after the test run / Confirmed. T10.8 calls j.ExportCsv("t10", 2) at line 533; CSSRJournal::ExportCsv does FolderCreate(SSR_JOURNAL_DIR) and writes SSReplay\journal\t10.csv (SSR_Journal.mqh:171-176). The section FileCloses the handle at line 570 and grep shows no FileDelete anywhere in T10, while T12 deletes six .ssr files and three sessions. Impact is genuinely minimal, though: nothing in the product auto-enumerates SSReplay\journal - CSSRClassReport::Scan defaults to SSReplay\class (SSR_ClassReport.mqh:33, 289) and CSSRSessionManager scans SSReplay\sessions - so contamination requires a coach to point SSR_ClassReport's InpFolder at the journal folder by hand, where a synthetic three-trade file named t10 would show up as a 'student'. IMPROVEMENT is the right level and the finder already set it

#### `tests-b-9` — T12.5's guard against storing derived statistics can never fail - no code writes a section named "statistics"

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T12_Session.mq5:445`** · category: session-resume

```mql5
      //--- and the file really does not contain them
      Check("no win rate was stored", !r.Select("statistics"));
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: T12.5's 'no win rate was stored' is a vacuous negative guard rather than an incorrect one: it asserts the absence of a section name nothing writes, instead of asserting the absence of derived keys (win_rate, profit_factor, average_r) anywhere in the file. Test-coverage gap, not a product defect.

**Why:** CSSRStatsEngine::SaveInto opens with `f.Section("equity");` and writes nothing else (SSR_Statistics.mqh:623-625). A grep for the literal "statistics" across MQL5/Include/SSReplay finds a single hit - `virtual string Name(void) override { return "statistics"; }` (SSR_Statistics.mqh:296), the observer's name, never a section key. So `r.Select("statistics")` is false for every possible session file this build can produce.

**Verifiers:** Provable from source: the only writers of session sections are f.Section("account"/"execution"/"positions") in SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:1047/1058/1066, f.Section("equity") in SSR_Statistics.mqh:625, plus "session"/"settings"/"stream"/"bookmarks"/"panel"/"presets"/"setup" elsewhere. A repo-wide grep for the literal "statistics" yields exactly two hits: this assertion (T12:445) and the observer name at SSR_Statistics.mqh:296. So r.Select("statistics") is false for every file this build can write and the assertion cannot fail. It also pins the wrong thing: the likely regression - adding win_rate to CSSRStatsEngine::SaveInto - would land under the already-open "equity" section and slip past. Severity reduced to IMPROVEMENT: this is test-hardening, there is no product defect, and a name-pinned negative assertion would still catch a literally-named "statistics" section / Verified: CSSRSessionFile::Select does an exact, case-sensitive name match and returns false with m_cursor=-1 when absent (SSR_SessionFile.mqh:297-310). Every section writer in the build is enumerable and none writes 'statistics': f.Section("account"/"execution"/"positions") in SSR_TradingEngine.mqh:1047/1058/1066, f.Section("equity") in SSR_Statistics.mqh:625 (the whole of CSSRStatsEngine::SaveInto), plus session/settings/stream/bookmarks/panel/presets/setup. So !r.Select("statistics") at T12:445 cannot be false for any file this build can produce - the negative guard for 'derived statistics were never written' probes one name no writer uses, and a regression adding f.Section("stats") or a win_rate key to the equity section would pass unnoticed / The 'can never fail' framing is wrong: this is a negative regression guard, and every negative regression guard passes on correct code by construction. It fails exactly when a writer emits f.Section("statistics") - and that is the single most likely spelling, because "statistics" is what CSSRStatsEngine::Name() returns (SSR_Statistics.mqh:296), i.e. the name the subsystem identifies itself by. So the assertion does bite on the most probable form of the mistake. The finder's counterexample substitutes a different section name ("stats") to manufacture the miss, which demonstrates incomplete coverage rather than vacuity. The genuine invariant is also defended twice over in the same section: T12.5 recomputes trades, net_profit, profit_factor, average_r and ambiguous_trades from the restored trades and asserts they match, so a stored-and-believed derived figure would have to agree with the recomputation anyway, and the design rationale is written out at SSR_Statistics.mqh:605-622. No reachable product state is affected

#### `tests-b-15` — T13.6's unsupported-timeframe assertion passes only because Add checks the timeframe before the duplicate name

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T13_Strategy.mq5:459`** · category: input-validation

```mql5
      Check("and an unsupported timeframe too",
            !host.Add(GetPointer(b), PERIOD_W1));
      Check("named as unsupported",
            StringFind(host.LastError(), "timeframe") >= 0, host.LastError());
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: Accurate as written, with the caveat that nothing is currently broken: the timeframe assertion in T13.6 is order-coupled to CSSRStrategyHost::Add's guard sequence and would silently start asserting the duplicate-name message if the guards were reordered. Fix is to use a fresh strategy with an unused name for the W1 case.

**Why:** CSSRStrategyHost::Add validates in the order NULL, capacity, `if(!SSRIsSupportedTimeframe(tf))` -> "unsupported timeframe", empty name, then the duplicate-name scan -> "two strategies named ... could not be told apart". `b` is a CSSRRefBreakout whose Name() is the constant "ref-breakout" (SSR_RefStrategy.mqh:48) and the section registered `a`, the same class, at line 448 - so both refusal reasons apply to this call and only the guard order decides which message LastError carries.

**Verifiers:** CSSRStrategyHost::Add (SSR_StrategyHost.mqh:105-144) validates in the order NULL (108), capacity (110), SSRIsSupportedTimeframe -> 'unsupported timeframe' (112-113), empty name (114), duplicate-name scan -> 'two strategies named ... could not be told apart' (122-128). T13.6 registers a (CSSRRefBreakout, Name() == "ref-breakout", SSR_RefStrategy.mqh:48) at line 448 and then passes b - a second CSSRRefBreakout with the same constant name - to the PERIOD_W1 call at line 459. Both refusal reasons therefore apply and only the guard order decides which string LastError carries, so the 'named as unsupported' assertion at line 461 is order-dependent rather than a test of the timeframe rule. Confirmed as described, but it is test brittleness: the timeframe guard is genuinely exercised today and no product behaviour is wrong, hence IMPROVEMENT / Guard order verified at SSR_StrategyHost.mqh:106-127: NULL, capacity, !SSRIsSupportedTimeframe(tf) -> 'unsupported timeframe', empty name, then the duplicate-name scan -> 'two strategies named ... could not be told apart'. T13:448 registers `a` (CSSRRefBreakout, Name() is the constant "ref-breakout" at SSR_RefStrategy.mqh:48) and T13:454 has already had `b` (same class, same name) refused as a duplicate, so the Add(GetPointer(b), PERIOD_W1) at T13:459 satisfies both refusal conditions and only the guard order decides which string LastError carries. The assertion is correct today but is not isolating the rule it names / Guard order verified at SSR_StrategyHost.mqh:105-127: NULL, capacity, !SSRIsSupportedTimeframe(tf) -> 'unsupported timeframe', empty name, then the duplicate-name scan -> 'two strategies named ... could not be told apart'. b is a CSSRRefBreakout whose Name() is the constant "ref-breakout" (SSR_RefStrategy.mqh:48) and a - the same class - was registered at T13:448, so both refusal reasons apply to the PERIOD_W1 call at line 460 and only the guard order decides which message LastError() carries. Accurate and correctly self-rated IMPROVEMENT: no product behaviour is wrong (both refusals are the right answer, Add returns false either way, host.Count() stays 1), and the only exposure is that a future reordering of Add's guards would make the assertion at line 461 fail while the timeframe rule it names still works - a misleading red, not a missed bug. Fix is one fresh strategy with an unused name

#### `tests-b-14` — T15.5's "cannot trade" assertion is a tautology: ReadState Init()s the struct before refusing

**`MQL5/Scripts/SSReplay/Tests/SSR_T15_Ux.mq5:336`** · category: zero-empty

```mql5
      SSRUiState st;
      Check("nothing to read", !port.ReadState(st));
      Check("cannot trade",   !st.can_trade);
```

**What goes wrong** *(as corrected by the verifiers)*: The assertion is vacuous with respect to the can_trade RULE, but it is not pointless: CSSRPanel::Render calls m_port.ReadState(m_state) and discards the bool (SSR_Panel.mqh ~:638-643), so the Init()-on-entry guarantee is precisely what keeps a refused read from greying nothing. T15.5:336 pins that contract; what is missing is a separate case with an attached account and no price to exercise out.can_trade = (m_acct.Bid() > 0.0).

**Why:** CSSRGroupPort::ReadState begins `out.Init();` and only then returns false when Primary() is NULL (SSR_GroupPort.mqh, ReadState head). SSRUiState::Init sets `can_trade = false;` (SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:273). The live value is computed at SSR_GroupPort.mqh:244 as `out.can_trade = (m_acct.Bid() > 0.0);`, a line this section can never reach. The section's other assertions (Buy/Sell/CloseAll/BreakEvenAll refused with a reason) do exercise the port and are sound.

**Verifiers:** CSSRGroupPort::ReadState opens with `out.Init();` and only then `if(c == NULL) return false;` (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:100-105), and SSRUiState::Init sets can_trade=false (SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:273). The live assignment `out.can_trade = (m_acct.Bid() > 0.0);` sits at SSR_GroupPort.mqh:242 inside the `if(m_acct != NULL)` block, unreachable on this path. So SSR_T15_Ux.mq5:337 `Check("cannot trade", !st.can_trade)` cannot fail for any implementation of the can_trade rule - it asserts Init(), not the port's judgement. Defect exists exactly as described; LOW because it is a vacuous test assertion, not a product fault, and the surrounding Buy/Sell/CloseAll/BreakEvenAll assertions do exercise real refusal paths / The mechanism is exactly as described: CSSRGroupPort::ReadState opens with out.Init() and only then returns false on Primary()==NULL (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:100-105), SSRUiState::Init sets can_trade=false (SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:273), and the live computation at SSR_GroupPort.mqh:244 is unreachable for a group with no controllers. So T15.5:336 cannot fail for any reason other than Init() forgetting the field, and a regression in the can_trade rule is invisible there. Downgraded to IMPROVEMENT rather than LOW because the assertion is weak, not worthless - see corrected_claim / Factually right: CSSRGroupPort::ReadState starts with out.Init() (SSR_GroupPort.mqh:102) and returns false at l.104-105 when Primary() is NULL, and SSRUiState::Init sets can_trade = false (SSR_ReplayPort.mqh:273), so the assertion cannot fail for any implementation of the live computation at SSR_GroupPort.mqh:244. But the impact is nil: the expected value is still the correct one for this scenario, the four assertions that follow (Buy/Sell/CloseAll/BreakEvenAll refused with a reason) do exercise the bare port, and T15.2 covers the no-price refusal path that can_trade mirrors. This is a redundant assertion, not a defect a user can ever observe, so it belongs at IMPROVEMENT rather than LOW

A sweep of every Section() label across the Tests directory shows T5.9 is the only duplicated numbered section, so the uniqueness sub-claim is also right.
